# crdtsync — worklist

**Derived from [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md).** ARCHITECTURE is the end-state — the full scope + design, everything meant to be built *eventually*. This board is the **prioritized breakdown of what's not built yet**: a rolling queue `cs-next` cuts from ARCHITECTURE and **refills as it drains**. It is regenerable — if the board and the code disagree, the code wins. Status lives here + in the code, never in ARCHITECTURE; design changes that implementation forced are logged in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md).

`/cs-next` reads this + the dependency graph, replenishes the queue from ARCHITECTURE when it's thin, breaks work into units, and hands the next to `/cs-implement`. **Test suites are the spec** — a unit is "done" when its suite is green + Miri-clean, merged to `main`. Breakdown + prioritization is autonomous; the human only edits ARCHITECTURE.

## Dependency order

Bottom-up; never advance a unit whose dependencies are red.

```
host → stamp → clientid → elementid → scalar → counter → register → element → map
                                                    ↓
                          op envelope → doc (transact/apply, buffering, displacement)
                                                    ↓
                       list (Fugue) → text (codepoint) → binary codec
                                                    ↓
              wire framing → room hub → session driver → connection registry → websocket
                                                    ↓
                            persistence → state codec → compaction → client session
                                                    ↓
                                    SDKs (FFI / wasm / Python / Go)
```

Element + Map are one coupled unit (Map slots hold Elements; Element forwards lifecycle to composites).

---

## ✅ Done (on `main`)

_Derived from code + git; a convenience view, not the source of truth._

**C14 — a redacted op delta left the same re-mint hole, and no frame could carry the ids it withheld (crates/server + crates/core) — DONE (#PR).** C9 (#357) closed the *snapshot* seam by keeping the recipient's own ids in a projected snapshot's frontier; an uncompacted room serves an op delta, and the per-op read filter is authorship-blind, so it withheld the recipient's own ops on paths it may no longer read. Reproduced on C9's own fixture before anything was written: a reader with write at `/` and read on `/a` writes into `/b` then `/a`, restarts, `next_seq` reports **0**, and the next write mints straight onto its own withheld id and dedups away at ingest, silently. **The state encoding was the carrier for the snapshot; an `Ops` frame has none, so one is added.** `Message::Frontier { channel, seqs, reach }` (tag 53, server→client) leads a redacted catch-up delta; `Document::note_published` folds what it names into a `reserved` set the mint searches. **C9 explicitly refused a wire frontier and every one of its four reasons is seam-specific**, which is why the answer flips here: it supplies no number to the counter (one named sequence is one held id and one search step — a frame naming `u64::MAX` leaves a fresh replica minting `0`); it carries the codebase's own decode bound; "a scalar cannot express a hole" is exactly why this is a **set**; and "`seen` rides `encode_state`" was the case *for* `seen` on the snapshot seam, while the delta frame has no state to ride — and what it installs rides `encode_state` anyway (`STATE_VERSION` 15). **Sequences, not `OpId`s**, so no other replica's id space is representable and the "only the recipient's own" rule is a property of the frame rather than a check on it — and the frame carries none of the targets or content that made the filing's other candidate shape (stop scrubbing the reader's own ops) unshippable. **A falsification pass broke the first cut twice and both are the substance.** *(1) A mint reads two records and the first cut carried one.* The lamport position comes from `mint_floor`, and the snapshot seam keeps it (`scrub_high_water_to`); the first frame carried only sequences, so every id derived from a stamp **alone** — an ACL tuple's, a ranged element's, an XML sequence child's — re-derived across a redacted delta. Measured: a restarted reader's next mark took the byte-identical `ElementId` of the mark its delta withheld, and the room dropped it at ingest. The frame now carries `reach`, taken from the withheld ops themselves so a branch stream reports its own and the figure is the tighter of the two available, clamped exactly as `record_stamp` clamps a folded stamp. *(2) Naming a sequence must not be refusing its op.* The first cut folded the run into the dedup set following C9, whose justification — "the ops sit below the snapshot's sequence, so no later delivery meets them" — a delta does not have: the client's cursor advances by the *delivered* batch length (C117), so a reader whose run ends in the withheld subtree resumes from below its own last ops and a widened grant re-serves exactly them. Measured: both dropped as replays while another author's write into the same subtree folded normally. The ids now go to `reserved`, cleared by `apply` when the op arrives. **The frontier leads the delta** because a provider opens the socket to app traffic *on a frame* and an app can author between two. Both the Python and JS providers completed the initial sync on the wrong frame (JS's gate read `lastSeenSeq(channel) !== undefined`, satisfied from the moment the channel is held, so *any* frame completed it); both now match the catch-up reply by tag and channel — an allowlist, Go's shape, chosen over excluding the new tag because a denylist is re-broken by the next server-directed frame. **The live fan-out is deliberately given nothing**, on the invariant that a replica applies its own op at authoring time — which the falsification pass broke as stated (two connections declaring one `ClientId` under one actor), so it is restated as a property of one replica per identity and filed as **C130**. Spec `crates/server/tests/delta_frontier.rs` (12), `crates/core/tests/op_seq_high_water.rs` (+15), `crates/core/tests/protocol.rs` (+4), `sdks/python/tests/test_provider_lifecycle.py` (+1), `sdks/js/test/provider.lifecycle.test.ts` (+1). ARCHITECTURE §Wire-Level Redaction + DECISIONS 2026-08-09. Three residues filed: **C130** (the live seam's exemption rests on one replica per identity), **C131** (the read half of the identity boundary C23 closed for writes), **C132** (the frame buys durably-retained ids at 8 wire bytes each, bounded only by the frame cap). → *Server / Fan-out*.

**C54 — a replicated room's op-version high-water is never carried, so the handshake range-check is inert on every replica (crates/server) — DONE (#396).** Found during C29 (#374), the sibling field of the same gap; filed as C34, renumbered because that id was taken. `Room::max_op_version` is the worst-case governing-app op version a joiner must down-reach, and `subscriber_reaches_governing` refuses an under-versioned joiner against it — but it was raised only by the version a client write carries into `Hub::ingest` (`schema_version`), which neither replication path supplied (`apply_replicate` ingests relay-style `None`; `install_snapshot` installs `Document` bytes carrying none). Measured against `main` on a two-node harness: a replica converged by ops and one installed from a state transfer both report `None`, a v1 joiner across a breaking rename is **admitted** on each, and admitted on the promoted leader after a failover. The admitted joiner is then served the room's ops **verbatim** — a replicated batch is untranslated (C71) and a replica's log untagged, so nothing down-translates; the harm is a client handed a state its own version cannot model, past the one check that exists to refuse it. `clone_room` reported `None` for the same reason. **The binding had to travel with it, and that is forced rather than adjacent:** a high-water is a number in the governing app's version space, `governing_target` abstains on an unbound room, and a pure-replication replica has neither a subscriber nor a store record to bind it — so carrying only the number would leave the *first* joiner at a replica admitted and every later one refused against a binding that first joiner invented at its own version. `persist_meta` already writes the two beside the creator as one `RoomMeta`; they now ride the wire as one record too. `Message::Replicate` and `Message::ReplicateSnapshot` gain `governing` and `max_op_version`, each composing against what the replica holds rather than replacing it — the root set-once (C29), the high-water as a **max** (the all-time worst case, so a re-sent or lower-naming frame never talks it down), the binding through `Registry::bind_room_app`, the same incumbent-app rule a subscribe and the durable load take — and the high-water **only under the app that won that composition**, since a frame whose app was refused names a number in a space the room is not read in, which the first cut adopted anyway and a cold review caught. **The filing's second half is settled explicitly, not incidentally: the follower's log stays a verbatim mirror.** `apply_replicate` still ingests with `None`; re-tagging is rejected because it cannot carry the fact at all on the ops-less state-transfer path, because one version per frame would mislabel a catch-up batch whose ops `Catchup::Ops` says may mix versions, and because **C71 owns it by name** including the mixed-version question. C54 does not close C71: a joiner the check now admits on a replica is still served the delta untranslated. The cost of that reading is stated — a replica's logged ops carry no versions, so it cannot rebuild its high-water from its own log, which widens **C55**'s failed-persist route to all three fields. This unit also closes the **replication half of C62** (the binding travels), leaving that unit its import case; and it closes the eviction hole it opens — a replicated lift now evicts a stranded follower-local subscriber on both seams, on the identical predicate the subscribe gate admits on, since before the change no replicated frame could lift a follower's high-water at all. The **ordering** is the load-bearing half and was measured wrong first: a leader's stranded peer receives nothing because the leader's fan-out translates and drops it, while a replica's fan-out is verbatim — so the eviction runs *before* the fan-out here, or the doomed peer is handed the op and told to update afterwards. **One resolution for the room's binding, which a falsification pass forced:** the replication record is read the way a write's own version tag reads it (the presence map, falling back to the hub), because a sweep prunes each source in a different case — the hub's for a room it does not yet hold, the map's for a room nobody subscribes. Reading the hub alone made a swept leader emit a bare high-water that every replica then discarded, re-opening this unit's own defect through its own gate; measured, and pinned. The clone seam keeps the same pair together, so no seam here mints a number with no app to read it in. **C55 (#397) built a metadata-only carrier after this was written** — `Message::ReplicateMeta`, the root with no ops beneath it — and the binding and high-water have exactly the shape that wants one: a replica logs its leader's batch untagged, so it cannot re-derive either from its own log. Widening that frame to the whole record is **C125**, deliberately left to it, because a root is set-once while these are not and a metadata-only assertion has to say whether it asserts the leader's current value or repairs a lost one. Residue filed as **C129** (a subscriber admitted against an unbound room's high-water is never re-checked once it binds the room itself — pre-existing, needs a ruling) and **C120**: the max-compose is unrepairable, so a cluster member asserting `u32::MAX` pins a replica's high-water there permanently — measured, every governing-app joiner is then refused and an honest later frame cannot bring it back down. Spec `crates/server/tests/replicated_op_version.rs` (32) plus the wire round-trips and truncation sweeps in `crates/core/tests/protocol_replicate.rs`. Design in DECISIONS (2026-08-09). → *Server / Replication*.

**C55 — a creator established by a duplicate write never reached the replicas (crates/server) — DONE (#397).** Found during C29 (#374), the residual case of its own fix. `ensure_creator` fired on any `Ok` from `Hub::ingest` — a batch the room's dedup swallowed whole included — while `Registry::deliver` enqueued replication only `if !broadcast.is_empty()`. So a room established anonymously (root `None`, replicated as such) whose next authenticated write was a pure resend of ops the hub already held — a client that wrote over an anonymously-admitted connection, authenticated, and flushed its outbox — gained its authority root on the leader **with no frame to carry it**, and the replicas stayed creatorless until the room's next *fresh* commit. **A second route reached the same state:** persisting the root is best-effort and set-once retries nothing, so a replica whose metadata write failed reloaded creatorless — and, serving no client write, waited on a commit a quiescent room never makes, since `catch_up_room_frame` declines to send an empty delta. Both leave `reads_whole_document` short-circuiting `true` on that replica, so every doc-ACL deny in the room decides nothing there — the hole C29 exists to close. **Fix: `Message::ReplicateMeta { room, epoch, creator }`,** the root with no ops beneath it. No branch and no sequence (it advances no stream), unacknowledged (there is no watermark to report), fenced against `main` like every other replication frame, and composed through the same `Hub::ensure_creator` every arrival seam uses — set-once, anonymous roots refused. **It never creates the room:** a node missing it is converged by the ops or snapshot catch-up, which carries the root itself. Sent at the two seams the routes name: `Registry::deliver` when a write established a root and broadcast nothing (`Hub::ensure_creator` now answers whether *this call* established it, carried out as `Response::root_established` — the signal C29 lacked), and `catch_up_room_frame` where it used to send nothing, so a follower rejoining a quiescent room is re-rooted by the dial. Both address the same followers through one `replication_followers` gate. **Rejected, in DECISIONS:** an empty `Replicate` — measured, not argued: handing a follower that does not hold the room an empty-batch `Replicate` leaves `holds_room` answering **true** for an empty replica at the head, pinned beside the metadata-only frame declining the same room in the same state and the root on the `ReplicaAck` round-trip (an ack answers a frame, and both routes are defined by there being none — a quiescent room would still wait for the commit whose absence is the defect). **C99's question is deliberately left open:** `ensure_creator` still admits a deduped batch where C23's claim does not, and this unit makes the root converge across the replica set whichever way that ruling goes — while route 2 needs a carrier no establishment rule supplies. **Cold review found the "creates no room" invariant enforced one layer too late.** `gate_replica_frame` advances *and persists* the leadership fence before its caller sees a verdict, and `Store::load` materialises a room from an epoch record alone — so on a store-backed node the frame wrote a `.epoch` file with no room behind it and the next restart came up holding an empty replica at the head, the very state an empty `Replicate` was rejected for. Reachable with no attacker, through C99's empty-`Ops` shape. Both assertion-free frames are now dropped *ahead of the gate* — an unheld room, and a frame naming no root, the latter otherwise letting a peer pin this node's fence at `u64::MAX` with contentless bytes. A dial also orders its frames now (`DialFrames`): the peer channel is bounded and drops on overflow, and a converged follower's reconnect newly costs one repair frame per rooted room, so every delta is queued ahead of every root. **An adversarial pass then falsified six claims, all corrected here** — five of them in the prose rather than the code: the route-1 narration said a client authenticated *in place* on an anonymous connection, which the session refuses as a violation (it reconnects); the dial-ordering rationale claimed a dropped delta is unrecoverable, and a dropped frame advances no watermark so both kinds return on the next dial (the real asymmetry is volume, and the entry says so); "every absence-negative also shows the seam is live" was untrue for five tests, so the claim is scoped and the two that *could* show it were rewritten to; the `u64::MAX` fence hazard survives the new guard through `Replicate` and overflows rather than sticking (**C128**); `drop_persisted_meta`'s comment described `atomic_write`'s failure as leaving no record when it leaves the *prior* one; and the catch-up's "a node missing the room is converged by the ops or snapshot catch-up" was false for a **zero-op** rooted room, which is now dialed nothing rather than an inert root forever. Specs: `crates/server/tests/creator_replication_repair.rs` (20, three Miri-skipped for driving the store on the filesystem), `crates/core/tests/protocol_replicate.rs` (5). Wire: `Message::ReplicateMeta`, tag 53. Filed **C125** (the same frame is where C54's op-version high-water repair belongs), **C126** (a bare epoch record still materialises an empty room on load) and **C127** (the metadata write is best-effort at all seven seams and nothing retries it — weighed as this unit's fix and rejected, since it would close *neither* route: a deduped-resend root has no frame whatever the disk did, and a quiescent replica has no next write to hang a retry on). See DECISIONS (2026-08-09). → *Server / Replication*.
**C31 — the catch-up `seq` was the room's global op counter, served unnarrowed to a zone-limited reader (crates/server) — DONE (#395).** Closes the two watermark scalars it names; §Zones' promise as a whole is *not* closed by it, and the remaining routes are enumerated as C115–C119 below. Filed during C15 (#368); **reproduced first, at the wire**: a `za`-limited reader reads the pair `(7, 12)` across a window of five writes into `zb` and `(7, 7)` across an idle one, on both `Message::Snapshot` and `Message::VersionState`. Their content is narrowed to the reader's partitions and their causal frontier is scrubbed to the recipient's own ids — `Document::scrub_high_water_to` pays a whole encode/decode round-trip to close exactly this channel on the id-space record — while the `seq` tagging them was the room's whole-log head, so the difference between two readings counted the ops written into partitions the reader is never served, against §Zones' promise that a hidden partition leaks "not the ops, snapshot, structure, existence, or size, and cannot infer activity from clock jumps". Enumerable rather than theoretical: version names are a room-read fact and `autoVersion` schedule triggers mint timestamped ones, so a reader walks the captures and charts a hidden zone's write volume from the scalars alone. **The ruling the filing left open — what a narrowed reader's watermark *means* — is a room sequence it was actually served, not a per-reader count.** **and it is two answers, not one — which the falsification pass forced.** On the **catch-up** frame, `Hub::partition_head`: the highest retained sequence in the reader's `(room, branch)` stream whose op its own zone scope admits, `0` where the retained log holds nothing it may see, floored at the watermark the reader arrived holding since that scalar *is* a resume cursor. On the **version** frame, `0` always. Giving both seams the `partition_head` answer was wrong on the version one in the unit's own terms: `partition_head` reads the *retained* log, auto-compaction fires on that log's length, so re-reading **one fixed capture** flipped when hidden writes alone pushed the floor past the reader's last visible op — measured `7 → 7` on `main` against `7 → 0` on the first draft, i.e. a channel opened where the unnarrowed scalar had none. A version read feeds no cursor and carries no floor, so it can refuse the field, and refusing is what makes its answer independent of the room's volume; One cost of refusing rather than narrowing, stated: the answer follows the *channel's scope*, not the actor's authority, so a fully-authorized client that opens a zone-scoped channel for its own reasons — §Zones calls zones a perf optimization, so self-narrowing is first-class — is told `0` on that channel while its whole-room channel reads the true point. Nothing consumes the field, so the cost is presently nil; it is named rather than discovered later. the narrowing is gated on `zone_narrowing`, the same predicate that decides whether the *state* projection runs, so the scalar and the bytes narrow together — including where that predicate declines, which is C106's shape and where the state goes out whole too, so the scalar tells that reader nothing the bytes have not; a whole-room reader is told the head unchanged. **Why not the per-reader count or the opaque token the filing floated:** `last_seen_seq` has two jobs and both need a room sequence — `Hub::catch_up` subtracts the compaction floor from it and *indexes the log*, and `read_redirect_response` compares it against the answering node's committed watermark — so a count indexes the wrong ops and is not comparable to a node's watermark at all, degrading the follower-read gate to always-admit or always-redirect, while making a count work needs a per-reader mapping that survives disconnects and scope changes and has no answer below the compaction floor. The token is the same number under a key, at the price of cluster-wide key material every follower can decrypt, stored client floors invalidated by a rotation, and `last_seen_seq`'s wire type changed through the codec, `ClientSession` and four SDKs. Read-your-writes survives, though not for the reason a first draft gave — a readable write sits at or below the watermark only *above* the compaction floor, and below it the watermark collapses to `0` and orders nothing. What carries it is that a readable retained write is admitted by the reader's own scope, and that a reader at `0` is served the stream from its beginning — the whole projected state on a compacted room, the whole log on one that never compacted, never a partial delta over content it lacks. Monotonicity is the half that does not survive; see the costs below. **Scoped to zones deliberately** — §Zones is the section that forbids the inference, while §Wire-Level Redaction says outright that a doc-ACL-denied reader still learns a redacted subtree's structure and activity, so a reader narrowed only by doc-ACL is told the head as before. Spec `crates/server/tests/catchup_watermark.rs` (14), every load-bearing branch mutation-checked — which caught the `owns_base` skip reddening nothing, now pinned by asking a snapshot fork below its first tail sequence and requiring `0` rather than a sequence out of `main`'s log. Every load-bearing branch was mutation-checked — the `zone_narrowing` gate, the version-seam refusal, the `owns_base` skip, the `.max(floor)` clamp and the reverse scan's cross-segment prune each redden the suite when broken, under statement *deletion* and not only condition inversion. Two did not, and neither was shipped unpinned: a `.min(at)` clamp both call sites make unreachable was **removed** and its precondition stated, and a `usize::try_from` fallback that no 64-bit target can reach was rewritten as a total expression. The gate's deletion mutant is what found a real hole — a whole-room reader of a room compacted to an empty retained log, which is every room between a compaction and the next write, was told the head only because `partition_head` coincidentally agreed; a test now requires it. The suite: the inference closed on both scalar seams — five hidden writes handing a `za` reader the same pair of watermarks as an idle window, on the catch-up snapshot and across two named captures — with the counterfactuals that keep it from being vacuous (both readings a live non-zero sequence, so an implementation answering `0` for every narrowed reader fails; a *visible* write moves the watermark, to that op's own room sequence; two captures spanning a visible write differ; a whole-room reader reads each capture's true point, and those differ), plus resume across a reconnect (the narrowed watermark comes back as an op *delta* carrying the reader's own partition's later write, none of the hidden one's, and nothing the watermark already covered), the floor never regressing, a whole-room reader's watermark still the room's head, and both branch stream shapes — a snapshot fork read in its own sequence space at the wire, a live-log fork read at the hub seam across its shared base and tail with the base bounded at the fork point. Two further cases pin the ruling's edges: a **doc-ACL**-narrowed reader — read on the room, a deny carving a subtree out, admitted to every zone — still told the room's head, with the zone-limited control over the same write; and a capture taken below the compaction floor reading `0` for a narrowed reader while a whole-room one reads its true point. The suite does not compile against `main` at all — the new `Hub::partition_head` is called from one hub-only case and from one assertion inside a wire case. Excise those and **eight of the thirteen wire cases fail on `main`**. The five that pass are the deliberate controls, which must pass either way: the whole-room and resume ones, the empty-retained-log one, and the fixed-capture regression guard — which passes on `main` *by design*, since it guards against a draft of this fix rather than against `main`, whose version scalar is already constant for one capture. **Costs of the ruling, stated:** where the retained log holds nothing a reader's scope admits its catch-up watermark is `0`, so it re-takes the whole projected state on each reconnect; and across a compaction that collapse is itself one bit about the room's total volume — strictly less than the exact count `main` hands over, and a refinement of a boundary the `Snapshot`-versus-`Ops` frame type already discloses, so it is folded into **C119** rather than claimed closed. Read-your-writes survives; **monotonicity** does not, for a reader whose watermark collapsed — the follower gate is vacuous at `0`, so a lagging replica may serve where it would have redirected, which is C117's composition reached by a second route. A pre-existing defect this ruling **widens**, measured and filed as **C117**: `ClientSession::receive` advances a room's cursor by each delivered batch's *length*, and the fan-out gives a zone-limited channel only its own partitions' ops, so the client's floor was already a per-reader count. Safe against `catch_up` alone (an under-estimate re-delivers and dedups) but not against the **follower-read gate**, which admits a follower reaching that floor and so admits one *behind* the reader; where that follower's compaction floor sits above the stale floor the catch-up is a `Snapshot`, which the client adopts by replacing the replica outright. The snapshot seam used to pad this by resetting the cursor to the room head. Measured on one fixture: cursor **13 against content held through 14 on `main`, 8 against the same 14 here**. **Five residues filed, and the count is the finding: narrowing these two scalars was necessary and is not sufficient for §Zones' promise, so the promise is now stated with its gap enumerated rather than implied.** **C115** — the value still names a position in the room's *shared* sequence space, and measured it is not a shadow of the closed channel but the same channel at full fidelity: a reader that may write probes its own watermark and reads the hidden volume between two probes at its own resolution (`6 → 14` across seven hidden ops). **C116** — `publish` and `restore_as_branch` mint the raw sequence *into the version name*, which the `Versions` reply hands to any room reader unnarrowed, so the scalar is narrowed while a name beside it spells the number out. **C117** — the client cursor above. **C118** — `Message::Branches` reports `main`'s head, which *is* `Room::head()`, to any reader holding room read: watermark flat at `7` while the branch head went `7 → 12` across the same five hidden writes, needing neither a compaction nor a capture to enumerate. **C119** — the follower-read gate and `catch_up`'s `Snapshot`-vs-`Ops` branch each answer a yes/no on an attacker-chosen floor, binary-searching the node's watermark (recovered `37`, truth `37`) and the compaction floor, the first of them resolving before the reader's zone scope is computed at all. C118 and C119 were found by a **falsification** pass — one asked to break the claim rather than check it — after two cold reviews had passed the unit; they are the reason this entry claims two scalars rather than a section. ARCHITECTURE §Zones + §Follower Read-Serving amended; DECISIONS 2026-08-09. → *Server / Zones*.

**C23 — one admissible op permanently write-locked a victim's mint, room-wide (crates/core + crates/server + ffi/wasm + JS/Python/Go SDKs) — DONE (#389).** Raised by C17 (#366) as the price a bounded id-space record could not pay off. A stamp at exactly `LAMPORT_STATE_CEILING` is a legal position, so `apply` admitted it; `record_stamp` pinned `stamp_high_water[victim]` there, and `mint_floor` reads that entry unconditionally across every partition — so the victim could never mint again, in any zone, on every replica that folded the op, and in the durable snapshot, irreversibly. Authoring it needed only what C8's authorship check allows: `session.rs` compared `op.id.client` against the identity the client *declared* at Hello, and its own comment recorded that authenticating that declaration was the transport's job. Nothing surfaced it either — `Document::can_mint` had no caller in core, server, FFI, wasm, JS, Python or Go, and every SDK mutation path discarded the empty `Vec` a refused `transact` returns, so the failure presented as edits that silently did nothing. **Fix, in the two parts the filing named as the substance.** *(1) The refusal is reported, and is a different question from capacity.* `can_mint` folded the refusal latch into a capacity predicate and answered "no room" for a replica that has room; it is now capacity alone, and `Document::mint_refused` reports whether a local edit in the intention most recently opened was refused. **A refusal is about the ids *this* edit needed, and it is read immediately or not at all**: a run reserves one id per codepoint, so a refused run leaves room for the shorter edit that follows and a refusal is never a claim that the space is empty; the edits a refused intention emitted *before* the refusal are applied and ship; and the reading is cleared by the next intention **opening**, never by one closing, so the caller that ran the transact, the atomic commit or the undo replay is the one holding the answer and a reading taken after a further edit answers for that edit instead — the latch no longer cleared at an intention's *close* (which is exactly what left the caller that ran the transact nothing to read), only as the next one opens, so nothing about when a mint is refused changed. `transact` keeps its signature — fallible would churn ~1300 call sites and `core::path` ~560 — and each SDK reads the report at the one mutation seam its handle graph funnels through: `MintExhausted` thrown in JS / raised in Python, `ErrMintExhausted` in Go (with `Doc.Err()` for the chaining and value-less mutators, Go having no exception to propagate), and `crdtsync_doc_mint_refused`/`crdtsync_client_mint_refused` returning `1`/`0`/`-1` on the C ABI — deliberately **not** a third meaning on the empty `CrdtBuf`, which **C75** records already carries both a bad handle and an inert edit. *(2) A replica identity is bound to an actor server-side.* `Room.client_actors` records, per room, which authenticated actor each replica identity belongs to — set once by that identity's first authenticated writer, never displaced, durable in `RoomMeta` beside the room's creator, which it is the same species of fact as (a stored op carries the per-device `ClientId`, never the credential actor, so the pairing is recorded rather than derived). `handle_ops` refuses a batch whose authoring identity another actor holds, beside the two authorship gates already there; each *channel's* derived identity is claimed as its own, since `for_channel` ids are as public as the base. Per room because an id space is a property of a document; enforced at the **write** rather than the subscribe, because a claim at the subscribe is a read lockout and a first-claim race there would be a sharper denial of service than the defect. The refusal is **recoverable** (`OpsRejected`/`Forbidden`, not a violation): ownership is server-side state a client cannot observe and cannot rotate away from, so a violation would loop a client whose actor legitimately changed. Only a batch that actually **lands** an op claims — an empty one authors no stamp, and one the room deduped away would let whoever replays another replica's historic ops take the identity that wrote them. An **anonymous** actor claims nothing on the rule `ensure_creator` already follows — an id minted per connection would refuse that client's own next connection — leaving an identity whose only writer was anonymous unprotected, stated and tested rather than hidden. Two cold-review passes and cubic drove the shape: the latch's clearing rule, the inert-edit seam in `core::path` (an early return opens no intention, so it left the *previous* edit's refusal standing and reported it as this one's), the JS funnel shipping what a refused call emitted before it, Python raising outside its `finally`, and the claim not being taken by a deduped replay are all review findings with tests. Spec: `crates/core/tests/lamport_mint.rs` (3), `crates/core/tests/client.rs` (1), `crates/server/tests/replica_identity_claim.rs` (13 — the ceiling plant refused with the victim still minting in root *and* zone across the room's own snapshot and still writing after; a derived channel identity claimed as its own; an unclaimed identity admitted and a reconnect unaffected; the anonymous rule; an empty batch claiming nothing; a refused attempt leaving the claim standing; the record restored through `Hub::from_rooms`), `crates/server/tests/store.rs` (1), `crates/ffi/tests/{ffi,client,header}.rs` (3), `crates/wasm/tests/web.rs` (1), `sdks/js/test/mint.test.ts` (3), `sdks/python/tests/test_mint.py` (3), `sdks/go/crdtsync/mint_test.go` (3). ARCHITECTURE §Client ID + §SDK; DECISIONS 2026-08-08. Seven residuals filed: **C94** (part 3 — identity rotation, the escape a locked replica still has none of, which needs a ruling ARCHITECTURE does not contain), **C95** (the claim is a client-plane gate and does not ride the replication frames, so a promoted follower re-bootstraps a room's claims from whoever writes next), **C96** (a claim established by first write is takeable in a room its owner has not written to, and the record's growth is attacker-influenced), two the review passes turned up that pre-date this unit — **C99** (an empty `Ops` frame bootstraps a room's doc-ACL authority root, the same reserve-by-no-op one tier up) and **C100** (a branch write's two-step persist reports a durable write as a failure, so no seam keyed on "this batch landed" fires for it) — **C105** (three Go mutators refused at the language's type boundary never reach the inert seam, so `Doc.Err()` keeps the previous edit's answer; closing it needs an inert step on the C ABI) and **C109** (a networked document reports an over-size blob as *stored*, because the client edit surface frames an edit that enqueued nothing — the size half of `SetBlob`'s answer, found by this unit's cold review and reproduced in Go and JS). → *Core / Security*.

**C33 — a version read is served off any replica, at that replica's ACL freshness (crates/server) — DONE (#394).** Filed during C15 (#368) by the review pass that asked what routes the request. `Message::VersionFetch` and `VersionList` resolved their room and answered from local state with **no routing gate at all** — unlike the version *mutations*, which go to the leader outright, and unlike `Subscribe`, which lets a follower serve only from a materialized replica caught up to the client's floor. **The defect the filing named, and the one that carries the unit, are not the same.** The filing's: a fetch answers with a version's captured bytes projected through the `acl_records` the *answering node* holds, and doc-ACL tuples ride the room's log, so a replica behind an unreplicated revoke redacts by grants it has already lost. True — but on its own it closes no disclosure, and the fix does not claim it does: the same replica's **live** stream is behind by the same records at the same instant and still serves that reader the same subtree, which this unit's own suite asserts. The one that carries it: the version index is written in exactly two places, a local capture and a load of this node's own durable store, so it is **per node, not per room** — a read answered off whichever node holds the channel answers about that node's captures, not the ones the client's own mutations built. Create-then-fetch is the plainest form and is now a test: the mutation is redirected to the leader and lands there, the read is answered here, and the client is told "no such version" for a version it watched itself create. **Deviation from the filing's fix direction, stated — the proposed fix was built first and reverted on evidence.** C33 asked for the `Subscribe` read gate carried by "a freshness floor a version request does not currently carry"; a `last_seen_seq` was added to both frames, filled from the channel's cursor, and gated by `read_redirect_response` — then measured, which disqualified it. A floor bounds a `Subscribe` because a subscribe arrives on a **fresh** connection carrying a cursor accumulated on another node; a version read arrives on an **already-bound** channel, and `ClientSession` advances that channel's cursor only from what the answering node delivered (`+= ops.len()` per batch, `= seq` from that node's snapshot; an `Accepted` moves it not at all). So the floor never exceeds the answering replica's own watermark, the watermark test can never fail, and the gate would refuse nothing — the wire change was reverted rather than shipped inert, and the property pinned as a test. Nor is there another signal: no frame, gossip tuple, or membership field carries the leader's per-room head, and the un-replicated case is by definition the one no frame reports; a "serve where the room carries no ACL records" gate fails open exactly where it matters, since a replica behind the commit that installed the tuples reads an empty record set as "nothing is denied". The leader is not a node whose index is "the room's" — no such index exists; it is the node the mutations already land on, so routing the reads there is what makes a room's captures answerable at all, and it carries the freshest records along with it. Replication carrying the log and never the captures is verified across `Replicate`, `ReplicateSnapshot`, export/import, clone, adoption and the store load, and pinned. So both reads take the leader gate the mutations take, as does **`DiffQuery { kind: Versions }`**, which hands out the same captured bytes through the same projection (the `Branches` arm is left to C103, which has a question a fetch does not). Routing now runs **before** the authorization on every channel-keyed frame of the seam — the ordering `CloneRoom` already states, since `Authorizer::observe` records the verdict and would otherwise leave a durable audit entry for a read the node then refuses; `channel_room` split into `bound_room` + `channel_authorized`. Its one observable cost is pinned rather than left to be found: a reader that bound a channel and then lost its room read is redirected by a replica where it used to be refused, disclosing a room the client itself named and a leader any authenticated actor already resolves through `Subscribe`. The five room-keyed **branch** mutations still authorize first, so they still write that entry — the same defect on another seam's frames, filed as **C112**. The JS provider's redirect handling was widened to fail one request per redirect rather than one per drain, since a replica now redirects reads and a client issues those concurrently; where that pairing mis-attributes is **C114**. Not a blanket centralization: the gate names the room's *effective* leader, so a promoted replica answers its own captures — and how strong that is, is stated rather than implied and filed as **C113**. Two C29 tests changed premise deliberately (`a_version_captured_on_a_replica_withholds_the_denied_key` and `a_diff_served_off_a_replica_withholds_the_denied_key`, both of which opened on the seam not being leader-gated); each now marks the primary down — the case the first's own comment already named — and the property each pins is unchanged. Spec `crates/server/tests/version_read_routing.rs` (16): the three premises first — a capture never crosses the replication seam, a bound channel's cursor never outruns the replica answering it, and a replica behind the ACL commit reads an empty record set as nothing denied, which is what disqualifies a records-empty gate — then create-then-fetch, the client-visible incoherence the unit is for; then the ACL half, a revoke committed and withheld, with its honest limit measured beside it rather than argued (the same node's live catch-up still serves that reader the denied subtree, so the fetch is not a disclosure that seam lacks); the leader serving that fetch redacted; a promoted replica serving its own captures, and a *lagging* promoted one still answering from its own records (C113's residue, pinned as what the code does); the diff seam's version arm gated while the branch arm is not; the list and the mutation likewise; and the fail directions (an unbound channel is a violation not a redirect, a revoked reader routed by the replica and refused by the leader, an unwritten room on its leader still answering, a stranded node refusing rather than answering alone, single-node unchanged). → *Server / Versions*.

**C51 — a branch whose durable base this node cannot read is reported as a branch that does not exist (crates/server) — DONE (#393).** Filed by review during C27 (#375) as reasoned-not-reproduced; **all three cases reproduced first**, each as a failing test before a line of the fix. `Hub::materialize_branch` answered `Option<Vec<u8>>`, and its `None` stood for three unrelated facts about a stream: a name the room's branch registry does not hold; a branch it *does* hold whose base this node cannot read — a durable snapshot base that no longer decodes, the corruption C15's version guard exists for, or — since C60 (#386), over the defect C88 files — a live-log fork whose shared base `main`'s compaction has dropped; and `main` on a room this node holds no replica for, which is every subscribed-but-never-written room. `Hub::diff_branches` mapped all three to `DiffError::UnknownBranch`, so the wire answered `NotFound "unknown branch draft"` for a branch `BranchList` enumerates and `NotFound "unknown branch main"` for a room that simply has no ops yet — both measured at the wire, the second off nothing but a `Subscribe`. **The three had to be told apart rather than merged**, which is what made it more than a widened success case: the publish path reads the same seam and *correctly* declines to freeze an empty replica over the branch it names, so admitting the middle case there would trade a wrong error message for silent durable loss. `materialize_branch` now answers a four-way `BranchState` (`State` / `Empty` / `Absent` / `Unreadable`). The diff seam maps `Absent` to the `DiffError::UnknownBranch` it always was, `Unreadable` to a new `DiffError::UnreadableBranch(name)` surfacing as `Internal` — the code the *version* seam already gives an archived side it cannot read, and neither closes the connection — and `Empty` to the empty document an unwritten room's `main` genuinely is, narrowed by the reader's projection like any other side. `publish` takes `State` alone, so both of its refusals stand unchanged, pinned as tests rather than left to the enum. **One deviation from the filing's fix direction, stated:** it proposed mapping unreadable onto `DiffError::Decode`; a named variant was taken instead, because C88's clipped shared base is not a decode failure and the variant names *which* branch is unreadable, while `Decode` keeps meaning what it says — the re-decode inside `diff_states`. **The residue the filing named is filed rather than covered, as C103**: the diff seam takes no read redirect, so on a follower — which replicates `main` alone — a branch the leader holds is genuinely absent locally, and the honest local answer is the misleading one. That needs the read-routing a diff request does not carry, which is the design C33 owns for the version fetch, so it is the same unit's question and not this one's. Review of the same seam turned up **C104**, filed: a snapshot fork whose base is gone is, at floor 0, not answered `Unreadable` at all but silently *reclassified* as a live-log fork, which is a wrong `Some` and so C53's family rather than this unit's. Spec `crates/server/tests/branch_state_taxonomy.rs` (7): the absent name still `NotFound`, an empty room's `main` both as a self-diff at the wire and against a branch written before `main` ever was, and the unreadable answer in both of its flavours — a genuinely damaged durable base, told from absence at the hub seam and surfacing as `Internal` at the wire, and the flavour that needs no damage at all, a live-log fork whose shared base a routine `compact` has passed, with a control that the same fork diffs fine before the floor moves — plus, on each of the two inputs the diff seam now answers *differently*, the publish refusing both, which is the constraint the split must not break. → *Server / Branches, Diff*.

**C16 — the reveal back-fill's batch dedup dropped the copy that was zone-protected (crates/server) — DONE (#391).** Filed by cold review during C11 (#359) as reasoned-not-reproduced; **reproduced, and the filing's reachability claim was wrong in the direction that made it look narrower than it is.** The live fan-out's reveal prefix force-stamps every shell and back-filled op with the placing move's zone precisely so a per-channel `zone_filter` cannot drop the content while keeping the shell. C11 made the back-fill skip an op the delivered batch already carries — right, since a second untagged copy takes the batch's tagged one out of its transaction's count — but the surviving copy is then the *batch's*, stamped in the partition its author resolved at emit time. `zone_of_op` resolves a tree op against the live tree, and within one commit an edit inside a node precedes the move that relocates it, so the edit takes the partition the node is moving **out of** while the move takes the destination's: a channel scoped to the destination zone kept the shell, the historical back-fill and the move, and dropped the batch's own edit — a materialised node the reader never finishes filling, diverging permanently from a fresh joiner. **C74 did not close it.** C74 changed how *governing-region* ops (Ranged, ACL) derive their partition; a tree op still takes the partition of the container it targets, resolved against the tree as it stands when the op is emitted, which is the whole mechanism. **Reachability is wider than filed: the shape does not need a named branch.** The filing reasoned that a cross-zone move is only un-gated on a branch (`handle_ops` gates `batch_zone_crossings` on `branch == MAIN_BRANCH`), but Zones-4's capability token admits exactly one crossing on `main`, and the batch riding that token may carry any number of *non*-crossing edits inside the moved node — an edit that moves a node the destination zone already holds into the revealed node crosses nothing, so one token authorizes the whole batch. That is the regression fixture: a card born in `/board` (za, doc-ACL-denied to the reader) carrying a private grandchild, a pin already in `/notes/pub` (zb, readable), one atomic transaction moving the pin into the card and then the card into `/notes/pub`, and a reader scoped to zb who reads `/notes` but not `/notes/priv`. **The first cut re-stamped from the move's envelope and a cold review reproduced a second hole of the same shape, which is why the ruling is not "stamp the batch copy too".** A *nested* reveal has two born-denied nodes in one batch, and the move that places the inner one into the outer is emitted while the outer still sits at its origin — so its envelope names the origin partition, and the inner **shell** derived from it was dropped by a destination-scoped channel while the outer's survived. Measured on the same fixture with `x`,`y` born denied inside `/notes/priv` and `card` denied in `/board`: two shells delivered instead of three, `x` never materialised, bob's tree frozen short of the author's. The same derivation also made the dedup's choice of which shell's zone to record depend on `reveal_ops`' element-id sort order, so an op `origin/main` delivered could be withheld. **Ruling: a relocated node's partition is the one it *lands in*, read off the tree the stream serves once the batch is folded — not the one its move's envelope names.** An envelope carries the partition its author resolved when the op was emitted, which is a statement about ordering inside a transaction, not about where the subtree ends up; the folded tree gives every node in one relocated subtree the same answer whatever order the transaction emitted its moves in. So `moved_nodes` resolves each node through the fan-out's own element-path index, and the shell, its back-fill, and the batch's surviving copy of any op the back-fill would have carried all ride that partition. A node the served tree does not resolve keeps its move's answer, the only other partition claim available. The re-stamp is per-recipient (the batch is cloned by `retain_atomic_cloned` before it), so no other recipient's verdict moves; the recipient's per-zone clock advances in the destination partition, which is where the content now lives, and `mint_floor` maxes the partition clock with the replica's own global high-water so a clock left short elsewhere cannot collide. Zone isolation does not move — the re-stamped set is a strict subset of what `reveal_backfill` already yields under the same per-recipient read gate, stamped in the same partition — and the landing partition is resolved under the room's *governing* schema, which is the id space a channel's zone scope was itself resolved in, where an author's envelope only coincidentally is. The `registry.rs` comment claimed more than either derivation supports (that a shell never rides where its placing move is dropped, which is a claim about an op keeping its own envelope) and now states what holds: a revealed node's shell and its contents travel together or not at all. Scope is `main`: `reveal_backfill` reads `main`'s log and document, so on a branch stream the back-fill and the batch never intersect — the branch half is C85's. Spec `crates/server/tests/reveal_zone.rs` (4), pinning the preconditions beside the outcomes — the two ops must carry different partitions, and (since C2 cuts a commit into one atomic transaction per partition) they are two groups, so the co-travel is the only thing keeping them on one channel — plus two controls: a reader no reveal fires for is not re-stamped, and a channel scoped to the partition the node *leaves* is told nothing of it rather than handed content it can never place, which is the fail-closed mirror of the defect and the one behaviour this changes for a reader `origin/main` served. → *Server / Fan-out*.

**C30 — a channel's zone scope was a set of positional ids that outlived the schema it was resolved against (crates/server) — DONE (#392).** A zone id is the zone's *index* into the schema's order-preserving `zones()`, and `zone_scope` resolved a channel's authorized set once, at Subscribe, storing the ids on `Subscription`. Nothing pins the room's acting schema to that moment — `bind_room_app` lifts the governing version whenever a newer client of the same app subscribes, a clone landing re-points a name's binding at the source's app — or *removes* it, where the source is ungoverned (C28), and a room nothing had bound acquires a schema the first time an enforcing client joins — so the stored ids stopped denoting what they were resolved to denote, and a channel that joined while the room declared **no** zones stored no set at all, skipping the projection wholesale and receiving every partition the room later declared, denied ones included (the reproduced shape). **Fix, in two halves.** The subscription now carries the **name** it was admitted under (the zone selector, empty for the whole room) plus the room-read verdict a per-zone abstain inherits, and one resolver — `acting_zone_scope` — turns it into ids against the schema acting *now*, re-taking each `Resource::Zone` read verdict on the way; the live fan-out's `zone_filter` (which takes the authorizer and the room's governing schema, both already in scope at both call sites), the version fetch and the diff query all go through it, and the catch-up, which already resolved against the schema it was then narrowed by, shares the wording. A selector that no longer resolves narrows to the **root partition alone**, never to `None` — C60's lesson applied forward, since `None` at this seam means "do not filter" and would serve the whole room on exactly the input that became unanswerable. Re-resolving also closes the **authorizer** half: a `Resource::Zone` read revoked after Subscribe now narrows a bound channel on the next op. And because re-resolution cannot recover ids already stamped into a room's log, `SchemaRegistry::register` refuses a `zones` block that is not an append-only extension of its predecessor's — a reorder, a rename, a re-root or a removal (`ZonesNotAppendOnly`); retiring a zone means leaving it declared — and **parses what it stores** (`Unparseable`), closing the third filed shape, a stored body that resolves to no schema and so reads at every zone seam as a room with no partitions. A zone-limited channel whose room resolves no schema is **refused** a version fetch or diff query rather than served: an op names its own partition so the empty set filters it, but a state's partitions are positions in a tree the schema names, so with no schema the projection is skipped and the whole room would go to the narrowest scope there is (cubic, on the head). Reached in three frames on one node — a subscribe binds a name before anything materializes under it, a clone from an *ungoverned* source then removes that binding, and every later frame resolves no schema since the connection's-own fallback is subscribe-only — pinned by `a_zone_named_channel_whose_room_loses_its_binding_is_refused_a_state_read`, which without the guard serves the whole cloned room to a `za`-scoped channel. The zero-zone twin of the same disagreement is **C106**. An unparseable body is a `400` on the admin plane — wrong at every position — while a non-append-only block is a `409`, since the same body is a valid version 1 and the refusal is a collision with what is registered. Re-resolving also forced C70's `governing_schema` reader: the fan-out read the live `room_apps` map alone where the frame path falls back to the room's own binding, so a room a dormant sweep dropped answered "no schema" — which a whole-room selector reads as "no partitions" and serves whole, in-process and with no restart. It now takes the same fallback. And a batch carrying no zoned op skips the resolve outright, since every scope admits the root partition. Resolving per use also meant the resolver could no longer cache a *miss*: `parsed_schema` cached the negative outcome, sound while the only unresolvable body was an unparseable one, but with registration parsing what it stores the sole remaining miss is a version this node does not hold — which the control plane can register at any moment, and a cached miss would outlive that for the process lifetime, turning C101's startup window permanent on the rooms that can least afford it. Only a resolved schema is cached now (the map's value type dropped its `Option` so a negative entry is unrepresentable); the cost is a registry lock, not a re-parse, while a bound version stays unresolvable. Spec `crates/server/tests/zone_rebind.rs` (7: the no-zones-then-zones lift, the ungoverned-room door, a mid-session zone revoke, a dormant room's fan-out, a named channel across an append, a version registered after a room already resolved without it, and a zone-named channel refused a state read once its room lost its binding), `schema_registry.rs` (the gate: reorder / removal / re-root refused, append accepted, v1 unconstrained, unparseable refused at the head and mid-chain), `admin_http.rs` (both refusals through the control plane, 400 and 409); C15's two drift tests are rewritten onto the append axis, the only `zones` change the registry now admits. Four of the seven `zone_rebind` cases fail under a frozen scope; the dormant one fails without the `governing_schema` fallback; the registration one additionally fails with the negative cache restored; the clone one fails without the unprojectable guard; the remaining one is the control on the append axis, answering identically either way, which is what says the fix does not widen a named channel while narrowing the whole-room ones. The registry-gate and control-plane cases are about what may be *stored*, so a frozen scope does not bear on them. ARCHITECTURE §Zones + DECISIONS 2026-08-08. **Residuals:** **C102** — `op.zone` is stamped by the *writing replica* against the schema it holds and the server relays it unchecked, so this unit makes the reader's half of the seam exact and leaves the **writer's half open**: a relay connection (no schema at all) and any client mid-rolling-upgrade both emit into the root partition, which every scope admits. A room bound to a version the registry does not *hold* (a restart before re-registration, an import or clone that was never bound) still resolves to no schema and serves a whole-room channel unfiltered — **C101** for the restart window, C62 for the never-bound one; and **C97** is not subsumed — the append-only rule makes two versions of one chain agree on every id, but `Document::set_schema` takes any `Schema`, core holds no registry, and C97's guard is at the mint. → *Server / Zones*.

**C2 — a cross-zone atomic group was emitted straddling both zones, and lost its atomic view at every zone-scoped subscriber (crates/core) — DONE (#390).** ARCHITECTURE §Scope Constraints has always said "a Tx must stay within one branch, one zone, one schema version", and §Not Shipped lists cross-zone txs outright; only the member cap on that line was enforced. `zone_of_op` stamps each op with the partition of the region it *governs* (C74), and `tag_atomic` then put **one** group id on every op of a commit whatever partition each fell in — so `atomic_transact` emitted a group no zone-scoped subscriber can receive whole: its subscription withholds the other partition's members and the seam destrands (C11) the survivors, so the group loses its atomicity at exactly the recipients zones exist to serve, while a full-doc subscriber keeps it. **The ruling (taken here, logged in DECISIONS 2026-08-08): split at the mint** — a commit closes one atomic transaction per zone partition its ops fall in, each id derived from its own members' sequences and each key spent at the author. A commit wholly inside one partition, which is every commit in a document declaring no zones, is byte-for-byte the group it always was. **Refusing the straddle at emit was rejected:** it has no honest landing place — the existing `emit_stamped` refusal drops the op and changes no state, so that shape would leave a *partially applied* intention, and refusing the whole commit needs either a rollback of state already applied as it was made or a fallible `atomic_transact` across core/C ABI/wasm/Python/Go, while buying the zone-scoped subscriber nothing the split does not (its delivered set is identical either way). A refusal costs the caller their edits; the split costs them atomicity *across* zones, which was never offered. The precedent is the cap on the same line of the same section — enforced at the mint by degrading (a group past it streams untagged, "a non-atomic transaction rather than a frame every peer rejects") — and the split is that treatment, gentler: per-zone atomicity instead of none. **Fix:** `Document::tag_atomic` buckets the commit's ops by `op.zone` and tags each bucket as its own group; the `MAX_TX_MEMBERS` range check moves with it, so it bounds a partition rather than the commit, and a partition outside the range streams untagged as before. No wire change, no signature change — every binding still passes `Vec<Op>` through. The ambiguous case is read off the envelope rather than re-derived: a mark whose anchors land in two zones names no partition (C74/C82) and groups with the root partition, which is a partition like any other here. Destranding is untouched and stays the floor — the doc-ACL read filter cuts on paths no group aligns to, and the wire admits a straddling group from a peer whose emitter does not cut — so C11's suites (`crates/core/tests/state_project_tx.rs`, `crates/server/tests/filter_split_tx.rs`) now assemble their straddling group by hand instead of from a local commit. Spec: `crates/core/tests/zone_tx_split.rs` (11 — the straddle emitting one group per zone with per-partition counts and distinct ids; a one-zone commit and an unpartitioned one each still a single group; the root partition taking a group of its own; a straddling commit still emitting every edit and converging; the zone cut running *between* groups rather than through one, so the surviving group reaches its subscriber tagged and holds until whole; the ambiguous mark joining the root group, with an agreeing-anchor control; a stray of *either* minted key landing at the author; the cap bounding a partition rather than the commit; a cross-zone undo intention replaying through the same cut), plus an end-to-end case in `crates/server/tests/filter_split_tx.rs` driving the real `Registry` and zone filter with the *emitter's* uncut output. ARCHITECTURE §Scope Constraints now states the enforcement point, and that read scope is deliberately not a constraint dimension. Cold review filed two residuals, both pre-existing and raised in consequence rather than in kind: **C93** (a one-member partition mints a spendable `resolved_tx` key for no atomicity — routine now that a straddling commit produces singletons; not a divergence, and the fix is a ruling about the *cap*) and **C97** (`set_schema` inside an open transaction re-indexes the zone ids the commit is cut on). The third shape — an `XmlMove` and an edit inside the moved node taking different partitions in one commit — is **C16**'s, already open. → *Transactions / Scope Constraints*.

**C60 — every seam that redacts a branch read still resolved against `main`'s tree (crates/server) — DONE (#386).** C32 (#378) settled the rule at the catch-up *snapshot* gate — a redaction decision is made against the state being served, and on a branch that state is not `main`. Three seams kept resolving through `hub.element_paths(room)` while acting on a `(room, branch)` stream: the **live fan-out** (`fan_out_ops_redacted` takes the branch, then indexes `main`), the **subscribe admission gate** (`recipient_reads_path` at the root then `has_any_read_grant`, which ran before the branch was even resolved), and the **`Catchup::Ops` per-op filter**. The fan-out is the reachable one: a reader whose catch-up C32 correctly narrows is delivered the very next live write into the subtree it is denied — no reconnect and no restore, just an open branch subscription and any branch write. **Two shapes leak through each, and the second is why this is not an element-scope corner.** An element-scoped tuple whose target has left `main` resolves to no path there and goes inert (an inert deny is no deny, an inert allow is no grant) — C32's shape. But an *op* whose container target `main` cannot resolve falls back to the **root**, which a root-readable but subtree-denied reader passes since a descendant deny does not `govern` it — so a **path**-scoped deny leaks too, on a room holding no element-scoped tuple at all, and C32's `resolves_elements` skip is not available to a seam that also has to place ops. **Ruling: the served tree is the stream's, and it is obtained by folding the stream itself.** `Hub::stream_doc` is `main`'s live replica or, for a branch, the fold of the derivation a subscriber joining at sequence 0 is served — `owned_base_doc` for a snapshot fork, off the same call `catch_up_branch`'s snapshot arm makes, and `catch_up_branch` itself for a live-log fork — so the tree a read is redacted against cannot describe a stream different from the one the read is served. **Except where the served stream is itself wrong, and there the answer is no tree at all**: a live-log fork's base is `main`'s *retained* log below its fork point, which compaction drops, so following the serve path narrows the tree — the fail-open direction, since a scope that stops resolving is an inert deny and an op target that does is root-bound. A review pass reproduced exactly that: on a compacted room a denied write reached the reader that `main`'s tree (the pre-C60 index) had withheld it from. A clipped shared base therefore yields `None` and the refusal below. What such a stream *serves* is wrong too and worse than a bad read — a **publish** of one freezes the base-less document into the published branch's base and into a permanent named version — and it is not confined to compaction, since `install_room_state` lands an empty log at a raised floor on every node that took a state transfer. Predates this; filed as **C88**. **Cost was the crux, and the answer is a held tree folded forward, not a fold per write.** The fan-out resolves an index per committed batch, and refolding a captured base or replaying a fork's shared history there is O(state)/O(log) per write against the O(tree) walk `main` already pays — so the hub holds a `StreamTree` per branch, folded on first demand and then folded forward by the tail ops appended since. One shape escapes the forward fold, measured: a fork point *above* `main`'s head leaves the shared-base window still growing, and a grown window refolds — confined to a construction already wrong for another reason (forking off a branch takes the source's *head* but `main`'s *log*), filed as **C89**. Memory is one document per branch some redaction actually asks about (`main` holds none — its replica *is* the served tree; a room with no doc-ACL tuples takes the plain fan-out and asks for none), bounded by the room's fork count, which no reader can widen — though a *document* per fork rather than the bytes a snapshot fork already holds, so for a live-log fork it is new resident state rather than a second copy. **Invalidation is a re-check, not a promise to remember**: the entry carries the fork point, the owned base's length, the window of `main`'s log a live-log fork draws its shared base from, and the branch's own tail length, and every call re-checks them — deliberately not "every mutation site remembers", since the sites span fork/publish/restore/rename/delete/compaction and two whole-replica installs and a missed one is a silent redaction bug. The window is **both ends**, which a review pass is what caught: `catch_up_branch` clamps the shared base to `fork_point.min(main.head())`, so a branch forked above `main`'s head — which `fork_branch` allows, clamping to the *source branch's* head, and a branch head counts its tail — keeps admitting `main`'s later writes, and a tree recording the fork point alone froze reaching *less* than the stream served, which is the fail-open direction. The explicit evictions that remain cover what the numbers cannot: a name is reusable and a re-forked stream is not the retired one's; the two whole-replica installs drop a room's trees outright, reseating base and tail in bytes a length can match. **Swapping the tree changes what "unresolvable" means, and a review pass reproduced the mirror leak that opens**: `op_read_path` roots an unresolvable container target, and against a branch's index that now also means "belongs to another stream" — a replica holding `main`'s state addressing a `main`-only container on a branch channel lands nothing on the branch but carries a denied subtree's payload to a root-readable reader. Closed by splitting the two readings the fallback conflated, which is C52 and C67's own boundary: a container the served state **retains** still roots (C67's ruling, untouched), one it has **never materialised** takes `WholeDocument` (C52's family) — `Document::container_ids` is the new co-input, and it is also what makes the joins agree at the serve: a narrowed snapshot clears the held-op buffer whole, so the reader the op-seam withholds from is exactly the one whose snapshot carries nothing to withhold. It widens C52's accepted residual rather than closing it — an op whose target is merely *buffered* is now withheld from a partial reader and is not re-fanned when the create lands, so that reader trails a fresh joiner until it reconnects, which the `Catchup::Ops` filter then heals. **Fail-closed where a stream has no tree, made true rather than assumed** — the fan-out sends such a stream nothing, and the subscribe now refuses the branch outright wherever the room holds tuples, since `Catchup::Unavailable`'s own ruling is that a subscriber *past the fork point* still gets its tail delta (it holds the base already), which was being filtered through `main`'s index; a room with no tuples skips the index and keeps that delta, an unredacted stream needing no tree. The admission gate alone still borrows `main`'s index, purely so "read denied" keeps precedence over the refusal. **The admission gate moves below the branch resolution and the branch *existence* check stays below the gate**, so reordering does not hand an unauthorized caller a branch-name oracle. **The mirror is a real behavior change and the intended one**: a reader whose only grant names an element the branch's tree does not hold is now refused a subscription `main`'s tree would have admitted onto a serve of nothing. Spec `crates/server/tests/branch_stream_tree.rs` (13), asserting on the **ops actually put on the wire** by target element rather than on the reader's folded replica — a redacted reader does not hold the denied container, so a leaked op buffers unapplied and a replica-level check would call the leak a pass; seven of the nine wire cases reproduce against a seam pointed back at `main`'s replica (the survivors being the `main` control and the held-container case, which reproduces against this unit's own first cut instead), and four hub-level cases hold the held tree against a fresh fold of the same stream (a shared base growing under a fork point above `main`'s head, a tail folded forward then a base repointed under it, a name re-forked so that every recorded input coincides, and a compacted shared base). Mutation-checked rather than assumed: pointing `stream_doc` at `main`'s replica reddens seven of the nine wire cases, and dropping the shared-base window, the compaction guard, the forward fold, or both eviction sites each turns a case red. The remaining token components (`base_len`, `fork_point`, the tail-shrink check) and the four singly-redundant eviction sites are *not* individually pinned — every shape that moves one moves another, so each is defence rather than the load-bearing check. ARCHITECTURE §Wire-Level Redaction + DECISIONS 2026-08-08. Residuals, all found by the review passes on this unit: the reveal-on-move-in shells and their back-fill still synthesize from `main`'s document (**C85**), the doc-ACL **write** gate on a branch-bound channel still resolves through `main` (**C86**), `rename_branch` leaves a branch's tail and owned base under the retired name (**C87**), and compaction destroys a live-log fork's shared base so the branch serves a base-less document (**C88**, which this unit fails closed over rather than fixes — including refusing a *publish* of such a stream, since that capture is irreversible). Two the unit declines rather than closes: a follower's read routing still takes the `main` fast path for an unnamed branch, which needs a ruling on whether the active-HEAD pointer replicates (**C91**), and a fan-out withholding a whole stream tells its already-bound subscribers nothing (**C92**). Two more the same passes turned up: a fork taken off a branch shares `main`'s log rather than the source's stream (**C89**), and branch-stream migration translation resolves its element types from `main` (**C90**) — the latter out of *this* unit because it narrows a type-scoped rewrite rather than a read verdict, not because it is harmless. `ranged_anchors` moved with the index, being the literal co-argument of the same `op_read_gate` call. Branch-level ACL — a tuple set taken from `main` while the bytes are the branch's — stays v0.4 scope, as C32 ruled. → *Server / Branches*.

**C68 — a container create racing a delete of its key encoded two ways (crates/core) — DONE (#388).** Two ops — `MapCreate{k}` and `MapDelete{k}` from one author — folded to the same reading and to **different bytes**: create-first left a deleted-container tombstone (slot tag 2, carrying a stamp and kind), delete-first a bare leaf tombstone (tag 1), because `Map::set`'s losing branch was a pure no-op, so a create the tombstone outranked was never recorded. 26 bytes on a 2-op pool; the convergence generator found it at seed 9 reversed. Pre-existing on `main`, found during C58 (#379) by the review pass that ran the generator's pools through `encode_state` instead of `fingerprint`. **The ruling (taken here, logged in DECISIONS 2026-08-08): a resurrectable container is identified by its *create*, not by the last write that kept it alive** — a slot retains the highest-ranked container create the key has ever seen, recorded whether that create won the slot, lost it to a delete, or was later shadowed by a leaf, and joined as a running maximum rather than carried by the LWW winner. The alternative (the slot's stamp at the delete, today's behaviour, extended with a runner-up so a leaf could clear it) was rejected: it needs a *leaf* write recorded under every tombstone to stay order-independent, taxing every tombstone in the document for a field only a deleted container reads. The rank answers the second question with it — two creates of different kinds at one key resolve by the highest create stamp (kind tag breaking a tie no honest pair of ops can produce), an intrinsic rank, following C24 rather than inventing a second convention. **Fix:** `Entry.container` replaces `Entry.deleted` and is maintained on every path a create takes through a slot — the winning install, the losing branch and the already-live path of `set` (which the get-or-create helpers now route through), `delete` (which now reads it off the slot instead of the value it tombstones), `merge` (a join by rank, so the create outlives a higher-stamped leaf tombstone), `put_slot_lww` and `insert_decoded`. New slot tag `3` spells the create out on a live leaf that shadows one; a live container is its own create and stays tag `0`, so the tax stays where DECISIONS 2026-07-15 put it. Every *container* create ranks, XML included: a migration leaves them all at their key, so ranking only the resurrectable kinds would let an XML element take the slot while a lower map create stayed the record and the snapshot resurrected the wrong container. Review (cubic) then caught that an XML **fragment** is key-derived like a map — only an **element** mixes its tag in below the key — so a deleted fragment now resurrects at its old key too, and only an element's key falls back to what the registry still holds there. STATE_VERSION 13→14. Migration gains reach from it: a container a leaf or counter displaced before the delete now resurrects, which the op seam always did. Spec: `crates/core/tests/state_order.rs` (both orders of the create/delete pair, all six orders of two-creates-of-different-kinds and of the leaf-shadowed pool, a live-shadow round trip, plus a generated shuffle sweep that fails on `main` at pool 1), `crates/core/tests/state_project.rs` (the delete-first order migrating to the same bytes as the create-first, a deleted XML fragment resurrecting at its old key while an element's key is carried verbatim, the highest create being the one a shared delete resurrects — the test an operator flip in the rank fails — the displaced-then-deleted container resurrecting, and the byte tax on each slot shape), `crates/core/src/map.rs` unit tests (merge joining the record; the rank). Review filed the residual as **C76**. → *Core / State codec*.

**C7 — `ClientSession::next_channel` exhaustion was unenforced while the identity scheme leaned on it (crates/core + ffi/wasm + Python/Go SDKs) — DONE (#385).** `subscribe_inner` did a bare `self.next_channel += 1` on a `u32` and never recycled a freed number. Non-recycling is *load-bearing* for C4's per-channel identity (#351) — ARCHITECTURE, the `for_channel` rustdoc and DECISIONS all state that reusing a freed number hands a fresh replica, minting from seq 0, the identity of ops the retired one still has in flight — but nothing enforced the ceiling: the counter panics on overflow in debug and wraps to 0 in release, at which point channel 0 is re-issued, `for_channel(0)` hands it the session's declared id, and `self.rooms.insert` silently replaces the live room bound to it — replica, outbox of unacknowledged ops and caught-up sequence gone with no error anywhere. Unreachable in practice (2^32 subscriptions on one session) and the handle collision predates the identity scheme, but a documented invariant with no guard is a defect regardless of its reachability. **Fix:** `checked_add` on the counter, the range's last value the exhausted marker rather than a channel (no second field to remember a spent range), and the number claimed *before* the room is bound so a refusal binds nothing. Recycling via a free-list was never open — it is the exact collision C4 closed. The refusal is surfaced in every binding, each on its language's existing convention rather than a parallel fallible API: core `subscribe`/`subscribe_branch`/`subscribe_zone` return `Result<(Channel, Message), SubscribeError>`; the C ABI returns an empty `CrdtBuf` leaving `out_channel` unwritten (the failure signal this family already uses); wasm returns `Result<_, JsError>` so JS throws and the `Provider` constructor propagates it; Python raises `ChannelsExhausted`; Go returns `ErrChannelsExhausted` as a third value (`NewNetProvider` closes the client and fails). Pre-release, so the infallible signatures are gone, not shimmed.
