Spec: `crates/core/src/client.rs` `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` — beside the private counter the tests park at the ceiling, since an integration test cannot reach it and an accessor added purely to test it would put the invariant's mechanism in the public surface — asserting the refusal instead of a wrap onto channel 0, that all three entry points refuse, that a refusal spends no number and binds no room, that a held channel keeps its room, outbox and identity across one, and that an unsubscribe does not hand its number back. ARCHITECTURE §"A channel's replica is its own author" now states the ceiling; see DECISIONS (2026-08-08). → *Client / Sessions*.

**C74 — a mark's and a grant's op was stamped in the root partition whatever zone the region it names fell in (crates/core + crates/server) — DONE (#387).** Found and measured during C52 (#383). `zone_of_op` resolved an op's partition from the op's **target**, and every `Ranged*` and `Acl*` op is emitted at the document root — `zone_id_of(schema, &[])` names no zone unless one is rooted at `/` — so the whole family carried `zone = None`, the root partition, which `zone_admits` carries to **every** zone-scoped subscriber. A mark annotating a sequence inside a zone was delivered whole on the op stream (its name, its scalar payload, its anchor's element id) to a subscriber scoped away from it, and so was an ACL grant scoped into it, which additionally revealed a subject and an effect. Measured on a `/notes`-rooted zone: `path::mark` over `/notes/seq` emitted `zone=None`, and so did a *path*-scoped `AclGrant` naming `/notes` outright — the sharpest form, and the reason this was never a pathless-scope corner. **Fix:** an op's partition is the region it **governs**, extending the precedent Zones Unit 3 (#278) set when a container-create moved to the created child's zone. A `RangedCreate` takes the partition of the sequences its endpoints anchor (read off the op), a `RangedSetPayload`/`RangedDelete` the same through the held anchor set, an `AclGrant` the partition its scope resolves into and an `AclRevoke` its tombstoned tuple's — the same resolutions `op_read_gate` performs for the read tier, so the two tiers now answer one question about one op. An op editing a mark's **composite payload** rides the mark as well (a payload hangs off the range rather than a map slot, so the walk gives it no path and its edits fell to the root while the projection cut them with the mark). Anchors **require agreement**, they do not compose: endpoints in two zones are a `CrossZoneAnchor` violation the read repairs away, so a straddling mark keeps the root rather than asserting a partition it is not wholly inside. The clock follows the stamp, which is the repair rather than a cost — a zone projection retains only the authorized partitions' clocks while the root clock is served whole, so a mark stamped in the root moved a clock every narrowed subscriber reads. Spec: `crates/core/tests/zone_governing_region.rs` (12 — the mark, the payload change/delete, the composite-payload edit, the path- and element-scoped grant and its revoke, the unzoned controls, the projection dropping the same mark and grant the op filter withholds, the per-zone clock going with them, an op-served replica converging with a snapshot-served one) and 3 in `crates/server/tests/zone_streams.rs` (live fan-out and catch-up, both directions). ARCHITECTURE §Internal Data Model + §Wire-Level Redaction + DECISIONS 2026-08-08. Three residuals filed, none of them the family this closed: **C82** (a governing region that resolves to no path names no partition and keeps the root while the projection drops the state form — `Option<u32>` has no value for "no partition", every value it takes naming a clock), **C83** (a keyed *non-create* op naming a zone-root slot still rides the parent's partition, and the state half needs its own ruling), **C84** (a follow-on op is stamped where the region is *now*, so an authorized cross-zone move splits an op family). → *Core / Zones*.

**C50 — two channels of one room overwrote each other's diff view, and neither could tell whose answer it held (crates/core) — DONE (#381).** C27 (#375) made `Message::DiffQuery` channel-keyed so a change list is narrowed by the subscription's zone scope, and left `DiffResult { room, changes }` room-keyed; `ClientSession::receive` folded it into a per-room view. The conflation was inert before C27 — two channels on one room were served byte-identical lists — and C27 is what made it bite: a wide channel and a zone-narrowed one get genuinely different answers, the second reply overwrote the first, and `diff(room)` could not say whose it held, so a caller reading the narrower answer concludes nothing changed where something did. Not a privilege crossing (one connection, one actor, the server sent both), and unreachable from `sdks/js` (one channel per `Provider`); reachable from `ClientSession`, the FFI, Python and Go. **Fix:** `DiffResult { channel, changes }`, keyed by the channel that asked as `VersionState` already is, and the client's view moves onto `Room` beside `version_states` — so it inherits the right lifetime, retiring with the subscription that gave the list its meaning. Accessors follow the key: `ClientSession::diff(channel)`, `crdtsync_client_diff_result(client, channel, out)`, Python `Client.diff(channel)`, Go `DiffResult(channel)`, wasm `diff(channel)` and its reply tag `{ kind: "diff", channel }` — which lets the JS provider's matcher key on channel like every other operator request it makes. A pre-release wire break, no migration path. `Branches`/`CloneRoomResult` stay room-keyed: their answers carry only names and a flag, nothing a zone scope narrows. Spec: `crates/core/tests/client_diff.rs` (two channels of one room, different zone scopes, each reading back its own diff in both arrival orders; a result on an unheld channel refused; an unsubscribe dropping the view), an end-to-end case in `crates/server/tests/diff_projection.rs` (the server serves a whole-room and a za-scoped channel genuinely different lists and one `ClientSession` reads each back), plus FFI/Go/Python round-trips. Review filed the residual as **C61**: the reply names the channel, not the query, so two diffs asked on one channel are still ordered rather than attributed — strictly weaker, since one channel is one scope and the second answer is never *narrower* than the first was asked for. ARCHITECTURE §Schema-Aware Diff's surface rule now reads on both halves; see DECISIONS (2026-08-06). → *Client / Diff*.

**C49 — a room-keyed management frame authorized under no schema at all (crates/server) — DONE (#382).** `Registry::deliver` resolves the *acting schema* — the one whose `@auth` grants every enforcement point composes under — from `authz_room`, a match mapping each frame to the room it authorizes against. It named the channel-keyed frames plus `Subscribe`, `CrossZoneToken` and a clone's `src`; the six room-keyed **branch** frames (`BranchList` and the five `Branch*` mutations) fell through to no room, so `acting_schema` was `None` and the schema tier of `acl::authorized` was **mute for the whole branch sub-protocol** — a schema `@auth` grant naming branch management was silently ignored and an abstaining deployment default-denied. Fail-*closed*, so the symptom is a grant that does nothing rather than a leak, which is why it went unnoticed. Each branch frame now keys off the room it names. The fall-through also hid a second question, which the fix settles the way C28 settled it for the clone: a room-keyed frame names a room the caller need hold no subscription to, so its acting schema is that **room's** binding, never the connection's self-declared app. C28's negative guard (every frame *but* a clone falls back to the caller's app on an unbound room) is inverted into the **positive list** — `Subscribe` alone, the one frame whose caller is about to become the room's incumbent — so the next room-keyed frame is governed correctly without having to remember to opt out. Spec `crates/server/tests/room_keyed_authz.rs` (7): the schema grant serving all six frames from a connection that never subscribed, a read-only schema listing but refusing all five mutations, the room's binding serving a caller its own app would refuse and refusing one its own app would serve (with a role-holder positive control pinning that the strict schema is the acting one, not an absent one), an unbound room lending a branch frame nothing, and the cross-zone token pair. ARCHITECTURE §Enforcement Points + DECISIONS 2026-08-06. Residual: every reader of the live room-app map other than the frame authz path lacks its hub fallback, so a dormant room goes schema-mute there — filed as **C70**. → *Server / Authz*.

**C63 — a cross-zone token named the caller's zone layout when the room is unbound (crates/server) — DONE (#382, subsumed by C49).** `Message::CrossZoneToken` was already room-keyed in `authz_room`, but an *unbound* room fell through to `acting_schema`'s connection fallback — the caller's self-declared app — and the handler resolves `zone_of`, `zone_root_keys` and both write verdicts off that schema, so for a room in any of C62's never-bound constructions the caller named the zone layout of the token authorizing its own crossing. Unlike the branch shape this **grants**: the pre-fix suite mints a real sealed token for an actor against a room it does not govern. Closed by C49's positive list, which is exactly the fix this entry called for. Pinned by `room_keyed_authz.rs`'s pair — the token refused on an ingested-but-never-bound room, and issued on the same room once its own zoned app binds it, so the refusal is the missing binding and not a broken issuance path. → *Server / Zones*.

**C52 — an element the live walk does not reach survives both read projections (crates/core) — DONE (#383).** Every read gate resolves by walking the live tree, so state whose governing element the walk does not reach had no verdict of its own — and the two stand-ins in place were wider than the reader entitled to it. An annotation is tombstoned only by an explicit delete and an element-scoped ACL tuple outlives its target, so deleting the container that held the annotated sequence left a mark whose anchor resolves to nothing: the read projection gated it at the **root** verdict, which a root grant with a subtree deny passes (a descendant deny does not `govern` the root query), so such a reader was served the mark's name, its scalar payload and its anchor's element id — or the tuple's subject and effect — out of the region the deny carves out of its grant; the zone projection decided by purge membership, and an unreached id is never purged, so "names no zone" read as "root partition" and reached **every** zone-scoped subscriber. **Ruling 1: a narrowing serve drops it, and the whole view is the caller's decline** — neither projection ever runs except to narrow (`project_served_state` skips the read one for a `reads_whole_document` reader, `zone_narrowing` the zone one for a whole-zone subscriber), so the reader entitled to everything keeps the entry by not being projected, which is what `project_zones` has said since it was written and what the read projection now matches. **Ruling 2: the whole-view verdict belongs to the authority, never to the document.** The first pass had core derive it from the tuples the document carries — same shape as `reads_whole_document`, wrong site, and a review pass measured both failures: `AclScope::Path` is raw bytes the evaluator prefix-matches and nothing validates, so core's parse-then-ask and the server's ask-the-bytes disagreed on a malformed scope and left the leak open; and the version fetch, the branch base and each diff side narrow *archived* bytes under the room's **live** authority, so a deny authored after the capture is absent from the document and core called the reader whole. **Ruling 3: the op seam names the same audience explicitly**, having no decline to lean on — `op_read_gate` answers `OpReadGate::WholeDocument` for an unresolved Ranged anchor, an unresolved range id and an unresolvable ACL scope, resolved by every consumer against the one `reads_whole_document`, **lazily and memoized per recipient**: the verdict costs an ACL fixpoint per governing tuple path, and the first pass's require-all path set turned one verdict per op into one per tuple — measured 427x on a 400-tuple room, on a shape any writer mints by marking a sequence and deleting its container. **A dropped mark takes its payload container with it:** a composite payload is registered under an id derived from the mark's and linked to it rather than held in a map slot, so the root walk never reaches it and no verdict either projection computes would ever cut it — dropping the `ranged` entry alone withheld the mark's name and anchor while re-encoding the container holding its content. Both projections now resolve which marks go before the registry retains and purge the payload **and its whole subtree** with them; wider than the unit as filed, since the same hole served a *live* mark's composite payload to a reader denied its region. The op seam had the mirror — an op *editing* a payload resolved through the element index, which holds a payload container no more than the walk does, so it fell to the root — closed by keying a payload **and its registered subtree** under its range's anchors in `ranged_anchors`, consulted only where the index does not resolve the target so a live element keeps its own path. **What it costs on the zone seam, and what it does not:** a mark's partition is unrecoverable from a snapshot (`ElementId::derive` is one-way), so dropping is the only closed answer and it narrows every zone-scoped subscriber, one of which may have owned the partition — but it is not a regression against a correct op seam. Measured: a `RangedCreate` over a sequence inside a declared zone carries `zone = None`, because `zone_of_op` resolves the **op's target** and every Ranged and ACL op is emitted at the document root, which names no zone; all of them ride the root partition, which `zone_admits` carries to every zone-scoped subscriber. On `main` such a mark reached all of them through the op stream, live anchor or not. The snapshot seam is now the stricter of the two and the residual is the op seam's own, filed as **C74**. **A displaced anchor is treated as a gone one, knowingly** — retention exists so a re-won slot restores a container whole, and its marks do not come back with it; both seams agree, so this is availability inside a redacted view, not divergence. **Explicitly not C67:** a retained *container* the walk does not reach is still served by both projections and still reads at the root on the op seam — those entries are what displace-then-recreate identity retains, and purging them would drop content a subscriber entitled to the partition gets back when the slot is re-won. The boundary is pinned by a test, not prose alone. An anchor also takes the containers' **prefix** rule now, not its own path's verdict: a sequence under an unreadable ancestor is purged even where a deeper grant re-opens its own path, so reading that path alone kept a mark whose sequence the same projection cut. Spec `crates/core/tests/state_project_reach.rs` (10) + `crates/server/tests/acl_redaction.rs` (4, the op seam and the snapshot seam agreeing on one carve-out fixture, including the reader denied nothing that both must still carry) + a version-fetch case in `version_projection.rs`, since the unconditional drop rests on every seam running that composition declining to project for such a reader. ARCHITECTURE §Wire-Level Redaction + DECISIONS 2026-08-06. → *Core / Redaction*.

**C28 — `CloneRoom` launders a room past every redaction that governs it (crates/server) — DONE (#376).** `Hub::clone_room` was `export_room(src)` into `import_room(dst)`, gated on `branch_authorized(src, Read)` — the room tier, where the doc-ACL tuples abstain *by design* so a subtree-scoped reader can still subscribe and be narrowed per op. A clone is not narrowed. Two escalations compounded: zone verdicts are `Resource::Zone { room, zone }`, keyed by room, so a deny on `src` decided nothing about the same bytes in `dst`; and `import_room` passed no creator, so `dst` came up with no authority root and every doc-ACL tuple that rode the snapshot was inert there (`reads_whole_document` short-circuits `true` on a creatorless room, `doc_acl_read_at` abstains) — a partial reader cloned `src` into a room it named, subscribed, and read every subtree it was denied. **The fix** gates the clone on `reads_source_whole`: `reads_whole_document` — the seam the catch-up snapshot and the version fetch already decide an unprojected read with — composed with every zone the source's acting schema declares being readable, since only the doc-ACL dimension lives in that seam and the zone one is keyed by the room the bytes leave. And it installs `src`'s creator as the clone's root (`install_room_state`'s creator argument, C29's set-once), so the denies that carved a subtree out of the source carve it out of the clone; the cloner does *not* become its clone's root, since the alternative rootings both re-open the laundering (no root leaves every deny inert; the cloner as root hands it `/`). The gate's inputs are per-node, so three things follow: a `CloneRoom` resolves its acting schema off the **source** (`authz_room`), never the caller's self-declared app — that fallback is a first-subscriber rule and a cloner is neither; `bind_governing` holds the binding without a store, since the registry's live map is rebuilt from present subscribers and so drops exactly the dormant template room a clone names; and the clone is served only where one node leads **both** rooms, a replica's copy being at its own replication and binding freshness (the C33 shape) — cross-leader is the `created == false` no-op the seam already documented. Spec `crates/server/tests/clone_redaction.rs` (9): the partial reader refused, the zone-denied reader refused, the whole-document reader's clone landing with the source's creator, the denies still live in the clone, the cloner not rooting it, both schema-substitution shapes, and the cross-leader no-op. Residual: a bound source whose stored schema will not parse resolves to no schema, so its zones read as absent — the shape C30 owns everywhere. ARCHITECTURE §Export / Import + DECISIONS 2026-08-06. → *Server / Rooms*.

**C32 — the catch-up gate resolved element grants against a tree the snapshot is not (crates/server) — DONE (#378).** `project_served_state`'s whole-document gate was fed the *live main* element index at the catch-up snapshot arm, while the state it decides for is a **branch's owned base** — a version's captured bytes installed by `fork_branch_from_version` (restore) or by `repoint_published` (publish), served with the branch's divergent tail folded in. `reads_whole_document` drops an `AclScope::Element` tuple whose id `scope_path` cannot resolve, so an element-scoped read-*deny* whose target had since left main resolved to nothing, no deny was found, the gate returned `true`, and the branch's state was served **unprojected** — the reader handed the very subtree it is denied. Ordinary rather than niche on both shapes: `restore_as_branch` is `fork_branch_from_version` + `set_active_branch` and a plain empty-branch Subscribe follows the room's active branch, while a published branch's whole audience is read-only consumers. The arm now derives the gate's index from the bytes it is about to hand out — the tail-folded base, since a tail can create or move an element the base never held — matching what the read projection already did, and paying the decode + walk only where the room actually holds an element-scoped tuple, since a path scope names its own position and needs no tree. An undecodable state is refused ("branch state is unreadable", without closing) rather than served unnarrowed — unreachable, since `catch_up_branch` already refuses a stored base that does not decode, but neither fallback is an answer: the live index reinstates the inert deny, and an empty index is inert by construction. ARCHITECTURE now states the resolve-against-the-served-tree rule as the seam's rather than the version read's. The live fan-out, the subscribe's *admission* gate and the `Catchup::Ops` per-op filter still read the live index and are filed as C60. Spec: `crates/server/tests/branch_base_gate.rs` (6).

**C57 — a reaped member's ack watermarks were never dropped, so `Replication` kept a map keyed on ids off the roster (crates/server) — DONE (#380).** `Membership::reap_dead` was complete over its own maps — `liveness`, `addrs`, `relay_down`, `configured`, `verifiers` (the departed member's own set and its entry in every other member's), with `adopted`/`cluster` derived — but `Replication`'s `acked: HashMap<(RoomId, NodeId), u64>` had no removal path: after a follower was reaped out of the roster, `replica_watermark(room, follower)` still read its last value. **Fix:** `Replication::forget_members` drops every room's entry for each of a reap round's departed ids in one pass over the map, and `Registry::reap_dead_members` hands it the whole round's set. Forgotten reads as `0`, the safe value in both directions — the member is credited toward no quorum, and a node id being its advertised address, a *returning* member (a fresh join, verified again — C25) is caught up from the start of the retained log instead of served a tail past a floor it can no longer prove. **`Registry::record_replica_ack` now drops a non-member's ack** so the same map cannot regrow through the other door: a replication link outlives the gossip verdict that reaped its far end, so an in-flight ack can land after the sweep, and it is owed nothing (a non-member is no room's follower, so its watermark counted toward no quorum). Roster membership, not liveness, is the gate — a `Suspect`/`Dead` member still acks normally. **A reap deliberately releases no withheld client ack** — a release pass this unit built and then reversed on itself: while the ring holds the replication factor a reap re-places a room at the same size, so the pass can realistically fire only once the ring has fallen *below* it, releasing a write held by fewer replicas than it was accepted against — on the minority side of a partition, one the majority side never saw (C69). Spec `crates/server/tests/reaping.rs`: no watermark in any room it acked while a survivor keeps its own, a returned member acks from nothing rather than from its old position, and a late ack from a reaped member records nothing while a `Dead`-but-unreaped member's still does.

**C5 — fan-out skipped the whole writing *connection*, so two channels of one session on one room never exchanged ops (crates/server) — DONE (#377).** `Registry`'s broadcast, its redacted sibling and the awareness fan-out all `continue`d on `*peer == id` — the writing **connection**, not the writing channel. That rested on one connection = one replica ("nothing echoes back to the sender"), a premise C4 broke by giving each channel its own `ClientId::for_channel` author: channel B's replica never received channel A's writes while the connection lived, and B's `last_seen_seq` never advanced over them, self-healing only on reconnect via `resume(B)`'s log delta. **Fix:** a `WriteOrigin { conn, channel }` — the channel the write arrived on — threaded into both op fan-outs; each connection's recipient set is its whole `(room, branch)` subscription minus that one channel, on the writing connection alone (handles are numbered per connection, so a peer holding the same handle is untouched). Resolving the recipient set *before* the read verdict and the migration translation also drops the redact-and-translate work for a connection with no channel to send on. **Awareness is ruled the other way, deliberately:** presence is one entry per room keyed by `(Hello client id, authenticated actor)`, both connection-scoped, so a connection's channels share one presence rather than holding two, and `AwarenessUpdate` carries only the actor — an echo would hand a client its own presence back as a peer's, indistinguishably. The whole connection stays excluded there; ARCHITECTURE §Connection / Multiplexing + DECISIONS record the asymmetry. Spec `crates/server/tests/channel_fanout.rs`: a sibling channel receiving a write with no reconnect, its `last_seen_seq` advancing, the redacting path taking the same exclusion, and the unchanged no-echo-to-self / stream-scoping / peer-delivery behaviour.

**C21 — a bucket resolved at a rewritten size committed the wrong members and held the right ones, order-dependently (crates/core) — DONE (#372).** A group's bucket key is `(author, TxId)`, and committing it *spent* the key with no record kept, so a later member of that key started a fresh bucket at an arrival count the group had already met and no arrival could meet again — and **which** members committed was the delivery order's, so one op set folded to two states. Three rewrites reached it, none malformed on any member's own terms: every member of a three-group rewritten to declare two; an unrelated op of the same author re-tagged with a live group's id; and one op id delivered under two envelopes that disagree, since `apply` dedups on op id before reading the tag. **The fix is the record C3 named and did not build:** `Document::resolved_tx: HashSet<(ClientId, TxId)>`, and a member arriving under a resolved key is untagged and merges standalone. A key is spent at each of the four points a bucket resolves — when it **commits** (`drain_buffer`); when the author **mints** the group (`tag_atomic`), because the author applies its own edits as it makes them and buckets nothing, so without this it holds a stray every receiver merges; when **eviction** gives up on it (`evict_partial_transactions`), or a later member waits on a bucket already released and two replicas on one policy disagree over which of them had ticked; and when a member arrives naming a group other than the one the buffer holds that same id under, which spends **both**, since only one of the two can ever hold the id. That fourth point fires only where `Document::apply` sees the second envelope: `Hub::ingest_records` drops an already-seen id from the batch before the fold, so a room replica never reaches it — it is the client, FFI/wasm/SDK and offline seams it protects. Rides `encode_state` (`STATE_VERSION` 12 → 13) so a restart does not re-open it; both projections drop it whole, a key naming an author and a group but never a partition. **Two rules deliberately not taken, and the first is what the review pass turned on.** The record does not fire on an id merely **applied**: a resend is ordinary traffic on any transport that retries, so spending a key there makes state a function of how often an op arrived rather than of which ops did — the same law broken in the other dimension — and a fabricated group id on a duplicate would grow the persisted record without bound (the first pass had exactly this, and a cold review caught it). And it does not release a bucket that merely *looks* unreachable, C3's standing rejection. `is_admissible` is now judged **before** the dedup, so an envelope no replica may hold decides nothing about the groups this one holds. Spec: the convergence law itself — every arrival order of one op set compared on `encode_state` bytes, for each of the three rewrites, plus the author/receiver pair and a snapshot restore. Five cold-review passes drove it: the record firing on a merely-*applied* id, an eviction regression against `main`, a 44x per-duplicate buffer scan, four new lines no test killed, an unpinned encode order, and a claim that the destranded race was closed when it is not. The residue is three further shapes, filed as **C46**, **C47** and **C48** — the first two order-dependent on `main` already, the third the one cost the record adds. Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-29). → *Transactions*.

**C27 — `DiffQuery` served an unredacted diff of the same bytes `VersionFetch` redacts (crates/server) — DONE (#375).** `Message::DiffQuery` resolved two states — `DiffKind::Versions` over two named versions, `DiffKind::Branches` over two `materialize_branch` results — and diffed them **raw**, replying with `Change`s carrying full `core::path`s and the scalar values at them, behind the abstaining room-read tier and with no channel binding at all. A zone-limited reader read any withheld partition as a diff (create `va`, wait for a write into the hidden zone, create `vb`, diff); `DiffKind::Branches` needed no versions to do it. Both are pinned as tests that fail against the previous behaviour. **The frame is now channel-keyed** — `DiffQuery { channel, kind, a, b }`, room resolved from the subscription like a version fetch, reply keyed by that room until C50 moved it to the channel — rather than given a room-plus-zone gate of its own: the alternative would put a second answer to "which partitions may this reader see" beside the one a bound channel holds, which is the drift `zone_narrowing` exists to prevent, and C15 (#368) pinned that a fetch narrows by the **channel's** set rather than the actor's entitlement. It also names the rule the surface already implied: a request that serves a room's *content* is channel-keyed, one that serves *names* or mutates may ride a room off the frame. **Both sides go through `project_served_state` before the engine sees them**, so a served change list is by construction `path::diff` of the two states that reader would itself have been handed — the causal frontier aside, which the two seams scrub differently and a change list does not carry (pinned directly against the fetch seam). Filtering the change list *after* the diff — the board's guess — was rejected: a mark change carries no path at all, and a filter is a second home for the redaction rule. `ClientSession::diff_query` refuses to frame on a channel the session does not hold, as `fetch_version` already does — the channel-keyed frame put it in that class, and the server answers such a frame with a connection-closing violation. `Hub::diff_versions`/`diff_branches` take the per-side redaction as an argument, so every call site states what it narrows by (a nudge, not an enforcement — an identity closure is still one keystroke, which is what the engine-pinning suites pass); no recipient is passed (a diff is never adopted as state, and a change list carries no frontier), so the scrub goes whole. **A second defect had to be fixed for the first to work:** `authz_room` had no `DiffQuery` arm, so a diff ran with *no acting schema* — fail-closed for the gate, but zone declarations live in the schema, so the zone projection would never have run. The room-keyed management frames are still in that blind spot, filed as C49; review also filed C50 (a `DiffResult` carried no channel, so with the answers now genuinely per-channel a result could not be attributed to the query that asked; since fixed), C51 (a branch whose durable base does not decode is reported as a branch that does not exist), C52 (an element the live walk does not reach survives both read projections — the shortfall reaches every state-serving seam, and its diff-visible face is an orphaned annotation) and C53 (a log-shared branch materializes truncated past a compaction floor). C32 does not reach this seam: each side derives its gate index from its own tree. **Two smaller alignments with the fetch came out of review**: a diff now records an `Action::VersionRead` when the change list goes out (the audit action's own definition already said "version-diff read", and the diff was the untraced way to read a version), and an archived side that does not decode refuses *without closing* — the engine always failed such a query, but through `internal`, which drops a live stream over an unreadable archive. Specs: `crates/server/tests/diff_projection.rs` (17 — both halves incl. a mark anchored in the hidden zone and a structural add, the readable half of each, positive controls on both branch diffs, the fetch-oracle equality in all three deny shapes, the channel-scope-not-entitlement rule, a query from a branch-subscribed channel, an element deny whose target has left the live room, and the unnarrowable room), the reworked `diff_query.rs` (8, incl. a diff on an unbound channel), two undecodable-side cases in `version_unreadable.rs`, a diff-audit case in `audit_query.rs`, and channel-keyed frames across `protocol_diff.rs`/`client_diff.rs`/FFI/wasm/Go/Python/JS. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-29). → *Server / Diff*.
**C25 — a member placed itself into any room's replica set by minting a node id, so C13's leadership and durability gates did not hold against a compromised member (crates/server) — DONE (#369).** C13 (#365) bound a peer link to a member and gated replication, leadership and durability on it. Every one of those gates asks the same question — is the sender in `replicas_for(room)` — and placement is HRW over the member set, a pure and publicly computable function, so **which rooms a node replicates follows from its node id**. Gossip's join path lets an unknown node introduce itself, and `add_members`/`merge_liveness` put it straight into the `Cluster` the ring is built from. So an admitted member ground an id HRW placed on the room it wanted, introduced itself under it, and was inside that room's replica set: it superseded the leader with a forged epoch, pushed ops in, and acked as one of five so majority-ack released a client `Accepted` for a write two nodes held. **The fix is that learning a member and placing rooms on it are two admissions.** The roster is what a node dials, probes and gossips about; the ring is built from the **adopted** members alone, and a gossip-learned member is *pending* — reachable, converging, advertised back, in no room's replica set and no room's quorum — until the cluster adopts it. **Adoption could not be a local check**: placement must be identical on every node or two nodes disagree about who leads a room permanently, and "verified" is per-node by construction. So the *evidence* is disseminated instead of the verdict — a node records only that it completed an identity-checked peer link to a member, and that claim rides the same anti-entropy as liveness, as one `verified` flag per `Message::Gossip` tuple, recorded **against the member the receiving link is bound to** and against nobody the payload names. A member is adopted once two already-adopted **trust units** have verified it. **Cold review overturned four things in the first cut, and each is the interesting part.** (1) *Adoption is derived, never accumulated.* Sticky adoption made the ring a function of history — a member adopted before its vouchers were reaped stays placed on the node that saw that order and is never placed on one that did not, permanently — so the adopted set is recomputed from `configured` + `verifiers` to a fixpoint on every change, and is a pure function of state. (2) *A verifier is a trust unit, and a trust unit is a host.* Counting node ids was the mint one level up: a certificate names a host (C13) and a host mints unlimited ids, so a member holding two of them raised the whole bar alone. The bar counts distinct verifier **hosts** and excludes the candidate's own — a member vouching for a sibling on its host vouches for itself. (3) *An inbound link verifies nothing*, however well its certificate names the member: the member chooses when to dial in, so the vouch is one it caused, and it could dial in under each ground id in turn. Verification is this node's own dial; an indirect (ping-req) confirmation is liveness only. (4) *A member is dialed at its own node id.* The recorded advertise address was first-write-wins over a field any peer may set, and the ring turned on it, so whoever advertised a joiner first decided what every later dial verified — two nodes that saw it in a different order placed 27 of 64 sample rooms differently, forever. A node id *is* an advertise address, so the second name is dropped and the dial address is a function of the id alone; that also closes the reply half's freedom at the root rather than by a filter. **The bar is a constant**, not a fraction of the cluster (clamping it made the bar node-local and let a reap *lower* it and retroactively adopt), with one exception keyed on configuration: a node configured with no peers has no cluster to be outvoted by and its ring is what it has itself reached — the single-node deployment. Configured members are adopted from birth. A majority was rejected (an honest node dials every member it knows, so the marginal verifier past the second only delays a joiner) and so was relaying another node's verifications (with no signatures on the wire, "A and B verified X" is free to write). On the reply half a claim counts only where the dial established the sender, so under `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_REQUIRE_PEER_IDENTITY=1` a plaintext member's vouches are dropped — sound outbound, and exactly what C13's pass 3 rejected inbound. **Cold review ran twice and pass 2 overturned two more, both about what a trust unit is:** the unit was the host as *raw text* while the certificate binding compares it semantically, so `evil.example` beside `evil.example.` (or an IPv6 literal beside its expanded form) was one certificate and two vouchers — one machine holding two units adopts any id **anywhere**, which is not the accepted residual; and the bootstrap exception read `configured.len() <= 1` off a set that shrinks, so a node given one seed fell to the single-node rule the moment that seed was reaped and began placing on one vouch while its peers held the constant. **Pass 3 found two more, one root: an advertise address had many spellings and nothing reduced them.** The dial URL was built from the authority verbatim while the host was read as everything before the first `:`, so `wss://a.example:1@b.example:9000` *read* as `a.example` and *dialed* `b.example` — a member certified for `a.example` alone ground such an id onto any room, bound its own certificate to it, and let honest nodes verify it by dialing the honest node it embedded (a full bypass under mTLS, not the bounded own-host residual). And ids were never canonicalized, so `x:9000` / `ws://x:9000` / `WS://x:9000` / `x:9000/anything` were four members one honest endpoint answers for: all truthfully verified, all adopted, none of them ever speaks, so a room whose replica set filled with them waited on acks that never came — targeted, since HRW is public. An advertise address is now an authority and nothing else, in one canonical spelling, enforced at config, at the gossip tuple and at a link's claim. A present-but-empty `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_PEERS` (a clustered node running the single-node bar of one) is refused at startup. **Pass 4 found three more, all in how an id is derived from configuration:** the bootstrap exception read a *de-duplicated set*, so a peer list of only this node's own address collapsed to one member and dropped the bar to a single vouch — the very state the empty-list refusal had been added to catch; `CRDTSYNC_NODE_ID` was the one door that did not canonicalize (a regression from the plain `trim()` it replaced), so a node written in a non-canonical spelling carried a **doppelgänger of itself** — adopted on two honest vouchers, placed on 468 of 2000 sample rooms it never answers for, unable to refute a suspicion of itself, dropping every follower-head report; and the port was not canonicalized, so `:9000`/`:09000`/`:009000` were three ring positions on one listener, which is the targeted write-stall pass 3 was supposed to have closed and which an operator reaches with no attacker by writing a leading zero. The bar now reads the list rather than the set, every door canonicalizes, the port is read as a number, and an address with no canonical form is refused where it is written. **Pass 10 caught a fix that was worse than the defect it closed, which is the reason the loop runs to a clean pass rather than to a fixed one.** Pass 9 measured a real window — a claim naming a member the receiver had not yet met was dropped with its tuple, so retention, and therefore the ring, depended on frame arrival order (959/2000 rooms). Holding the claim instead closed it and opened an unbounded map: those ids are on no roster, so `reap_dead` can never strike them and `rebuild_placement` never scans them, making them state nothing reclaims. One inbound 24.9 MB frame retained **376.5 MB — 15x the wire** — and stalled the single-threaded registry actor 591 ms; at the default 64 MiB frame cap that is 3.3 million claims per frame. It also made the tombstone refusal expire with `TOMBSTONE_RETENTION_TICKS`, letting a returned member be adopted pre-verified (980/2000 rooms). The window was the cheaper defect and is not even a loss, since a verifier re-advertises what it verified every round: a claim that outruns its subject is re-sent on the next round with that same verifier — O(cluster size) intervals, since a node gossips to one uniformly-random peer per interval and claims are never relayed (C39). Measured at n=33: mean 6.8 intervals to hold any voucher's claim, p95 23, max 86; mean 22.6 to adoption, max 160. Reverted, with the healing property pinned instead. The regression test written for the held-claim version was itself vacuous — it drove `merge_liveness`, which admits the unmet member from the same tuple, so it passed on the very commit it was written to catch. **Passes 5 and 6 found two more, both places where a rule was stated but not made structural:** the canonical form was checked to be a fixpoint over a list of spellings rather than by construction, and brackets — the IPv6 literal's syntax — were never required to *contain* an IPv6 literal, so `[10.0.0.1:9000]:9443` (an operator typo, no attacker) parsed as a host whose own text carries colons and yielded the id `10.0.0.1:9000:9443`: accepted at the config door, adopted from birth as a configured member, placed on every room HRW gave it, and dialable by nobody, so those rooms could reach no quorum — and written as `CRDTSYNC_NODE_ID` it has no canonical form at all, so peers drop it from gossip and refuse its `PeerAuth` and the node joins nothing while believing it has. And a verifier claim gated the *node* it was about but never the **sender** it was stored under, so a certified host opening a link per port banked an unbounded verifier entry per link against every member — keyed on ids no reap ever strikes, because reaping strikes members. Both bars are now the same one predicate on both sides. **Residual, pinned as a passing test and filed as C34:** a member that owns a host owns every id under it, answers at each ground id, and honest nodes verify one *truthfully* — so adoption bounds the mint to the member's own host and to reachable ids, and cannot bound it further; closing it needs the ring to weigh trust units rather than ids. With no certificates the bar is not a bar at all (a secret-holder binds a link to any id, C13's own residual) and what remains is reachability. Suites: `crates/server/tests/adoption.rs` (51); `gossip.rs` convergence tests now drive rounds until the ring converges as well as the roster; C13's residual test is removed from `peer_identity.rs`. Wire: `Message::Gossip`'s tuple gains a trailing flag byte (`MemberAdvert`). See DECISIONS (2026-07-28). → *Cluster*.
**C58 — `encode_state` was not order-canonical for a displacement-heavy pool (crates/core) — DONE (#379).** Byte-equality of `encode_state` is what the snapshot/compaction arc treats as replica identity, and a pool of creates outranked by later scalars broke it two ways while **every read agreed** — which is why no convergence test saw it. **First, displacement was treated as a reason to wait.** `ready` gated on the target being *installed*, so a write whose container had lost its map slot was buffered — and a slot need never come back, so it was buffered forever: one replica held the op while another that saw it a moment earlier had it applied into the retained container. That is also an unbounded retention shape (the class C3 bounds for transactions), reachable on honest traffic rather than on a forged envelope. **The ruling — a displaced container is a target, not a reason to wait.** Readiness now gates on the target being *materialised* (`Document::materialised`), and the five `is_displaced` guards on the routing path go with it (map slots, counters, `create_container`, and the two sequence handles, renamed `list_at`/`text_at`), since they would drop what the gate admits. Dropping the op instead was never open — the early replica has it in the retained container, so the late one would diverge outright the moment the slot is re-won. Retention exists so a reinstated container comes back whole, and an op addressed to it is part of what it comes back with: the rule §Tree Moves already followed for children sequences, restated as one rule over every op family. What still waits is an op whose target this replica has never seen created, bounded by a create already in flight. **Second, the buffer was encoded as it arrived.** `encode_state` now writes it in **op-id order** — `(author, that author's sequence)`, the key the causal frontier is already encoded by, total because an id names one op. Imposed at the encode, not maintained as the buffer fills: a sorted insert costs a memmove per arrival, and a sender picks both how much a replica holds (nothing caps the buffer — C20's open point) and where each arrival lands in it, so a backlog delivered newest-first was quadratic — measured 40 000 held ops, 10 ms → 8.9 s, on the first pass, which is where that pass put it. What the storage order still decides — which of several ready ops the drain replays first, which of several complete groups commits first — is not a state decision, because the drain runs to a fixpoint and re-holds any member an earlier commit left unready. No `STATE_VERSION` bump: the section's bytes are unchanged, only their order. Spec `crates/core/tests/state_order.rs` folds a displacement-heavy pool in every rotation, reversed, and under a seeded permutation sweep, pins applies-rather-than-waits and the visible-on-reinstate preservation, and pins the buffer's order against both a seq-only sort and a reversed one. Every removed guard was mutation-checked: restoring any of the six is killed by a named test, four of them by pre-existing convergence tests — which is what makes the routing removals part of the same ruling rather than a drive-by. The undo seam moves onto the same rule — `replay_steps` dropped an inverse whose container a peer had displaced *because* a peer would have buffered it, and a peer no longer does, so that undo stopped being silently discarded; `resolvable` survives where *installed* rather than merely held is the real question — whether a movable node renders, and whether an undo has to revive a container before emitting into it. **Two costs this unit does not close.** Filed as C68: a container create racing a *delete* of its key still encodes two ways, found by running the convergence generator's pools through `encode_state` rather than `fingerprint` — a rule about what a deleted-container tombstone records, pre-existing on `main` and untouched here, which is why the order-canonical claim is scoped to a displacement-heavy pool rather than to every pool. Filed as C67: both snapshot projections purge by live path, so a displaced container is served whole, and moving the later writes out of the (filtered) buffer and into it widens that from the pre-displacement writes to all of them. ARCHITECTURE §Map Slot Safety + §Dependency Model amended, DECISIONS logged. → *Core / State codec*.

**C24 — two ops carrying one stamp into one children list resolved by arrival order (crates/core) — DONE (#371).** A children-list position is keyed `(list, stamp)` and a document holds at most one, since `read_state` refuses a duplicate. #366 made the writers honour that; it left *which* of two colliding ops wins to delivery order, so two `XmlMove`s under one `ClientId` at one `Stamp` (both admissible — dedup is on `OpId`, the id-space record bounds only an honest mint) moved different nodes on two replicas that folded the same ops, and their `encode_state` bytes differed. `XmlInsertChild` reached it more quietly: `xml_child_id` mixes the kind in, so a tagged and a tagless insert at one stamp derive *different* children that collide on the position without colliding on the element. **Fixed by an intrinsic rank** — `placement_index` now carries the owning node rather than being a set, and the key goes to **a birth over a move, then the smaller element id**: a total order over data both replicas already hold. That rank orders *nodes*, so two claims naming one node — two moves of it, two inserts whose kinds agree — are not a contest at all: the key is already the claimant's own, and the sequence re-seats the id at the **meet** of the two anchors, which cannot show which arrived first. A contest is what takes the winner's position outright, since the loser's is nothing to it. The *tag* two such inserts carry is a second thing they name in common and is still the first arrival's, filed as C44. A contest there would have had to know what put the incumbent at the key, and nothing answers that: the move log dedups on the stamp alone, so a move of another node at that stamp into another list takes the slot and the key-holder records no edge. A birth outranks because the key is where the born node's id comes from; only a claim naming a child this key derives can take a birth's, and only two ever do (a stamp's tagged and tagless child are the whole field). A later-arriving winner *evicts*: the incumbent's `Placement` goes, its move edge is withdrawn (`TreeMoves::remove`, by the same undo-and-replay a late arrival uses, so what is left is the fold of the surviving log), its reachability edge is re-derived from the surviving log (a born node keeps its birth list, a shell reaches nothing), and a node left with no placement at all returns to `revealed_pending` — stranded with neither, every later move of it would be refused forever. A **refused** claimant is recorded pending for the same reason, and a decode restores it, so a reload does not forget a loser was movable. Two order-free properties the rank leans on came out of review: the birth test reads the key, not the registry (a forged `XmlReveal` under the other kind would otherwise invert the rank), and a node's placement record is written key-sorted, because grown-in-arrival-order it made two *honest* replicas encode different bytes. A node *born* at the key keeps its birth list as its edge, written whether or not the birth took the key — and a decode re-derives it from the parent link, since a birth left placeless has none to read it back from (one that kept a move placement is not recovered — C45), before the log replay rather than after it, at the projection re-fold as well as the decode. The created-under relation also runs through the map half one hop at a time, so the cycle check reaches as deep as the nesting. The sequence slot is re-seated (`List::reseat`) rather than re-inserted, since `insert_at` is idempotent on the id — and it reaches the tombstones, because a delete landing between the two ops freezes the *loser's* anchor into the dead run on one replica and the winner's on the other, so `reseat` lifts the id out of its run, re-seats it and re-deletes (a delete stays terminal; only the position is restated). One further edge, found through eviction but reachable from three honest ops (birth, delete of the birth slot, move): `refold_moves` skipped re-pointing `parents` for a node it found deleted, leaving that edge at whatever the previous fold wrote, and `parents` rides the snapshot; it is now re-pointed unconditionally. Spec `crates/core/tests/xml_stamp_collision.rs` folds each shape in both orders and compares snapshot bytes. **The shell case is conditional on C41**: a born loser's movability survives a reload (the decode re-derives it from the parent link), a *shell's* does not, because a shell has no such link — filed there, not here. ARCHITECTURE §Tree Moves + DECISIONS updated. The plain-`ListInsert` sibling is out of scope and filed as C40. → *Core / XML*.

**C29 — a room installed by snapshot has no creator, so both catch-up seams serve it unredacted (crates/server) — DONE (#374).** `ensure_creator` fired only on the leader's *client* ops-write path, and a follower serves no client write — so a room a node held purely through replication had no creator, no doc-ACL authority root, and therefore no redaction at all: `reads_whole_document` short-circuits `true` on a creatorless room and `doc_acl_read_at` abstains, while `acl_records` is non-empty because ACL ops ride the log like any other. **Reproduced against `main` before the fix**, on three seams at once: with the schema tier granting root read to any authenticated actor and the creator denying bob read at `/secret`, the leader withheld the key and a follower served it — through the op catch-up, through the snapshot catch-up (`project_served_state`), and through a version fetch alike. Not a narrowing error; the redaction was skipped wholesale. **The creator is now replicated room metadata.** `Message::Replicate` and `Message::ReplicateSnapshot` each carry `creator: Option<Vec<u8>>` (an optional byte string, ahead of `Replicate`'s remainder-consuming op batch); the leader reads it off the hub per frame, a follower adopts it through `ensure_creator` on the ops path and through `install_snapshot` on the state-transfer path. **Both frames, not just the snapshot** — a room *born* on the leader reaches its followers as an ops delta and may never take a state transfer, so a snapshot-only field leaves the common case creatorless indefinitely; the binding is set-once and immutable, so re-sending it costs an actor id per frame and can never conflict. `install_room_state` takes the root and composes it set-once (`standing.or(incoming)`), as does the durable load's own snapshot branch, because each *replaces* the room and a state transfer is idempotent by design: taking the incoming root would let a re-sent frame re-root a room, and one naming none would drop the authority every deny in the state is decided under. It also persists the room's metadata — so an installed root is durable on the same footing a written one is — and therefore carries the room's monotone op-version high-water across the replace, since writing the post-install `None` into that record would have put a *lower* all-time high-water on disk than the one already there. **The escalation is the sharper half:** failover makes a replica a leader, a leader takes client writes, so `ensure_creator` finally fires there and the *next actor to write* took `/` — ownership, not just an over-wide read. **`import_room` deliberately still installs none** — portable snapshot bytes are a `Document` encoding and name no creator, so there is nothing to carry; the laundering that follows from it is C28's, filed and left standing (it holds a source room and can copy its root, which is a different fix). **Only an actor that could come back is admitted, wherever it arrives from:** the write path's anonymous-writer refusal moved down into `Hub::ensure_creator`, the install composition, and the durable load — the three places a root arrives — as the one predicate `acl::is_authenticated`, since set-once means a root that can never re-present wedges the room's authority unrepairably. **An empty actor is deliberately admitted**: refusing to root is not fail-closed, since a rootless room reads every deny in it as inert, and every other tier already counts an empty actor as authenticated. That unrepairability is the deliberate half — a replica rooted wrongly stays that way, which is filed as **C56** (set-once versus epoch-led repair, a ruling that belongs with the real election), alongside **C54** (nothing carries the op-version high-water to a brand-new replica) and **C55** (a root established by a dedup no-op reaches no replica until the room's next fresh commit). Tests: a new `replicated_creator.rs` (leader control + creator carried by each path + each state-serving seam redacting on the replica — the op catch-up, the snapshot catch-up, the version fetch, and the diff query C27 (#375) made a fourth reader of the same projection + a promoted replica keeping its root + set-once through both wire frames and through the hub call + a root that could never re-present rooting nothing + a rootless frame pinned as leaving the replica rootless), the replication spec's frame equality now pinning the creator, creator round-trips on both wire frames with the truncation sweeps widened to cover the field, an installed-creator restart, a stored anonymous root rooting nothing, and a duplicate stored record — with and without a snapshot — failing to re-root, all in `persistence.rs`, and the high-water preserved across an install in `snapshot_transfer.rs`. **The suite was grafted onto the parent commit (66eb12a) and watched fail there** rather than reasoned about: 8 of the 10 that compile against it fail — the three redaction seams reproduced, the promotion escalation, and each carrying assertion — while the leader-side control still passes, so the failures are specific to the replica rather than a broken fixture; the durable-load guard fails there too, beside its pre-existing sibling that passes. **Mutation-swept against an isolated copy of the committed tree** (`--no-fail-fast`, named test lines only, with a floor on suites-actually-reported so a dead run reads as NO-RUN rather than a pass — which it did twice, once when a `kill -9` left a mutant applied and the fresh run captured it as pristine): **22 mutants, 22 killed, 0 survivors**, each by a *named* test. Every seam that carries or judges the root is pinned separately — the two send paths, the two dial arms, both adopt paths, the set-once composition in each direction, the persist, the high-water carry-across, the anonymous check at all three arrival seams, set-once and the high-water carry at the durable load's snapshot branch, set-once at its meta block, and the four codec mutations including a field-order swap. **Two runs reported something other than a kill, and neither was a hole in the tests** — which is why each was reproduced rather than reasoned about. The mutant meant to root *before* the ingest only *inserted* a call, leaving the original in place, so it could not change behaviour; formed correctly as a move it dies to `a_room_born_in_one_commit_carries_its_creator_on_that_commit` and nothing else. And the high-water anchor became ambiguous once the durable load grew the same composition, so the sweep declined to guess; disambiguated, `install_room_state`'s copy dies to `an_installed_snapshot_keeps_the_rooms_op_version_high_water` and the durable load's to `a_second_stored_record_carrying_a_snapshot_keeps_the_high_water`. Re-adding the empty-actor guard DECISIONS argues against dies at each of the three arrival seams separately — `an_authenticated_actor_roots_a_room_whatever_its_id_looks_like` for the write, `an_installed_root_is_admitted_whatever_its_id_looks_like` for the install, `a_stored_root_is_admitted_whatever_its_id_looks_like` for the durable load — since one test covering one seam would have left the ruling unpinned at the other two. See DECISIONS (2026-07-29). → *Server / Replication*.

**C26 — the SDK apply seams reported a permanent refusal as silence (crates/ffi + crates/wasm + sdks) — DONE (#370).** `crdtsync_doc_apply` and `WasmDocument::apply` folded a decoded batch as `ops.iter().filter(|op| doc.apply(op)).count()`, so an app learned how many ops applied and nothing else — while `Document::apply` answers `false` for three unrelated reasons of which only one is permanent. A buffered op (which a later arrival commits) and an op no replica will ever hold both read as "not counted", and the two want opposite responses: wait, versus surface a client bug. C19 (#367) made the distinction answerable (`Op::is_admissible`, public and pure) without plumbing it; this plumbs it. **Every binding now reports both counts, classified by that same predicate**, so this seam and the server's ingress cannot disagree about *which* refusals are permanent: the C ABI takes a nullable `uint32_t *out_refused` (header regenerated), wasm's `apply` returns `{ applied, refused }`, JS an `ApplyOutcome` object, Python an `ApplyOutcome` NamedTuple, Go a second return value. **The judgement is shared with the server's ingress; the batch policy deliberately is not** — this fold applies the admissible remainder where `handle_ops` refuses the whole frame, because only the server has an ack frontier taken as a max over the batch. **A refused-op *list* was considered and rejected** (a `takeRefused` drain mirroring `takeRejected`): per-handle state with its own lifetime rules, a semantically muddy stub on every client-backed replica whose refusals arrive as the server's `OpsRejected` instead, and a refusal is a fact about *this* batch. `crates/core/src/client.rs` is untouched — advancing `last_seen_seq` past a refused op is correct, since no replica will hold it. **One caveat is stated in all five bindings and pinned by a test rather than left to prose:** `applied` counts ops as they arrive, so an op that a later op in the *same* batch releases from the buffer is not among them; making that truthful needs a core seam this unit does not need. Suites: a refused-vs-buffered discrimination test per binding (FFI, wasm, JS, Python, Go), each also pinning the mixed batch (2 applied, 1 refused), a duplicate replay as not-refused, and a malformed batch as neither — with the C ABI's count proven *written* as zero there, so a caller reusing one variable across batches cannot read the last batch's refusals as this one's. The three SDK tests read the stamp's client id back before forging it, so a codec reorder fails by name. **Three local cold-review passes**, each caught something the last did not: the uninitialised-out-param reuse and an unreachable refusal cause in the doc comments; then that the count was still written from inside the panic guard, contradicting its own contract, and that the `applied` undercount had prose but no test; then that the seam was described as *relay*, which is false — `handle_ops`' `is_admissible` gate is schema-independent, so a relay-tier connection answers `MalformedOp` like any other server, and the seam with nothing upstream is offline, direct exchange, or a byte pipe the app carries itself. **Mutation-swept (`--no-fail-fast`) against an isolated clone of the committed tree**, named-test kills only: FFI 6 mutants (refused always zero 1, unfiltered count 1, `len - applied` 1, predicate negated 1, counts swapped 2, out-param never written 2); wasm 3 (1, 1, 6); and the always-zero lib kills 2 in the Python suite and 2 in the Go suite, so the SDK layers are not blind to it. The unmutated tree fails 0. Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-28). → *SDK / Ops*.

**C15 — `VersionFetch` served a captured room state with neither projection applied (crates/server) — DONE (#368).** `Message::VersionFetch` answered with `hub.version_state` verbatim, gated only on the deployment/schema-tier room `Read` that `version_room` resolves, while every other seam that hands a client a state blob runs `project_read_paths` then `project_zones` first. A version is the same bytes at an earlier sequence, so a reader that passed the room gate fetched the whole room out of any named version — withheld zones, doc-ACL-denied subtrees, and the full causal frontier naming every other replica's ops in the partitions it may not read. The fetch now runs the Subscribe composition in the same order over the version's own state, with two inputs taken from the channel rather than re-derived: its own `Subscription.zones` — the very set the live fan-out filters that channel's ops by, so the fetch narrows to the partitions the stream carries rather than to the actor's whole entitlement — and the channel-derived recipient C9 threads, so a version a reader adopts does not re-mint. **Element-scoped grants resolve against the version's tree, live records still govern.** Authorization is a decision about now; an element's redaction *path* is where it stood at capture. Resolving against the live room leaves the deny inert the moment the element leaves it — and an inert deny is not a narrower read, it is the whole version. **An unprojectable version is refused, not served:** version bytes come back off durable storage, so an undecodable one is reachable in a way a just-materialized snapshot is not, and the raw bytes still carry what a redaction would have cut; `zone_narrowing` is now the single home of "would the zone projection narrow at all", shared by the projection and that guard, and it decides *whole-zone* as "the set is exactly the declared id range" rather than "the set has as many members" — a set is resolved once, when a channel subscribes, and the room's governing schema lifts underneath it, so a set can both miss a declared partition and name ids no longer declared, and a count says nothing about which of the two it is (C30 is the cause). The refusal is non-closing, and it asks what a redaction is *configured over* for these bytes on this channel — the room holds a tuple, the channel holds a partial zone set — not what would actually have been cut: that answer needs the element index, the index needs the decode, and the decode is what failed. **Both seams now share one `project_served_state`**, rather than the fetch copying the snapshot arm's composition — duplicating it would have re-created, inside this unit, the shape the unit exists to answer. The *gate* is deliberately not widened to Subscribe's subtree-scoped admission — narrowing what a reader is served is this unit, widening who may fetch is not. Spec `crates/server/tests/version_projection.rs` (16) + `version_unreadable.rs` (5, non-Miri: it damages a durable versions file). Mutation-swept against an isolated copy of the committed tree, unmutated baseline 0: dropping either projection, `reads_all = true`, `recipient: None`, `session.client` instead of `for_channel`, the live element index, a narrowability test that ignores either half, serving an undecodable version, `zone_narrowing` always/never narrowing, `zone_narrowing` back to either half of its rule alone (the cardinality proxy, or containment without it), and a zone scope re-derived whole-room at fetch time instead of the channel's own each fail a named test — each anchored on the shared composition, so no mutation can land on a sibling seam and read as covered.

**C19 — a refused op was persisted, dedup-poisoned, fanned out and positively ACKed (crates/server) — DONE (#367).** `Hub::ingest_records` called `store.append` **before** `room.doc.apply`, discarded `apply`'s `bool`, and did `room.seen.insert` and `room.log.push` unconditionally; `handle_ops` computes `through` as a `max` over the *submitted* batch, so the session replied `Accepted { through }` and the author's outbox dropped the op. An op the replica refused was therefore durably logged, permanently deduped (a corrected resend of the same `OpId` swallowed forever), broadcast to every peer, replayed on every room reload, and **acked as landed while landing nowhere**. **The board understated the reach, and the correction is the reason this was a defect rather than hygiene.** C3 (#363) concluded "no *new* reachable trigger — the wire path decodes first and the codec fails the frame before `apply` sees it, so only an in-process caller reaches this refusal". That holds for C3's own `Tx.count` refusal, which the codec does bound, and **does not carry to the stamp predicates C17/C18 (#366) added**: `codec.rs` references none of `stamp_names_its_author`, `stamp_occupies_a_mintable_position` or `reservation_end` (grep count 0), and `decode_ops` bounds neither `stamp.offset`, nor `stamp.lamport`, nor `stamp.client` against `id.client`. The predicate gate at `doc.rs:2172` is on the *state-decode* path (a snapshot's encoded buffer), not the wire op path; the wire refusal is inside `apply`, after `decode_ops` has already succeeded. So a peer could send an op that decodes cleanly, is refused by every replica, and still poisons the durable log and draws a false ack — pinned by `a_refused_op_survives_a_codec_round_trip`. **What `apply`'s `false` meant, enumerated, is the whole fix.** Three unrelated situations, only one permanent: a **duplicate** (already applied or already held); a **wait** — target not yet reachable, or an atomic group incomplete — which a later arrival ends; and a **refusal forever**. Only the third may be dropped, so it is now named: `Op::is_admissible` is a public pure predicate over the op alone, `apply` and the snapshot decode both gate on it (one home for the rule, no drift), and every ingest seam can tell "never" from "not yet" without reading a bool that cannot distinguish them. **Refusing rather than logging is safe for exactly one reason, and it is the argument the unit rests on:** each condition reads the op and nothing else, so two replicas at unrelated states reach the same verdict, and the room converges on the op's *absence* rather than splitting over it. Dropping state a peer might still apply would not be safe; dropping state no peer will ever apply is. **The gate is at ingress and the seams hold it independently.** `handle_ops` refuses the batch recoverably (`OpsRejected` / a new `ErrorCode::MalformedOp`, tag 9, mirrored through wasm/FFI/Go/Python and both codec round-trip lists) so the author keeps its ops and is not told a lie — the frame was well-formed, so this is not a disconnect. The batch, not the op, because the ack frontier is a max over the whole batch and acking the admissible half would acknowledge the refused op's sequence with it. `Hub::ingest_records` and `ingest_branch` filter independently rather than trusting the session, because **`apply_replicate` reaches `Hub::ingest` from a peer's `Replicate` frame without crossing `handle_ops` at all** — and the branch tail matters on its own, since a tail folds into a document only at materialization, so an inadmissible op there would sit durable and undetected until the fold. `replay::canonical_tail` takes the same filter: it numbers a room's sequences from the tail the hub commits, so against bytes this node did not write an unfiltered tail would slide every later op's sequence by one and reconstruct the wrong point in time. **The waiting cases are untouched and pinned as such** — a buffered op and an incomplete transaction member are both logged, fanned out and acked exactly as before, the second because refusing a member for not applying yet would strand its whole group forever. **Deliberately not done:** the `bool`→enum refactor across `client.rs`, FFI, wasm, JS, Python and Go. The public predicate is what makes "never" answerable at any seam that cares; `client.rs` advancing `last_seen_seq` past a refused op is *correct* (no replica will ever hold it, so nothing is missed). What remains is that the SDK apply paths reduce the result to a count and report a refusal as silence — filed as **C26** rather than folded in. Suites: 17 cases in `crates/server/tests/refused_op.rs`. **Mutation-swept (`--no-fail-fast`) against an isolated `git archive` copy**, 9 mutations, none zero: no ingress gate 4; no `ingest_records` filter 3; no branch-tail filter 1; the gate filtering the offending op instead of refusing the batch 4; `is_admissible` ignoring the author check 4, the position check 10, the tx-count check 2; no `replay::canonical_tail` filter 1; `is_admissible` refusing a held transaction member 1. The unmutated tree fails 0. **The sweep audited the suite twice over**: the held-transaction-member mutant and the replay-tail mutant both survived the first sweep, and each is now killed by the test written in response. Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-28). → *Server / Ops*.

**C17 — the lamport mint did not clear its own id space, so an impersonating peer could plant the victim's next stamps (crates/core) — DONE (#366).** C8 (#355) bounded what a folded op may do to a partition clock — right, and reverting it reinstates a strictly worse overflow — but it also stopped the clock being an upper bound over the stamps the document holds, and `emit_stamped` still minted from the clock alone. **C8's `op.stamp.client == op.id.client` guard is not the invariant that protects an id space and blocked none of the attack it was added for:** both fields ride the same op, `session.rs` checks `op.id.client` only against the identity the client *declared* at Hello (authenticating that claim is the transport's job), and nothing checks `ClientId` uniqueness — so the attacker authors the whole batch under the victim's id, satisfying it. With the fold clamped, a run planted at `WIRE_CEILING + 1` moved no clock and landed exactly on the victim's next mint; measured on `main`, after an 8-char plant the victim's own `"A"`/`"B"`/`"C"` inserts were all dropped as replays and the text read `"MMMMMMMM"`. **The fix is C6's `free_seq` discipline applied to the stamp:** `Document::stamp_high_water` records the highest lamport under each client id, reservations included, and `mint_floor` starts the mint above the partition clock *and* this replica's own entry — reading the ids it holds rather than a position anyone handed it. **A held id counts, not merely an applied one** — a buffered op's stamp is recorded on the way into the buffer and a snapshot's own encoded buffer is folded in explicitly, or which replica re-mints onto a waiting run is a function of arrival order (reproduced). **Stored beside the clocks (`STATE_VERSION` 11 → 12), because deriving it from content cannot be completed, and the derive-only first cut was wrong in the mainline case:** a sequence encodes a dead run as `(head, len)`, so **deleting a plant hid up to 2^20 ids per chunk** and the victim's next insert after the reload was dropped as a replay; a counter persists no stamp and an ACL or ranged entry only the id *derived* from one; and a projection drops content wholesale, so an adopting replica re-issued its own live ids on honest traffic with no attacker. Storing it also makes a projection safe by default. **A stored figure is supplied by whoever hands the bytes over, so it only ever raises:** a decode **floors** the declaration with every stamp the bytes visibly carry plus the whole reservation of every op in the encoded buffer — without which a 24-byte edit zeroing the section hands a reload the planted ids back. Storing is what makes the record complete; the floor is what makes it trustworthy, and the invariant that falls out (a document's record dominates its own content) is why a projection re-floors after scrubbing. **The projection scrub is the causal frontier's rule applied to the record:** the recipient's own entry stays (a replica adopting a snapshot naming none of its own ids mints onto ids the room holds) and every other client's goes, since each counts what that replica minted inside the withheld partition. **The floor is global and unconditional, and two partition-scoped cuts were both wrong** — a cold pass on this branch forced it twice, so the reason is recorded: a stamp is a *document-global* id (a RangedElement's and an ACL tuple's ids derive from one alone, the move log orders every move by one), and every scoping rule is defeated by two inputs the replica does not control — an op's envelope names its own partition while its ids land in its *target's*, and a snapshot may declare any clock it likes. **The cost is a documented zone property and it is flagged, not quietly amended:** a zone's lamports now count on from the author's whole stamp position, so their gaps leak how much it minted elsewhere — the activity-inference channel ARCHITECTURE says zones close. Folding is untouched (an op still advances its own partition alone, so the streams stay causally independent), and the leak is numbering, not content — but the ruling is **C22**'s, below. C8's authorship check is **kept and re-documented honestly** (it refuses a malformed op and raises the bar from *naming* a `ClientId` to impersonating one; it does not protect an id space), now enforced on the decode path too, and joined by an id-space gate — a stamp whose *reservation* reaches past `LAMPORT_STATE_CEILING`, or that sits off the lamport axis at all, occupies no id and is refused. Without the first, one op at `(u64::MAX, u64::MAX - 1)` under a victim's id panicked its **second** edit after it, across reloads; without the second, two stamps differing only in `offset` derive the same `acl_id`/`ranged_id`/XML-child id, since `stamp_key` is `lamport ++ client` — which is also what killed the two-dimensional-successor design the first cut used. Its two flawed tests are repaired in place: the forged-stamp one rescoped to the mismatched-author shape it covers, and the save/reload sweep's `continue` (which ate exactly the iteration exposing C18) deleted. **Residuals stated:** lamport ordering in a partition parked at the clamp stays spent (C8's price), and a peer impersonating a `ClientId` can still inflate that client's high-water — server-side identity binding belongs beside this but cannot *be* the fix, since an offline or P2P replica has no server. **Mutation-swept on the merged head (`fdd8b86`) against an isolated `git archive` copy of the committed tree:** the mint reading the partition clock alone — this defect itself — fails 20; the unmutated tree fails 0 of 2981. The suite's other 21 mutations are **not measured on this head**: the sweep was cut short twice by the machine and every earlier run measured a superseded commit, so those numbers are deliberately not carried over. The harness and its mutation set are kept so the remainder can be measured without rebuilding them. See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Core / Ops*.

**C18 — `encode_state` could emit bytes `decode_state` refuses, and the swallowed decode destroyed a published branch (crates/core + crates/server) — DONE (#366).** C8's refusal of a declared clock above `LAMPORT_STATE_CEILING` is right in principle, but it bounded only the way *in*: the comparison is `>`, so a clock of exactly the ceiling is accepted and one mint puts the replica above it — measured on `main`, after **one** ordinary edit `decode_state(&doc.encode_state())` returns `Err`.
