The severity was downstream, because three callers read that refusal as an *absence*: `Hub::catch_up_branch` turned it into `Catchup::Ops(Vec::new())`, `materialize_branch` folded the empty delta into a fresh `Document`, and `publish` → `write_branch_base` froze that **empty** replica over a published branch and its captured version, durably, with no error anywhere; and `index.rs`'s three simulation decodes failed **open**, reading an unreadable room as "crosses no zone" and "violates no schema". **The core half is one line, and it is C17's other half:** the local mint's clock advance is clamped at the same `LAMPORT_STATE_CEILING` `read_state` enforces, so every path into a clock is bounded by it (a fold clamps lower still at the wire ceiling, a decode refuses above it, a mint clamps at it), `encode_state ∘ decode_state` is total, and `>` is correct as written. **Clamping the stored clock is exactly what C8 could not do** — a stored clock is a high-water over published ids, so lowering one re-issues them — and it is safe here for one reason only: the mint no longer reads that slot for id-freedom, C17's record does. **Bounding a stored record bounds the id space, and there refusal is the only total answer, so `emit_stamped` returns `Option`:** clamping the record was measured to re-issue forever (a replica at the ceiling mints `CEILING + 1`, records it clamped back to `CEILING`, mints `CEILING + 1` again), and the sub-lamport `offset` is no escape because the derived-id key omits it. Exhaustion is now a visible refused edit (`Document::can_mint`), and it takes the **whole** transaction — a create refused in an exhausted partition would otherwise leave the writes into it addressing a container no replica can ever hold, which a peer buffers forever. **One constant bounds the whole id space, and that is a deliberate departure from C8's apply-with-clamp:** the wire gate, the mint and the decode all stop at `LAMPORT_STATE_CEILING`, because a gate at a *lower* constant than the mint is exactly the divergence C8 measured (an op at the gate parks the clock there, the next honest mint is one above, every peer refuses it). `LAMPORT_WIRE_CEILING` answers a different question and is untouched — what a *fold* may do to a *clock* — so an op between the two is still applied with the clock clamped under it, and the 2^62 gap stays the runway a clock-parked replica mints into. **The swallowing callers fail closed, with "unknown" as its own answer:** `Catchup::Unavailable` — distinct from an empty delta on purpose, since reading unknown as "nothing to send" is what let a publish freeze an empty document over a live branch — makes `materialize_branch` answer `None`, so `publish` publishes nothing and the target branch and its durable base are untouched; a subscriber gets an error rather than being told it is at the head of a stream it holds none of; replication catch-up dials nothing. In `index.rs` both gates are reject boundaries, so `batch_zone_crossings` returns `Option` (`None` = no verdict ⇒ the session refuses the batch) and `batch_introduces_schema_violation` returns `true`, both split into pure-function-of-the-state inner forms so the unreadable case is decided and tested where it lives (the core fix makes it unreachable through a live room). **Checked against C19 and out of its way:** neither what `Document::apply` returns nor when changes for any op C8 already admitted, so `Hub::ingest_records` is untouched and C19 still owns that seam. See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Core / Ops*.

**C13 — every gate downstream of peer admission trusted any cluster member equally (crates/server + crates/core) — DONE (#365).** C10's cluster secret is one deployment-wide value, so it separated a member from a stranger and members from each other not at all — and three gates downstream of admission were written as though it did. **Leadership**: `gate_replica_frame` checked only that *this* node held the room, and superseded its own claim on any strictly higher epoch, so any admitted peer could push ops into any room the node replicated and strip the leader of any room it led. **Membership**: `apply_gossip` merged whatever a peer advertised, so an admitted peer planted an arbitrary address in every node's member set — which every node then dialed and handed the cluster secret to, and which joined the placement ring and counted toward each room's quorum. **Durability**: `FollowerHeads` named its own reporter and `catch_up_follower_reporting` overwrote *that* node's watermark, so one member credited a third with data it does not hold and `write_has_majority` released a client `Accepted` for a write no majority ever held. All three were reproduced against the pre-fix tree before anything was changed. **A link now says who it is**: `PeerAuth` (tag 52) carries the dialer's node id alongside the secret and the acceptor binds the connection to that member; `Conn::peer` went from `bool` to the bound `NodeId`, and the three handlers that decide against a member take the sender as an argument. **The binding is the member's advertise host**, checked against any *host* the verified mTLS client certificate names — its `dNSName`/`iPAddress` SANs only, never an e-mail or URI SAN, never the Common Name, never a wildcard — one rule, case-insensitive, port excluded (no certificate carries one), all names rather than the leading one (a node cert conventionally spells its host as a DNS name *and* as the IP literal an address may use), and the *same fact the dialer already verifies in the other direction* under C12 when it authenticates an acceptor against the address it dialed. Rejected: per-node shared secrets (closes the hole by closing the gossip-join path, O(N) rotation), a self-asserted id checked only for membership (any member can assert any member's id — it stops only the stranger the secret already stops), an explicit subject↔node map (a second source of truth, updated on every join, skewable per node), and matching the whole advertise address (no certificate can carry a port). Stated cost: members sharing a host share a trust unit. **Identity is declared like transport** — `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_REQUIRE_PEER_IDENTITY=1` refuses every certificate-less peer, and a node requiring it while verifying no client certificate or presenting none of its own refuses to start; C10's "an optional gate cannot close an ingest seam" does not transfer, since the secret remains the mandatory admission gate and identity only refines trust *among* admitted members. `request`-mode client-cert verification suffices, so ordinary clients still connect certless. **Gossip's rule is asymmetric on purpose**: the inbound half introduces only the sender, the reply half (from a node this one *dialed*, i.e. already in its member set) introduces freely — which is what keeps a joiner converging, at the cost of one extra round of dials. **Cold review ran nine times and seven passes overturned part of the last** — Copilot was quota-exhausted for the whole unit and cubic reported `skipping`, so local review was the only external gate. **P1** argued a frame from a member outside a room's replica set should be *fenced* rather than dropping the link, and found two real holes: the gossip rule must constrain the dial *address* too (constraining the node id alone left the same channel open one field over, since a newly-learned member's address is recorded verbatim and every node dials it), and *all* of a certificate's names must be weighed rather than the leading one. **P2** found P1's fence wrong — the drop *is* the repair path, since the steady path mirrors only fresh commits and the backlog is re-sent only by the redial catch-up, so a silently fenced frame is never re-driven and later frames stack on a gap the leader never learns about — and found the minting residual below, a re-bindable `PeerAuth`, and that an IP literal must be compared as an address rather than a string. **P3** overturned two of P2's own additions: a `FollowerHeads` member check that changed no watermark (`rooms_led_for` already filters by placement) but dropped every inbound link during a join window, and a startup refusal of plaintext members resting on a wrong premise — a member's advertised scheme describes *its own* listener, while the link carrying its identity into this node is the one it dials. **P4 found the binding itself still open**: the certificate reader returned e-mail and URI SANs and the Common Name alongside host names and the binding compared all of them, so a certificate legitimately issued for one node, carrying another node's host in its e-mail SAN or CN, spoke as that node. **P5** found P4's split had made a *presented* certificate that names no host fail **open** — it looked certless, so the claim stood unchecked where before the split it was compared and refused. **P6** found P5's IP rule one character short: the reader tested raw SAN text while the comparison normalizes first, so `10.0.0.6.` passed as a name and matched as an address. Both now go through one normalizer — a reader and a comparator that disagree about what a value *is* are a hole wherever they meet. **P7 raised no HIGH and confirmed the binding sound**, finding P6's un-gating of the self-certificate refusal one step too broad. **P8** found P7's carve-out had dropped a refusal nothing replaced — an advertise address naming no host reached neither check — which now belongs to the dial policy, where it is an address error rather than a certificate one; and drew out the recorded residual that the client-certificate posture must be uniform cluster-wide, since the condition reads this node's own listener as a proxy for whether its *peers* request a certificate, which a rolling change makes wrong in both directions. The non-verifying case therefore warns rather than refusing. **P9** raised no HIGH and found the hostless-address refusal scoped to this node alone while the docs promised it for every member — a member's hostless address was not even a permanent dial error, so it was redialed forever with no startup refusal; it now lives in the address parse every consumer shares. **Surfaced and fixed en route**: `actor_from_client_cert` read only DNS/email/URI SANs, so an **IP-address** SAN — what a cluster addressed by IP literal issues — yielded no identity and fell through to the Common Name; IP SANs now read as their textual form (the live two-node test failed on exactly this). **Two limits, each pinned as a passing test rather than implied.** Inside a room's replica set the epoch is still the only arbiter, because a genuinely promoted replica must be able to supersede a stale leader — the election's question (HRW+epoch → Raft), not a credential's. And placement is HRW over the member set, a pure and publicly computable function, while the join path lets an unknown node introduce itself — so a member can **mint** a node id that places it into a chosen room's replica set and reach these gates from inside it. Identity bounds the mint space to the member's own certified host, which is what makes the gates hold against a member *misreaching*; against a **compromised** member they need placement over verified members, which cannot be a local filter (placement must be identical on every node or the ring diverges). Filed as **C25**, a prerequisite rather than a follow-on, and stated plainly: this unit closes cross-member impersonation and third-party assertion, not a member that has been taken over. **Deployment** — the third deployment-facing change in this area, after C10's required `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_SECRET` and C12's `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_CA` + client-cert knobs. A cluster with no peer certificates runs unchanged. One already running peer mTLS (i.e. any C12 deployment that set `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_CLIENT_CERT` *and* `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CLIENT_CA`) must have each node's certificate name that node's advertise host **before** upgrading, because a presented certificate is decisive whether or not identity is required — such a node now refuses to start, which is the loud version of what its peers would otherwise do silently. To identify members: give each node a certificate whose `dNSName`/`iPAddress` SAN is its own advertise host, point `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_CLIENT_CERT`/`_KEY` at it, set `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CLIENT_CA` with `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CLIENT_AUTH=request` (the default `require` would lock out every certless application client), then set `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_REQUIRE_PEER_IDENTITY=1`. Keep the client-CA and peer-certificate settings uniform across the cluster: a node holding a mismatched certificate while verifying none itself only warns, and its peers refuse every link it opens. **Mutation-swept with measured counts** — 29 mutations, every one re-measured under a floor guard that refuses to report unless the run compiled, was not signal-killed, and cleared 24 of its 25 suites; all 29 cleared it, none returned zero. (The floor exists because an earlier reading came back `0` from a run killed under machine contention, and a second mutation silently failed to compile — both would otherwise have read as coverage holes.) *The binding*: admitting a certificate that does not name the claimed member fails 2, reading a *presented* certificate that names no host as certless 1, admitting a certless link under a required identity 1, admitting an empty claim 1, allowing a re-bind 1. *What names a host*: letting an e-mail/URI SAN name one 4, the Common Name 5, dropping the IP-SAN reader 6, letting a `dNSName` be an address 2, testing that without the binding's own normalization 2, keeping only the leading host name 2, folding an arbitrary run of root labels 1. *The comparison*: comparing an IP as a string 1, keeping the root label 3, preferring an address over a name for the session actor 1. *The gates*: letting the sender be this node 1, a member that does not replicate the room 5, removing the gossip filter 3, leaving its dial address unconstrained 1, leaving the head-report reporter unchecked 1. *The dial*: claiming no node id 8, an address naming no host 2. *The startup refusals*: no membership 1, no client-certificate verification 1, no identity of its own 1, a member naming no host 1, this node's own certificate naming the wrong host 2, gating that on the policy 1, refusing rather than warning where no certificate is asked for 1. **Three fixes were shipped unpinned and the sweep is what caught them** — the un-gated self-certificate refusal, its warn-vs-refuse condition, and the single-root-label fold. Each is now killed by exactly one test and that test is the one added in response, confirmed by capturing the killing test per mutation rather than inferred: `a_certificate_for_another_host_refuses_to_start_even_mid_rollout`, `a_certificate_for_another_host_still_serves_where_no_peer_asks_for_one`, `only_one_root_label_folds`. So the sweep audits the *suite*, not only the source: all three were production changes the tests appeared to exercise and did not. Specs: `crates/server/tests/peer_identity.rs` (34 — a reproduction for each of the three, the replica-set supersede pinned as the residual, the gossip narrowing including the hidden-injection and still-disseminating-a-death cases, the join path, the forged-report-releases-no-Accepted durability chain, per-connection identity, the required-identity refusal, every node-to-node frame still working, a second `PeerAuth` refused on an admitted link, plus a real mTLS cluster replicating end to end with a certless client, a certificate naming its host several ways still binding it, a cluster-signed certificate for another address reaching no peer plane, and the startup refusals), 14 binding/normalization tests in `dial.rs` and 11 certificate-identity tests in `tls.rs`, with `peer_auth.rs`/`gossip.rs`/`self_heal.rs` updated where the old contract was the defect. See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Cluster*.

**C3 — a malformed `Tx.count` was an unbounded remote memory-retention primitive (crates/core) — DONE (#363).** `count` tells a receiver to hold a group's members until that many arrive, and nothing bounded it at the decode boundary or ever gave up on a partial group: `count == 0`, `count == u32::MAX`, more members held than the count admits, and members of one `(client, tx id)` disagreeing on the count all left members buffered for the life of the replica — persisted by `encode_state`, and (since C6) counted among the ids the replica holds. Completeness was also decided against `buffer[idxs[0]].tx.count`, whichever member landed first, so a group told it was smaller than it is committed at that size and left the rest holding a size their bucket had already spent. **The cap is now a protocol constant, not the "default 1000" ARCHITECTURE named and nothing enforced** — a receiver decides completeness from a declared count, so a node with its own cap would emit groups every peer refuses — enforced at *both* ends: the codec refuses `0` and anything past `MAX_TX_MEMBERS`, and `tag_atomic` leaves an oversized group **untagged** rather than tagged with a size whose refusal takes the whole framed batch with it (an oversized transaction would otherwise become dropped ops, not a non-atomic one). **The line two cold-review passes drew, and the substance of the unit: only a judgement on a member's own declared size is order-free.** The first cut untagged a whole *bucket* the moment it looked unreachable; whether it looks that way is a function of which members have landed, so `m1(3), m2(3), m3(2)` delivered `m1,m2,m3` released all three while `m1,m3,m2` released two and then held `m2` against a bucket already given up on — one op set, two states, and stranding on 4 of 6 orders where the defect it replaced stranded on 2. So a size outside the cap is **refused** where it arrives — at the codec, which fails the frame carrying it, and at the `apply` seam an in-process relay or SDK reaches without decoding, which drops the one op and lets its group-mates through; refusing holds nothing either way. And a bucket's size is what its members **agree** it is: a completeness test, not a release, so a bucket without unanimity is never complete and is held. **What no count rule reaches, stated rather than claimed:** a rewrite consistent across every member is, to a receiver, an honest group of the smaller size followed by a stray — it commits at that size, over whichever members arrived first. Two review passes caught this being sold as fixed, the second finding a third route to it (two copies of one op id under different envelopes, so the bucket reads whichever won the dedup — a hostile duplicate, or a destranded copy racing the tagged one). All three are pinned by tests asserting the two orders *disagree*, and filed as **C21**. **Everything left is undecidable locally** (a member that never arrives is indistinguishable from one still in flight), so `Document::evict_partial_transactions` is a way to give up rather than a rule: it untags every group still waiting, reports how many, and how long to wait first is the caller's policy since the core reads no clock. It untags rather than discards for C11's reason — the members are ops the replica holds and no peer will resend — and leaves C6/C9's id accounting untouched (an evicted member is applied or still buffered, never free to re-mint) and C1's readiness gate running (a member whose container is absent waits on alone). **The consequence is stated, not hidden: a replica that never evicts does not converge with one that does.** **Feeds C19, and is checked against it:** this adds a second permanent refusal to `Document::apply`, whose `bool` `Hub::ingest_records` discards — so a refused op is persisted, dedup-poisoned, fanned out and ACKed `Accepted`. No *new* reachable trigger: the wire path decodes first and the codec fails the frame before `apply` sees it, so only an in-process caller reaches this refusal, and every replica refuses the same op (a pure function of it), so state still converges. C19 owns the seam. Rejected: verifying the group id against `TxId::derive` of its members — with unanimity the wrong-size commit is already gone, so it buys nothing state-observable while newly depending on no seam ever renumbering a seq under a live tag. Suites: 18 cases in `crates/core/tests/transaction.rs` (each shape reproduced first, including a spliced snapshot for the presentation that never commits and a six-order sweep pinning that nothing is released early) plus a malformed-count arm in `crates/core/tests/convergence.rs` that fails at seed 0 against the bucket-wide rule and separately pins that a group rewritten smaller converges from every order once evicted. Mutation-swept across the whole workspace (`--no-fail-fast`): no `count` bound at the codec fails 2; no refusal at the `apply` seam 1; the count read off the first member rather than agreed 4; no cap at the mint 1; eviction made a no-op 10; eviction discarding instead of untagging 11; a committed member waved past the readiness gate (C1's rule) 8. The unmutated tree fails 0. Miri-clean. Filed rather than bundled: **C20** (nothing calls the eviction seam yet) and **C21** (a well-formed forged group still diverges before eviction). See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Transactions / Scope Constraints*.

**C12 — a TLS-terminated cluster could not dial its own peers, so the peer link was plaintext-only (crates/server) — DONE (#361).** Both outbound node-to-node dials hard-coded `ws://` — `runtime::connect_peer` and `gossip::relay_roundtrip`, the latter shared by the anti-entropy exchange and the SWIM ping-req — and the server had no client-side `rustls::ClientConfig` at all; `tls.rs` built `ServerConfig` only. So `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CERT` on a clustered node gave it a listener its own peers could not speak to and the node started, bound, and never converged; and because C10's cluster secret is a *bearer* credential, the one link that must not be plaintext could be nothing else. **The transport is now per member, declared by its advertise address** — `wss://host:port` terminates TLS, `ws://host:port` or a bare `host:port` does not, any other scheme is refused rather than folded into a hostname. Nothing else could decide it: whether *this* node terminates TLS says nothing about the member it dials, and the advertise address is the one per-member datum the cluster already agrees on and already gossips, so it needs no new wire field and cannot skew between nodes (the cost: a scheme change re-identifies the node, so a rolling migration is a rolling re-join through the existing reap path). **Trust anchors are explicit** (`CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_CA`) with no platform store or bundled public roots standing in — the link carries write access to every room, so an ambient store would widen the issuer set to every CA on the host — and a `wss://` member with none configured is a startup error, not a dial that fails every round. **mTLS is the same handshake read both ways** (`CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_CLIENT_CERT`/`_KEY`), deliberately its own configuration rather than defaulting to the listener's cert: a `serverAuth`-only server certificate would make that default work in a lab and fail in production. **A cluster may mix plaintext and TLS members** — a live replicated store cannot switch every node at one instant and forbidding it would make a rollout a flag-day restart — but never silently: a TLS node names its plaintext peers at startup, and `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_REQUIRE_TLS=1` declares the rollout finished and refuses them. **The silent non-convergence is now a startup error**: a node whose advertised transport disagrees with the one it terminates refuses to start in both directions, as does one that requires TLS of its peers while serving plaintext; a gossip-learned member is checked at its dial instead, and a peer never opened is named after a run of attempts (`DialStreak`, distinct from C10's `RefusalStreak` over links that opened and died). **Closes C10's harvest for TLS members** — the acceptor's certificate is verified before the first frame is written, so a squatter at a member's advertise address gets a failed handshake instead of the credential (pinned by asserting the impostor's socket never sees the secret's bytes). Cold review added the remaining defects: the dial had no timeout, so a far end that accepted the socket and went silent wedged the task owning that follower's link forever (now bounded like the accept loop's handshake); `require_peer_tls` was checked against peers only; a *permanent* dial failure (a transport or trust refusal, decided before a socket opens) was redialed — and the follower reported down — four times a second forever, so `DialError::is_permanent` now separates it from a peer that is merely down; and `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_REQUIRE_TLS` resolved anything unrecognised to *off*, the permissive setting, rather than erroring. Left filed, deliberately: the bearer-replay property itself (a challenge-response credential — now worth designing, and wanting a per-member identity) and C10's admission confirmation. Mutation-swept whole-crate: restoring the pre-fix `ws://` fails 10, dropping the rustls connector 4, folding an unknown scheme into the authority 3, dropping the trust-anchor requirement 2, dialing plaintext under a required-TLS deployment 2, skipping this node's own transport check 2, unbounding the dial 1, treating a configuration refusal as transient 1, admitting peer TLS material with no cluster 1; the unmutated crate fails 0. The sweep found the self-transport require-TLS test passing with its rule deleted (its peer was refused first) and that is closed; removing the redial loop's streak reset fails 0 and is recorded as untested-by-construction, since it is a warning *not* printed on a live node's stderr. Suites: `crates/server/tests/peer_tls.rs` (20), 14 unit tests in `dial.rs`, 4 for `DialStreak`, 3 for the require-TLS switch. See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Cluster*.

**C11 — a per-op filter split an atomic group and stranded the survivors forever (crates/core + crates/server) — DONE (#359).** Five per-op filters withheld individual members of an atomic transaction and left the rest carrying a `count` their bucket could never reach: the catch-up delta's read-ACL filter and its zone filter, the live fan-out's per-recipient read filter, the per-channel `zone_filter`, and `project_zones`'s filtering of the buffer a snapshot carries. The survivors were buffered at the recipient forever — invisible, never collected, and (since C6) counted as ids it holds. **The rule is the translation seam's, generalized rather than reinvented:** `Chain::translate_ops_scoped` already poisoned `(client, TxId)` on a dropped member and re-emitted survivors with `tx: None`; that is now `split_groups` + `destrand_split` in the core beside `Tx`, called by every seam (translation included, which lost its private copy), with the server filters sharing one `retain_atomic` helper so a new seam gets the destranding by construction. A group a filter carries whole keeps its tags and stays atomic — destranding is keyed on a member *this* filter dropped, never on a group merely in flight. `Hub::reveal_backfill` untags unconditionally instead: a re-delivery of committed history carries no group whole, and a group-mate the reader already holds — applied *or* buffered — discards the replay rather than counting it. **Cold review then caught the debt that owed:** the log the back-fill reads already holds the batch being fanned out, so an op could ride twice in one frame, untagged in the reveal prefix and tagged in the batch; the reader folded the untagged copy, discarded the tagged one as an id it held, and the group lost that member from its count for good — leaving a materialized reveal shell it could never place. The back-fill now skips any op the batch already carries. **The second defect, the colliding group id, is closed too:** `TxId::derive` hashes the group's whole sorted member-sequence set through the `uuid::Uuid::new_v5` primitive `ElementId::derive` uses, so the `[0,1,2]`-then-`[0,3,4]` collision a re-minted hole produced is gone — still derived from ids the members carry, still no state to persist. The *hole* itself is C14's. **Also closes C2's stranding** (the zone half of the same defect, `project_zones`'s buffer included); what remains of C2 is whether §Scope Constraints' one-zone-per-Tx rule should be enforced at *emit*. Suites: `crates/server/tests/filter_split_tx.rs` (all four wire seams, each reproduced before the fix), `crates/core/tests/state_project_tx.rs` (the projection seam), plus the group-split and id-derivation cases in `crates/core/tests/transaction.rs`.

**C9 — a restarted partial reader re-minted across a projected-snapshot catch-up (crates/core + crates/server) — DONE (#357).** C6 made minting walk the ids a replica holds, which a full snapshot and an op delta both carry — but `project_zones` and `project_read_paths` cleared `seen` before encoding, so a zone-scoped or partial reader's snapshot named none of the ids that reader had published. A client that persists its `ClientId`, restarts, and adopts a *projected* snapshot fell back to `decode_state_as`'s `next_seq`, which a fresh replica reports as 0, and re-minted into ids the room's log already holds — every one of those writes deduped away at ingest, the silent loss C6 removed everywhere else. **Of the two shapes the board named, the projections keep the recipient's own ids; `Message::Snapshot` gains no field.** A wire frontier would be a server-supplied number landing straight in the op counter — the primitive C6 removed and C8 had to bound — and would reach only the server's own snapshot seam, while the state encoding carries the ids to every consumer of `encode_state`. The caller supplies the recipient (`for_channel` of the id declared at Hello): a projection cannot know who it serves, and `session.rs` does. **The privacy property, stated exactly:** the frontier a projection serves names one replica, the one adopting it. Every other author's ids go, so the existence and count of their ops in the withheld partition stay absent — the scrub's whole purpose, and unchanged. What the recipient gets back is its *own* authorship, which it originated, as bare `(client, seq)` pairs carrying no zone, path, or payload. That disclosure is bounded by who may present the identity, which is the transport's credential check and not this seam's — the same boundary that already lets a declarer author under an id (**C14** files binding it). **Cold review broke two things and found a third.** (i) The frontier is read *before* the buffer is filtered, since an own op the buffer holds is as published as an applied one, and a projection scrubs the buffer too — capturing after the filter loses exactly the withheld partition's buffered own ids. (ii) The kept set is cut against what survives in the buffer: an id in both `seen` and the buffer is a state no decode accepts. (iii) `Subscribe` did not require a Hello, while the transport-authenticated fast path leaves `client` unset — so a Subscribe-then-Hello order bound a channel whose catch-up was served with no recipient at all, and then authored under one. A Subscribe is now refused before a Hello, so every served catch-up has a declared recipient. Specs: `crates/core/tests/state_project_seen.rs` (18), `crates/server/tests/projected_restart.rs` (6 — both projections end to end through the `Registry`, the doc-ACL half through a real `ClientSession`, plus a session's *second* channel, where a wrong `for_channel` would hide). Mutation-swept across the whole workspace, named failures for every behaviour: scrubbing the frontier whole fails 15 core + 5 server; keeping every author fails 4 core + 2 server; dropping the buffer from the captured set fails 2; dropping the buffered exclusion fails 2; capturing after the buffer filter fails 1 (zones) and 1 (read paths); passing the connection id instead of `for_channel` fails 1; dropping the Subscribe gate fails 1. The unmutated tree fails 0. Miri-clean. Filed rather than bundled, both reproduced: **C14** (a redacted op *delta* leaves the same hole, with no frame able to carry the ids it withholds) and **C15** (`VersionFetch` serves a captured room state unprojected). See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Client / Sessions*.

**C8 — an op's `stamp.lamport` was an unbounded remote overflow primitive on the live path (crates/core) — DONE (#355).** `apply_now` raised a partition clock to `op.stamp.lamport.saturating_add(span - 1)` with nothing bounding `lamport` off the wire, and `emit_stamped` stamped the next local edit with a bare `self.clock(zone) + 1` — so **one op from any peer carrying `lamport: u64::MAX` overflowed the next ordinary local edit**, a panic in debug and a wrap to 0 in release. No id guessing, no catch-up, no forged authorship; the server's own room replica folds every op it ingests. `encode_state`/`read_state` carried the same primitive through the snapshot, root clock and per-zone. **The bound lives in the clock merge**, and the op is still applied. Both obvious fixes are wrong and both are now pinned by mutations. *Saturating the clock* re-issues one stamp forever, and a sequence node's id **is** its stamp — C4's collision from the other side (fails 6). *An acceptance gate* is one op away from the clock it bounds: an op at exactly the ceiling is admissible, parks the clock there, and the replica's next ordinary mint sits above it where every peer would refuse it — the room diverges on honest traffic (fails 8, including a ceiling-run test predating this unit), and it buys nothing on ordering, since a peer dominating LWW just sits one below any gate. So `apply_now` clamps the whole **reservation** — not the base; a run takes one lamport per codepoint, and clamping the base instead fails 2 — at `LAMPORT_WIRE_CEILING = u64::MAX >> 2`, and `read_state` **refuses** a decoded clock above `LAMPORT_STATE_CEILING = u64::MAX >> 1`. Both are constants, so each is a pure function of the input and every replica decides identically; a relative bound (`clock + k`) is arrival-order-dependent and could not. Zones fall out of the same line — the clamp is partition-agnostic. **Cold review broke the first cut twice, both now pinned.** (i) The state bound was a *clamp*, and a **stored clock is its author's high-water over ids it published** — clamping one down handed the replica live node ids, its next mint landed on a char id still in the state it had just decoded, and `List::insert_at` dropped the edit as a replay: silent write loss on the author and every peer, on the ordinary offline-first adopt/edit/persist/relaunch path. Refusing closes the loop clamping opens and restores `encode_state` byte-stability (a clamp fails 3). The gap between the two ceilings is what keeps the refusal off every honest snapshot (closing it fails 2). (ii) Nothing required `op.stamp.client == op.id.client`, harmless only by accident — folding an op used to raise the clock past any stamp forged in a victim's space. The clamp removes that, and the region above the ceiling is one admissible op away: an op at the ceiling parks every clock, a second plants a run stamped `(ceiling+1, victim)`, and the victim's next edits are swallowed as replays — write loss plus permanent divergence. `apply` now refuses a stamp naming a client other than its author; every op the codebase emits (server reveal shells included) satisfies it, and it is *categorical*, not a threshold, so nothing sits one op from its boundary (dropping it fails 2). **Exhaustion cannot be reached**: after the clamps the top half of the space is enterable only by local minting, one step per real edit, so its end is 2^63 mints away in one partition; `emit_stamped` is made total (`checked_add(..).expect(..)`, `list::run_id`'s existing idiom) and the invariant stated rather than handled, because at exhaustion every answer but stopping re-issues a stamp. **The cost, stated:** once a hostile lamport parks a partition at the ceiling, lamport ordering there is spent — which an *unbounded* clock also pays, on top of the overflow. What survives and is tested: no overflow, no wrap, no re-issued stamp, and convergence. No server change — its replica folds through the same `Document::apply`, and a refusing server would reject honest post-clamp client writes. Specs: `crates/core/tests/lamport_ceiling.rs` (24). Mutation-swept across the whole workspace (`--no-fail-fast`): no wire clamp 15, no state bound 2, base-not-reservation 2, saturating clock 8, acceptance gate 8, decode clamp instead of refusal 3, no stamp-authorship check 2; closing the runway is a build failure (the `const` assertions in `stamp.rs`), never reaching the suite. The unmutated tree fails 0. Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Core / Ops*.

**C10 — `Message::Replicate` was an unauthenticated ingest seam that bypassed every write gate (crates/server + crates/core) — DONE (#353).** `Registry::deliver` intercepted `Replicate` / `ReplicateSnapshot` / `Gossip` / `FollowerHeads` / `PingReq` before the session step, for any connection, with no Hello, no `Auth` and no author check, and `apply_replicate` called `hub.ingest` directly — so on any clustered node a bare socket that had said nothing could push arbitrary ops into any room in that node's replica set, and an ordinary subscriber read them. **The peer plane is now a credential, not a frame tag.** A new `Message::PeerAuth { secret }` (tag 52), sent once per link after the peer `Hello`, admits *that connection*; the five node-to-node frames are honored only there, and on any other connection they fall through to the session step and are answered as the client-plane protocol violation they are — so the `violation("client sent a replicate")` arm stopped being dead code and the client data plane became structurally incapable of reaching a peer handler. **Why a cluster secret and not the other two candidates.** mTLS is not selectable today at all: `runtime::connect_peer` and `gossip::relay_roundtrip` both hard-code `ws://` and the server has no client-side rustls config, so a TLS-terminated cluster cannot dial its own peers (filed as C12); and even built it is opt-in by configuration (`request` mode admits cert-absence by design), and an optional gate cannot close an ingest seam, while "pinned to the member set" would need a node-id↔cert-subject mapping the member set does not have and would refuse a gossip-joined node. A separate listener authenticates nobody — it relocates a port — and its one real benefit (a client plane that cannot carry peer frames) falls out of the credential for free; a private-interface bind remains a deployment choice. **C6's constraint honored**: every node dials under the same reserved all-zero replica id, so a handshake-keyed gate would refuse replication itself — the `Hello` is explicitly not the credential, and neither is any frame field (a `FollowerHeads` names whichever node it likes). **Fail-closed at configuration**: `serve` refuses a membership with no secret, a secret under 32 bytes (a rejected guess costs one reconnect, so a short secret is brute-forceable), and a secret with no membership; a `Registry` with none configured admits no peer at all. A rejected `PeerAuth` is answered with nothing and dropped — which is what hides whether a secret is configured at all — and where one is, the content is compared constant-time (`subtle`), so a rejection leaks no prefix of it. Peer admission and client authentication are disjoint both ways. The forged-epoch leader stepdown is closed against a stranger (pinned) and explicitly **not** against an authenticated member — a promoted leader must be able to supersede, so intra-cluster epoch forgery belongs to the election design, not this gate. Known limit, stated rather than papered over: the secret is a *bearer* credential, so the peer link wants transport encryption, which C12 blocks. **Deployment**: `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_SECRET` is now required alongside `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_PEERS`; single-node is unchanged. Specs: `crates/server/tests/peer_auth.rs` (23 — the reproduction for each of the five frames including "nothing to a subscriber, before or after", the forged epoch, wrong/empty/unconfigured secrets, both disjointness directions, every frame working for an admitted peer, the three startup misconfigurations, plus two live nodes replicating end to end, two whose cluster secrets differ failing to, and a real node refusing a bare socket, over real sockets), `crates/core/tests/protocol_peer_auth.rs` (7), and 8 unit tests in `runtime.rs` over the refused-link streak (never-warns, threshold, repeat-per-run, reset, boundary, backoff cap, saturation, and the threshold's own shape), and `replication.rs` pinning that the leader's outbound dial presents the secret before any node-to-node frame. Mutation-swept whole-workspace with named failures: restoring the unconditional interception fails 13, skipping the secret comparison 3, storing an empty secret 1, dropping the `PeerAuth` from the replication dial 3, from the relay dial 3, admitting a membership with no secret 1, and — for the refused-link heuristic — never resetting the streak 1, removing the redial backoff 2, warning once instead of per run 1; the unmutated tree fails 0 (190 binaries, 2743 tests). Miri-clean. Three follow-ons filed from what implementing it surfaced: **C12** (the peer link is plaintext-only, so a TLS cluster cannot dial itself and the bearer secret cannot be encrypted), **C13** (every gate downstream of admission trusts any member equally — forged epoch, forged `reporter`). See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Cluster*.

**C6 — an op-delta catch-up did not lift the replica's op-seq high-water (crates/core + crates/server) — DONE (#PR).** A replica that persists its `ClientId` and rebuilds a fresh `Document` is caught up by ops it authored before the restart; the counter ignored them and minted from 0 into ids the room's log already holds, and an `OpId` is the dedup key — every post-restart write dropped at ingest, silently, the same shape as C4. Implementing it found the **snapshot path loses the same writes**: `decode_state_as` was believed to lift the counter, but it lifted from the *encoded* counter — the snapshot author's — and the author is a server replica that never mints, so it is 0. Both catch-up shapes, and both `ClientSession` counterparts, are pinned. **The counter no longer holds a maximum at all.** The naive repair (`seq = seq.max(op.id.seq + 1)`, written and reverted during C4) repairs the restore and hands any frame a write primitive: `seq` is an unbounded `u64` off the wire, so one op at `u64::MAX` — or `u64::MAX - 1`, which lands the counter *exactly* on the ceiling — overflows the next ordinary local edit. Restricting the lift to catch-up ops narrows who can do it without removing it, and a `checked_add` at the emit site still has to choose between a refused local edit and a panic. So **minting walks the dedup set** instead: it steps over each id under this replica's identity that `seen` already holds and takes the first it does not. `seen` rides the state encoding, so one mechanism covers the op delta, the adopted snapshot, and any embedder replaying a log; and because the walk advances one step per id already in memory, no `seq` off the wire ever reaches the counter — a forged frame contributes one entry the walk will not reach, and the end of the space is 2^64 folded ops away. A hole in the durable run is a free id and is reused. Cost is one hash lookup per emit plus a one-time walk of the replica's own run after a catch-up, which a hostile peer cannot amplify (lengthening the walk costs more to ingest than to step over). `decode_state_as` also stops merging in the snapshot author's counter — the amplifier that carried a foreign replica's count into every adopter — keeping `next_seq` for the projected-snapshot case whose dedup set is deliberately scrubbed. **Server half: the node's replica identity is reserved at the op gate, not the handshake.** A node's rooms are held under one node-wide `ClientId` and the shipped binary uses the fixed all-zero constant — publicly guessable, unlike a client's 122 random bits — and channel 0 authors under the declared id unchanged, so it was the one identity a client could deliberately write under, into the replica whose state every joiner adopts. A handshake gate was implemented first and is wrong: a node-to-node link opens with a Hello declaring its own node id (`runtime::connect_peer`, `gossip`) and every node ships with the same constant, so it would refuse replication itself; authorship is what must be refused, so the reservation sits in `handle_ops` beside the identity check already there. Cold review caught two defects in the first cut, both now pinned: the walk consulted `seen` alone, so an op **held** in the buffer (waiting on its transaction group, or on a target no create has made reachable) had its id treated as free — re-minting it leaves the replica applying two different ops under one identity once the buffer drains, divergence rather than a dropped write; and the end of the sequence space was treated as a wall, which a second pass showed to be a point check on a range problem — a position at `u64::MAX - k` buys `k + 1` good mints and then re-issues one id forever. The space is a finite *set*, and what a replica holds is bounded by memory, so the search **wraps**: a free sequence always exists, and one step per held id reaches it (after that many misses the candidates were all distinct and all held, so the next cannot be). No bound on any decoded value, and no arithmetic that can overflow. Specs: `crates/core/tests/op_seq_high_water.rs` (22), `crates/server/tests/server_identity.rs` (6 — including the peer-link Hello that pins why the gate is not at the handshake, and a restarted client caught up by a real op delta through the driver). Mutation-swept across the whole workspace, named failures for every behaviour: dropping the search over the ids the replica holds fails 10 (8 core + 2 server); consulting `seen` but not the buffer fails 1; stopping the search at the end of the space instead of wrapping fails 3; restoring `decode_state_as`'s `max` fails 1; dropping the node-identity reservation from the op gate fails 3; and writing the reverted naive lift (`max(op.id.seq + 1)` in `apply_now`, search removed) fails 10 — every ceiling and forged-frame guard, plus the two snapshot-restore cases the naive lift never reached. The unmutated tree fails 0. Miri-clean. Three adjacent defects found here are filed rather than bundled, all reproduced: **C8** (lamport ceiling), **C9** (projected-snapshot restore), **C10** (unauthenticated `Replicate` ingest). See DECISIONS (2026-07-27). → *Client / Sessions*.

**C4 — a `ClientSession`'s per-channel replicas shared one client id (crates/core + crates/server) — DONE (#351).** `subscribe_inner` built `Document::new(self.client)` per channel, so two channels bound to the **same room** — `subscribe` beside `subscribe_zone`, two `subscribe_zone`s, a branch beside the default — held replicas that both minted op seqs from 0 *and* lamports from 0, producing identical `OpId`s **and identical `Stamp`s** for unrelated edits. One consequence is what is actually observed, because it fires first and hides the rest: an `OpId` is the dedup key, so any replica holding both drops the second channel's op as already-applied — the write is gone, unrecoverably, which is strictly worse than a divergence because nothing downstream can detect it (the scratch repro loses a whole atomic group this way). **Fix: a per-channel replica identity, not a channel-scoped seq.** Repairing the envelope alone stops the dedup drop and loses the data one layer down — `Stamp = (lamport, client, offset)` collides in the same cases and a sequence node's id *is* its stamp (as are text char ids, XML child ids, ranged and ACL element ids). The counterfactual is pinned directly by `distinct_op_ids_alone_do_not_save_two_inserts_that_share_a_stamp`: give two inserts distinct op ids, leave the identity shared, and the nodes still collapse into one. Widening `OpId` is also a codec change (it rides every op frame, the snapshot `seen` set, and `Accepted`'s `through` frontier) where a derived identity changes no encoded shape. C1's group id (the group's lowest member seq) needs no change — disjoint op ids make group ids disjoint, which is the property C1 chose that derivation for. `ClientId::for_channel(channel)` returns the declared id unchanged on **channel 0** — so `ClientSession::new(id)` still means "I am this replica" — and a UUIDv5 over `(id, channel)`'s four big-endian bytes beyond it; channel numbers are never recycled, since reusing a freed one would hand a fresh replica minting from seq 0 the identity of the retired one's in-flight ops. Pure derivation, no `Host`, so it is **protocol, not client-side convenience**: `handle_ops` already had `channel` in scope and its gate went from "every op carries the Hello client" to "every op carries the identity *this channel* authors under" — channel-*binding* rather than strictly stronger (identical predicates at channel 0, disjoint accepted sets beyond it), with the namespace/width/endianness pinned by a golden vector. Snapshot adoption preserves the channel's identity (`room.doc.client()`). **A second id-space leak was found here and deliberately left to its own unit (C6):** the op-delta catch-up path does not lift the seq high-water the way `decode_state_as` does, and the obvious repair in `apply_now` turned one silent-loss bug into a one-frame remote panic on the ordinary edit path, so it needs `checked_add` at the emit site and a lift restricted to catch-up ops. Scope named honestly: distinctness *across* sessions rests on the 122 random bits an embedder mints from (every SDK goes through `from_bytes`, which stamps no version), one `ClientId` still names one replica, and a derived id is not authenticated. Connection-level identity is deliberately untouched (Hello, hub presence/awareness, presence-steal address the *connection*); ACL identity remains the server-derived actor. Specs: `crates/core/tests/client_channel_identity.rs` (18), `crates/server/tests/channel_identity.rs` (5 — two channels of one connection both reaching the room's log through a real `ClientSession`, and the gate acknowledging each channel's own identity while refusing a sibling's or a stranger's), `crates/core/tests/clientid.rs` (+7 derivation properties incl. the wire-constant vector). Mutation-swept across the whole workspace, named failures for every behaviour: dropping `for_channel` in `subscribe_inner` fails 16 tests (15 core + 1 server); pointing the snapshot path back at the session id fails 1; unbinding the server gate from the channel fails 7 server tests (including the pre-existing `multiroom`/`branch_ops` writers on non-zero channels); collapsing `for_channel` to the identity fails 22 (20 core + 2 server); the unmutated tree fails 0. Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-26). → *Client / Sessions*.

**C1 — atomic-transaction convergence bug (crates/core) — DONE (#350).** Two replicas that had applied an **identical** op set read different state whenever the edits rode `begin_atomic`/`commit_atomic` groups — a CRDT-law violation on `main`, predating the undo seam (reproduced on plain `Document`s, no undo origin, no `undo`/`redo`). **Two independent mechanisms, each divergent on its own.** (1) A group's members applied unconditionally once the group was complete: `tx_group_ready` credited an intra-group create as satisfying a later member's target, which is sound for *materialisation* and unsound for *liveness* — when the create lost its slot to a concurrent higher-stamped write the container came back materialised-but-**displaced**, and the member behind it applied into it, where `live_list` / `live_text` / the `is_displaced` guard on map-slot routing drop the mutation and return. Marked seen, never retried, gone — which is why both replicas report the same `seen()` count over the whole log and still disagree. (2) Gating the whole group on *every* member resolving made commit a **window**: `ready` is not monotone (installed → displaced → re-installed), so one transiently-unresolvable member blocked members that were perfectly applicable, and whether the group ever hit a moment where all of it resolved depended on arrival order. **Fix:** completeness is the only group-level condition (`tx_group_ready` and its five dependency-credit sets deleted, −145 lines); members apply in seq order, each through the ordinary `ready` gate, and one that is not ready has its `tx` tag cleared and rejoins the ordinary buffer, draining behind the op that installs its container exactly as the ungrouped edit would. Atomic delivery is now a permutation of ready applies — what "convergence is unchanged by construction" (#102) actually requires. The all-or-nothing view survives because a `ready`-false member is almost always one the current state cannot express (unresolvable target renders nothing; a delete names absent nodes; a ranged/ACL change names a missing entry), so a commit applies exactly the members an observer could see; ARCHITECTURE names the two honest exceptions (a partly-arrived multi-id text delete, and a move whose destination sequence is unmaterialised). **Diagnosis method worth reusing:** the ungrouped **control** (same generator, grouping removed — 0/200 vs 134/200) is what ruled out the generator and pinned atomicity as the trigger, and shrinking killed the standing hypothesis — Fugue anchor placement is innocent, list-only edits diverge **0**/200 and it takes a *displacement* to reproduce. Specs: `crates/core/tests/transaction.rs` (4 deterministic regressions — list / text / nested-map member surviving its own create losing the slot, and a member waiting on an outside op not holding back its group; the shrunk repro is 5 ops, no RNG) and `convergence.rs::atomic_groups_do_not_change_what_ops_merge_to` — the existing 28-op-family generator run twice per seed, atomic and ungrouped, both required to converge under shuffled re-delivery, closing the atomic op-family gap B1 flagged. **A third defect, found by cold review rather than the oracles:** `TxId` came off a per-document counter a snapshot restore reset to 0, while `decode_state_as` — the reconnect path — preserves the client id *and* the op-seq counter. The receiver buckets buffered members by `(author client, tx id)`, so the first group minted after a restore collided with a stale partial group the peers still held: the two merged into one bucket, the size gate committed a mixed set (one group's member visible without its sibling — an observable partial transaction) and stranded the leftovers permanently. The id is now the group's lowest member seq — minted by the counter that already distinguishes the group's own members, and carried, not reset, by the restore paths that preserve identity — so group identity inherits exactly the guarantee op identity has, with no separate state to persist, and `next_tx` is gone. Regression: `a_group_minted_after_a_restore_cannot_collide_with_one_minted_before`. **A fourth, from the final review pass:** `take_complete_tx` chose among complete groups in `HashMap` iteration order, and which group commits first decides whether the other's members still resolve — so two replicas decoding *identical* snapshot bytes reached different state, within a single process (`RandomState` is per-map). Draining to a fixpoint keeps a replica's own buffer to at most one complete group, so this is unreachable on the live path and is hardening for a peer-supplied snapshot, which is exactly where untrusted bytes enter; lowest buffer position now wins. Regression: `a_snapshot_holding_two_complete_transactions_decodes_the_same_way_every_time`, which forges the multi-group buffer through the public `encode_ops` + `encode_state` framing. Mutation-swept (delete any of the four behaviours and a named test fails — the fourth was caught by this sweep having no coverage at all on the first attempt). Miri-clean; Miri seed-capped per this file's convention. See DECISIONS (2026-07-26). → *Transactions*.

**Epic B / B1 — core undo record-seam (core + ffi + wasm) — DONE (#348).** Undo is no longer a helper you have to edit *through*: the `Document` records the inverse of every op it **emits**, at the mint point (`emit_stamped`), so a cursor edit, a path-façade edit, and a binding's own handle-graph `mutate` all record identically, on an offline replica and on a channel's replica alike — the per-document, per-channel stack ARCHITECTURE asked for, with no per-channel plumbing (each `ClientSession` room already owns its replica). A remote op is folded in by `apply` and never emitted, so a collaborator's edit is *structurally* incapable of landing on a local stack. **All op kinds, root and nested:** register/map-scalar/blob-ref/element-ref, counter inc+dec, every container create (map/list/text/xml element/xml fragment), list insert+delete, text insert+delete, xml child insert+delete, xml move, ranged marks (create / set-payload / delete), and ACL grant/revoke — the inverse is read off the state the op is about to overwrite, before it lands. **Origin/scope tagging:** recording is opt-in (`set_undo_origin`), undo takes the newest intention *of the origin asked for* and skips any another interleaved, and a fresh edit clears only its own origin's redo — so a user's manager and a subtree-scoped one coexist on one document. **An intention** is one transact, one explicit `begin_intention`/`end_intention` group, or one atomic transaction, which undoes *and* redoes as one atomic transaction (tx-tagged ops asserted). **The mirror is recorded, not computed:** replaying an intention emits ordinary forward ops that go through the same seam, so undo/redo alternate indefinitely off live state. **The replay is planned, not merely reversed** — the reversal breaks dependencies the forward order carried, and each break was a real wrong answer local review or the oracles found: the reversal is kept — two writes to one map slot are ordered by it, and moving either past the other inverts which value the slot ends up holding — and a container a step needs is instead re-created on demand by emitting a *copy* of the op that restores it (an op on an unreachable target is inert here but *buffered and later replayed* by a peer); a step is subsumed only under a tombstoned **sequence node**, the one container that leaves the tree for good, never under a vacated **map slot**, whose container is retained by id and comes back with whatever the intention left in it the moment anything re-installs the slot; a revival re-points the intentions still stacked beneath it in five places — the sequence id of a delete, the element id of a move/annotation/ACL revoke, any anchor naming a replaced node, the target of a step addressing a container derived from a revived element, and the fresh id an ACL re-grant mints (without which a full undo left a live `Allow Write` standing) — so "type, delete, undo, undo" does not leave the revived characters behind, keyed by `(sequence, Stamp)` because a stamp is unique per **zone**, not per document; a movable node is revived only when the delete is what takes it out of view, or a still-rendering node gets a duplicate beside it; and a counter delta inverts to two steps because it re-wins its slot on the way past. A replay that emits nothing still closes a mirror, so N undos owe N redos. A snapshot catch-up carries the recording origin across (it silently stopped recording on the late-join and reconnect paths); a migration drops the stack, which ARCHITECTURE already claimed and the code did not do. **Revival rebuilds:** a tombstone drops its value, so undoing a list delete re-inserts it and undoing an *XML child* delete rebuilds the node from a depth-bounded subtree snapshot (tag + attrs + children, recursively) out of ordinary forward ops — "delete this paragraph, ctrl-Z" brings the text back. Both anchor as the **left** child of the tombstone, which renders exactly where the node was however the sequence has shifted (right-anchoring was the first reading; self-review's position test caught it landing after a later insert). Undo is refused while an atomic transaction is open. **Surfaces:** `Document::{set_undo_origin, clear_undo_origin, undo_origin, begin_intention, end_intention, can_undo, can_redo, undo, redo}`, the same six on `ClientSession` per channel (an inverse rides the normal outbox, so it is acked and resent like any authored op), `UndoManager` reduced to an origin handle (`track`/`untrack`/`group`/`atomic_group`/`undo`/`redo`/`can_*`), C ABI `crdtsync_doc_*` + `crdtsync_client_*` undo entry points (the whole per-kind `crdtsync_undo_*` family deleted — with the seam in place it was a second way to spell an ordinary edit), `WasmUndo` + `WasmClient.setUndoOrigin/undo/redo/canUndo/canRedo`, and the Python/Go `Undo` handles. **No wire change, no server-side undo state** — peers converge on an undo like any edit (asserted across op kinds, and against a peer editing the same text concurrently). Specs: `crates/core/tests/undo.rs` (90) — a named, mutation-checked regression for every hazard above (delete the behaviour, the named test fails) plus **two** seeded property oracles, because they catch different classes: a two-replica *convergence* oracle (every op either replica emits is exchanged, an inverse included) found the installer-ordering divergence a single-author replay cannot see, and a single-replica *round-trip* oracle (undo everything → the starting state; redo everything → the state undo left, fingerprinted over a depth-first XML render, nested attr paths, the annotation set and the ACL set, with multi-edit intentions) found a node duplication and both retained-container losses, which every replica would have lost identically. Both swept to 40 000+ seeds clean while developing; 300 each in the suite. **Found while building, out of scope, needs its own unit:** atomic transactions can leave two replicas diverged after applying identical op sets — reproduced on `main` with no undo involved, so it predates this seam, but an atomic intention now undoes and redoes as an atomic transaction, so undo rides it. Known imprecision, documented rather than papered over: a tree move's inverse re-derives its anchor from live state, so its redo position is exact for a reorder on its own but can drift after unrelated structural edits — never losing or duplicating the node, and never diverging. Also the FFI undo + per-channel-seat tests, wasm `web.rs` undo + channel-undo tests, `sdks/python/tests/test_undo.py`, Go `crdtsync_test.go`. Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-26). → *Undo / Redo*.

**Epic A / A3 — Go networked Provider (sdks/go) — DONE (#344).** The Go SDK is a first-class networked collab client: `crdtsync.Connect(ctx, url, room, opts)` owns a socket and a wire session and returns once the room has synced, with the ergonomic handle graph on top — an edit frames, outboxes, and sends in one step, an inbound frame folds into the same channel replica, and a dropped socket resumes from the caught-up position and resends the outbox. Mirrors the JS provider's model; the Go-specific parts are the concurrency and the transport. **`Backend` seam:** `Doc` now sits over a `Backend` interface (`*Document` satisfies it unchanged) so one handle graph serves a local replica and a channel of a wire `Client` via the new `ClientBackend`, over A1's eleven per-channel entry points — one replica per room, never two divergent copies. The eleven Go `Client` bindings for A1's C ABI landed here (`SetScalar`/`GetScalar`/`MapKeys`/`List*`/`Text*`). **Transport:** a `Transport`/`Dialer` seam with a built-in RFC 6455 WebSocket client (`DialWebSocket`), so the SDK stays dependency-free and a test can hand the provider a socket it can sever; masking, fragment reassembly, ping/pong, control-frame + RSV validation, size and fragment ceilings. **Concurrency:** the provider shares the `Doc`'s mutex, so an inbound frame and a local edit never touch the replica at once; listener callbacks always run with it released; a separate send lock orders the wire, taken *before* the doc's lock and held across the handshake's transition and frames so an edit authored in that window queues behind the Subscribe instead of arriving on an unbound channel; the doc's lock is never held across a socket write. **Three protocol corrections over the JS reference** (all regression-tested): only an `AuthOk` (tag 7) opens the session — an enforcing server answers the Hello with a `SchemaAdvert` first, and subscribing on it is a violation; the initial sync completes only on the `Ops`/`Snapshot` catch-up reply *for this channel* that the core actually applied — `last_seen_seq` is `Some(0)` from `subscribe`, so the JS gate is vacuous and an awareness frame could report a synced-but-empty doc; the reconnect backoff resets only when a socket answers the resume, not on its `AuthOk`, so a server that authenticates then drops is not redialed at the 250ms floor forever. **Credential** rides the in-band `Auth` by default (the carrier every transport supports); `AuthAtUpgrade` (implied by an `Authorization` header) declares the server's upgrade fast path instead, where an in-band `Auth` would be a violation. **Stage deadlines:** `ConnectTimeout` bounds the dial, the authentication, and the subscribe-or-resume that asks for the room; the subscribe reply gets its own far looser `CatchupTimeout` (unset = 5 min, negative = indefinite), because that reply is the whole room with no progress signal on the wire and a budget a large room cannot meet would sever every attempt and never converge. Also: `SetAwareness`/`Awareness`/`AwarenessLen`, `OnState`, `OutboxLen`, `Actor`, `SchemaVersion`/`Schema`, `AclGrant`/`AclRevoke` on the op path, and the `OnError`/`OnOpsRejected`/`OnRedirect` server signals. `Close` frees the wire session under the lock (a refused handshake included), so a rejected connect leaks nothing. Specs: `netprovider_test.go` (42, over a fake transport that speaks the real wire frames the core decodes — handshake ordering, auth gating, catch-up from ops and from a snapshot, edits framing + sending, idempotent replay, offline outbox + resend, reconnect resume-from-position, state transitions, awareness, the three server signals, refused handshake / timeout / refused dial / close-before-sync, backoff growth and reset, per-stage deadlines — a socket that never authenticates and one that never answers the Subscribe are both recycled, a catch-up slower than the handshake budget still syncs, the connect budget stops governing once the room has been asked for but does recycle a socket whose handshake write wedges — on a first connection and a reconnect alike — a resumed socket waits on no catch-up budget, an edit authored mid-catch-up still reaches the wire, a Close landing mid-handshake-write stays final, remote frames and local edits both fire diff-derived reactivity, a subtree observer sees only its own subtree, the blob/xml/cursor/mark/counter reads answer off the channel replica, and a peer frame landing mid-transaction is not credited to that transaction, wire ordering under concurrent editors, a protocol-version drift guard against `core::protocol`) and `netprovider_integration_test.go` (8, against the real server binary over the built-in WebSocket — two-client convergence, remote reactivity, late-joiner catch-up, reconnect resend without duplication, upgrade fast path, awareness fan-out, concurrent editors, close). CI's `go-sdk` job now builds the server binary so the integration suite runs. Race-detector clean. **Per-channel reactivity** rides A1b's (#343) `channel_state`: the channel replica is snapshot-and-diffed exactly like a local one, so a networked `Doc` fires `OnUpdate`/`Observe` for both local edits and inbound frames, and A1b's blob/xml/anchor/mark reads make the rich handle surface answer off the room's replica too. Its `get_counter` closed a hole the seam had of its own — `Backend` carried `Inc`/`Dec` with no read-back — so `GetCounter` joined the interface. The one thing a networked `Doc` still lacks is `SetSchema`: binding a schema is replica-local runtime state with no per-channel seat, so it reports no repairs and its marks take the default object flavor. No core CRDT change, no FFI change. → *SDK-Ergonomic-Surface / Networking*.
**Networking / Protocol** — **codec negotiation over the Hello handshake (core + server) — DONE (#342). Strikes codec negotiation from ARCHITECTURE's "Also absent" list.** Realizes ARCHITECTURE §Handshake's "Hello — version + codec negotiation" as a forward-compat reservation. `Message::Hello` gains a trailing optional `codecs` advertisement; `core::protocol::select_codec` picks the highest version both ends hold (`SUPPORTED_CODECS`, today just `CODEC_V1`); the server records it on the `Session` (`session.codec() -> Option<u32>`), answers with a new `Message::CodecSelected` (tag 51), and closes a client sharing no codec with a clean `UnsupportedVersion`. **Silence carries `CODEC_V1` in both directions** — an omitted advertisement *and* an absent selection both settle there, so the server answers only a selection that moves off the default and the seam adds no frame to any connection's reply stream until a second codec exists. Total-decode throughout (truncated / over-counted / partial / present-but-empty advertisements → `ProtocolError`, never a panic); on the client an unsupported selection is permanently fatal (`ClientError::UnsupportedCodec` refuses every later frame) and a selection that *changes* the settled codec is a violation, so there is no silent wrong-codec decode either way. Cluster peer links advertise nothing (nowhere to fold an answer — negotiating them is a follow-on). **No second codec was invented** — the deliverable is the seam, so the selection rule is pinned by passing `select_codec` a supported set (highest-shared-wins / disjoint / empty-advertisement over multi-codec sets), while the three branches that only fire with a second codec — emitting the selection frame, its ordering ahead of `SchemaAdvert`, and refusing a *changed* selection — are specified, not covered; DECISIONS lists them with their follow-ons. Suites: `crates/core/tests/codec_negotiation.rs` (20), `crates/server/tests/codec_negotiation.rs` (8), plus the socket-level `ws.rs` handshake now advertising. See DECISIONS (2026-07-25). → *Handshake / Not Locked*.

**In-memory tombstone range representation (core) — DONE (#341).** Closes the last open half of §Tombstone GC: the state codec already collapsed contiguous deleted runs to range records, but `List` still held one `Node` per tombstone, so live RAM grew with deletes. It now holds the *same* range records live. `crates/core/src/list.rs` splits storage into `nodes` (ids that still carry a value — live, or suppressed by a tree move) and `dead` (deleted ids as `DeadRun { len, parent, side }` keyed by run head), both ordered by `(client, offset, lamport)` so a run's ids are contiguous and an interval query is a range scan. **Deletion is now representation-terminal:** `delete_id` moves the id out of `nodes` into a run and drops its value (the codec already dropped dead values, so this only makes RAM match the bytes), and `add_dead` welds the record onto an adjacent run whose chain it continues — so a contiguous delete stays *one* record however its pieces arrive (front-to-back, back-to-front, or alternating). **Convergence is untouched:** a run carries every id, `merge` absorbs a peer's run in O(1) when the region is unseen (`bury`/`unseen_from`), and the Fugue walk works in *spans* — `children()` cuts a run wherever an outside record attaches inside it, so an insert anchored at a deleted id still lands there while the storage stays one record. **Codec byte-compat:** runs are maximal by construction, so `encode_state_into` is the same function of logical state it was — byte fixtures captured pre-change are asserted verbatim. **The decoder's global tombstone budget is removed** (DECISIONS): it existed because decode materialised a node per id, and it had become a cliff — a run that costs one record to hold could exceed it, so a long-lived document would emit a snapshot it could not load back. The per-record cap stays. Spec `crates/core/tests/dead_runs.rs` (23): record-count assertions (a `LONG` contiguous delete → 1 record, whatever the delete order), extend/weld, disjoint runs stay separate, insert-inside-a-run splitting on both anchor sides, relative-position resolution through a run, four merge laws over runs, snapshot round-trip + delete-order byte-equality + the pre-change fixtures, a chained-record weld past the per-record cap, a randomized delete-heavy 3-replica merge-order property (300 seeds; identical bytes under every order + re-delivery), and the document-layer interactions (undo revives across a collapsed run; a *live* moved-away node is not folded, so its birth placement survives to be revealed by a move back; delete-wins-over-move across a run). The convergence oracle gained three delete-heavy op families (multi-codepoint text runs, contiguous text-span delete, contiguous list-span delete). Miri-clean. → *Snapshots / Tombstone GC*.

**Epic A / A2 — Python networked Provider (sdks/python) — DONE (#345).** `connect(url, room)` opens a `Provider` that owns a WebSocket and backs its `Doc` with that connection's room replica — ARCHITECTURE §Sync-Provider's model, over the per-channel C ABI A1/A1b added. **Transport:** `crdtsync/_websocket.py`, a stdlib-only RFC 6455 client (the SDK stays dependency-free) — upgrade handshake, masked binary frames, fragmentation, ping/pong/close, `SO_KEEPALIVE` liveness, `SO_SNDTIMEO`-bounded writes; refuses masked frames, reserved bits, oversized/fragmented control frames, text frames, a non-upgrade 101, header CRLF injection, and a message over a 32 MiB ceiling; keeps bytes a peer pipelines behind the 101. **Provider:** handshake (header → Hello → Auth → Subscribe) → catch-up → reconnect with jittered clamped backoff (resume + resend), offline outbox, connection state + `on_state`, awareness (republished after a reconnect), `acl_grant`/`acl_revoke` over the op path, and the `on_error`/`on_ops_rejected`/`on_redirect` signal hooks. **Concurrency:** a reader thread folds inbound frames into the same native session application threads edit — two locks, send lock outermost, held by an author from before its ops are stamped until after they are written (an ack is a watermark, so a later op acked ahead of an earlier one would drop the earlier from the outbox unsent); the replica lock is released before any socket write and before reactivity is published. `close()` never frees the session under a thread that may be inside it, and never waits on the lock a parked writer holds. **`Doc`:** gains `_networked` over a backend + wire seam (local path unchanged); `transact`/`_mutate` ship what they committed even if the body or the change-set read raises; `CrdtList` resolves live indices inside the edit; multi-step handle reads take the replica once. An edit that matched nothing reaches neither the wire nor the listeners (the client seat frames every call, the document seat does not). **`Client`:** bindings for all of #340's and #343's per-channel entry points, plus the encoded-scalar mark seams. **Renamed:** the transport-agnostic seam is now `LocalProvider`. **Specs (82 across three suites):** `test_websocket.py` (framing, refusals, upgrade, bounded write), `test_provider_lifecycle.py` over a fake transport (handshake ordering, catch-up, reconnect resume/resend, offline outbox, state transitions, awareness fan-out + republish, ACL, server signals, callback failures, backoff), `test_provider_network.py` against the real server (handle graph, reactivity, cursors, transactions, concurrent authors, a dropped socket). Nine cold-review rounds incl. a mutation sweep. CI: the python job caches Rust, builds the server binary, and installs `pytest-timeout`. **Enumerated follow-ons:** the operator-tier request/reply surface (needs a C-ABI `take_replies`); a networked doc binds no schema (the client seat reads the server's advert but has no surface to bind one, so its repair signal stays silent); an outbox resend past the server's 16 MiB frame limit needs a core-side split. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-25). → *SDK-Ergonomic-Surface / Networking*.

**Epic A / A1 — C-ABI per-channel expansion (crates/ffi) — DONE (#340).** The C client seat now mirrors `WasmClient`'s per-channel sequence/scalar/map surface, so a networked Go/Python provider reaches list/text/scalar/map through the room replica and the outbox rather than the doc-level surface. Eleven entry points on `CrdtClient`: `crdtsync_client_{list_insert,list_delete,list_len,list_get,text_insert,text_delete,text_len,text_get,set_scalar,get_scalar,map_keys}`. Edits route through `client_edit` (ops enter the outbox, resent/acknowledged like every routed edit); reads go through two new helpers `client_read_buf`/`client_read_usize` — the per-channel analogues of the doc-level `read_buf`/`read_usize` — keeping the 1/0/-1 status idiom (`crdtsync_client_get_bytes` folded onto the same helper). `map_keys` frames the same `u32`-count, `u32`-length-prefixed key list the SDKs already decode; `set_scalar`/`get_scalar` carry an encoded `Scalar` so a leaf keeps its type across the wire. cbindgen header regenerated; the header symbol-assert carries the trailing `(` of each prototype so a doc cross-reference can't vacuously satisfy it. Spec `crates/ffi/tests/client.rs`: list/text edits frame their Ops message, enter the outbox, and converge on a peer; codepoint-not-byte text indexing; scalar type survives the wire; `map_keys` distinguishes a live-but-empty Map (1) from a non-Map (0); channel isolation and an unheld channel; null handle / null out / null payload-or-path pointer all rejected without a dereference. Miri-clean (UB + leaks). No core CRDT change. → *Networking / SDK*.

**Epic A / A1b — C-ABI client-seat read parity (crates/ffi) — DONE (#343).** The reads that were still doc-surface-only now sit on `CrdtClient`, closing the seat against `WasmClient`: `crdtsync_client_{channel_state,get_blob,marks_at,relative_position,resolve_position,xml_tag,xml_children_len}` plus `crdtsync_client_get_counter` — the read back for the already-present `inc`/`dec`, which the JS seat still lacks. **`channel_state` is the load-bearing one:** it snapshots a subscribed room's replica to a canonical state buffer, the before/after the ergonomic SDKs diff to derive change events (an inbound frame does not surface the ops it applied), so per-channel reactivity in the Python (A2) and Go (A3) networked providers rides on it. The path-addressed reads go through the `client_read_buf`/`client_read_usize`/`client_read` helpers on the 1/0/-1 idiom — an unheld or unsubscribed channel reads absent (0), distinct from a bad handle's -1; `channel_state` takes no path and hand-rolls the same handle/`out` guards. Entry points sit in their existing family sections (blobs / marks / xml navigation), with a new "client channel state and anchors" section for the snapshot + anchors. cbindgen header regenerated; symbol-asserts carry the trailing `(` of each prototype (these names cross-reference each other in doc comments, which cbindgen copies into the header).
