Spec `crates/ffi/tests/client.rs`: per-channel round trip for each; `channel_state` diffs through `crdtsync_diff` to the same `Change` an SDK derives, for a local edit and for a peer's inbound frame, and reopens via `crdtsync_doc_decode_state`; anchor sides 0/1 bind to different characters and separate on an insert at the gap over Text, and a List anchor tracks its item across an insert before it; channel isolation with a positive control on the holding channel (so a 0 on the sibling can only come from the channel); unheld, unsubscribed, torn-path, null handle / null out / null path-or-position pointer. Miri-clean (UB + leaks). No core CRDT change. → *Networking / SDK*.

**Tooling** — **replay tooling — read-only point-in-time reconstruction + diff CLI — DONE (#PR). A non-UI operator tool.** Realizes ARCHITECTURE §Debugging's "replay" as a `crdtsync-replay` bin in the server crate over `server::replay::{reconstruct_at, diff_at, load_room, head_seq, floor}` library fns. **Read-only over the durable store:** opens the same `<room>.log` + `<room>.snap` the server persists, rebuilds in memory, never writes — no watermark advances, no durable byte changes. **Reconstruct-at-seq** reuses the server's own restore path: `reconstruct_at` truncates the `RoomLog` to the target seq (retained op `log.ops[i]` carries seq `floor+i+1`, keep the first `seq−floor`) and runs `Hub::from_rooms` — the nearest snapshot at/below the target seeds state, the tail replays onto it; byte-identical to the room's live export at that seq, across a compaction floor (op-join ≡ snapshot-join). **Diff-two-points** reconstructs both and runs the existing `core::path::diff`. **Range:** valid targets `[floor, head]` — below the floor its ops are compacted away (`BelowFloor`; earliest reconstructable = the floor = snapshot alone), above the head not yet written (`PastHead`). **Server-id caveat:** `encode_state` embeds the doc's replica id — a snapshot-backed room pins it (any server id → byte-identical), an uncompacted room takes it from the passed id, hence the `server` param + CLI `--server-id` (default placeholder). **CLI (clap, server crate, never core):** `crdtsync-replay --data-dir <dir> <room> --to <seq> [--out <file>]` and `... --from <a> --to <b> --diff [--out <file>]`; scriptable text summary, `--out` writes raw encoded state / encoded diff. Specs `crates/server/tests/replay.rs` (10): reconstruct == live state at each seq / at head / seq-0 empty, across-compaction uses the snapshot + floor == snapshot alone, below-floor + past-head rejected, unknown room, diff == direct `core::path::diff` + identical-seq empty, read-only (every durable file byte-identical after a full replay+diff sweep). No `crates/core/src` change (no Miri). **Deferred/last:** richer CLI UX (op inspection, timeline/causal-graph viz) and the **admin-UI dashboard**. Design in DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Debugging (2026-07-20). → *Debugging*.

**Tooling** — **audit-query surface — durable audit trail + operator query endpoint — DONE (#PR). The first tooling unit (non-UI operator tool).** Realizes ARCHITECTURE §Audit's read-only-action access log as a durable store + query surface. **Store:** an append-only structured file-log (`AuditLog`, `crates/server/src/audit.rs`) — the op-store durability shape (length-framed records: `u64` timestamp, action/decision tags, length-prefixed actor, tagged resource; flush-before-return; never mutated or removed; torn-tail tolerant, complete-undecodable = corruption). **Not a DB** — a DB store is a deliberately-deferred scale option (Revisit), no DB dep added. **Three new `Action` variants** (`Connect`, `Export`, `VersionRead`) — additive to `authz::Action`, the two `Action→Capability`/`Action→SchemaAction` maps kept exhaustive (new arms → `None`, audit-only operator actions). **What's audited + where:** security-relevant only — every **denied** decision + every **write** (ACL grant/revoke ride the write path) via an `Audited`→`DurableAccessLog` sink at the enforcement seam; **`Connect`** at the registry auth transition + fast-path connect via `observe`; **`VersionRead`** at the session version-fetch site via `observe`; **`Export`** at the blob-fetch route. Routine *permitted* reads/publishes are **not** persisted (no noise). **Query surface:** operator admin-HTTP `GET /audit` (own `serve_audit` listener, `CRDTSYNC_AUDIT_ADDR`), gated by the same verifier + authorizer as schema-registration — requires `Read` on the reserved `$audit` app, so the trail is never on the app→wire path — filters actor/action/room/time-range (half-open `[since,until)`), returns JSON; **read-only** (never writes/mutates). Straight scan (v1); in-memory index deferred. **Fail-loud integrity:** a failed append returns the IO error + latches a health flag; the endpoint `500`s on an unhealthy or corrupt log rather than a clean partial read. Opt-in via `CRDTSYNC_AUDIT_LOG` (zero cost when off). Specs `crates/server/tests/audit_query.rs` (8): append/replay across reopen, filter by actor/action/room/time-range + empty=all, torn-tail dropped + corrupt surfaces, connect + denied read audited but permitted read is not, version-fetch → VersionRead, operator read + filters over HTTP, read-only + 401/403/400 authz boundary, corrupt trail → 500. Local review **HIGH** (security/audit boundary). No core change (no Miri). **Follow-ons (deferred):** (a) in-memory query index / DB store if the trail grows large; (b) log rotation/retention — the log grows unbounded, bounded only by the security-relevant-event filter (a size/time rotation is the noted follow-on); (c) auditing a *rejected* credential (anonymous failed-auth) — success connects are audited, a rejected credential takes the connection-closing error path. Design in DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Audit (2026-07-20). → *Tooling*.

**Zones** — **Zones Unit 4 — cross-zone move via an AEAD capability token (server + core protocol) — DONE (#PR). UN-DEFERS the last zone unit; the Zones epic is now COMPLETE.** The authorized escape hatch for the cross-zone tree move the op-submit gate (1b-ii-b) otherwise rejects: a **server-sealed capability token authorizes exactly ONE cross-zone move**; every un-tokened / forged / expired / mismatched crossing stays rejected exactly as before. **Token:** ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RustCrypto, `crates/server` only — never the wasm core) AEAD-seals the binding tuple `(room, actor, element, src_zone, dst_zone, expiry)`; the whole tuple is the authenticated plaintext, so it is unforgeable and non-transferable to a different actor/element/src/dst/room; fresh random 96-bit nonce per seal; `open` total + fail-closed (`crates/server/src/zonetoken.rs`). **Key:** 32-byte zone-master key held on the `Hub` (`set_zone_key`), server config like the TLS cert — `ServeConfig::zone_key` ← `CRDTSYNC_ZONE_KEY` (64 hex, clean startup error on malformed); unset ⇒ escape hatch off, every crossing rejected (fail-closed default). **Issuance:** `Message::CrossZoneToken{room, element, dst_zone}` (wire tag 44, room-keyed, leader-served) → ACL-authorized through the **existing `authorized` evaluator** (move authority = write at the element's path, via new `acl::doc_acl_write_at`; **AND** dst-zone write authority = `Resource::Zone{room,dst}`, or the room for the unzoned root) → `CrossZoneTokenGrant{token}` (tag 45), or `Error{Forbidden}` and no token. **Redemption:** `Message::CrossZoneOps{channel, ops, token}` (tag 46 — a distinct message, so plain `Ops` stays literally unchanged and token-free; both share the extracted `session::handle_ops`) → at ingress decrypt+authenticate under the zone key, check `is_live(now)` and the sealed binding matches the batch's **actual** crossing (`index::batch_zone_crossings` generalizing `batch_crosses_zone`) → admit that one move, else keep the `OpsRejected{Forbidden}`. Token consumed at ingress — never logged, never fanned out, committed move token-free. **Replay** bounded by the binding + 30 s expiry + op-id dedup (seen-nonce cache = hardening follow-on, unbuilt). Specs: `crates/server/tests/cross_zone_token.rs` (17: issuance grant+readback, issuance denials [dst-write/unknown-element/undeclared-zone/no-key], accepted move **+ convergence** on a replica, un-tokened crossing still rejected even with a key, forged/foreign-key/expired rejected, one-field binding mismatches [element/actor/src/dst/room] each rejected, correctly-sealed accepted, same-zone needs no token), `zonetoken.rs` unit tests (6), `crates/core/tests/protocol_cross_zone.rs` (6 wire round-trip/tags/truncation). Local review HIGH (crypto/security boundary). Targeted + full Miri on touched core suites (protocol, client) clean; `crates/server` dep-liberal (`chacha20poly1305`). Design in DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Zones (2026-07-18). → *Authorization/zones*.

**Networking / Transport** — **mTLS "request-but-not-require" mode (server) — DONE (#PR). CLOSES the mTLS unit.** The #305 follow-on: an optional **opportunistic mTLS** mode alongside the existing require mode. `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CLIENT_AUTH=require|request` (`ClientAuthMode`) selects strictness when a client-CA is configured; **default stays `require`** (the secure #305 behavior is unchanged — request is strictly opt-in), and an unrecognized value is a clean `TlsConfigError::BadClientAuthMode` startup error (never silently the permissive mode). **request mode = `WebPkiClientVerifier::builder(roots).allow_unauthenticated().build()`** (`server_config_from_pem_with_client_ca_mode`): the verifier **still validates a presented cert** against the roots — an untrusted/invalid *presented* cert is **still rejected at the handshake** — but a client presenting **no** cert is admitted. **The only relaxation is cert absence**; a bad presented cert is never treated as anonymous. **Certless fall-through reuses the existing path:** a request-mode certless client yields `peer_cert_actor → Ok(None)` (the same result server-auth-only TLS produces), so `Cmd::Connect.cert_actor = None` and it falls through to the established in-band-credential / anonymous rules — no new session-actor concept, `runtime.rs` unchanged behaviorally. A presented valid cert still binds its SAN→CN actor exactly as require mode. Specs `crates/server/tests/tls.rs` (+7): request mode — valid cert binds identity, certless connects + authenticates in band (NOT rejected), untrusted *presented* cert still rejected; request-mode config construction; `ClientAuthMode::parse` (default/require/request case-insensitive; unknown → `BadClientAuthMode`). Require-mode certless-rejected + default-is-require carried by #305's specs. Excluded under Miri (real I/O). Local review **HIGH** (security-authentication boundary). `crates/server` dep-liberal; core untouched. Design in DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Transport (2026-07-19, no deviation from the entering design). → *Networking / Handshake (mTLS)*.
**Networking / Transport** — **mTLS client-cert credential carrier (server) — DONE (#305).** The TLS #300 follow-on: mutual TLS as a **credential carrier for the ACL authenticated-actor identity** (not a parallel identity concept). Setting `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CLIENT_CA` (a PEM trust-anchor bundle) swaps the #300 `with_no_client_auth()` slot for a `rustls::server::WebPkiClientVerifier` against those roots (`server_config_from_pem_with_client_ca`); without it, TLS stays server-auth-only exactly as #300 (**no regression**). **Fail-closed at the handshake:** the verifier *requires* (not requests) a client cert, so a client presenting no cert, or one that does not chain to a configured root, is rejected by rustls **before the connection reaches the session layer**. **Cert identity → the existing authenticated-actor plumbing:** a verified connection's leaf cert is parsed (`x509-parser`) for its **SAN** (first DNS/email/URI general name), **falling back to CN**; that name rides `Cmd::Connect.cert_actor` from the accept loop (which reads `peer_certificates()` off the accepted `TlsStream`) to the registry actor, which binds it via the *same* `connect_authenticated(Identity::new(actor))` the fast-path credential + anonymous mode use — the ACL evaluator sees the cert actor at every read/write point, and the client learns its actor via the unsolicited `AuthOk`. It **takes precedence** over any in-band credential on the same connection. **Three fail-closed points:** no/untrusted cert → rejected at handshake; a verified-but-identity-less cert (no SAN, no CN) → connection dropped, never anonymous; an empty client-CA bundle → loud `TlsConfigError::NoClientCa`, and a client CA without a server cert/key → clean startup error (mTLS needs TLS). Specs `crates/server/tests/tls.rs` (+9, rcgen test CA + CA-signed client certs): SAN binds as the actor (over CN); CN-only falls back to CN; an ACL read decision is keyed on the cert actor; no-cert rejected; untrusted-CA rejected; a no-client-CA server still accepts a certless client (#300 regression); mTLS `ServerConfig` construction (valid / empty→`NoClientCa` / missing→`Io`). Excluded under Miri (real I/O). Local review **HIGH** (security-authentication boundary). `crates/server` dep-liberal (`x509-parser` added); core untouched. Design in DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Transport (2026-07-17, no deviation from the entering design). → *Networking / Handshake (mTLS)*.
**Networking / Transport** — **TLS termination at the server listener (server) — DONE (#300).** Realizes ARCHITECTURE §Transport's "WSS over TLS in production" — TLS is terminated at the server's own listener (terminate-at-server, not an assumed external proxy), via **rustls** (pure-Rust, ring provider — the one reqwest already pulls in, so no OpenSSL/system dep, hermetic build) + `tokio-rustls`. `server_config_from_pem(cert, key)` (new `server::tls`) loads a PEM cert-chain + private key into a `rustls::ServerConfig` at startup; `ServeConfig.tls: Option<Arc<ServerConfig>>` carries it into the accept loop, which wraps each accepted socket in a rustls session before the **unchanged, transport-agnostic** wire protocol runs over it. The session logic is not forked — `handle` was made generic over the stream (`AsyncRead + AsyncWrite + Unpin + Send`) so the same driver serves plaintext `TcpStream` and TLS-wrapped streams alike. **Opt-in / plaintext-preserving:** with no cert configured the listener binds plaintext exactly as before (dev frictionless); `main.rs` gates on `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CERT` + `CRDTSYNC_TLS_KEY` (both required together → `wss://`; only one → clean startup error). **Fail-closed security invariant:** a configured-but-broken cert/key (missing / empty / mismatched) is a **loud startup error**, never a silent downgrade to plaintext (which would silently ship an unencrypted deployment that asked for encryption) — explicitly tested. **Pre-auth DoS bound:** the TLS handshake is wrapped in a 10s timeout so a stalled client can't pin a task + FD; a failed/timed-out handshake drops only that connection. **mTLS (client-cert verification) is the FOLLOW-ON this unblocks** — the `ServerConfig` is built with `with_no_client_auth()`, exactly the seam a later mTLS unit replaces with a client-cert verifier without touching cert/key loading; not built here. Specs `crates/server/tests/tls.rs` (8): rcgen self-signed cert drives a real TLS handshake + wire-frame round-trip over the encrypted stream, a plaintext client gets no session on the TLS listener, and the `server_config_from_pem` construction + config gating each fail loudly (missing cert / missing key / empty cert / no key / mismatch). Excluded under Miri (real I/O); `crates/server` is dep-liberal. Local review (medium → added the handshake timeout). Design in DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Transport (2026-07-16). → *Networking / Transport (mTLS follow-on)*.
**SDK surface** — **Unit 9c — producer-side reject-before-send (server op-ingress) — DONE (#302). The last Schema slice; Unit 9 is now COMPLETE.** The enforcing server's op-ingress is the **authoritative reject boundary**: on a main-branch write to a room it holds an enforcing schema for, `session::step` simulates the batch and refuses one that *introduces* a **runtime-kind mismatch at a declared slot** — a slot materializing the wrong element kind (a counter where the type declares a register), unrepairable at read *and* inadmissible (unlike an undeclared slot, which is unrepairable but admitted) — with an `OpsRejected` / new `ErrorCode::SchemaViolation` (wire tag 8, distinct from the authz `Forbidden`); the op never enters the log, the author keeps its ops (existing `onOpsRejected` surface). **Forced deviation from the entering design, logged in DECISIONS (2026-07-16):** the design named two non-repairable violations to reject (kind mismatch + undeclared map slot) plus a sequence-`min` underflow example — but a sequence `min` is rejected at schema *parse* time (closure invariant, no runtime underflow exists) and an **undeclared map slot is NOT rejected** (a Map is an open container — slot membership is not a schema dimension; rejecting it would break the ubiquitous open-content-model pattern that 12 server suites rely on and contradict ARCHITECTURE §Closure). So the reject set narrows to the **single** rejectable violation: a declared-slot kind mismatch. **Mechanism:** `ViolationKind::rejects_at_ingress()` (core, `KindMismatch`-only classifier) + `index::batch_introduces_schema_violation` (server before/after `validate` delta over a decoded-copy simulation, mirroring `batch_crosses_zone`) + `Hub::batch_violates_schema` (an opening write to an unknown room validates against a fresh empty doc). Only a *newly-introduced* mismatch is refused — one already standing (from a non-enforcing write) is left to the Map slot's LWW (§Invariant Repair "Map slot type mismatch handled by the algorithm, not repair"), so an unrelated write never wedges on it. The delta compares mismatched **locations**, not a bare count (**HIGH-review finding:** a count nets to zero and admits a fresh mismatch when a batch heals one standing mismatch while planting another); the location key resolves each sequence index to its **stable Fugue node stamp**, so an unrelated insert/delete that renumbers a standing mismatch is not misread as new. A **relay** connection (`schema` `None`) never validates (pass-through). No STATE_VERSION change; Unit 2 read-repair unchanged. Specs `crates/server/tests/schema_reject.rs` (reject a kind mismatch + name the author's seqs; **admit** an undeclared slot; **accept + repair-at-read** an over-`max` regression; a valid write unaffected; a relay passes the mistyped op through; post-reject replicas converge on the valid op alone; refusal survives a store reopen) + `crates/core/tests/protocol.rs` (round-trip the new `SchemaViolation` + previously-uncovered `NotFound`). Local review **HIGH** (enforcement trust boundary). Targeted Miri clean (core validate/repair/protocol, server schema_reject); full Miri concurrent. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-16). → *SDK surface*.
**Audit fix** — **A tree-move (and its delete / insert) must survive a parent's displacement (core) — DONE (#299).** A cs-crdt-audit **HIGH** (CRDT convergence violation), surfaced by the convergence oracle once its tree-move arm was restored (left out of #296): a Kleppmann `XmlMove` **did not converge when the move's source or destination parent was concurrently displaced** — its map slot lost to a higher-stamped scalar / other create. The move's `XmlInsertChild` (materialise the node), the `XmlMove` (relocate it), and a `ListDelete` (delete-wins-over-move) all gated on `resolvable(target)` / `live_list`, which treat a **displaced** container identically to an **unseen** one. So a parent **born displaced** (its displacing op applied before its create) **buffered the child insert forever** (node never materialised → move never applied → node lost), **dropped a move into a displaced destination** (never entered the lamport log → the folded tree diverged from a replica that applied it while the destination was briefly installed), and **buffered the delete of a moved node's birth placement** while the move committed under the new parent (delete-wins held on one replica, not the other). Same op set, different tree by arrival order. Fix: gate `XmlInsertChild` / `XmlMove` / `ListDelete` readiness (`ready`, `tx_group_ready`) on the target children **sequence being materialised, not installed**, and record into the materialised list handle regardless of its displaced flag (`insert_xml_child`, `apply_move`, the `ListDelete` apply path) — a displaced parent **retains** its children (the existing *Displacement retains, it does not forget* invariant, now extended across the children axis), so the edit is applied and **retained hidden**; the move fold is now a pure function of the move-set + tombstone-set, independent of any parent's transient slot state. A truly **unmaterialised** parent (create unseen) still buffers. No STATE_VERSION change. Specs `crates/core/tests/xml_move.rs` (move survives source displacement; move into a displaced destination converges; delete still wins over a move when the birth parent is displaced — each red before, green after) + `crates/core/tests/convergence.rs` (tree-move **and** xml-child-delete arms restored to `random_edit`; full shuffle + reverse + double sweep green at 8000 native seeds — two residual seeds, source- and destination-displaced, found and fixed with no deferral). Local review **HIGH** (a second finder pass caught the delete-wins gap after the initial move fix; fixed with its own regression test). Targeted + full-local Miri clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-16). → *Authorization*.

**Testing / Correctness** — **Convergence + idempotence coverage hardening (core + ffi tests) — DONE (#296).** cs-crdt-audit test-rot / coverage-gap findings, additive test-only (no core behavior change). The strongest convergence oracle (`convergence.rs` — reverse + 8 shuffles + doubled over 400 native seeds / miri-capped) now folds the **ranged-mark** (create / set-payload / delete) and **ACL** (grant / revoke) op families: `random_edit` emits them and `fingerprint` renders the doc-level annotation + ACL sets (sorted, id-stable) so a cross-permutation divergence in those families surfaces; the mark arm forces a text body so it is not starved by displacement. Plus: focused element-scoped-ACL × `XmlMove` read-decision convergence under a Fisher-Yates shuffle sweep (`acl_element.rs`); migration-rename-then-reordered-delivery convergence (`migration.rs`); zones convergence broadened from forward+reverse to shuffles (`zone_lamport.rs`); standalone RangedDelete / AclRevoke double-apply idempotence (`ranged.rs` / `acl.rs`); a born-in-denied-then-moved snapshot no-crash decode guard (`state_project_read.rs`); FFI header requires the six omitted exports (`client_take_rejected`, `client_declare_app`, `client_active_schema`, `client_active_schema_version`, `doc_encode_state`, `doc_decode_state`) with the `active_schema` / `active_schema_version` substring shadow disambiguated (`header.rs`). **Two real behavior findings surfaced and DEFERRED (asserts not weakened to pin the bug):** (1) `Text::insert_run` **char-id collision at the lamport ceiling** — `saturating_add` collapses consecutive codepoints at `u64::MAX`, so a multi-codepoint run based at the ceiling drops all but the first (data loss; still convergent); (2) `XmlMove` **non-convergence when the moved node's source container is concurrently displaced** — same op set diverges by arrival order (5-op minimal repro at seed 278). The oracle's tree-move arm is therefore left OUT pending the core fix; the controlled (non-displacing) tree-move × ACL convergence is covered by the `acl_element.rs` test. CI sharded-Miri gated all crates; targeted core (convergence / acl_element / migration / zone_lamport / ranged / acl / state_project_read) Miri clean locally. Local review (medium). → *Testing / Correctness*.

**Audit fix** — **Migration re-key must displace the container it evicts (core) — DONE (#295).** A cs-crdt-audit MEDIUM (displacement-signal class): `Map::put_slot_lww` — the LWW slot installer snapshot-migration re-keying drives (`migrate_leaf_slots_scoped`) — overwrote an occupied slot **without displacing the live composite it evicted**, unlike `Map::set`. A migration renaming a leaf field onto a key holding a **live container** (Map/List/Text) whose stamp the leaf outranks dropped the container from the slot but left it `is_displaced() == false` → the detached container read **installed**, so a later op targeting it passed the `is_displaced` route-mutation guard and **silently mutated it** — the exact failure Map Slot Safety forbids. The op-seam peer (via `Map::set`→`evict`) displaces the loser, so applied-op sets **diverged** until a snapshot decode's `mark_displaced` healed it. Fix: displace the evicted live composite before the overwriting `insert`, mirroring `Map::evict`, skipping a same-handle re-install — in `put_slot_lww` itself so all three callers (leaf move, resurrect install, delete re-key) are covered; the resurrect loop's own `won`/`displace` reconciliation is unchanged. Specs `crates/core/tests/state_project.rs` (a leaf renamed onto a live map/list/text reads the evicted container DISPLACED; a converge test proves snapshot-seam ≡ op-seam bytes and that an op targeting the detached container is dropped on both seams) — red before the fix, green after. Targeted + full-local Miri clean; local review (medium). → *Authorization*.

**Authorization** — **Reveal-on-move-in — a node born in a denied subtree, revealed when moved into a readable one (core + server) — DONE (#294).** Resolves the mirror-case limitation #293 flagged: a `card` **born in a denied subtree** (its `XmlInsertChild` create has a denied read path — the birth position) then **moved into a readable one** via `XmlMove` did not converge. A fresh op-catch-up joiner never received the create and the readable move could not materialize a node it never had → op-join **lacked** the node, while the snapshot projection (judging by the readable current position) **kept** it. **User-chosen semantics: REVEAL ON MOVE-IN** — a node whose current position a reader can read becomes visible to that reader there (drag a private page into a shared folder → collaborators see it), consistent with #293's "you see the node at positions you can read." **Mechanism — synthesize the node's current state by current position, not birth path:** a new redaction-only `OpKind::XmlReveal { node, tag }` (never authored/logged/persisted; op wire tag 21) carries the node's stable id + current tag with **no placement**; applied, it materializes the node shell (attrs Map + children List by derived id) and marks it revealed-pending, and the reader's readable `XmlMove` then lands its **first** placement (`apply_move`/readiness accept a revealed-pending node; a move of any other placement-less node stays a no-op). The node's readable subtree **content** ops need no synthesis — their targets are the node's derived sub-container ids at its now-readable path, so the ordinary per-path redaction delivers them and the apply buffer folds them onto the shell. `Document::reveal_ops(reads)` is the exact **mirror of `project_read_paths`** (emits a shell iff birth path denied AND current path readable — the nodes the projection keeps), so the two seams agree by construction: **catch-up** prepends `reveal_ops` to a partial reader's filtered delta (a shell only for a node whose move survives the delta); **live fan-out** prepends the shell **plus the node's now-readable subtree content replayed from the log** (`Hub::reveal_backfill` — content authored while private is absent from the move's batch, so the replay is what makes a mid-session live reader converge with a fresh joiner). Holds for **path** and **element** scope (same element-context resolver both seams). **No history leak** — the reveal carries only current identity + tag (shell stamped from the birth stamp under a node-derived id in a reserved namespace, disjoint from real client ids, never a real authored op); no origin op (birth create, origin fragment, pre-move denied content) is delivered. **`XmlReveal` is server-only** — the op-submit gate rejects a client that authors one as a protocol violation, so it never enters the log/doc. Convergence bar = **materialized content** (deep `board_render` equality + canonical re-encode), not whole-`encode_state` bytes — the op-joiner and snapshot-joiner legitimately differ in the identity header + the causal `seen` frontier the projection clears for a partial reader; a whole-document reader is byte-identical past the header. No STATE_VERSION change. Specs: `crates/core/tests/state_project_read.rs` (`reveal_ops` reveals the born-denied node, empty for a born-readable node + whole-document reader, shell+move converges with the projection) + `crates/server/tests/acl_redaction.rs` (op-join≡snapshot-join for path + element scope, a **deep** subtree reveals its whole current state, a **live** reader is revealed on move-in, whole-doc reader unchanged, no-ACL regression, no origin leak; #293 forward case still converges). Bundled the #293 unused-`mut` lint cleanup. `project_zones` shares the current-position gap class and is left out of scope. **The redaction×move convergence work is now COMPLETE both directions.** Local review (HIGH). CI sharded-Miri gated all crates; targeted core + server acl/state/doc/move Miri clean locally. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Authorization*.

**Authorization** — **Move-into-denied snapshot convergence + dangling-decode fix (core + server) — DONE (#293).** A pre-existing correctness bug in the doc-ACL snapshot projection (`Document::project_read_paths`), surfaced by element-id ACL but affecting **path scope** too: a movable XML node **created readable** at its origin (`XmlInsertChild` admitted at its birth list's path) then **moved into a denied subtree** via `XmlMove` (whose read path is the denied *destination*, so the reader never receives the move-away) was redacted by its **current** position. This **diverged op-join from snapshot-join** and left a **dangling reference** that crashed the snapshot's `decode_state` (`BadTag "dangling reference"`) — a partial reader could not cold-start any room holding such a node. The fix makes the projection judge a moved node the way a fresh op-catch-up joiner does: **a node created readable and moved into a denied subtree is kept at its readable origin**, un-purged with the attrs map + children list a decoded `XmlElement` needs; its move placement/log edge into the denied destination and every placement/move into a denied position are dropped, the fold is rebuilt (`refold_projected_moves`, the same reconstruction `restore_moves` runs on decode), and the children list it grew in the denied position is **cleared** (not tombstoned — a tombstone leaks the dropped descendants' derivable ids and adds a slot the op joiner lacks). Holds for **path** and **element** scope (an element grant on the origin container resolves to the origin path identically on the op-fan-out and snapshot seams). No STATE_VERSION change. Specs: `crates/core/tests/state_project_read.rs` (move-into-denied kept at origin + re-encode canonical; nested keep-and-clear drops a grandchild grown in the denied position), `crates/server/tests/acl_redaction.rs` (op-join≡snapshot-join for path scope + element-grant-on-origin, no dangling on re-encode). **Documented limitation (needs a design decision):** the mirror — a node **born in a denied subtree then moved into a readable one**, and the general case of a node **modified after** moving into denied — does not converge from a current-state snapshot; it needs an op-stream withdrawal (deliver a node's create by its current position, or Fugue-safe single-node list removal), a redaction-architecture change beyond the projection. Local review (HIGH, 8 angles) drove the scope: the review found the single-level fix leaked/diverged on nested subtrees, refining it to keep-at-origin + clear. Design + limitation in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Authorization*.

**Authorization** — **Element-id ACL — grants that follow an element across a tree-move (core + server) — DONE (#291).** Doc-ACL grants were keyed only by a **position path**, a security hole under the shipped Kleppmann `XmlMove` tree-move: move a restricted element and its `deny` strands at the OLD path (governing whatever slides in) while the element lands at a NEW path under possibly-open ACL — an actor could **exfiltrate a restricted element by dragging it to an unrestricted location**, and an unrelated element could **inherit a restriction by landing where the moved one used to be**. Adds `AclScope { Path | Element }` on `AclTuple` and `OpKind::AclGrant`: **path/zone ACL keys stable structural locations** (a zone, a well-known field — governs whatever occupies the slot), **element-id ACL keys movable content** that carries its own permission (a Notion page dragged between parents, a Kanban card across columns, a folder reparent). An `Element` scope resolves to the element's **current** path at check time, so the grant moves atomically with the element and both holes close. **Core stays index-free:** the pure evaluator takes an injected `id → current path` resolver (`&dyn Fn(ElementId) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`), resolves every tuple's scope up front, then the whole existing deny-overrides / inheritance / provenance-rooting lattice runs over the resolved paths unchanged (the delegation/attenuation walk resolves the scope BEFORE rooting); an **unresolvable id is inert / fail-closed** (roots nothing, governs nothing, denies nothing — never fails open), and a `Path` scope never consults the resolver so all path-ACL behavior is byte-identical. **Server** injects its element-context index (`index::element_paths`) as the resolver at every doc-ACL seam — the write gate, per-op read redaction (fan-out + catch-up), and snapshot projection — so an element resolves to the same current path everywhere; snapshot projection derives its resolver from the decoded snapshot it projects so it cannot drift, and an unresolvable-element tuple redacts by root read on both catch-up seams so op-served and snapshot-served readers converge. Hand-rolled scope codec (tags 0=Path/1=Element), STATE_VERSION 10→11, pre-release break (a stale version rejects hard). Specs: `crates/core/tests/acl_element.rs` (move-safe grant + no-strand + no-exfil at the core level via a real `XmlMove`, unresolvable=inert, element scope in the rooting/revocation/bounded-deny walk, scope codec round-trip/truncation/bad-tag, snapshot round-trip, projection root-fallback convergence) + the three security properties over the server redaction seam with real `XmlMove` in `crates/server/tests/acl_redaction.rs` (move-safe/no-strand/no-exfil, whole-doc reader sees all, op-join↔snapshot-join convergence, no-ACL move regression). Local review (HIGH, 8 angles) — fixed an op-join/snapshot-join divergence for unresolvable element scopes (+ regression), deduped the resolver, made the element-index build lazy; branch/post-apply-index findings are pre-existing limitations element scoping inherits (branches are main-gated). CI sharded-Miri gated all crates (core 20m, server shards 15–20m, ffi 5m, ≤30min); targeted core + server ACL/state/doc Miri clean locally. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Authorization*.

**Snapshots / Export-Import** — **Clone-room client-wire surface (core + server + FFI/Go/Python/wasm) — DONE (#288). Closes the #196 "wire/session/SDK exposure deferred" note** — the `Hub::clone_room(src, dst)` primitive (#196, "duplicate this doc as a template": `export_room` + `import_room`, create-only, `Ok(false)` if src unknown or dst exists) is now reachable by clients over an in-band frame. Mirrors the branch-management 6b room-keyed client frame EXACTLY. **core `protocol.rs`:** `Message::CloneRoom { src, dst }` (wire tag **42**, next free after the Branch block 27-33 + Diff 40/41) + a `CloneRoomResult { dst, created }` reply (tag **43**) — a small explicit result, not a list, since clone has no post-state collection to echo; hand-rolled total codec (src-then-dst; dst-then-created-byte). **core `client.rs`:** `ClientSession::clone_room(src, dst)` frames the request (mirrors `fork_branch`); a `CloneRoomResult` folds into a per-`dst` `clone_results` view read via `clone_result(dst) -> Option<bool>` (keyed by dst like `branches`/`diffs` are keyed by room); `CloneRoom` gets its own client-to-server-only refuse arm. **server `session.rs`:** dispatch to `Hub::clone_room`. **Authz composes** read-on-`src` (the `branch_authorized` Read tier a branch list / diff uses — doc-ACL abstains, deployment+schema decide) with the branch-management **write tier on `dst`** (the create); deny either → recoverable `Forbidden`, unauthenticated → violation, persist `io::Error` → `internal` (closes), never a panic. The create is **leader-redirected on `dst`** like a branch mutation. **Cross-shard limitation (documented, not a bug):** the clone reads `src`'s state from the node that persists `dst`, so `src`/`dst` must co-locate on one leader; a cluster where they hash to different leaders reports `created == false` rather than misplacing `dst` — cross-node state transfer is out of scope for this single-hub primitive (all 3 review angles converged here; documented in the dispatch comment + DECISIONS). **SDK fan-out:** FFI `crdtsync_client_clone_room` + `crdtsync_client_clone_result` (cbindgen header regenerated, symbol-assert extended), Go `CloneRoom`/`CloneResult`, Python `clone_room`/`clone_result`, wasm `cloneRoom`/`cloneResult` — each mirrors its BranchFork sibling. Core-touching (`protocol.rs`, `client.rs`) → CI sharded-Miri gates all crates; targeted core + server + FFI clone-suite Miri clean locally. Specs: `crates/core/tests/protocol_clone.rs` (round-trip / truncation / distinct-tag), `crates/core/tests/client_clone.rs` (frames the request, reply folds per-dst, server-sent request refused), `crates/server/tests/clone_room.rs` (7: populated clone creates dst with src's state seen over a fresh subscribe, unknown-src / existing-dst no-op, reader-without-read-on-src Forbidden, unauth violation, origin/clone diverge independently over the wire), + FFI/Go/Python/wasm marshal+receive tests. Local review (high, 4 angles) — fixed a timeless-comment violation + documented the cross-shard limit; cleanup/altitude/gate all clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Snapshots / Export-Import*.

**Tooling/CI** — **Miri gate ≤30min — shard the server crate across 3 nextest partitions — DONE (#282).** The gate wall-clock is the slowest single job, and the `crdtsync-server` Miri job had grown to ~31m — over the 30-min ceiling (core ~20-23m, ffi ~4m). The `miri` matrix converts from a bare `crate` list to explicit `include` entries so only the server crate fans further into three parallel jobs, each `cargo +nightly miri nextest run -p crdtsync-server --partition count:N/3`; core + ffi stay single jobs. nextest `--partition count:N/M` hash-splits the tests into M disjoint buckets (locally verified: 748 server tests → 270/250/228, no overlap, union = full set — no silent under-run). The three shards share one `rust-cache` key (`crate`, not partition) — identical crate, same warm build, no clobber. **The Miri gate ≤30min now rests on three mechanisms: per-crate matrix (parallel jobs, wall-clock = slowest crate) + warm build cache (#280) + server nextest partitioning (#282).** Measured on the landing run: server shards 12m44s / 21m19s / 15m47s, core 23m25s, ffi 4m09s — slowest job core ~23m, under 30min. CI-yaml-only (`.github/workflows/rust.yml`), no source change. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Tooling / CI throughput*.

**Zones** — **Zones Unit 5 — zone-scoped subscribe SDK surface (core + FFI/Go/Python/wasm) — DONE (#281). THIS COMPLETES THE ZONES EPIC'S CLIENT SURFACE — declaration → resolution → enforcement → per-zone streams → SDK subscribe.** Forwards the Unit-3 `Subscribe.zone` selector (already on the wire + enforced server-side #278) out through the client-session subscribe surface and all four SDKs. **No new wire tag, no `protocol.rs` change** — the `zone: Vec<u8>` field already rides `Message::Subscribe`. **Core (`client.rs` only):** `ClientSession::subscribe_zone(room, zone)` structured exactly like `subscribe_branch`; the empty-zone `subscribe`/`subscribe_branch` shortcuts now route through a shared `subscribe_inner(room, branch, zone)`. **`Room` records the channel's `zone` so `resume` re-issues it** — the pre-Unit-5 `resume` hard-coded `zone: Vec::new()`, silently widening a zone-scoped channel to the whole room on reconnect; now it clones the stored zone, mirroring how it already preserves `branch` (a `zone(channel)` accessor mirrors `branch(channel)`). Empty zone = whole room (every zone the actor may read + root); a name = that partition + root. **SDK fan-out:** FFI `crdtsync_client_subscribe_zone` (header regenerated, symbol-assert extended), wasm `subscribeZone`, Go `SubscribeZone`, Python `subscribe_zone` — each mirrors its `subscribe_branch` sibling. Core-touching (`client.rs`) → CI Miri gates the PR; targeted core + FFI client-suite Miri clean locally. Specs: `crates/core/tests/client.rs` (4: zone rides the frame decoded off the wire, empty-zone shortcut stays empty, `resume` preserves the zone), FFI/Go/Python/wasm marshal tests mirroring each existing subscribe test. Local review (medium) clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). **Unit 4 (cross-zone token) is now DONE (#PR) — the Zones epic is COMPLETE.** → *Authorization/zones*.

**Tooling/CI** — **Miri CI throughput — cap ungated fuzz loops + parallelize the job — DONE (#279).** The Miri gate had grown to ~4h wall and blocked every core PR. Two independent test/CI-yaml-only wins (zero `crates/*/src` change): (1) **cap the still-ungated high-iteration loops under `cfg!(miri)`** — Miri validates the memory model (UB/leaks/aliasing), which a handful of iterations exercises fully; the large counts exist for statistical/logic convergence that the native run already provides at full count. Only the `cfg!(miri)` branch is capped, native counts untouched, following the pre-existing idiom (`convergence.rs`, `json_differential.rs`, `diff_property.rs`, plus `doc.rs`/`validate.rs` deep-nesting tests already `#[cfg_attr(miri, ignore)]`). Gated: core `treemove` 200→4, `xml_move` 80→4, `marks` 64→4 (heavy per-seed ranged-text build+fingerprint), `ranged`/`ranged_payload`/`acl` 64→8; server `failover` 3000→50, `membership` 500/1000/2000→32, `placement` 1000→20, `routing` 200→8. Each gated loop checks a per-iteration-independent invariant or an independent fuzz seed, so the reduced count preserves the memory-safety coverage; the `0..1_000_000` server room-search loops are early-`return`/`.find` and left alone. (2) **Parallelize the Miri job** — shard the serial `-p core -p ffi -p server` into a per-crate `strategy.matrix` (`crdtsync-core`/`crdtsync-ffi`/`crdtsync-server`), 3 parallel jobs, so job wall-clock is the slowest crate not the sum (no branch protection requires a bare `miri` status, so the matrix names are free). Measured before→after under Miri: `treemove` seed suite ~485→~11 virtual-s, `failover` suite 552→18 virtual-s (10s wall), `marks` seed test 174s→99s wall; all six core fuzz suites together now ~5 min wall (incl. compile). Net: ~4h → well under 1h. Merged on `test` + 3 SDK green (Miri non-required). → *Tooling / CI throughput*.

**Zones** — **Zones Unit 3 — zone-scoped subscription + per-zone replication streams + wire redaction (server) — DONE (#278).** Turns the Unit-2 op-envelope `zone` substrate into actual per-zone STREAMS: **with this the zone isolation model is functionally real** (declaration → resolution → enforcement → per-zone streams). `Message::Subscribe` gains a `zone: Vec<u8>` selector (empty = whole room = every zone the actor may read + root; a name = that partition + root; a no-`zones` room ignores it — one implicit root partition, byte-identical to today). `Resource::Zone { room, zone }` gates a zone subscription; a nonexistent name and an unauthorized zone are refused with the **same** generic `forbidden("read denied")`, so a refusal never confirms the zone exists (existence itself concealed). Zone default-visible within a readable room; an explicit per-zone deny carves the partition out — expressible on the shipped `Acl` via a new `ResourceMatch::Zone`. Streams are **one channel per room + a per-recipient `op.zone` filter** at the exact seam doc-ACL redaction / awareness auth-filter use (not a channel-per-zone): fan-out, catch-up delta, and cold-start snapshot each redact by partition so an unauthorized zone's ops/state/structure/count/existence — and any clock jump (per-zone clocks) — are **wholly absent**, not redacted-but-present. Snapshot to a zone-limited subscriber is projected (`Document::project_zones`) to its authorized partitions. **Refines Unit 2:** a container-create now belongs to the created *child's* zone (`zone_of_op`), not the parent it targets — so a zone owns its root container's creation and it is hidden from an unauthorized subscriber on every seam, closing a stream↔snapshot divergence (Unit-2 clock specs updated); `MapCursor::map`/`into_map` also stop re-emitting a redundant `MapCreate` for a live map. Server-plus-one-core-frame; specs `crates/server/tests/zone_streams.rs` (9) + updated `crates/core/tests/zone_lamport.rs`; local review (4 finder angles — fixed the create divergence + the shipped-Acl gap; documented the reachable-only-projection / doc-level-op-on-stream first-cut limitations). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-14). **Zones Unit 5 (SDK subscribe surface) is DONE (#281); Unit 4 (cross-zone token) is now DONE (#PR) — the Zones epic is COMPLETE.** → *Authorization/zones*.

**Zones** — **Zones Unit 2 — per-zone lamport clocks + op-envelope `zone` dimension (core) — DONE (#276).** The core substrate for per-zone replication streams (Unit 3). `Op` gains `zone: Option<u32>` — a **compact zone id** (the target's index in the order-preserving `Schema::zones()`, `None` = the root/unzoned partition, `zone::zone_id_of`; `zone_of` now delegates to it). **Corrects the board's claim the field was "already reserved" — it was not on `Op`; this unit adds it** (pre-release op-wire/state break, no migration). The Document's lamport allocation is **partitioned**: the root rides `lamport`, declared zones ride `zone_clocks: HashMap<u32,u64>`. A local emit resolves the target's zone (`zone_of_target` walks the live tree via `element_paths`, gated on a bound non-empty `zones()` so the no-zones path never walks) and stamps from *that* partition's clock; a merge advances the **op's envelope zone** by per-partition `max` — honored from the envelope, never re-derived, so a buffered op still advances the right clock and a later stream routes by it without materializing the tree. Causally independent: an op in one zone never bumps another's, so two zones' ops are concurrent by construction (no false causal edge); within-zone causality is intact; the no-schema/no-zones path is byte-for-byte the old single global clock. Stamp keeps its `(lamport,client)` shape — uniqueness holds within a partition, which suffices because a reachable container's zone is structurally fixed and cross-zone moves/anchors are forbidden, so no two ops that could collide on a stamp-derived id ever span zones. State codec persists the per-zone clocks (STATE_VERSION 8→9); `Document::zone_clock(zone)` exposes a partition's high-water for Unit 3. Core-only; specs `crates/core/tests/zone_lamport.rs` (10: stamp-from-zone-clock, one zone doesn't bump another, two-zone concurrency, within-zone order, unzoned=root, merge advances only the op's zone, multi-zone convergence over state + clocks, snapshot round-trip, no-zones regression, zoned xml/list cursor emit locking the borrow-drop discipline) + `zone.rs` (`zone_id_of`) + `codec.rs` (zone round-trip/truncation/bad-flag); targeted + full-local Miri clean; local review (medium: a double-borrow audit confirmed the emit sites drop their container borrow before the tree walk; a stamp-collision finding refuted — cross-zone moves are forbidden by construction). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-14). **Unblocks Zones Unit 3** (server per-zone streams + zone-scoped subscribe/redaction). → *Authorization/zones*.

**Awareness** — **Awareness auth-filter — recipient-read-gated presence fan-out (server) — DONE (#252).** Closes ARCHITECTURE §Awareness "Auth-Aware Filtering" (line 850): presence fans out only to peers authorized to *read* the room, reusing the exact `registry::peer_may_read` gate the op-redaction fan-out already uses (§Per-recipient read redaction #83) — so awareness and ops agree on who may read a room, and the "see" permission is the recipient's room read authority. Every awareness fan-out is gated: an `AwarenessSet` broadcast and a per-key/actor `AwarenessClear`/`AwarenessClearKey` removal (all via the `peer_may_read`-filtered `fan_out_removals`), and a joiner's presence replay (the Subscribe `Action::Read` gate refuses an unauthorized joiner before it replays anything). Symmetric — an unauthorized peer neither receives others' presence nor is a recipient its own would leak to; a peer that loses read mid-session stops receiving further sets/clears at once (same as op fan-out). The gate landed with the schema-authz composition (#176); this unit adds the dedicated privacy spec suite locking the property in and closes the queued board item. Server-test-only diff (no `crates/core/src`, no src change → CI miri `-p crdtsync-core` unaffected); spec `crates/server/tests/awareness_authz.rs` (5: unauthorized reader refused + no set fan-out, denied joiner replays nothing / authorized joiner does, actor-clear + key-clear withheld from a revoked reader, permissive-default regression). **Zone/branch-scoped awareness filtering stays deferred** behind the Zones enforcement seam (ARCHITECTURE §854 "Branch and Zone Scoping" — a cursor in a private *zone* filtered from clients without that zone's access needs the not-yet-built zone auth seam); room-level read authority is the shipped scope. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). → *Awareness / Auth-Aware Filtering*.

**Webhooks** — **Webhooks — outbound `EventSink` over the engine event bus (server) — DONE (#253).** Realizes the §Versioning "event bus = webhook substrate" item — the event bus (#191) built for exactly this. `WebhookSink` (`crates/server/src/webhook.rs`) is the second built-in `EventSink` after `AutoVersionSink`: on each room-bearing lifecycle event it serializes a JSON body and `try_send`s it to a bounded (1024) mpsc queue that a separate spawned reqwest worker drains, POSTing **off the hot path** — `on_event` never blocks or panics the hub commit path, so a slow/failing/absent endpoint can't stall emission or the op path. **Delivery = best-effort, at-most-once, no cross-restart ordering:** a full queue drops the event, a failed POST (transport/timeout/non-2xx) is logged and never retried, a single serial worker keeps in-run FIFO. **Worker placement:** the registry actor's `current_thread` runtime has no I/O reactor, so the worker is spawned on the main multi-thread runtime in `serve_with_authorizer` (before the registry thread); the sink carries only the channel `Sender` (Send) into the registry thread to register on the hub. **Event JSON:** `{"type": "<kebab>", …}` — `version-created {room,name}` / `version-renamed {room,from,to}` / `version-deleted {room,name}` / `compacted {room,floor}` / `subscribed {room}` / `after-restore {room,branch}` / `before-publish {room,branch}`; byte names as UTF-8 (lossy for non-UTF-8, as elsewhere); roomless connect/disconnect + reserved `before-migration` filtered out (same room-bearing filter as `AutoVersionSink`). Optional shared secret in the `X-Crdtsync-Webhook-Secret` header (over TLS, never the body). **Opt-in:** `ServeConfig.webhook: Option<WebhookConfig>`, `main.rs` reads `CRDTSYNC_WEBHOOK_URL` / `CRDTSYNC_WEBHOOK_SECRET`; unset ⇒ no sink, no worker, zero per-event cost (mirrors the optionally-wired admin plane). Server adds `reqwest` (rustls TLS) + `serde`, neither reaching core. Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri non-required/unaffected); spec `crates/server/tests/webhook.rs` (4, hermetic loopback axum capture server: shape+secret header, compaction floor + no-secret-header, no-sink-no-delivery, dead-endpoint-past-capacity never blocks/panics). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). → *Versioning / Debugging (event bus = webhook substrate)*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 7 — schema-aware branch/version diff (server) — DONE (#251).** The last branch-layer unit: a diff between two branch HEADs / named-version snapshots, computed by feeding two decoded snapshots into the already-built core diff engine (6a-v `core::path::diff`) — no new diff semantics. `Hub::diff_versions(room, a, b) -> Result<Vec<Change>, DiffError>` decodes both named versions' captured `version_state` and runs `path::diff`; `Hub::diff_branches(room, a, b)` materializes each branch via the existing `materialize_branch` (Unit 3/4 shared base + tail, or a snapshot fork's owned base; `main` via `export_room`) then diffs — so a branch against its fork source yields only the divergence. A shared `diff_states` helper decodes two encoded states and runs the engine. Edge cases are clean errors, never panics: an unknown version → `DiffError::UnknownVersion(name)`, unknown branch → `DiffError::UnknownBranch(name)`, an encoded state that does not decode → `DiffError::Decode`; a snapshot against itself → empty. *(Superseded by C51: `materialize_branch` answers a four-way `BranchState`, so a branch this node holds but cannot read is `DiffError::UnreadableBranch(name)` rather than `UnknownBranch`, and `main` on a room with no replica is the empty state.)* Schema-aware = reuse the existing `Change` set + `diff.rs` renderers (text char-level / xml structural / map·register·counter·marks value diffs) unchanged — no missing renderer case surfaced. **Exposure decision:** the server-side `Hub::diff_*` computation + its test coverage is the unit; returning a diff to a *client* needs a new wire frame or an admin-plane route (the same client-wire-vs-admin question as Unit 6b), so the client/SDK query surface is deferred to **Branches Unit 7b — diff query surface** (below). Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri non-required/unaffected); spec `crates/server/tests/branch_diff.rs` (9: value/text/structural-add/list-insert version diffs, self-diff empty, unknown version/branch errors, branch-vs-fork-source divergence). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). **With Unit 7 done the branch layer is functionally complete** — the remaining branch items are the two gated exposure follow-ons (6b branch-management SDK, 7b diff query surface — both on the client-wire-vs-admin-plane decision) + cross-branch merge (out of v0.x, ARCHITECTURE line 636). → *Versioning and Branches / Schema-Aware Diff*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 6b — branch-management client-wire surface (core + server + FFI/Go/Python) — DONE (#275).** Resolves the 2026-07-14 Q2 design gate: app-facing branch ops ride **new in-band client-wire frames**, not the admin HTTP plane (that plane stays reserved for operator observability). Mirrors the Version* frame set for branches. **Core (`protocol.rs`, tags 27–33):** `BranchList { room }` → `Branches { room, branches: Vec<BranchInfo> }` (`BranchInfo { name, fork_point, head, published }` — what a client needs to enumerate + act); mutations `BranchFork { room, name, from_branch }` (forks off the source's HEAD, `fork_branch` with `at = u64::MAX` clamped to head), `BranchForkFromVersion { room, name, version }`, `BranchRestore { room, name, version }`, `BranchPublish { room, published }`, `BranchDelete { room, name }`. **Room-keyed, not channel-keyed** (unlike Version*, which rides a subscribed channel) — branch management is a room-level operation a client runs *before* it holds any subscription (e.g. list-to-pick-which-to-subscribe); the diff-query frame (7b) fits this same room/version-keyed shape. Hand-rolled codec, total decode (round-trip + truncation + bogus-count + distinct-tag tests). `ClientSession` frames each request and folds a `Branches` reply into a per-room view. **Server (`session.rs`):** each frame dispatches to the existing `Hub` branch seam, gated by the **version-management authz tier** (doc-ACL abstains, deployment+schema tiers decide — a branch op is a room-management action, no new authz axis); a read lists locally, a mutation is leader-redirected then replies with the fresh set; unauthenticated → violation, denied → recoverable `Forbidden`, a persist error → `internal`, never a panic. **SDK (full 4-way fan-out):** FFI (`crdtsync_client_list_branches`/`_fork_branch`/`_fork_branch_from_version`/`_restore_branch`/`_publish_branch`/`_delete_branch` + `_branch_count`/`_branch_at` getters — cbindgen header regenerated, symbol-assert extended), Go `(*Client).ListBranches`/`ForkBranch`/… + `Branch` struct, Python `Client.list_branches`/… + `Branch` NamedTuple, wasm `listBranches`/`forkBranch`/… + `branches` → `{name, forkPoint, head, published}[]` (`crates/wasm`, the wasm-pack standalone crate). **Core-touching (`protocol.rs`) → CI Miri gates the PR**; targeted core + FFI Miri clean locally. Specs: `crates/core/tests/protocol_branches.rs` (8), `client_branches.rs` (5), `crates/server/tests/branch_ops.rs` (9), FFI `client.rs` branch round-trip, Go/Python/wasm marshal tests. Local correctness review (medium) clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-14). → *Versioning and Branches*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 6 — branch subscribe SDK surface (FFI + Python/Go/wasm) — DONE (#250).** Forwards the already-built `ClientSession::subscribe_branch(room, branch)` (Unit 2 #244) across the four SDKs so a user can sync a specific `(room, branch)` — no new wire (Unit 2's Subscribe already carries the branch). FFI `crdtsync_client_subscribe_branch(client, room, room_len, branch, branch_len, out_channel)` mirrors `crdtsync_client_subscribe` (empty branch → default/active, via `as_slice`'s `Some(&[])`; null handle → empty-buffer sentinel, no channel written); cbindgen header regenerated + the header symbol-assert extended. Python `Client.subscribe_branch`, Go `(*Client).SubscribeBranch`, wasm `subscribeBranch` — each a faithful mirror of that layer's existing `subscribe`, default (empty branch) stays main/active so existing callers are unchanged. Spec-first per layer (named branch rides the Subscribe frame — FFI/wasm decode + assert `Message::Subscribe.branch`, Python/Go byte-substring; empty/default stays main-scoped; FFI null-handle sentinel). SDK/FFI-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri non-required/unaffected). **Branch-management ops (fork/publish/restore/list) are NOT exposed here** — they are server-only `Hub`/`Registry` methods (`crates/server/src/{store,registry,lib}.rs`) with no client wire frame and no `ClientSession` method, so client exposure needs a new wire frame or an admin-plane ruling → deferred to **Branches Unit 6b** (below). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). **Remaining branch units:** 6b (branch-management SDK — exposure-path question), 7b (diff query surface — exposure-path question); Unit 7 (schema-aware branch diff, server) is now DONE (above). → *Versioning and Branches*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 5 — publish/draft + per-user branches (server) — DONE (#249).** The draft→publish gate over the active-HEAD + branch-write machinery, closing the last reserved branch engine event. **Publish/draft:** `Hub::publish(room, published)` (`Registry::publish` wraps it to drive the auto-version drain; default target `PUBLISHED_BRANCH = b"published"`) points a **read-only `published` branch's HEAD** at the active editor branch's (`main` by default, or a restored HEAD) current materialized state — a snapshot fork owning that state as its base, so read-only consumers subscribe `(room, published)` and are served the state as it stood at the last publish while editors keep writing `main`. **Republish** repoints `published`'s base to the newer editor state; each published state is first captured as an immutable named version `publish/<published>/<source>@<seq>` (keyed on the source branch + its head, so the same head number on a switched editor branch stays a distinct rollback), so **the old published state stays reachable as a version** — apps roll published state back independently of the editor branch. **Read-only target:** a client `Ops` write to a `published` branch is refused recoverably (`OpsRejected{Forbidden}`, the same idiom as an authz denial — the author keeps its ops), enforced by a durable `Branch.published` flag; a published branch never diverges, so a stale tail under its name (a name reused from a former writable fork) is dropped on load, never folded onto the base. **Engine event:** `BeforePublish` (the last reserved branch event) now fires *before* the repoint; `AutoVersionSink` maps it to `TriggerEvent::BeforePublish`, so a schema's `autoVersion: [{on: before-publish}]` trigger captures at the publish point — the branch/migration engine-event reservations are now fully consumed on the branch side (only `BeforeMigration` remains, for the migration layer). **Per-user branches:** the same primitive — a `user/<id>` branch forked via `fork_branch`/`fork_branch_from_version` edits in isolation (Unit 3 branch-scoped write/fan-out); confirmed with test coverage proving isolation (neither user's writes reach the base or each other), no new machinery. Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri unaffected; `TriggerEvent::BeforePublish` already existed in core); specs `crates/server/tests/publish.rs` (10, incl. durable reopen + switched-branch-rollback + orphan-tail regressions), `engine_events.rs` (+before-publish fires-once). Two correctness-review agents (caught a rollback-name collision across a switched editor branch + a partial-rollback tail hazard → both fixed with regression tests). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). **Deferred:** branch SDK surface (Unit 6), schema-aware branch diff (Unit 7), cross-branch merge (out of v0.x), per-user branch *authorization* (ACL slice 4). → *Versioning and Branches*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 4b — restore-as-branch + active-HEAD switch + `AfterRestore` event (server) — DONE (#248).** Completes Branches Unit 4 over the 4a fork-from-snapshot base. **Active HEAD:** a durable per-room default-served-branch pointer (`Hub.active_branch`, `<room>.active`, absent ⇒ `main`); `session::step`'s Subscribe resolves an *empty* branch to `hub.active_branch(room)` (replacing the old empty⇒`main` normalize), while an explicit name (incl. `main`) is taken as given. The resolved branch is stored on the channel, so a channel bound *before* a restore keeps its branch — an offline op in flight against the old HEAD lands on the old branch (Unit 3 branch-scoped write), never on the restored state; a new plain subscribe *after* restore follows the restored branch. **`Hub::restore_as_branch(room, version, new_branch)`:** forks `new_branch` from the version snapshot (4a), captures an **audit version** of the pre-restore live `main` state (`audit/restore/<branch>@<seq>` — keyed on captured seq so a reused branch name still audits each restore), switches the active HEAD, and emits `EngineEvent::AfterRestore`. Rewrites no history, resets no sequence; the old branch stays immutable + subscribable by name; deleting the active branch resets the HEAD to `main`. Durable throughout → the whole switch **replays on reload** (`.active` + restored `.branches`/`.bbase` + audit `.versions`; a dangling `.active` falls back to `main`). **Engine event:** `AfterRestore` (event bus #191) now fires; `AutoVersionSink` maps it to `TriggerEvent::AfterRestore` and `Registry::restore_as_branch` drives the auto-version drain, so a schema's `autoVersion: [{on: after-restore}]` trigger **captures the restored state — closing the parked branch-trigger item**; `BeforePublish` stays reserved for Unit 5. Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri unaffected); specs `crates/server/tests/restore_as_branch.rs` (9), `auto_version.rs` (+after-restore), `engine_events.rs` (+fires-once); two correctness-review agents (a caught audit-name-collision-on-reuse bug → keyed on seq + regression test). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). → *Versioning and Branches*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 4a — fork-from-snapshot (server) — DONE (#247).** Lands the shared-base-below-a-fork-point machinery Unit 3 deferred: a branch may fork from a **named version's snapshot**, not only a live point in `main`'s log. **Base machinery:** `Hub::fork_branch_from_version(room, new, version)` records the branch pointer at `fork_point = version.seq` and copies the version's encoded whole-replica state into the branch's **owned base** — `Hub.branch_bases: room → branch → Vec<u8>` in memory, a durable `<room>_<branch>.bbase` file (state verbatim, whole-file read, room+branch hex-joined like `.blog`) — so the branch survives the source version's deletion. The presence of a base entry marks a branch a snapshot fork; a live-log fork (Unit 3) has no entry and reads `main`'s log as before. **Catch-up:** `catch_up_branch` serves the owned base ++ tail, never `main`'s post-version ops — a fresh subscriber (below `fork_point`) gets the base with the tail folded in as one `Catchup::Snapshot { seq: fork_point + tail.len() }` (decode → apply tail → encode); one already past the base gets just the tail as `Catchup::Ops`. **Lifecycle:** the `.bbase` lands before the `.branches` pointer (rolled back on a pointer-persist error), is dropped with the branch (mem + disk, so a re-fork inherits no stale base), and is reseeded from disk on reload — an orphan base whose branch is not a registered fork is pruned, so it never shadows a live-log fork reusing the name. Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri unaffected); `crates/server/tests/branch_from_snapshot.rs` (9, incl. durable reopen + orphan-base regression); two correctness-review agents (catch_up sequence/fold + store round-trip; the catch_up agent caught the orphan-base-resurrection hazard → hardened + regression test). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). **Deferred to 4b:** restore-as-branch (active-HEAD switch + audit version) + the `AfterRestore` engine event. → *Versioning and Branches*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 3 — branch-scoped subscribe + log resolution (server) — DONE (#246).** Makes a `(room, branch)` subscription actually serve that branch's stream — wiring the Unit 2 wire `branch` (#244) and the Unit 1 fork points (#245) into real branch-scoped catch-up, write, and fan-out. **Log resolution:** `Hub::catch_up_branch` composes the shared base (`main`'s log records ≤ the branch's fork point, read straight from `main`'s log — never duplicated) ++ the branch's own divergent tail past the fork; `main` (empty branch) is the whole existing log, unchanged. **Storage shape = only the divergence, per `(room, branch)`:** `Hub.branch_logs: room → branch → BranchLog { ops, seen }` holds just the tail; a durable `<room>_<branch>.blog` append-log (main-log framing, room+branch hex-joined) mirrors `.log`; branch head recomputed as `fork_point + tail.len()` on reload (a torn head/tail write never leaves head trailing the ops); a delete drops the tail (mem + disk). **Branch-scoped write:** `Hub::ingest_branch` appends to the branch tail + advances its head (never `main`'s log); `Response.broadcast_branch` scopes fan-out to `Session::channels_for_stream(room, branch)`, so a branch write reaches only that branch's subscribers and a `main` write only `main`'s. **Unknown non-main branch → refused** (`Error{UnknownRoom}`, non-closing), never silently served `main`. **Forced Unit-1 refinement:** the fork point is clamped to the source's head (forking history that doesn't exist leaves a sequence-space gap + leaks the source's later writes into the branch base — divergence); Unit-1 fork-past-head tests updated. **Deferred:** shared-base compaction below a fork point (fork-from-snapshot → Unit 4); range-check/translation + awareness/version/ACL stay main-/room-scoped. Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri unaffected); `crates/server/tests/branch_sync.rs` (11) + updated session/branches tests; targeted server Miri clean; two correctness-review agents (caught + fixed the fork-past-head divergence, added regression tests). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). → *Versioning and Branches*.

**Branches** — **Branches Unit 1 — branch registry + model (server) — DONE (#245).** First foundational unit of the branch layer (§Versioning-and-Branches). A branch is a named pointer into the op log: `Branch { name, fork_point, head }` + a per-room `BranchRegistry` defaulting to `main` (fork_point 0, head tracking the room's server-sequence log head). Pointer semantics only — `fork(new, from, at)` (shares history ≤ `at`; refuses a duplicate name / unknown source), `branch` lookup, `branches` list (deterministic name order), `rename`, `delete` (`main` never deletable or renamable). Exposed on the `Hub` (`branches`/`branch`/`fork_branch`/`rename_branch`/`delete_branch`), persist-before-commit with rollback, mirroring the named-versions surface. Durable `<room>.branches` store record: atomic write, only forks past `main` persisted (`main` synthesized on load), absent/malformed decodes to `{main}` (never a panic) — mirrors the versions index + the `.meta` record. A never-forked room materializes no branch state and no file (behaves as today). Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI miri unaffected); `crates/server/tests/branches.rs` (12); two local `/code-review` passes (caught + fixed a no-op mutation materializing a stray registry entry). Log resolution / branch-scoped subscription deferred to Unit 3. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). → *Versioning and Branches*.

**Branch layer** — **Branches Unit 2 — channel branch dimension (wire, core) — DONE (#244).** The forward-compat wire carrier for the `(room, branch)` replication unit, reserved before the server branch-scoping (Unit 3) that resolves it — mirroring how `Hello`'s `app_id`/`schema_version` and the zone envelope field were reserved on the wire first. `Message::Subscribe` gains `branch: Vec<u8>` (empty = the default `main`), length-framed beside `room` in the hand-rolled codec — placed on `Subscribe` (per-subscription), **not** `Hello` (per-connection), because a connection multiplexes several `(room, branch)` subscriptions and `branch` rides exactly where `room` does. `ClientSession::subscribe_branch(room, branch)` sends it and records it on the per-channel room view (`branch()` accessor); `subscribe(room)` stays main-scoped (empty), so existing single-arg callers are unchanged; `resume` re-sends the stored branch. The server `step` **carries and ignores** the branch (subscribes as today, empty == main) — branch-scoping the log and rooms is Unit 3. Codec round-trip (empty + non-empty), truncation-at-every-cut → `DecodeError`, trailing-bytes rejection; `tests/protocol.rs`/`protocol_channel.rs`/`client.rs` + a server carrier test; targeted Miri clean (protocol/protocol_channel/client). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). → *Networking / Versioning*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 9b-SDK — `onOpsRejected` SDK surface (FFI + Python/Go/wasm) — DONE (#240).** Forwards `ClientSession::take_rejected()` across the FFI boundary + each SDK, mirroring the `take_repairs` drain idiom and 9a's error-code surface — pure plumbing over the core+server mechanism 9b landed (#239), **no `crates/core/src` change** (CI miri unaffected/non-required). `crdtsync_client_take_rejected(client, out) -> i32` drains the session's rejected buffer into one buffer the SDK decodes: a `u32` count, then per batch the channel (`u32`), the reason discriminant (`i32`, reusing 9a's `error_code_discriminant`), and the ops (a `u32` op-count then per op a `u32`-length-prefixed `encode_op` byte string — a refused op crosses carrying its bytes for the app to show/discard/export). `1` on success (empty → a bare zero count), `-1` on a bad handle / null out; caller frees via `crdtsync_buf_free`; header regenerated. Each SDK decodes to an idiomatic list + its exported `ErrorCode`: Python `take_rejected() -> list[Rejected]` (a `Rejected` NamedTuple `channel`/`reason: ErrorCode`/`ops: list[bytes]`, both exported); Go `TakeRejected() []Rejected` (struct `Channel`/`Reason ErrorCode`/`Ops [][]byte`); wasm `takeRejected()` → array of `{ channel, reason, ops }` (`ops` an array of `Uint8Array`). Harnesses in all four languages receive an `OpsRejected` frame and assert the drain yields the batch (channel + reason + ops), an empty drain is empty, a bad handle / null out is `-1`. **Unit 9 is now mechanically complete pending the 9c design gate — 9c (producer-side reject-before-send) is the sole remaining Unit-9 slice, human-design-gated on the repair-closure question.** Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-09). → *SDK surface*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 9a — server error code surface (`onUpdateRequired` observation) — DONE (#238).** First slice of Unit 9. Surfaces the server `ErrorCode` from the client receive path across FFI + Python/Go/wasm — the carrier already existed on `Message::Error`/`ClientError::Server`, so this is pure plumbing. `crdtsync_client_receive(client, msg, msg_len, out_error_code: *mut i32) -> i32`: on a `ClientError::Server { code }` it writes the discriminant to `out_error_code` and returns 0; every other outcome (applied 1 / undecodable 0 / bad-handle -1) leaves it untouched; a null out-param skips the write. **Breaking C ABI signature change (pre-release)** — header regenerated, all callers updated. Stable discriminant mapping mirrors the wire tags (`0` ProtocolViolation … `5` Forbidden, `6` UpdateRequired = `onUpdateRequired`). Each SDK forwards it in its own idiom: Python `receive` raises `ServerError(code: ErrorCode)` (both exported); Go `Receive(msg) (int, ErrorCode)` with a `NoErrorCode` sentinel; wasm `receive` throws the exported `ErrorCode`. FFI/SDK-only diff (no `crates/core/src` change) — core already covered the mechanism (`a_server_error_surfaces_to_the_caller`); new FFI/Python/Go/wasm harnesses assert `UpdateRequired` surfaces + a normal frame still applies. `onRepaired` was already surfaced via `take_repairs`; **9b (`onOpsRejected` wire frame + server emit + client emit) landed as the core+server mechanism (#239); 9b-SDK (its FFI/Python/Go/wasm surface) + 9c (producer-side reject-before-send, needs a design look) remain.** Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6b-i — FFI xml navigation C ABI (ffi) — DONE (#229).** First SDK-binding slice of Unit 6b — the C ABI the Python/Go/wasm bindings (6c–6e) forward to. Forwards the 6a-i/ii/iii xml navigation façade over **two** surfaces: a **doc surface** `crdtsync_doc_xml_*` (ops-returning, matching `list_*`/`text_*`) — `xml_element`/`xml_fragment` (install at a map-slot path), `xml_tag` (1/0/-1 out-param; fragment reads absent), `xml_insert_element`/`xml_insert_text`/`xml_child_delete`/`xml_children_len`, `xml_move` (the sole two-path fn — parent+child_index → new_parent+dest_index, both paths null-checked); and a **client surface** `crdtsync_client_xml_*` routing authored ops through `ClientSession::enqueue_ops` so an SDK xml edit is acked/resent/drained like every other edit (the doc surface has no outbox — the enqueue guarantee is only reachable client-side). Client xml is edits-only (reads stay on the doc surface, matching the existing minimal client read surface); an inert held-channel edit frames a zero-op `Ops` frame (the atomic-tx empty-frame convention). 14 new symbols; cbindgen header regenerated + the header-symbol-assert test extended with all 14. Spec-first FFI tests (convergence, tagless fragment, children insert/delete/len, tree-move relocation, bad-handle/non-node inert, client enqueue+resend+drain); targeted `cargo +nightly miri test -p crdtsync-ffi` clean; local review (high) caught + fixed overclaiming doc-comments + a non-isolating null-handle assertion. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6c/6d/6e — Python + Go + wasm bindings for xml/marks/diff/schema — DONE (#235/#236/#234). Completes Unit 6 — the whole XmlElement SDK surface, and the XmlElement epic (Units 1–6) end to end.** Each language SDK forwards the full surface: xml navigation (element/fragment/tag/children/move), marks (author/set-value/delete/marks_at), diff (encode + the boundary decode), schema-bind + `take_repairs`. Python (ctypes) + Go (cgo) forward the 6b C ABI (`crdtsync_doc_*`/`crdtsync_client_*`); wasm (wasm-bindgen) binds `core::path` directly (workspace-excluded, its own `wasm-sdk` CI job). Uniform **doc-vs-client split** mirroring the C ABI: edits exposed on both the doc surface (return ops) and the client surface (route through the ack/resend outbox), reads on the doc surface only. Each binding **added the diff decode side** (`diff_decode`/`DiffDecode`/`decode_changes`) and **fixed a real change-decoder gap** — the Go and (per its own review) sibling change decoders only handled diff tags 0–7, so a diff over mark-bearing snapshots errored; extended with the mark-change variants (tags 8/9/10) + `elementref`, regression-tested (a caught bug → a test). K=3 concurrent fan-out (disjoint language dirs), merged on green language + workspace `test` checks (miri is core-only, unaffected by an SDK-only diff, non-required). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6b-iv — FFI schema/repair C ABI (ffi) — DONE (#233). Completes Unit 6b — the entire C ABI.** Forwards the 6a-vi schema/repair façade, doc-surface only (a schema bind is local runtime state that authors nothing → no client/enqueue surface; `take_repairs` is a read). `crdtsync_doc_set_schema(doc, ptr, len) -> i32` (total parse: 1 bound / 0 malformed-rejected / -1 bad-handle|null); `crdtsync_doc_take_repairs(doc, out) -> i32` drains the `onRepaired` signal as one length-framed buffer (`u32` count, then per path a `u32`-length-prefixed `encode_repair_path` byte string — the same single-buffer list convention as `marks_at`/the diff change-list, not an array of `CrdtBuf`; the drain reseeds the baseline so a standing repair reports once — settle-point contract preserved through the FFI); `crdtsync_repair_path_decode(bytes, len, out)` — a total boundary validator, mirroring 6b-iii's decision to expose the decode side. Header regenerated + symbol-assert extended (3 new symbols). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). **The whole C ABI (xml/marks/diff/schema) is now complete — 6c Python / 6d Go / 6e wasm bindings forward it.** → *SDK surface*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6b-iii — FFI diff C ABI (ffi) — DONE (#232).** Third slice of Unit 6b. `crdtsync_diff` already shipped with the Schema-Aware-Diff work (#113 — forwards `diff`+`encode_changes` to the encoded change list), so this slice's real gap was the boundary READ: `crdtsync_diff_decode(bytes, len, out) -> i32` — the 6a-v `decode_changes` inverse, turning opaque diff bytes back into the structured, SDK-marshalable change list (the shape 6c/6d/6e will marshal). Total on any input (`catch_unwind` + the crate's 1/0/-1 out-param idiom — truncated/garbage/empty → 0 + empty out, null out → -1, never a panic across the frame, since a diff crosses an untrusted boundary). Read-only → doc surface only (no client/enqueue). Header regenerated + symbol-assert extended; round-trip + mixed xml-attr/mark-change tests. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface / Schema-Aware Diff*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6b-ii — FFI marks C ABI (ffi) — DONE (#231).** Second slice of Unit 6b.
