Forwards the 6a-iv marks façade over the C ABI, mirroring 6b-i's doc+client split: marks authoring (`mark`/`mark_set_value`/`mark_delete` — they emit ops) rides the **client surface** (`crdtsync_client_mark_*` via `enqueue_ops` so a mark edit is acked/resent), while `marks_at` (a read) is on the **doc surface**. `Side` crosses as an int (0=left/1=right, matching `relative_position` #137), the `Scalar` value + `mark_id` (`ElementId` bytes) via the existing scalar/id crossing conventions, `marks_at` returns the variable-length resolved-marks list per the established convention. Header regenerated + symbol-assert extended. Spec-first FFI tests (author/read/change/delete, non-sequence inert, enqueue+resend+drain, bad-handle inert); targeted Miri clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface / Marks*.

**Authorization** — **Doc-level ACL slice 3b-ii — provenance-bounded deny + creator-deny immunity (core) — DONE (#230).** Final core provenance slice — completes the doc-ACL evaluator. A `Deny` overrides a grant only when its author has authority over that grant (validly owns the grant's path or an ancestor, at/above the granter — its own subtree): an out-of-authority deny by a subordinate or peer is disregarded, so a deny can't do what a revoke may not (the anti-backdoor property). The in-authority cases are preserved: slice-2 deny-overrides (a deny beats an allow by the same authority), superior carve-outs (`deny own` on a subpath binds the subtree), and "deny beats static/default grants always." The synthesized creator-owns-`/` tuple is immune to a subordinate's `Deny(Own)` (no one is above the creator). Deny-provenance folds into the 3b-i iterative least-fixpoint — a deny whose authority is itself contingent resolves fail-closed (an ambiguous deny doesn't bind), order-independent, Miri-clean on cyclic provenance. Every prior acl test stays green (the bounding refines the honored set, not a regression). `tests/acl_bounded_deny.rs`; targeted Miri clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *Authorization*.

**Authorization** — **Doc-level ACL slice 3b-i — attenuated recursive delegation chain (core) — DONE (#228).** Makes authority a recursive attenuated walk up the grant chain to the creator root, closing the hole 3a deferred (3a checked revoke-authority ownership as-present, so a revoked owner could still authorize a revoke). `decide_capability_with_authority` now **roots** every ALLOW tuple: a grant confers authority only if its grantor validly owned the grant's path (or an ancestor), recursively up to the creator (the un-granted root who owns every path; the synthesized creator-owns-`/` needs no grantor); an unrooted grant is inert. **Attenuation**: only ownership confers granting power (a reader can't delegate), and a grant can't exceed the granter's scope (no `Own` above what the granter owns). Ownership resolved from actor-id `Own` grants only (3a's fail-closed rule — `Group`/class confers nothing). Rooting + revocation are mutually recursive (a revoke's authority is an ownership; an ownership is a rooted-unrevoked grant), solved as an **iterative least-fixpoint** — each pass recomputes `rooted`/`revoked` from the prior snapshot, order-independent, no native recursion (a crafted cycle can't stack-overflow), pass count bounded `2N+2`, fail-closed on oscillation (an ambiguous/forged-cycle revoke never strips a grant; a forged chain roots at no creator → confers nothing). Rooting gates only ALLOW grants — a `Deny` stays as-present (applying rooting to denies would fail-open; deny provenance is 3b-ii). `tests/acl_delegation.rs` (13); targeted Miri clean (cyclic provenance). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *Authorization*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6a-vi — path façade schema-bind + repair surface (core) — DONE (#227).** Sixth + final core slice of Unit 6's `core::path` façade. `set_schema(doc, schema_bytes) -> bool` parses schema bytes (total-decode; malformed → `false`, never a panic) and binds the opt-in `Schema` via `Document::set_schema`; `take_repairs(doc) -> Vec<Vec<u8>>` surfaces `Document::take_repairs` — the `onRepaired` located paths whose repaired reading newly changed, each encoded via `encode_repair_path` (a `Step` list → the façade's path encoding, `parse_repair_path` the inverse for the SDK to decode back). The repaired *value* is observed via a normal `path::get_*` read (repair applies at read, per Schema Unit 2b) — no redundant value accessor. Settle-point contract preserved (no spurious repair reports; empty with no schema bound). Thin façade — no repair/validate engine change. `tests/path_schema.rs` (11); targeted Miri clean; local review quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). **Completes the 6a `core::path` façade** — attrs (i) / children (ii) / move (iii) / marks (iv) / diff (v) / schema-repair (vi), the whole path-addressed xml/marks/repair/diff surface the four SDK bindings (6b–6e) forward to. → *SDK surface*.

**Authorization** — **Doc-level ACL slice 3a — provenance foundation (creator-owns + provenance revocation, core) — DONE (#226).** Adds the authority layer's foundation on slices 1 (tuples) + 2 (as-present evaluator). **creator-auto-owns-`/`**: the doc creator implicitly holds `Own` at root (and every capability everywhere by the own-implies lattice) with no explicit tuple — a synthesized root-owning tuple folded into the decision. **Provenance-based revocation**: a revoke tombstone is honored only when its author is the revoked grant's `grantor`, the creator, or an actor-id `Own`-owner of the grant's path; an unauthorized revoke is disregarded and the grant stays effective. Evaluation-layer only — the set still tombstones every revoke content-neutrally (no merge change), authority is decided over the merged view via `evaluate_with_authority`/`acl_records()` (never the raw `acl_tuples()`). `AclRecord{tuple, revoked_by}` records the grow-only union set of revokers; a tombstone is honored iff any recorded revoker was authorized. Creator id is a caller-supplied `ClientId` param to the authority evaluator (a property of the document, not the querying `AclActor`; core hosts no identity provider). Revoke authority is actor-id-scoped — class/`Group`/`Authenticated`/`Anyone` `Own` confers no revoke power (an arbitrary actor must not inherit it), fails closed (regression-tested). `tests/acl_provenance.rs` (15); targeted Miri clean; local review caught + fixed a real hole (a class `Own` grant let any actor's revoke count as authorized). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *Authorization*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6a-v — path façade diff surface (core) — DONE (#225).** Fifth slice of Unit 6. Exposes the snapshot-diff read model over `core::path` for the SDK bindings. `diff(old, new) -> Vec<Change>` (structured), `diff_encoded(old, new) -> Vec<u8>` (diff + `encode_changes` in one call — the opaque bytes the FFI/wasm boundary carries), `decode_changes(bytes) -> Result<Vec<Change>, DecodeError>` (the total-decode inverse the SDK side reads a diff back with — truncation/garbage → a typed error, never a panic, since a diff crosses an untrusted boundary). Thin façade over the existing `diff.rs` engine (`diff`/`encode_changes` + the `Change` tree+mark variants) — no detection/semantics change. Round-trips every `Change` variant (tree + mark) through encode→decode. `tests/path_diff.rs` (+ suite); targeted Miri clean; local review quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface / Schema-Aware Diff*.

**Authorization** — **Doc-level ACL slice 2 — the evaluator + effective-roles (core) — DONE (#224).** The pure decision function over the slice-1 tuple set (#219). `decide_capability(tuples, actor, path, cap) -> AclDecision{Allow|Deny|Abstain}` is the composable tier seam; `evaluate(...) -> bool` the total deny-by-default entrypoint; `effective_roles(tuples, actor, path)` = token roles ∪ per-doc assignments (to the actor or its groups, deny-overrides, path-prefix inheritance). `AclActor{id,groups,roles,authenticated}` — the caller supplies membership (core hosts no identity provider, mirroring how slice 2 keeps identity out of core). **Deny-overrides** (an explicit deny of a cap beats any allow on the same or a broader path; a broader deny is a hard floor a deeper allow can't re-open → path specificity is not a tiebreaker); **default deny-unless-granted** (empty/no governing tuple → Abstain at the tier so the schema `@auth` tier composes above before terminal default-deny); **`Own` implies Read/Write/PublishAwareness** over its subtree, deny capability-separated (`Deny(Own)` strips ownership only, `Deny(Read)` blocks reads even for an owner); **roles are grants, not subjects** (an `AclGrant::Role` is a per-doc role *assignment*; turning roles into capabilities is the `@auth` server tier — out of this slice — so no role→role edges, resolution is a single non-recursive pass, no cycle). Reads tuples as present — no authority/provenance check (slice 3). Landed in CORE (the evaluator is pure); the server enforcement seam (path-carrying `Resource`, decision-flow step 4) consumes `decide_capability`/`effective_roles` in the wiring slice. `tests/acl_eval.rs` (20); targeted Miri clean; local review quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *Authorization*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6a-iv — path façade marks surface (core) — DONE (#223).** Fourth slice of Unit 6 (SDK surface). Exposes value marks over the `core::path` façade. `mark(doc, seq_path, start_index, start_side, end_index, end_side, name, value) -> (Vec<Op>, Option<mark_id_bytes>)` authors a mark over a span on the sequence (Text/List) at `seq_path`, both endpoints captured as `RelativePosition` anchors (reusing the 6a-i/#137 `relative_position` resolution), returning the emitted ops + the mark's `ElementId` as the byte handle. `mark_set_value(doc, mark_id, value)` / `mark_delete(doc, mark_id)` operate by that handle; `marks_at(doc, seq_path, index) -> Vec<ResolvedMark>` is the read model over `Document::marks_at`. All inert (empty ops, `None` id) on a non-sequence path or an anchor that can't resolve. The mark id crosses the SDK boundary as `ElementId` bytes, mirroring other ids. Cut: single-sequence spans only (start+end on one seq) — cross-element mark spans stay deferred (4b-ii); schema-bind/`take_repairs` split to 6a-vi. `tests/path_marks.rs` (+ suite); targeted Miri clean; local review quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface / Marks*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6a-iii — path façade exposes xml move (core) — DONE (#221).** Third slice of Unit 6 (SDK surface). Adds `xml_move` over the `core::path` façade: the mover element and its destination parent are named by path, the insert index within the parent's children List by param. Resolves both element ids by path (`XmlCursor::id`) and forwards to the core `move_xml` tree-move (the Kleppmann lamport-ordered parent-edge log, XML Unit 2). Inert (empty ops) on a non-element mover or destination, a fragment destination that can't hold the mover, or a cycle-forming move that the core move-log would reject. `tests/path_xml.rs` extended; targeted Miri clean; local review quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *SDK surface / CRDT Model*.

**Zones** — **Zones Unit 1b-i — zone-membership resolution + cross-zone detection (core) — DONE (#222).** Pure resolver over the parsed `zones` block (1a #218): `zone::zone_of(schema, path_keys) -> Option<&str>` maps a path to its innermost governing zone by longest-prefix match (zone root strings split segment-wise, `/` = doc root), `same_zone`/`crosses_zones` answer whether two paths sit in one zone. Nesting allowed (innermost wins); an unzoned path counts distinct from every zone (and from other unzoned paths for crossing purposes — a move out of a zone into unzoned space crosses). Detection only — no enforcement wiring (that's 1b-ii). Pure `&Schema`, total, order-independent; own test suite; Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *Authorization/zones*.

**Zones** — **Server element-context index + Zones Unit 1b-ii-b — cross-zone move rejection (server) — DONE (#PR).** The foundational id→context seam + its first consumer. **The index:** a lean derived `id → {path, zone, (later) declared type}` resolution over the room's *already-materialized* document projection — `index::element_paths(doc)` (reusing the read-redaction walk) + `zone::zone_of` — **not** a hand-maintained parallel structure. The design gate (human, 2026-07-14) asked for a lean index over "not a full Document"; the premise was counterfactual (`Hub` already folds every op into `room.doc`), so the lean answer is to derive from that one authoritative tree rather than maintain a second that must replicate its buffering/refold/LWW/subtree-delete semantics (a first incremental cut drifted on all four — three review agents converged, one case failing *open*). **The consumer (1b-ii-b):** a cross-zone tree move is not statically detectable from the post-move tree, so `Hub::batch_crosses_zone` **simulates** the op batch on an independent doc copy (`decode_state(&encode_state())`) and flags any `XmlMove` whose mover's zone changes before→after (a mover created/deleted in-batch is not a crossing — "absent" ≠ "unzoned"). Refused at the **op-submit gate** (`session.rs`, `OpsRejected{Forbidden}`, the doc-ACL-denial idiom) so the op never enters the log and replicas converge on its absence; main-branch-gated. Server-only (zero `crates/core/src`). `tests/element_index.rs` + `tests/zone_move.rs`; targeted Miri clean; local review (drove the derive-from-doc pivot + an absent-vs-unzoned false-positive fix). Unblocks Zones Unit 2/3 + migration Unit 8 type-scope/container-field. See DECISIONS (2026-07-14). → *Authorization/zones*.

**Zones** — **Zones Unit 1b-ii-a — cross-zone anchor rejection (core) — DONE (#237).** Wires `crosses_zones` (1b-i #222) into the read-time `validate()` pass: a `RangedElement` (mark/comment/highlight — all conventions over the one ranged primitive) whose two anchor endpoints resolve to different zones is flagged `ViolationKind::CrossZoneAnchor { id }` and repaired as `RepairKind::Dropped` (reads absent), mirroring the `DisallowedChild`→`Dropped` arc. A pure additive static check over `doc.ranged_elements()` — no op-reject (a CRDT can't reject at merge), no new seam, fits the confirmed read-time-normalization model. The move half (1b-ii-b) is gated on the op-apply seam. `tests/zone_validate.rs`; targeted Miri clean; local review quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-08). → *Authorization/zones*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6a-ii — path façade addresses xml children (core) — DONE (#220).** Second slice of Unit 6 (SDK surface). Extends the `core::path` façade (6a-i #217) to address an `XmlElement`'s / `XmlFragment`'s children sequence — the element/fragment is named by a path, the child by an index within its children List (mirroring `list_insert`). New path fns: `xml_insert_element(doc,elem_path,index,tag)` inserts a nested element child, `xml_insert_text(doc,elem_path,index,s)` inserts a text-run child filled in the same transaction, `xml_child_delete(doc,elem_path,index)` tombstones the child at a live index, `xml_children_len(doc,elem_path)` reads the live child count. All inert on a non-element/fragment target; delete additionally inert (no op, no materialise) on an out-of-range index (the `list_delete` `slot_ok` no-materialise guard). Both XmlElement and XmlFragment children handled. The write side resolves the element from its holding map via a new `MapCursor::xml_children(key)` that yields an `XmlChildrenCursor` over an existing element/fragment slot's children (mirrors 6a-i's `xml_attrs_id` seam). Cut: children are index-addressed, a fresh child has no stable path key → grandchild navigation (editing a child's attrs/children by path) stays deferred (5d-i). `tests/path_xml.rs` (+19, 35 total); targeted Miri clean (path/path_xml/xml_children/xml_doc); local review quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *SDK surface / CRDT Model*.

**SDK surface** — **Unit 6a-i — path façade descends into XmlElement attrs (core) — DONE (#217).** First slice of Unit 6 (SDK surface). Teaches the stateless `core::path` façade — the navigation every language binding forwards to — to address an `XmlElement`'s attrs, so 6b–6e can reach xml over the same path-addressed surface. `resolve_map` (read) + a contextual write descent (`MapCursor::child`/`into_child` branching on `xml_attrs_id`) descend a further path key into an element's attrs Map (an element's attrs are an ordinary keyed Map), so the existing `register`/`get_int`/`set_bytes`/`delete`/… fns address an attr by naming the element then the attr key — no attr-specific op. New `xml_element(doc,path,tag)` / `xml_fragment(doc,path)` create at a map slot; `xml_tag(doc,path)` reads the tag (`None` for a fragment or non-element). A fragment has no attrs → a read-only `writable` pre-check zero-ops a dead-end write at any depth, preserving the emptiness-as-effect contract a caller broadcasts off. Children/move/marks/diff are later slices. `tests/path_xml.rs` (16); targeted Miri clean (path/path_xml/xml_doc/state_doc); two local `/code-review` passes (caught + fixed a nested-fragment emptiness regression the single-walk refactor introduced). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *SDK surface / CRDT Model*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5d-ii — marks value diff (core) — DONE (#216). Completes Unit 5d and Unit 5 (XmlElement/marks schema + repair + diff).** The annotation half of schema-aware diff — mark value diffs over the document RangedElement set, a dimension separate from the element tree 5d-i (#215) covered. Three path-less `Change` variants — `MarkAdded { id, seq, name, value }`, `MarkRemoved { id, seq, name, value }`, `MarkChanged { id, seq, name, old, new }` — since a mark annotates a sequence by anchors, not a tree slot, so it can't reuse a path-addressed variant. `diff_marks` runs after the tree walk (`diff()` still returns one `Vec<Change>`): a **merge-join** over the two id-sorted `ranged_elements()` slices — new-not-old = added, old-not-new = removed (value/seq/name pulled from the OLD snapshot, since it's gone from new), in-both with a changed scalar = changed. Deterministic, total-ordered by `id.as_bytes()` independent of iteration order (both inputs are id-sorted). `mark_views` filters to scalar-payload marks with `name.is_some()` — a composite-payload (object) mark is silently skipped (unconstructible via the public `mark()` API, which takes `Scalar` only — a correct deferral, not a drop of a reachable mark); a `name: None` composite annotation is not a mark and excluded. Codec tags 8/9/10 round-trip exactly (id + seq 16 bytes, name via `put_bytes`, scalars) and decode stays total on truncation (mark variant in the truncation suite). render: `+ mark <name>: <v>` / `- mark <name>: <v>` / `~ mark <name>: <old> -> <new>`; wasm `change_to_js` gains `markAdd`/`markRemove`/`markChange` arms. `tests/diff.rs` +6 (added / removed / value-change / silent / follow-tree-changes / deterministic), `tests/diff_codec.rs` (3 variants in round-trip + truncation), `tests/diff_render.rs` (render lines); targeted Miri clean (diff/codec/render/property/marks/ranged); local `/code-review` quiet. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *Schema-Aware Diff / Marks*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5d-i — XmlElement/XmlFragment structural diff (core) — DONE (#215).** The tree half of schema-aware diff (§Schema-Aware-Diff). `diff_elem` gains `(XmlElement, XmlElement)` / `(XmlFragment, XmlFragment)` arms: an XmlElement diffs as its attrs (a keyed Map → `diff_map` value diffs at the attr key path) then its children (a sequence → `diff_list` structural `ListInsert`/`ListDelete`, `SeqItem::Composite`); a fragment as its children alone; a changed tag is a replace (`Removed`+`Added`), since the tag is part of the element's identity. Fixes a latent `unreachable!` panic — two XmlElements fell through to `scalar_of`, so diffing any xml doc panicked. No new `Change` variant, no path-model change. Sequence-composite **survivor recursion** (recursing into a surviving child whose contents changed, + the index-stepped path aligning with `validate::Step`) is DEFERRED: unreachable via the current edit API (no in-place xml-child cursor; plain lists hold only immutable scalars), so no drivable state to diff/test — it lands with the xml-child editing unit. `tests/diff.rs` +13; targeted Miri clean (diff/codec/render/property). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *Schema-Aware Diff*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5c-iii-b — per-type cardinality drop repair (core) — DONE (#214).** Enforces the `children.<T>.max` cap parsed in 5c-iii-a — the "exclusive collision → keep lamport-oldest" rule, completing 5c-iii and the 5c structural-repair arc. `queue_xml_children` counts each element's children resolving to a capped type; the lamport-newest instances beyond `max` are reported at their sequence position as `ViolationKind::ExcessChild { max }`, keeping the oldest (child `List` `node_ids`, total-ordered by `(lamport, client)` — same drop-newest order a sequence `max` uses, so replicas converge; `max` 0 drops all). `ExcessChild` reuses the drop path (`RepairKind::Dropped` + `RepairId::Drop { path }`, joining the `DisallowedChild` arm); an excess child is not descended, and only children resolving to an allowed type participate in a cap. Stamp walk paid only when a child type declares a cap. `tests/xml_schema.rs` +8. Read-only, deterministic, Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *Schema / CRDT Model / Invariant Repair*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5c-iii-a — per-child-type `max` parse (core) — DONE (#213).** The schema surface for the exclusivity repair (5c-iii-b). ARCHITECTURE pins "exclusive collision" as `children.<T>.max` (a per-child-type cardinality cap), so 5a's `children` array (`Vec<String>`, `["Span"]`) becomes an **object** (`{ "Span": {}, "Heading": { "max": 1 } }`) → `TypeDef::Xml.children: Vec<(String, Option<u64>)>`. New `parse_xml_children` (object iteration, per-child `reject_unknown_fields` allowing only `max`, negative `max` → `BadRange`); `marks` stays an array. `resolve_child_type`/`queue_xml_children`/`validate_references` iterate `(name, _)`, otherwise unchanged; the `max` is stored but no enforcement reads it (5c-iii-b). All xml test schemas (`schema`/`xml_schema`/`xml_marks`) converted array→object; `tests/schema.rs` +3. Broke the schema format (pre-release). Parse-only, Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *Schema / CRDT Model*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5c-ii — orphan-inline wrap (core) — DONE (#212).** Second structural repair (§Invariant Repair), the one **element-creating** one. Loose inline text under a type declaring `repair.orphanInline` reads wrapped in a synthesized block of that type: new `ViolationKind::OrphanInline { block }` (validate splits the 5c-i "unresolved child" branch — an unresolved *text* child under an orphan-inline type is an orphan, everything else stays `DisallowedChild`) → new `RepairKind::Wrapped { block, id }`. The wrap is *described*, not materialized — core's repair layer is observation-only for every kind (`repairs()` hands the SDK descriptions to apply), so the element-creating repair fits the path-based model as a description carrying the **derived** wrapper id, exactly as `Truncated` carries `keep` indices. `id = ElementId::derive(orphan.id(), block, XmlElement)` — the orphan's own (position-independent, stamp-seeded) id, so the wrapper id is deterministic across replicas and a later op can target it; `RepairId::Wrap { id }` keys onRepaired by that id alone, immune to the `Step::Index` churn `Drop`/`Clamp`/`Keep` still carry. `tests/xml_schema.rs` +3 (reported, reads-wrapped-in-derived-block, deterministic-id). Read-only, Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *Schema / Invariant Repair*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5c-i — disallowed-child drop (core) — DONE (#211).** First of the three structural repairs (§Invariant Repair). An xml child whose tag matches no allowed child type — 5b-i's `resolve_child_type` → `None`, which used to be silently not-descended — is now a `ViolationKind::DisallowedChild` reported at its sequence position (`Work::Report`, tree order) and dropped from the read (`RepairKind::Dropped` + `RepairId::Drop`). Reuses the 5b-i attr-drop path whole: no new `RepairKind`, no element resolution (a drop reads nothing), the existing `element_at` xml `Step::Index` traversal covers it; the child is still not descended, so its subtree drops with it. Shared by XmlElement + XmlFragment children (one `queue_xml_children`). `tests/xml_schema.rs` +5 (report-at-position, nested-through-recursion, conforming-clean, sequence-order, reads-dropped). Read-only, deterministic, Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *Schema / Invariant Repair*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5b-ii — mark-on-disallowed-type filter (core) — DONE (#210).** Marks read active only when the containing XmlElement's declared type allows them. A read-time filter in `Document::marks_at` — a covering mark whose name is outside the enclosing element type's `marks` allowlist is dropped from the read; the `RangedElement` stays in state and raw reads (`ranged_on`) are unfiltered. No new `ViolationKind`/`RepairKind`: a mark is a whole-annotation targeting root, not an element at a tree path, so the path-based Violation/Repair model (5b-i) can't key an `onRepaired` observation on it → the drop is a `continue`, symmetric with the existing boolean-off omission. New `validate::marks_allowlist(doc, schema, seq)` resolves the allowlist by the *same contextual top-down walk* `validate` uses (map slot→child type, list→`items`, xml child→tag-matched `resolve_child_type`) and returns the `marks` of the XmlElement directly holding `seq`, or `None` when there's no XmlElement container — a map-slot text, a plain list, or a **tagless fragment** all keep every mark (fragments carry no mark concept). Resolved lazily, at most once per call (depends on `seq`, not `index`; a mark-free set skips the walk). `resolve_child_type`/`expected_kind` promoted to `pub(crate)`. High `/code-review`: pinned keep-all under a disallowed-tag element (deferred to 5c) with a regression, folded the per-index re-walk into the deferred batch `marks_on(seq)`, kept the walk-skeleton duplication (divergence-prone logic already shared) pending 5c's contextual resolver. `tests/xml_marks.rs` (7). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-07). → *Schema / Marks / Invariant Repair*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5b-i — attrs schema enforcement (core) — DONE (#209).** The validate/repair engine descends xml subtrees (5a left it inert). New `(TypeDef::Xml, XmlElement)`/`(_, XmlFragment)` validator arms: an element's attrs Map checked against its type's `attrs` allowlist (disallowed key → new `ViolationKind::DisallowedAttr`; wrong-kind value → `MistypedAttr { expected, found }`, checked before recursing so it never becomes a generic `KindMismatch`; a right-kind value recurses to its declared attr type → existing bounds rule), children resolved by tag against `children` (`resolve_child_type`) and recursed so nested attrs are reached (disallowed-child *drop* is 5c). New `RepairKind::Dropped` + `RepairId::Drop` — a disallowed/mistyped attr reads absent; an out-of-range attr value reuses `Clamped`. The contextual element→type binding comes from the walk (no id→type registry). Review-caught + fixed: `element_at` now traverses xml so a `TooLong` nested in xml gets a repair (closure break), and attr violations route through a `Work::Report` stack item so they emit in sorted tree order. Known follow-up (pre-existing, not 5b-i): a sequence-positioned repair (an xml child, or a bounded register list item) carries a `Step::Index` in its `RepairId` that can churn `onRepaired` — needs a node-stamp key. `tests/xml_schema.rs` (12); review-clean, Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-06). → *Schema / Invariant Repair*.

**XmlElement/marks** — **Unit 5a — schema XmlElement/XmlFragment type parse (core) — DONE (#208).** The type kind the schema layer (Units 1–8) never parsed — every Unit 5 enforcement rule (§Schema repair table) needs an xml type to read from. `TypeDef::Xml { tag: Option<String>, children: Vec<String>, attrs: Vec<(String, String)>, marks: Vec<String>, orphan_inline: Option<String> }`; `kind: "xml"` (element, `tag` required, discriminates `Some` → XmlElement) / `kind: "fragment"` (tagless root, `None` → XmlFragment, no `attrs`/`marks`). `children` = allowlist of child type names (array — several types, unlike List's single `items`), `attrs` = key→value type, `marks` = allowlist of mark names, `repair.orphanInline` = default block type (nested under `repair` for namespace room). Reference closure extends: an xml type's child/attr/orphan types must be declared (`UnknownTypeRef`), its `marks` must be in the top-level `marks` block (new `UnknownMarkRef`). `validate::expected_kind` gains the two xml kinds; parse-only, enforcement-inert (no xml constraint checked, no recursion into xml subtrees — 5b/5c). Total-decode (`hostile_inputs_never_panic`), review clean, Miri-clean. `tests/schema.rs` +17. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-06). First slice of Unit 5. → *Schema / CRDT Model*.

**Marks** — **Unit 4b — the mark read model (core) — DONE (#207). Completes Unit 4 (marks) + the XmlElement/RangedElement/marks epic.** Marks are a read-time convention over the RangedElement set — no new op, no per-character storage. A mark is a named RangedElement: `name: Option<Vec<u8>>` on `RangedCreate` / `RangedElement` / the `RangedEntry` state record (None = plain 3a/3b annotation); the value rides the scalar payload, whose `payload_stamp` is the LWW clock. `Document::marks_at(seq, index) -> Vec<ResolvedMark>` gathers every live same-named range covering the char and combines per the schema `MarkFlavor`: **boolean** → highest-`payload_stamp` presence (LWW), **value** → highest-`payload_stamp` value, **object** (also the default for an unschema'd / non-UTF8 name) → the id-sorted set of covering instances; a boolean that resolves to *off* is omitted (the read is the *active* marks). Span coverage is `resolve(start) <= index < resolve(end)`, same-sequence, via a presence-aware `List/Text::resolve_position_present` that yields `None` (not a clamped boundary) when an anchor codepoint hasn't arrived — so a mark applied before its span's inserts covers nothing until they arrive (accept-and-store), rather than painting the whole current text. Anchor gravity (Before/After) IS the expansion. `RangedCursor::mark(name, start, end, value)`; `core::marks::{MarkState, ResolvedMark}`. `STATE_VERSION` 6→7 (name in the ranged record, `put_opt_bytes`/`opt_bytes`). `tests/marks.rs` (12): cover, LWW, object-coexist, grow/no-grow boundary, insert-shift, snapshot, 64-seed fuzz, + the absent-anchor & removed-boolean regressions. Two high `/code-review` passes: fixed the absent-anchor whole-text-painting bug + the active-set filter (both with regressions), rebutted schema-not-persisted (runtime binding). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-06). Deferred (4b-ii / Unit 5): cross-element span; object composite-body ergonomics; a batch `marks_on(seq)` (per-index resolve is O(N·M·seq) at render). → *Marks*.

**Marks** — **Unit 4a — schema `marks` parse (core) — DONE (#206).** Models the top-level `marks` block (was a declared-but-ignored `TOP_LEVEL_KEY`). `core::schema` `MarkDef { flavor: MarkFlavor(Boolean|Value|Object), expand: MarkExpand(None|Before|After|Both) }`; per mark name a required `flavor` (boolean = LWW-by-stamp presence, value = LWW value, object = independent instances) + optional `expand` (the gravity of the range's anchors, default None). `Schema.marks: Vec<(name, MarkDef)>` (declaration order) + `marks()`/`mark(name)`; `parse_marks` mirrors `parse_awareness`, total-decode (new `UnknownFlavor`/`UnknownExpand` kinds, in `hostile_inputs_never_panic`). Mark value-shape deferred (like grant value-shape); per-type marks allowlist out of scope. `tests/schema.rs` marks block; Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-06). First slice of Unit 4; 4b = the read model. → *Marks / Schema*.

**RangedElement** — **Unit 3b — composite (Element) payload (core) — DONE (#205).** A RangedElement's payload generalizes from a leaf `Scalar` (3a) to a nested container (Map / List / Text), edited through the ordinary container ops. `op::RangedInit { Scalar | Composite(ElementKind) }` on `RangedCreate`; a composite payload installs at `payload_id = derive(range_id, "payload", kind)`, parent = the range, in the existing maps/lists/texts registries + retention (reuses `registered_handle`). A RangedElement is a **virtual container** in the reachability model: `displaced_container` reports a *materialised* range (tombstoned or not) as installed, `resolvable` treats its parent as root. **Delete-wins is a read-layer filter** (`ranged_element`/`ranged_elements`/`ranged_on`/`ranged_payload` all `.filter(!tombstone)`), not a reachability break — so a peer payload edit racing a delete applies to the retained-hidden container instead of buffering forever (a permanent buffer would leak + desync the snapshot, since `encode_state` serializes the buffer); `mark_displaced` seeds every materialised range's payload id. `RangedPayload { Scalar | Composite { id, kind } }` read view (+ `RangedElement::scalar()`), `Document::ranged_payload(id)` handle, `RangedCursor::create_map/list/text` + `payload_map/list/text` (gated on a live range of the matching kind); `set_payload` is scalar-only (a composite set would be an inert op). `STATE_VERSION` 5→6 (tagged payload record; a composite's data rides the container registries, decode rejects a composite entry whose container is absent). `creates_container()` = true for a composite create. `tests/ranged_payload.rs` (12); Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-06). **Unit 3 (RangedElement primitive) complete; unblocks Unit 4 (marks).** → *CRDT Model / Marks*.

**RangedElement** — **Unit 3a — annotation-set spine (core) — DONE (#204).** The document-level CRDT set of generic ranged annotations: `core::ranged` `RangeAnchor { seq, pos: RelativePosition }` (endpoints may name different sequences — a range spans elements) + `RangedElement { id, start, end, payload: Scalar }`, held on `Document` keyed by RangedElement id. Ops `RangedCreate`/`RangedSetPayload`/`RangedDelete` (codec tags 16–18) via `tx.ranged().create/set_payload/delete`; concurrent creates union to distinct ids (id derives from the op stamp under a fixed `crdtsync\0ranged\0` namespace), payload LWW-by-stamp, delete wins over a concurrent payload change (tombstone retained). A create is accept-and-store (opaque anchor, no dependency); set-payload/delete buffer until the create (single-op `ready` + `tx_group_ready`), and a local set/delete on an unseen id emits nothing (mirrors `move_xml`, so the author can't diverge). `STATE_VERSION` 5 persists the set (id-sorted, tombstones incl.) between the tree-move placements and the parent links; `ranged_element`/`ranged_elements`/`ranged_on` queries. `tests/ranged.rs` (11); Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-06). 3b generalizes the payload to a composite Element. → *CRDT Model / Marks*.

**XmlElement / XmlFragment tree move** — **Unit 2 (2a/#201, 2b/#202, 2c/#203) — DONE. Completes XmlElement Unit 2.** Kleppmann-2021 replicated tree move over the built children List + registries: a lamport-ordered move log (`core::treemove::TreeMoves`, undo-and-replay on out-of-order arrival, one-parent / no-cycle / no-dup / deterministic) decides which of a node's Fugue placements renders; a move *adds* a placement in the destination list and a reversible per-node `moved_away` fold suppresses the rest, so identity + subtree ride along free. `OpKind::XmlMove` (codec tag 15); delete wins over a concurrent move; a static base-parent map (from the creates) lets cycle detection see the whole tree; `MapCursor::move_xml` + `XmlCursor::id`. **2c — state codec (#203):** `STATE_VERSION` 4 persists the move log + only the placements it can't rebuild (a moved node, or one with a tombstoned placement); a single-live-placement node is reconstructed from its live children-list node, so a move-free XML snapshot carries no placement records. `moved_away` + the base map are re-derived, not encoded; decode re-runs `reject_parent_cycles` after replay so a crafted snapshot that folds into a cycle is rejected rather than hanging a later walk. `tests/treemove.rs` (10) + `tests/xml_move.rs` (14, incl. three snapshot round-trips) + an in-crate cycle-rejection regression; Miri-clean. Design + integration in DECISIONS (2026-07-06). → *CRDT Model*.

**XmlElement / XmlFragment** — **Unit 1d — state codec, round-trip an XML tree (core) — DONE (#200). Completes XmlElement Unit 1 (1a–1d).** An XML tree round-trips through `Document::encode_state`/`decode_state`, replacing the `unreachable!` asserts 1a/1b left in the map-slot and sequence-node encode paths. Sequence node values are `ElementKind`-tag-prefixed (scalar inlined tag 0; composite a kind-tagged id-ref, tags 2..=7), so a children List encodes `XmlElement`/`Text` nodes; bare `List`/`Text` reject composite refs. `List::decode_state_from -> (List, Vec<NodeRef>)` makes list decode two-phase like Map: `read_state` builds every shell (counters, list shells+refs, texts, map shells, `xml_elements`/`xml_fragments` registries) then resolves map slots + sequence-node refs via the shared `resolve_ref` (XML arms + registries). An element's attrs Map / children List ride the map/list registries under derived ids; the decoded element shares those handles (`from_registry`). Displacement stays **per-slot**: `mark_displaced` installs any container some map/list holds live now, independent of that parent's reachability, then propagates to an installed XML node's halves — a nested child of a displaced ancestor keeps its flag clear, so re-winning the ancestor restores the subtree (regression `a_nested_container_under_a_displaced_one_reconverges_after_a_snapshot`). `STATE_VERSION` 2→3 (format break, pre-release, no migration). XML in `state_doc`/`durability_fuzz` random_edit; `state_doc` (11); Miri-clean. Review-caught: a reachability-walk displacement bug (reverted to the flat per-slot scan + regression) + a version-history comment. → *CRDT Model*.

**XmlElement / XmlFragment** — **Unit 1c — children sequence (composite nodes) + text runs (core) — DONE (#199).** `OpKind::XmlInsertChild { tag: Option<Vec<u8>>, anchor }` (codec tag 14) inserts a nested `XmlElement` child (`Some(tag)`) or `Text` run (`None`) into a children List; the child's element id derives from the op stamp (`xml_child_id(list_id, stamp, kind)` — one helper across apply / readiness gate / cursor), so every replica builds the same child and `insert_at` is idempotent on the stamp. Deletion reuses `ListDelete` on the same children List. `insert_xml_child` materialises the child (+ its sub-containers) via the existing `registered_handle`; `tx_group_ready` counts the child's targetable ids so an atomic insert-then-edit commits; `emit_stamped` returns the minted stamp so `XmlChildrenCursor` (from `XmlCursor::children()`/`XmlFragmentCursor::children()`) hands back a cursor over the freshly inserted child. `insert_element`/`insert_text` bail without emitting when the children List is absent (matches sibling sequence mutators — never broadcast an op the author didn't apply). `List::merge` already recurses into same-kind composite node values, so nested trees converge under state merge too. Convergence-tested over nested trees; `tests/xml_children.rs` (9); Miri-clean. Review-caught: an emit-on-absent-list asymmetry (fixed + guarded). → *CRDT Model*.

**XmlElement / XmlFragment** — **Unit 1b — create + attrs through ops (core) — DONE (#198).** `OpKind::XmlElementCreate { key, tag }` / `XmlFragmentCreate { key }` (codec tags 12/13; both `creates_container()`) install an XML node in a **map slot** via `create_container`. The `tag` folds into the element's derived id (`XmlElement::node_id(parent,key,tag)` = `derive(derive(parent,key,XmlElement),tag,XmlElement)`; fragment id is tagless), so a concurrent same-key/different-tag create is a distinct identity the slot LWW resolves — a retag is a replace. The node's attrs Map + children List are registered in the document's own `maps`/`lists` under derived ids (so attrs reuse the existing `MapSet`/`RegisterSet` ops — no new attr op), with parent links `attrs→node`, `children→node`, `node→map`; `xml_elements`/`xml_fragments` identity registries retain nodes across displacement, which propagates to both halves. `MapCursor::xml_element(key,tag) -> XmlCursor` (`.attrs()`); `xml_fragment(key) -> XmlFragmentCursor` (attr-less by type — children in 1c). `tx_group_ready` counts an XML create's attrs/children ids as reachable so an atomic create+attr commits; `migration.rs` `op_key`/`with_key` relocate an XML slot key. Convergence-tested (XML edits in the fuzz); `tests/xml_doc.rs` (10); Miri-clean. Review-caught (3 correctness fixes + a test-gap, all with regressions). → *CRDT Model*.

**XmlElement / XmlFragment** — **Unit 1a — in-memory types + `Element` integration + merge/lifecycle (core) — DONE (#197).** `src/xml.rs`: `XmlElement { id, tag: Vec<u8>, attrs: Rc<RefCell<Map>>, children: Rc<RefCell<List>>, displaced: Cell<bool> }` + `XmlFragment { id, children, displaced }` — reuse the built Map (attrs, LWW) + List (children, Fugue ordering/tombstones) wholesale; `attrs`/`children` ids derive from the element id so replicas agree, `tag` immutable. `merge(&self, src)` delegates to `attrs.merge` + `children.merge` (tag is identity); `deep_clone` mints fresh handles; `displace`/`reinstate`/`is_displaced` over the own `Cell`. `ElementKind::XmlElement`/`XmlFragment` + id-derivation tags 6/7 + `from_tag` arms; `Element::XmlElement`/`XmlFragment` variants wired through every exhaustive match (`kind`/`is_container`/`id`/`merge`/`deep_clone`/`displace`/`reinstate`/`is_displaced`, `map.rs` `same_handle`/`same_composite_kind`, `doc.rs` `handles_eq`, `diff.rs`/wasm `kind_name`). `List.Node.value` already holds any `Element` in-memory, so children nest with no engine change — only the state codec (1b) needs generalizing; a map slot / seq node encoding an XML value asserts `unreachable!` here, `resolve_ref` folds the tree kinds into the unresolved arm. Spec `crates/core/tests/xml.rs` (19 direct-construction tests) + attrs-aware XML fingerprints in the convergence/durability fuzz oracles; Miri-clean. → *CRDT Model*.

**Snapshots / Export-Import** — **Snapshot clone (live template) — DONE (#196).** `Hub::clone_room(src, dst)` = `export_room(src)` + `import_room(dst, …)` — the "duplicate this doc as a template" primitive, cloning a room's live state into a fresh room id within one hub. The clone renumbers its own room-scoped server sequences, so it takes edits independently of the origin; no id-namespacing needed (room-scoping already keeps the replicas distinct, per the resolved design). Identities ride the snapshot as for import — the clone inherits the origin's element ids + op-dedup set, so a resend of an already-authored op is deduped (idempotency), while a new author diverges freely. Create-only (`Ok(false)` if src unknown or dst exists), persist-before-commit; carries the live state, not the named-version history. Hub primitive only (wire/session/SDK exposure deferred). Spec `crates/server/tests/export_import.rs` (duplicate, unknown-src/existing-dst false, independent divergence, dedup-carry, durable restart); Miri-clean. → *Snapshots / Export-Import*.

**Auto-version triggers** — **Unit 4 — schedule triggers `every: <duration>` (server) — DONE (#195).** `Registry::sweep` (the awareness grace/TTL sweep #71/#72) runs `fire_schedule_triggers(now)` after `reconcile_room_apps`, so schedules ride the existing tick + `Clock` — no separate timer. For each bound room's governing schema, `Trigger::Every(millis)` triggers arm to `now` on first sight (no capture on the binding sweep) and capture once the interval has elapsed, at most once per sweep — a long gap yields one capture, not a catch-up burst. Last-fire tracked per `(room, schedule-origin)` in `Registry.schedule_fires`; entries for unbound rooms pruned each sweep, so a rebound room re-arms. `schedule_origin(millis, template)` = `every\0<millis>\0<template>`, distinct from any `on:` origin, so retention windows never collide; `keep` prunes via the Unit 3b provenance path (tighter `keep` wins on a shared key, monotonic). The `draining` latch wraps the captures so a scheduled version never cascades into an `on: version-created` trigger; the event + schedule paths share `capture_version(room, name, origin, keep)`. Hardening: a **zero interval is rejected at parse** (core `parse_duration_millis`); the fire decision reads the last-fire map at sweep start and **defers stamping** so same-key schedules don't shadow; a **backward clock step re-arms** rather than stalling; the scan runs **unconditionally** (arming happens inside `parsed_schema`, so an `is_armed` gate would strand a schedule under an injected awareness policy). **Completes the auto-version trigger arc (Units 1–4).** Spec `crates/server/tests/auto_version.rs` (arm-then-fire, no-burst, keep-prune, backward-clock, injected-policy, dormancy re-arm); Miri-clean. (DECISIONS 2026-07-05.) → *Versioning / Auto-Version Triggers*.

**Auto-version triggers** — **Unit 3b — retention window `keep` via durable per-version provenance (server + store) — DONE (#194).** A trigger's `keep` bounds its own captures, exactly and durably — the only sound design (a name-pattern prune deletes manual versions / collides / wall-clock-orders; an in-memory tally resets on restart). Each `Version` gains `origin: Option<Vec<u8>>` (authoring trigger identity `(event, template)` NUL-joined, `None` = manual) + a monotonic `ordinal: u64` (hub-stamped, restored past the max persisted via `saturating_add` on cold start). `Hub::retain_by_origin(room, origin, keep)` filters the index by origin, selects the oldest `remove` by ordinal (linear `select_nth`, cloning only the doomed), evicts the batch in-memory, persists **once**, and fully restores on a persist error — never a partial prune. A rename detaches a capture (`rename_version` clears `origin` → operator-curated, exempt); retention runs even on a name collision (`Ok(false)`) so the tighter `keep` governs a shared group; `keep: 0` prunes the whole group, `keep: none` never prunes. Store `<room>.versions` record carries a 1-byte origin present-flag + the 8-byte ordinal (format break, free pre-release). `capture_auto_version` skips retention only on a capture *write* failure (retention writes the same store, would fail identically → one accurate log). Manual `create_version` stays 2-arg (origin `None`) so its 28 callers + `session.rs` are untouched; the auto path uses `create_auto_version`. Spec `crates/server/tests/auto_version.rs` (rename-detach, collision-retain, batch-prune, NTP-safe ordering, keep:0/none, store-restart survives); Miri-clean. (DECISIONS 2026-07-05.) → *Versioning / Auto-Version Triggers*.

**Auto-version triggers** — **Unit 3 — AutoVersion sink: event-driven capture (server) — DONE (#193).** A built-in recording `EventSink` (`crates/server/src/auto_version.rs`) + a post-delivery drain in `Registry`: on a matching `on:<event>` `EngineEvent` (#191), the room's governing schema drives `Hub::create_version` (#86) with the expanded template name (`${timestamp}` fixed-width via the Clock seam, `${event}` via `TriggerEvent::as_kebab`). The sink can't capture from `on_event` (a sink observes after commit; capture mutates the same hub), so it's a passive recorder over a shared `Rc<AutoVersionState>` queue that `deliver` drains after commit; trigger matching lives at the registry (it owns `room_apps`→`parsed_schema`). Room-bearing events only (subscribe / version create/rename/delete / compaction); connect/disconnect roomless (deferred), `every:` schedules → Unit 4. A `draining` flag stops an auto-created version cascading; an `armed` latch raised in `parsed_schema` (before the Subscribed emit) keeps a no-`autoVersion` deployment cost-free while still recording the arming subscribe; clock read once per drain, off the op hot path. `keep` retention **descoped to Unit 3b** (needs durable provenance — every heuristic unsound), parses but inert. → *Auto-Version Triggers*.

**Auto-version triggers** — **Unit 2 — schema `autoVersion` parse (core) — DONE (#192).** `core::schema` parses the top-level `autoVersion` array into `Vec<AutoVersion>` (`AutoVersion { trigger: Trigger, name: String, keep: Option<u64> }`; `Trigger::On(TriggerEvent)` | `Trigger::Every(millis)`; the kebab-case event vocabulary `connect`/`disconnect`/`subscribe`/`version-created`/`version-renamed`/`version-deleted`/`compaction`/`before-publish`/`after-restore`/`before-migration`; `<n><unit>` s/m/h/d duration → millis). Exactly-one-of `on`/`every`, non-empty `name`, `keep >= 0`; total-decode → `SchemaError` (`BadTrigger`/`UnknownEvent`/`BadDuration`/`EmptyName`), never a panic — covered in `hostile_inputs_never_panic`. `Schema::auto_version()` accessor; `TOP_LEVEL_KEYS` 7→8. Hand-rolled over `core::json`, mirrors the `@auth`/awareness parse. The AutoVersion sink (Unit 3) reads these triggers off the registered schema and drives `create_version` off the Unit 1 event bus (#191). → *Auto-Version Triggers* (design human-resolved 2026-07-04).

**Auto-version triggers** — **Unit 1 — engine event bus (server) — DONE (#191).** `EngineEvent` + `EventSink` (`crates/server/src/event.rs`): the hub owns one sink list (empty default → no emission cost) and fans a lifecycle event out after it commits — `VersionCreated`/`VersionRenamed`/`VersionDeleted` (real change only, never the `Ok(false)` no-op), `Compacted { floor }` (only when a non-empty log is reclaimed), `Connected`/`Disconnected` (balanced), `Subscribed` (on the not-subscribed→subscribed transition, so a rejected re-subscribe never re-fires). The registry feeds connection/subscribe events into the hub's seam via `Hub::emit` — one seam, both layers. Reserved `BeforePublish`/`AfterRestore`/`BeforeMigration` variants for the branch/migration layer, no enum break. A sink is a passive observer; the audit `AccessLog` stays a separate authz-axis seam, convergence deferred (DECISIONS). The substrate the AutoVersion sink (Unit 3), schedule triggers (Unit 4), and webhooks consume.

**Core primitives** — all green, Miri-clean:
scalar / counter / register / element / map (#22–#27), list Fugue (#24), text codepoint (#25), op envelope (#22), doc transact/apply (#30/#31), out-of-order buffering + persistent container identity (#32), binary op codec + log framing (#33).

**Server** — wire message framing (#40), room hub: op-log + idempotent ingest + catch-up (#41), session protocol driver (#42), connection registry fan-out (#43), tokio WebSocket transport (#44), durable op-log disk persistence (#47).

**SDKs** — C ABI / FFI (#34–#36), cbindgen header (#37), Python (#38), Go (#39), shared `core::path` navigation façade (#45), WebAssembly / JS (#46).

**Merged-code audit hardening** — a multi-agent adversarial audit of every already-merged subsystem (run after Copilot surfaced several real bugs in review) found six confirmed defects, all fixed spec-first (red repro → fix), batched by area: **snapshot-adoption seq rewind** — `Document::decode_state_as` carries the seq high-water mark so a client adopting a room snapshot can't rewind its op counter and silently diverge (#126); **untrusted-decode overflow class** — uniform `saturating_add`/`checked_add` at the codepoint/counter/tx-group decode sites, mirroring the sibling guards (#127); **server presence bugs** — unauthenticated `ClientId` presence-steal + reconnect race in the registry, and an unbounded awareness map (per-client key cap) (#128). 13/19 audited subsystems came back clean, including all FFI/wasm/SDK memory-safety + the authz seam.

**Correctness** — counter identity across displacement fixed (#48); randomized convergence property harness (#49/#50); server durability property fuzz (#51); schema-aware diff soundness/completeness property (#114 — over random edit sequences, `diff` is empty iff an observable read of the two replicas is equal, round-trips through its codec, and is deterministic; oracle is a materialized read, not `encode_state`, since a no-op edit shifts causal metadata the diff correctly ignores).

**v0.2 state codec + compaction (arc complete)** — leaf-value (#52), sequence-CRDT (#53), whole-replica document (#54) state serialization, in-memory op-log compaction + `Message::Snapshot` (#55), durable disk-log compaction crash-safe (#56), SDK snapshot-decode (#57), automatic compaction policy (#58).

**v0.2 wire / client** — client session / reconnect driver `core::client::ClientSession` (#59).

**Op acknowledgement (wire frames)** — `Message::Accepted { channel, through }` (tag 18, server→client, highest per-client `OpId.seq` durably committed — drains the author's outbox) and `Message::Ack { channel, seq }` (tag 19, client→server, the applied server sequence — feeds the GC watermark) + codec + `protocol_ack.rs` round-trip/total-decode (#121). Unit 1 of the op-ack gate; placeholder match arms (client refuses `Accepted` until the Unit 2 outbox; server accepts-and-ignores `Ack` until Unit 3 records it) that later units replace.

**Op acknowledgement (client outbox)** — `ClientSession` retains authored ops per channel; `edit`/`atomic_edit`/`commit_atomic` enqueue, `receive(Accepted{through})` prunes `id.seq <= through`, `resend(channel)` re-emits the unacked tail after reconnect, `outbox_len(channel)` is the queue depth (#122). `client_outbox.rs`. Unit 2 — the **offline-queue** half at the client seat; peer ops fanned in never touch the outbox, so `Accepted` alone drains it.

**Op acknowledgement (server ack)** — session `step` replies `Accepted{channel, through=max OpId.seq of the submitted batch}` to the author after a durable `Hub::ingest` (over the whole batch, so a resent-but-known op is still acked; empty batch acks nothing) (#123). **Offline queue now works end-to-end** — client outbox ↔ server ack, proven by a `ClientSession`↔`step` drain test. Unit 3 of the op-ack gate; `Ack`-recording-into-the-watermark deferred to Unit 4 (with its GC consumer + Clock/horizon).

**Op acknowledgement (SDK surface)** — `resend`/`outbox_len` over FFI (`crdtsync_client_resend`/`_outbox_len`) + Python/Go/wasm, each with an offline-queue drain test (#124). Unit 5. Fixed a latent bug it surfaced: façade edits (`document_mut` + `path::*`) bypassed the outbox — now routed through `ClientSession::enqueue_ops`, so SDK edits enqueue and resend/prune like closure edits. Offline queue is now surfaced end to end in every SDK.

**Tombstone compression** — sequence tombstones (List/Text) are compressed, never removed (#125). The state codec encodes live nodes in full, then run-length-encodes contiguous deleted runs (consecutive stamps on one insert's parent-chain) into single `(start, length, anchor)` range records and drops dead values; a deleted region costs O(runs) not O(deleted items). `STATE_VERSION` → 2; Text decode validates live codepoints only; a run past a cap splits on encode and is rejected on decode (decompression-bomb guard). Convergence-trivial (identical logical state, fewer bytes). Replaces the abandoned watermark-removal GC (see DECISIONS 2026-07-02); the `Ack` wire frame is now a reserved no-consumer slot.

**Forward-compat reservations** — blob-ref value slot `Scalar::BlobRef` reserved in the op envelope + codec (#60); error-envelope `details` byte string reserved in `Message::Error` + codec (#108) — round-tripped, empty, no producer yet, so the SDK error surface stays code + message; element-ref value slot `Scalar::ElementRef(ElementId)` reserved in the op/state codec (scalar tag 5) — a bare same-room element id, LWW leaf, round-tripped + total-decode, no producer/consumer yet (#151).

**Channel multiplexing** — one connection multiplexes many rooms via client-assigned `Channel`; server session holds channel→room, registry fans out per peer-channel (#61); SDK-side `ClientSession` holds N rooms, each with its own replica + last-seen seq, routing inbound frames by channel, reconnect via `resume(channel)` (#62). Arc complete.

**Handshake auth** — three-phase Hello → Auth → Subscribe. Wire `Auth`/`AuthOk` messages (#63); server pluggable `Verifier` + session actor gate, dev-mode `AllowAll` default (#64); client `ClientSession::auth`/`actor` (#65). Server derives actor; client never asserts it.

**Auth fast path + anonymous mode** — `Session::authenticated` + `Registry::connect_authenticated` open a connection already authenticated (#73); runtime verifies an `Authorization`-header credential during the WS upgrade and sends an unsolicited `AuthOk`, skipping the in-band Auth phase; `ServeConfig::anonymous` mints `actor = anon:<random>` from transport-layer entropy (#74). Header carrier done.

**Verifier injection** — `serve_with_verifier` plugs a real `Box<dyn Verifier + Send>` (JWT/OIDC/API key) into the runtime; `serve`/`serve_with` default to dev `AllowAll` (#75). Real end-to-end map + reject now exercisable.

**Auth carriers** — fast-path credential read from four carriers in precedence order: `Authorization` header → `crdtsync.auth.<v>` subprotocol (echoed so browser negotiation succeeds) → `crdtsync_credential` cookie → `?credential=` query param (#76). Browser-reachable carriers (subprotocol/query/cookie) covered. mTLS deferred — no TLS layer yet (see Queue).

**Awareness (core)** — ephemeral presence: wire `AwarenessSet`/`AwarenessUpdate` (#66); server fan-out per peer-channel, actor-tagged, never logged/snapshotted (#67); client `set_awareness` + per-channel `(actor,key)` LWW view (#68); server-side ephemeral store → late-joiner replay on Subscribe + clear-on-disconnect (#69). Publish + fan-out + client view + late-joiner replay done.

**Awareness reconnect grace** — `AwarenessClear` wire message (#70); server `Clock` seam (`SystemClock`/`ManualClock`) + grace window (default 5s) + `Registry::sweep` fanning `AwarenessClear` to room peers, reconnect within window cancels the clear (#71); periodic sweep wired into the tokio runtime via `serve_with`/`ServeConfig` so grace expiry fires in production (#72). Session-TTL (grace) complete end-to-end. Timed-TTL + throttle are schema-gated (see Queue); auth-filter done (**Awareness auth-filter**, below).

**Auth fast path + carriers** — connection opens pre-authenticated (`Session::authenticated`/`connect_authenticated`, #73); `Authorization`-header credential verified at the WS upgrade with an unsolicited `AuthOk` + anonymous mode (#74); real `Verifier` injectable via `serve_with_verifier` (#75); credential carriers extended to subprotocol/cookie/query with precedence (#76). mTLS deferred (no TLS layer).

**SDK wiring — wire client** — the full `CrdtClient` C ABI (`ClientSession`: lifecycle, receive, per-channel edits/reads, auth, awareness, last-seen; core `document_mut`) (#77/#78), wrapped in the Python (#79), Go (#80), and wasm (#81) SDKs. Every SDK can now drive the sync protocol, not just the local `Document`.

**Authorization seam** — pluggable `authz::Authorizer` (`Action` × `Resource::Room`), default-deny contract, dev `PermitAll`; enforced at Subscribe (read) / Ops (write) / AwarenessSet (publish); non-closing `ErrorCode::Forbidden`; injectable via `set_authorizer` (#82). Room-level enforcement points that exist today; two-tier policy + redaction + zones + audit remain (see Next/Queue).

**Per-recipient read redaction** — the registry re-checks `Read` on every fan-out (ops + awareness), so a peer whose read is revoked mid-session stops receiving the room without resubscribing; enforces the "filter every op send" invariant against a dynamic policy (#83). Room-level today; the per-send hook is where element/zone redaction slots in. Doc-level ACL-CRDT + finer-grain snapshot redaction + audit log remain (see Next/Queue).

**ACL decision flow** — `acl::Acl`, a concrete tuple-walking `Authorizer` (the first real policy on the seam): allow/deny rules over `Subject` (`Actor`/`Authenticated`/`Anonymous`/`Anyone`) × action (`Option<Action>`) × `ResourceMatch` (`AnyRoom`/`Room`), evaluated explicit-deny-wins → allow → default-deny; order-independent; plugs in via `set_authorizer` (#84). Role/group subjects (need a claims model) and schema `@auth` role grants (need the schema layer) deferred. Doc-level ACL-as-CRDT feeds this same evaluator later.

**Declarative policy file** — `Acl::from_policy(&str) -> Result<Acl, PolicyError>` loads the `acl::Acl` from a line-oriented text policy — the file-loader half of the ACL flow (the `Acl` was programmatic-only since #84). One rule per line `<effect> <subject> <action> <resource>` (`allow`/`deny` × `actor:<hex>`/`authenticated`/`anonymous`/`anyone`/`*` × `read`/`write`/`publish_awareness`/`*` × `room:<name>`/`*`); `#` comments + blank lines ignored, whitespace-tolerant. Dependency-free, total-parse — a malformed line yields a `PolicyError { line, kind }` (`PolicyErrorKind`: Arity/Effect/Subject/ActorHex/Action/Resource) pinned to its physical line, never a panic. A parsed policy authorizes identically to the same programmatic `allow`/`deny` rules (proven over the full actor×action×room matrix) (#129). Realizes §Authorization "Engine Ships: Declarative policy enforcement"; feeds the later admin/audit-query consumers.

**Declarative policy enforcement (deploy seam)** — `runtime::serve_with_authorizer` threads a deployment `Authorizer` (e.g. an `Acl::from_policy` result) into the tokio runtime, calling `Registry::set_authorizer` inside the registry actor — the served server previously always ran the default `PermitAll`, so a declared policy had no way to reach it. `serve_with_verifier` is now a thin wrapper passing `PermitAll`. A ws integration test drives a policy-backed `Acl` over the real loopback socket: the granted actor subscribes, an actor outside the policy gets a `Forbidden` frame (#130). Closes the parse→enforce loop for §Authorization "Declarative policy enforcement."

**Declarative policy — operator file path** — `Acl::from_policy_file(path)` reads a policy file and parses it (`PolicyFileError`: `Io` file-unreadable vs `Parse` malformed-line, distinct arms with `source()` + `Display`); the stock server binary honors `CRDTSYNC_POLICY_FILE` — set it and the shipped server enforces the declared policy via `serve_with_authorizer`, unset it stays dev-permissive (#132). Batteries-included operator path — an operator drops a policy file, no embedding needed. Completes declarative policy end to end: parse (#129) → inject (#130) → operator config. §Main-Product-Goals "Batteries-Included Deployment". **Caveat:** the stock binary still uses the dev-mode `AllowAll` verifier, which takes the credential verbatim as the actor id — the client controls the whole id, including the `anon:` prefix that distinguishes anonymous from authenticated. So under the stock binary every subject but `anyone` is spoofable (`actor:<id>`, `authenticated`, `anonymous` alike); a policy is only a real boundary once a `Verifier` that derives the actor from a validated credential is injected (embed + `serve_with_verifier`). Documented in `main.rs`. **Resolved by the credentials-file verifier below** — configure one and the actor is server-derived, so those subject rules become real.

**Static-token verifier (operator credentials file)** — `auth::StaticTokens`, a `Verifier` over a fixed secret-credential → actor table, the engine's minimal built-in token validation (§Authorization "Engine Ships: Token validation" — not an identity provider). `verify` returns the mapped actor for a known credential, `None` (refuse) for an unknown one, so the actor is server-derived, not client-chosen. `from_credentials`/`from_credentials_file` parse a line-oriented `<credential> <actor>` file (`#` comments + blanks ignored, whitespace-tolerant) totally — `CredentialsError { line, kind }` (`Arity`/`DuplicateCredential`) and a distinct `CredentialsFileError` (`Io` vs `Parse`, `source()` + `Display`), never a panic. The stock binary honors `CRDTSYNC_CREDENTIALS_FILE`; `main.rs` composes it independently with `CRDTSYNC_POLICY_FILE` (verifier ∈ {AllowAll, StaticTokens} × authorizer ∈ {PermitAll, Acl}, one uniform `serve_with_authorizer` dispatch). A ws integration test proves end-to-end enforceability the dev-mode default lacked: the secret holder authenticates as its actor and the policy admits it, an unknown credential is refused at the handshake and cannot spoof the actor (#133). Closes the #132 caveat — completes the batteries-included auth story (§Main-Product-Goals "Batteries-Included Deployment"). A signed-token/OIDC verifier stays an embed-only follow-on (crypto/format design).

**Access log** — `audit::{AccessLog, AccessRecord, Audited, Decision}`: an `Audited` decorator wraps any inner `Authorizer`, forwards its verdict, and emits each decision (actor, action, resource, verdict) to a pluggable sink — logged at the seam every enforcement point consults, so read-only accesses (subscribe) are captured alongside the writes the op log already records (#85). Never logs the credential; an awareness publish logs the decision only, never the entry's key/value. A distinct `Connect`/snapshot/branch audit action + the query surface are follow-ons (need those actions / an admin CLI).

**Named versions (index)** — `Hub` versions layer over the snapshot primitive: `create_version` captures a room's whole-replica state + covered seq under a name; `version_seq`/`version_state` read back (export/diff); `version_names` lists sorted for pagination; `rename_version`/`delete_version` complete the index. Versions are immutable point-in-time; taken names refused (#86).

**Named versions (durable)** — the index persists to a per-room `<room>.versions` store file (framed `(name, seq, state)` records), rewritten crash-atomically on each change (temp→fsync→rename→dir-fsync) and reloaded on reopen; `create`/`rename`/`delete` are now `io::Result<bool>`, persisting before commit with in-memory rollback on IO failure (#87).

**Named versions (wire)** — seven wire messages (tags 11–17): client→server `VersionCreate`/`VersionRename`/`VersionDelete`/`VersionList`/`VersionFetch`; server→client `Versions{names}` (state-based ack + list reply) and `VersionState{name,seq,state}` (fetch reply). Codec + round-trip suite; total decode (#88).

**Named versions (server handling)** — `step` serves the five requests on the channel's room: mutations run the `Hub` op and reply the fresh `Versions` list; list replies the same; fetch replies `VersionState` (hit) or the list (miss). Gated on `Write` (mutations) / `Read` (list/fetch); denial → non-closing `Forbidden`, unbound channel → violation, persist failure → closing `Internal` (#89).

**Named versions (client view)** — `ClientSession` frames the five requests for a held channel (`create`/`rename`/`delete`/`list`/`fetch_version`) and folds replies into a per-room view: `Versions` replaces the known names, `VersionState` caches fetched bytes by name; exposed via `versions(channel)` / `version_state(channel,name)` (#90). Server-authoritative — no optimistic local version state.

**Named versions (FFI)** — the `CrdtClient` C ABI exposes the five issue methods (`crdtsync_client_{create,rename,delete,list,fetch}_version` → request frames) and the view (`version_count` + indexed `version_name`; `version_state` by name) (#91). Header symbols asserted; Miri leak-clean.

**Named versions (language SDKs)** — Python/Go (over the C ABI) and wasm (over `ClientSession`) wrap the five issue methods + the view (`versions` list, `version_state` by name) (#92). **Arc complete** — named versions run end to end (index → durable → wire → server → client → SDKs). Restore-as-branch + auto-triggers stay blocked (branch layer / engine-event hooks).

**Composition cookbook** — executable recipes (`crates/core/tests/cookbook.rs`) building Set, bounded counter, multi-value register, and a tagged document from the closed primitive set — no new engine support — with convergence assertions (#93). The "compose, don't add primitives" thesis, kept honest by tests.

**UndoManager (scalars)** — `core::undo::UndoManager`: per-user undo/redo over root Register + Counter slots. Records each edit made through it (register/inc/dec/delete), capturing the overwritten value; `undo`/`redo` replay ordinary forward ops (no server state, no wire change) and converge on peers like any edit; a fresh edit clears the redo stack (#94).

**UndoManager (grouping)** — an undo step is one *intention*: `group(doc, |b| …)` records several edits (via a `Batch`) as a single undo/redo step, reverting them together; the single-edit methods are one-edit groups (#95). Matches ARCHITECTURE's "intentions = op groups." Nested paths + list/text revival are the next slices.

**Counter `dec` end-to-end** — a Counter decrement surfaced through the whole binding stack: `path::dec` → FFI `crdtsync_doc_dec`/`crdtsync_client_dec` → Python/Go/wasm `dec`, mirroring `inc` (#96). Closes an SDK gap (the PN-counter's negative direction had no path/FFI/SDK entry point) and readies nested-counter undo.

**UndoManager (nested paths)** — undo is now path-addressed: every `UndoManager`/`Batch` method takes an encoded path, so a scalar slot inside a nested Map undoes exactly as a root one; a group can revert root + nested edits as one intention (#97). Backed by `path::register` (generic scalar) + `path::get_register`. List/text revival is the remaining undo slice.

**UndoManager (list revival)** — undo of a list insert deletes the node it minted (addressed by stable id, not a drifting index); undo of a list delete revives the removed value as a fresh insert at its place — the op log has no un-tombstone (#98). Backed by `List::live_index`, `ListCursor::delete_id`, `path::list_delete_id`/`list_live_index`. Text revival is the last undo slice.

**UndoManager (text revival)** — undo of a text insert deletes the run's char_ids; undo of a text delete revives the captured substring as a fresh run at its place (#99). Backed by `Text::live_index`, `TextCursor::delete_ids`, `path::text_delete_ids`/`text_run_ids`/`text_live_index`. Core undo now spans every value type (scalar / counter / list / text, root or nested); only the SDK surface remains.

**UndoManager (FFI)** — a `CrdtUndo` handle over the C ABI: `crdtsync_undo_new`/`_free` plus register_int/inc/dec/delete/list_insert/list_delete/text_insert/text_delete (each returns the ops to broadcast) and undo/redo/can_undo/can_redo (#100). The manager is a handle distinct from the `CrdtDoc` it drives, named on every call. Bytes-in-Register has no existing reader, so it is not exposed. Python/Go/wasm wrappers next.

**UndoManager (SDK wrappers)** — Python `Undo`, Go `Undo`, and wasm `WasmUndo` over the FFI/core surface: register_int/inc/dec/delete/list_insert/list_delete/text_insert/text_delete + undo/redo/can_undo/can_redo, each naming the document it drives (#101). Undo/redo now works end-to-end in every SDK; the subsystem is complete.

**Atomic transactions (core)** — `Document::atomic_transact` tags a group's ops with `Tx { id, count }` (op envelope + codec extended from the reserved `tx` slot); a receiver buffers members until the whole group is present and its external deps resolve, then applies them together in seq order — an all-or-nothing *view* boundary that preserves convergence (verified against the convergence harness) (#102). Buffered partials ride the existing op-buffer, so they survive a snapshot. Design logged in DECISIONS. Wire/server/client drive path + SDK surface are the follow-on slices.

**Atomic transactions (drive path)** — `ClientSession::atomic_edit` mirrors `edit` but tags the group as one transaction, sent as an ordinary `Message::Ops` (membership rides on the ops, no new wire message) (#103). The server needed no change: `Hub::ingest` returns every fresh op regardless of buffering, so a whole atomic batch fans out intact and a fresh subscriber's catch-up carries the full group — verified end to end (client peer folds it in all-or-nothing; hub preserves the tags through ingest + catch-up). SDK `atomic_edit`/`atomic_transact` is the remaining slice.

**Atomic transactions (SDK surface, doc-level)** — a begin/commit atomic API on the `Document` seat, since the C ABI has no closures: core `Document::begin_atomic`/`commit_atomic`/`is_atomic` (edits between them accumulate into one group; `atomic_transact` is now a thin wrapper), exposed as `crdtsync_doc_begin_atomic`/`_commit_atomic` (FFI), `begin_atomic`/`commit_atomic` (Python, Go), and `beginAtomic`/`commitAtomic` (wasm) (#104). Client-seat (per-channel) atomic wrappers over `ClientSession::atomic_edit` are the last slice.

**Atomic transactions (SDK surface, client-seat)** — per-channel begin/commit on the wire client: core `ClientSession::begin_atomic(channel)`/`commit_atomic(channel)` (the latter returns the group as one `Message::Ops`), exposed as `crdtsync_client_begin_atomic`/`_commit_atomic` (FFI) and `begin_atomic`/`commit_atomic` (Python, Go) / `beginAtomic`/`commitAtomic` (wasm) (#105). Edits recorded between them travel as one atomic group over the wire — a peer folds them all-or-nothing. **Atomic transactions are now complete end-to-end** (core → drive path → doc + client SDK seats).

**Atomic-transaction undo** — `UndoManager::atomic_group` records a gesture as one atomic transaction and its undo/redo replay as atomic transactions too, so a peer never sees a partially-undone group (#106). Realizes ARCHITECTURE §Transactions "a transaction is naturally an undo intention": an `Intention` carries an `atomic` flag and `apply` wraps an atomic intention's inverse ops in a fresh atomic tx via the doc's `begin_atomic`/`commit_atomic`. Core-only, matching `group` (closure-shaped, not in the SDK surface).

**Schema-aware diff (core)** — `core::diff::diff(old, new) -> Vec<Change>` computes the structural changes between two replica snapshots by walking the Element trees in lockstep (#109): slots `Added`/`Removed` (with kind), scalar/register `Value` and `Counter` old→new value changes, nested maps recursed so a deep edit reports at its own path. Path-addressed (shared `path` encoding) and ordered by path, so diffing a pair is deterministic. Backed by `Map::keys()`. Pairs with named versions + export/import (compare any two snapshots).

**Schema-aware diff — change-list codec** — `core::diff::encode_changes`/`decode_changes` serialize a `Vec<Change>` to a tag-led byte buffer (`u32` count then each change) and back, so a diff crosses the language-SDK boundary as one buffer (#112). Round-trips every variant, decodes totally (`BadTag`/`TrailingBytes`/`UnexpectedEof`), reuses the op-codec primitives (`put_scalar`/`Cursor`). Not durable — a diff is transient. Backed by `ElementKind::from_tag`.

**Schema-aware diff — FFI entry point** — `crdtsync_diff(old_state, new_state) -> CrdtBuf` over the C ABI: decodes two snapshot buffers, diffs them, returns the encoded change list (#113). Stateless (no doc handle) — the inputs are any two state blobs (a `version_state`, an exported room, `encode_state`); empty on malformed input or a bad snapshot, never a panic. Header symbol asserted, Miri leak-clean.

**Schema-aware diff — wasm surface** — `WasmDocument.diff(oldState, newState)` returns an array of structural change objects, each with an `op` tag, a `path` (Uint8Array), and its variant's fields (#115). A scalar is a tagged `{ t, v }` object (`int`/`bool`/`null`/`bytes`/`blobref`) so binary values and full-range ints read unambiguously; list items are `{ scalar }` or `{ kind }`. Throws on a malformed snapshot. This establishes the cross-language change representation.

**Schema-aware diff — Python surface** — module-level `diff(old_state, new_state)` returns a list of change dicts over the C ABI `crdtsync_diff` (#116): a pure-Python `_Reader` decodes the change-list bytes into the same shape as wasm — `{"op", "path", ...}` with tagged `{"t","v"}` scalars and `{"scalar"}`/`{"kind"}` list items. Raises `ValueError` on a malformed snapshot.

**Schema-aware diff — Go surface** — `crdtsync.Diff(oldState, newState) ([]Change, error)` over the C ABI `crdtsync_diff` (#117): a `changeReader` decodes the change-list bytes into typed `Change` structs (`Op`/`Path` + per-variant fields), with a tagged `Scalar` and `Item` (scalar-or-kind), mirroring the wasm/Python shape. Errors on a malformed snapshot. **Diff runs end to end across every SDK** (core → codec → C ABI → wasm/Python/Go).

**Schema-aware diff — default renderer** — `core::diff::render(changes) -> Vec<String>` gives the engine's default human-readable rendering, one line per change (#119): `+`/`-`/`~` for add/remove/change, slash-joined paths, plainly-printed scalars, sequence runs with index + contents. Realizes ARCHITECTURE §Schema-Aware-Diff "engine ships sensible default renderers; apps can override" (an app renders the structured `Change`s its own way). XmlElement/marks/attrs diffs remain blocked on those unbuilt primitives.

**Schema-aware diff — sequence detail** — Text and List diff to runs by stable id: Text → `TextInsert{index,text}`/`TextDelete{index,text}` codepoint runs (#110), List → `ListInsert{index,items}`/`ListDelete{index,items}` item runs (`SeqItem::Scalar`/`Composite`) (#111). Stable-id identity makes both exact (no heuristic alignment); consecutive same-op elements coalesce into runs, deletes (old index) before inserts (new index). Text is codepoint-indexed. Realizes ARCHITECTURE §Schema-Aware-Diff "Text values produce char-level diffs" + structural list change lists. **Core diff now spans every built primitive** (map/register/counter/list/text). XmlElement/marks/attrs diffs (unbuilt primitives), renderers, and the SDK surface remain follow-ons (see Queue).

**RelativePosition / anchors (core)** — `core::anchor::RelativePosition`, a stable cursor/selection position in a List or Text sequence: `Start`/`End` boundaries + `Before(id)`/`After(id)` bound to a CRDT item id (left/right gravity), not an integer offset. `List::relative_position(index, side)` / `resolve_position` (+ Text codepoint wrappers): capture binds to the id at the gap; resolve returns the live index — a live binding to its item's edge, a deleted one walking the retained tombstones (#125) to the nearest live neighbour on the gravity side (clamping to 0/len), boundaries to 0/len. So an insert before shifts it, an insert at the gap lands per gravity, a delete of the bound item doesn't strand it — and it resolves identically on converged replicas. Binary codec (`encode`/`decode`, total-decode) so a position can cross the wire (awareness cursors carry one). Spec `crates/core/tests/anchor.rs` (#134). Realizes §Anchors "Exposed at SDK level as RelativePosition ... survive concurrent inserts and deletes without drifting. Core primitive." Unblocks marks/comments/RangedElement + awareness cursors; the SDK `RelativePosition` surface is the follow-on (see Next).

**Snapshot export / import** — a room's whole-replica state is portable across hubs: `Hub::export_room` hands back the snapshot bytes (`None` for an unknown room); `Hub::import_room` rebuilds a fresh room from them, persisting the snapshot durably before it commits so the import survives a restart (#107). Backup / cross-server-move / debug-repro: the merged state, element/client identities, and dedup set all ride the bytes, so a client resending its ops is deduped exactly as against the origin, and a fresh subscriber is caught up with the imported snapshot.
