Import is create-only (refuses an existing room rather than clobbering live state) and rejects malformed bytes as `InvalidData`, never a panic; sequences renumber from the imported op count (server-local, so no collision with the origin). Cloning under a *new* room id — clock bumps + id namespacing so two live copies can't collide — stays a follow-on (see Queue).

**Follower fan-out (C59, #384)** — a batch a follower ingests from a `Replicate` frame is fanned out to that follower's own subscribers, so a client subscribed on a caught-up follower — the ordinary shape since follower read-serving (#316) — advances over the leader's commits instead of freezing until its next reconnect. It rides the *same* seam a locally-authored write takes (per-recipient doc-ACL redaction, the whole-document read gate, per-channel zone scoping), reached by extracting the write path's two fan-out branches behind one `fan_out_ops` dispatcher. Two rulings, in DECISIONS: the **exclusion set is empty** — the batch was authored on the leader, so no channel here already holds it, which makes `WriteOrigin` the two shapes the seam has (`Local { conn, channel }`, still excluding exactly what C5 says, and `Replicated`, excluding nothing) rather than a nullable conn; and **every per-recipient verdict is re-decided against this replica**, never inherited, because what the leader computed was an answer for its own subscribers and the frame carries none of it (C28 applied where it was not yet applied; the acting schema comes from the room the frame names, C49). Migration translation does not run on this path — the relay seam replicates untagged, so the fan-out and the follower's own catch-up serve the identical verbatim delta; carrying the author's version across the frame is filed as C71, and the snapshot-install path as C72. ARCHITECTURE §Follower Read-Serving amended: a served subscription is a live stream, not a point read.

Edits refuse via `onOpsRejected` (never an `Error`), so the only mid-session non-request `Error` is `UpdateRequired` → `onError`. No core change. The **repair signal** turns on: `doc.on("repair", cb)` delivers the locations whose repaired reading changed against the schema after an edit, each a `RepairStep[]` step path (map keys + sequence indices, decoded from the core's `encode_repair_path`). A repair names a *location*, not an edit (re-read it), so the event carries **no origin** (DECISIONS 2026-07-23). Emitted from every mutation path (local + the provider's `applyRemote` bracket), drained only when observed. Deterministic tests for flavor + repair fire/no-fire/unsubscribe; an integration test fires the signal on a remote edit against a locally-bound schema over the real server.

Native marshaling matches the JS/Python boundary **exactly**, routed through the canonical `encodeScalar`/`changeReader` so it can't drift from the path SDK's `Scalar` codec (`string`↔Scalar::Bytes utf-8 with a 0x01 string / 0x00 binary discriminator, `int`/`int64`↔Scalar::Int — native `int64`, no overflow guard needed, `bool`↔Scalar::Bool, `nil`↔Scalar::Null, `[]byte`↔Scalar::Bytes raw). Explicit leaf/container boundary — `Set(k, map/slice/float)` returns an **error** (deep-seed rejected). Map `Set/Get/Delete/Has/Keys/Entries/Len` + `GetMap/GetList/GetText`; List `Insert/Append/Delete/Get/Len/Values` (negative index, `error` out of range); Text `Insert/Delete/String/Len`. `Get` resolves a container slot to a nested handle. `OnUpdate(cb) → off()` (`UpdateEvent{Origin, Ops}`, fires local + remote) + `ApplyUpdate` for the two-doc convergence base test. **FFI:** the three C-ABI parity wrappers (`set_scalar`/`get_scalar`/`map_keys`) already landed with the Python lift, so Go only added their Go bindings (`Document.SetScalar`/`GetScalar`/`MapKeys`); **no core CRDT change, no FFI change.** Tests: `handle_test.go` (14 — round-trip all scalar types, string/look-alike-bytes distinct, cross-SDK known-good encoding, no-deep-seed error, nested composition, list/text semantics, keys/entries, sequential + concurrent convergence, snapshot round-trip, on-update local/remote/unsub, malformed-scalar no-panic regression). Two local `/code-review` passes (medium): dedup marshaling through `encodeScalar`/`changeReader` (removing a panic-prone hand-rolled decoder), and fixed a `Has`/`Get` xml-slot disagreement (both now consistent; xml handles land Slice 3).
  - **Slice 2 — reactivity — DONE (#336).** `OnUpdate(cb)` carries diff-derived `Changes []EventChange` re-marshaled from the `DiffEncode`/`decodeChanges` seam: each a typed `EventChange` (Go's idiom for Python's plain dict) with `Kind` (update / counter / list_insert / list_delete / text_insert / text_delete / add / remove / mark), an ergonomic key-or-index `Path`, native `Old`/`New`, and the event's local/remote origin. `handle.Observe(cb)` (Map/List/Text) fires only for its subtree via a framed-byte path-prefix match (sound — each key is length-framed, so `a` never matches `ab`). `OnRepair(cb)` + `SetSchema` deliver the schema-repair signal (located `RepairEvent.Paths`, each a `[]RepairStep` of map keys / sequence indices; no origin). Snapshot+diff runs only when observing (unobserved doc pays nothing); a remote ack (no changes) fires nothing; a listener/observer subscribed during dispatch doesn't see the in-flight event (snapshotted sets). A **map leaf** decodes its scalar by stripping the string/binary discriminator, a **list item** by re-running the full `unmarshalValue` (they are stored differently — see DECISIONS). Pure SDK — no core/FFI change. Tests: `reactivity_test.go` (13 — value/list/text change shape, counter/backend-bypass, late-listener, unsubscribe-stops, subtree-only observe, remote origin, repair fire/no-fire/no-schema/unsubscribe). Local `/code-review` (medium) clean.
  - **Slice 3 — Xml + marks + cursors + atomic tx + blobs — DONE (#337).** `CrdtXml` (`Element`/`Fragment`, `Tag`, index-addressed children insert/delete, identity-preserving `Move`/tree-move, `Len`, fluent builder returns; `Get` resolves an xml slot to a handle) — same core limit as JS/Python (index-addressed, no recursion into a child element's contents). Text/List cursors (`RelativePosition(i, side)`/`Resolve(pos)`, `"before"`=left / `"after"`=right). Text marks (`Mark`/`MarkWithGravity` → id / `SetMarkValue` / `DeleteMark` / `MarksAt` → `[]MarkInfo`), value marshaled through the shared `marshalScalar` seam so a value-flavor mark round-trips a native scalar cross-SDK. `Transact(fn)` (one atomic batch, one update, nested-flatten, defer-committed). Map blobs (`SetBlob` → bool, `SetBlobRef`, `GetBlob` → `BlobRef`). **Fixed a pre-existing Go change-decoder gap:** `kindNames` lacked the XML element kinds (tags 6/7), so a diff over XML edits errored — extended with `xmlElement`/`xmlFragment` (the Go analogue of Python's `_KINDS` fix; regression-covered). `marshalValue` refactored to share `marshalScalar`. Pure SDK — no core/FFI change. Tests: `rich_test.go` (20 — xml install/children/tree-move/converge, text+list cursors incl. concurrent-remote, mark author/read/delete/sync/reactivity/value-flavor, atomic single-update/peer-atomic/nested-flatten, blobs inline/ref/over-ceiling/generic-get/converge). Local `/code-review` (medium) clean.
  - **Slice 4 — offline-first Provider seam — DONE (#338).** `NewProvider(doc, send)` — the embedded/offline-first realization of the §SDK-Ergonomic-Surface provider over a `Doc`'s apply/emit seam: the app supplies the transport (`send(ops)` for outbound local edits; feed a peer's ops to `provider.Receive(ops)`), the provider owns the connection state (`State`/`Connect`/`Disconnect`/`OnState`) and an **offline outbox** so edits made while disconnected queue (`OutboxLen`) and flush in order on `Connect`. Inbound ops apply and fire `remote` reactivity; a remote apply never re-emits as local, so a pair of linked providers can't loop (echo-safe). Starts disconnected — `Connect` is the single go-online call (the JS `connected=true` start is `Connect` before the first edit). `Close` unbinds (idempotent). Also unifies Doc + Provider listeners onto an insertion-ordered `listenerList[T]` so callbacks fire in registration order (deterministic, cross-SDK parity — a Go map would randomize), releasing removed closures for GC. Pure SDK — no core/FFI change. Tests: `provider_test.go` (9 — two-doc sync, no-echo-loop, offline-flush-on-connect, state transitions + notify + dedup, remote reactivity on receive, close-stops-forwarding, connected-edit-sends-immediately, listener-order) + a Doc listener-order test. Local `/code-review` (high) clean.
  - **GO HANDLE-GRAPH LIFT COMPLETE (Slices 1–4). Cross-SDK ergonomic epic COMPLETE — JS (reference), Python (#331–#334), Go all share the pinned handle-graph shape.** Handle-graph (Map/List/Text/Xml) + native marshaling (JS/Python-exact, explicit leaf/container boundary) + reactivity (Observe / OnUpdate / OnRepair) + cursors + atomic tx + blobs + marks + offline-first sync provider — the full ergonomic surface, in Go idiom (exported methods, `(value, ok)` reads, `error` returns, callbacks + unsubscribe `func()`).
  - **Deferred (shared with Python, enumerated):** the **networked socket-owning provider** (JS-style `connect(url, room)` over a single wire-client replica, with awareness + operator-tier request/reply) needs the same per-channel C-ABI methods (`list_*`/`text_*`/`set_scalar`/`get_scalar`/`map_keys` on the `Client` wire surface) the Python follow-on names — not built here; A1 (#340) added them. **Not built (same as JS/Python):** undo/redo (DESIGN FORK — human-gated). **Rejected non-goal (permanent, cross-SDK):** Automerge-style deep-seed.

- **Branch layer (§Versioning-and-Branches) — FUNCTIONALLY COMPLETE (cs-next 2026-07-12).** Replenished from ARCHITECTURE (Unit 9 + reachability-gate arc complete; the rest of the *visible* frontier was gated, so the frontier was refilled from end-state scope, never parked). **Eight PRs landed** — Unit 1 (branch registry, #245), Unit 2 (wire carrier, #244), Unit 3 (branch-scoped subscribe + log resolution, #246), Unit 4a (fork-from-snapshot, #247), Unit 4b (restore-as-branch + active-HEAD switch + `AfterRestore`, #248), Unit 5 (publish/draft + per-user branches + `BeforePublish`, #249), Unit 6 (branch subscribe SDK surface, #250), Unit 7 (schema-aware branch/version diff, #251) — all in Done. Closed the deferred restore-as-branch + the parked auto-version branch triggers. **Remaining branch work is exposure-gated / out-of-scope:**
- **Small ungated v0.2 items — DONE (cs-next 2026-07-12):** Webhooks (outbound `EventSink` over the event bus #191, #253) + Awareness auth-filter (fan-out gated on recipient read authority — found already in place from #176, added the privacy spec suite + closed the stale note, #252). See Done.
- **Distributed cluster (§Horizontal-Scaling) — COMPLETE (2026-07-14, Unit 7b #272).** The last big designed epic, now fully landed: consistent-hashing room sharding (1), static membership (2), redirect routing (3), leader→follower replication (4), majority-ACK durability (5), liveness failover + epoch/term split-brain fence (6a/6b), and gossip discovery + SWIM failure detection (7a/7b). The concrete consensus/replication protocol was directional; each harder unit carried its own design (logged in DECISIONS). **Remaining cluster work is all documented follow-on/refinement** (SWIM indirect probing #310, member reaping #311, late-joiner replication-dial #314, below-floor state-transfer #315, epoch persistence #313, transparent-proxy / follower-read-serving #316 — all DONE) — none blocking. Sliced dependency-order:
  - **Cluster Unit 1 — consistent-hashing room placement (server) — DONE (#254).** A pure, deterministic `room_id → ordered replica set` mapping by **rendezvous (HRW / highest-random-weight) hashing** over a member set (`crates/server/src/placement.rs`, `Cluster::replicas`/`primary`, `NodeId`) — the placement foundation routing/replication/failover all consult. Each member is scored for a room, the top-`n` are the replicas, the highest the primary. **HRW over a consistent-hashing ring** (DECISIONS 2026-07-12): no virtual-node ring to size/tune, balanced + minimal-reshuffle for free, natural leader-first order — satisfies §Room-Based Sharding ("consistent hashing on `room_id` for deterministic, balanced placement"). **Deterministic + portable hash**: FNV-1a room seed → per-member FNV-1a → SplitMix64 finalizer, fixed-width `u64` wrapping arithmetic, no per-process seed/floats, so every node computes the same placement. Guarantees (tested, `crates/server/tests/placement.rs`, 16): determinism (input order / duplicates irrelevant), balance (10k rooms over 10 nodes, each within 20% of a fair share), minimal reshuffle (a join/leave moves ~1/(k+1) of primaries, far below modulo churn; a room changes only if it hosted the leaver), N-clamp (N > member count → all, no dupes/panic; empty set / N=0 → empty; single node owns all), stable leader-first order (a larger replica set is a growing prefix). Server-only (no `crates/core/src`), no new dep — CI miri unaffected. No consensus, no wire — pure logic.
  - **Cluster Unit 2 — cluster membership + static join (server) — DONE (#255).** A node learns its member set from **static config** (`crates/server/src/membership.rs`, `Membership`): its advertise address (or explicit node id) + a peer list, building the Unit-1 `Cluster` from `{self} ∪ peers`. Exposes the node view routing/replication consult — `members`/`self_id`/`is_self`/`replicas_for`/`primary_for`/`owns`/`is_primary_for` — each the shared placement evaluated for self's id, so two nodes from the same peer set agree on every room. **Portable id** `NodeId::from_addr` = trimmed advertise-address bytes (a pure function), so every node derives the same id for a peer. **Env config** (`main.rs`): `CRDTSYNC_CLUSTER_PEERS` (comma-separated addresses) joins; self from `CRDTSYNC_NODE_ID`/`CRDTSYNC_ADVERTISE_ADDR`; `CRDTSYNC_REPLICATION_FACTOR` (default 3, rejects 0); unset peers ⇒ single-node, every room local, unchanged. Malformed peer list / blank self id / non-positive factor ⇒ clean startup error, never a panic. Threaded onto `ServeConfig`/`Registry` as `Option<Membership>` (`None` = single-node) — **held, not yet routed on** (Unit 3). Server-only (no `crates/core/src`), no new dep — CI miri unaffected. Tests (`crates/server/tests/membership.rs`, 17): parse edges, single-node default, self-view ↔ shared-placement agreement, two-node cross-agreement, address→id determinism.
  - **Cluster Unit 3 — request routing (redirect to leader) — DONE (#256).** Where placement first changes runtime behavior. A node that is **not** a room's leader (`!Membership::is_primary_for`, leader = the placement primary, pre-election) declines to serve it and answers `Message::Redirect { room, leader_addr }` (wire tag 23, core `protocol.rs`) — the primary's advertise address — instead of subscribing, ingesting a write, or persisting a durable version mutation; the client reconnects to the leader. **Redirect, not proxy** (DECISIONS 2026-07-12): the simpler MVP — no inter-node connection; the node points the client at the leader rather than forwarding. Direction guard: the client `receive` surfaces it via a drainable `take_redirects` (core holds no socket — it exposes the target, the transport reconnects, mirroring `onOpsRejected`); the server `step` rejects a client-sent `Redirect` as a protocol violation. **No membership (single-node) ⇒ never redirects — current behavior byte-identical** (regression tested). `step` gains `membership: Option<&Membership>`, wired from the `Registry`'s held view. Tests: core codec round-trip/truncation/trailing (`protocol_redirect.rs`) + client surface (`client_redirect.rs`); server `routing.rs` (subscribe/ops/version-write redirect off the leader, self-led served normally, single-node/solo never redirects, direction guard). Targeted Miri clean (protocol/client). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-12). Deferred follow-ons below.
    - _**Unit 3b — transparent proxy** (the node forwards a room's traffic to the leader over an inter-node connection, instead of redirecting the client) — the ARCHITECTURE proxy-OR-redirect alternative; needs the Unit 4 leader↔follower connection. **Follower read-serving — DONE (#316):** a caught-up follower serves a READ (a Subscribe/catch-up) from its own replicated state — **bounded staleness by default + read-your-writes / monotonicity** via the client's `last_seen_seq` floor (its highest observed server seq). It serves iff it is a replica of the room, holds a materialized copy (caught up, never torn), and its watermark (`Hub::seq`) ≥ the floor; any failure — uncaught, non-replica, read past the floor, or a write — **fails safe by redirect** to the leader. Reuses `last_seen_seq` as the floor rather than a new wire field (a dedicated `min_read_seq` would churn 118 `Subscribe` sites for no shipped-slice gain; the pre-echo write-seq RYW token for a *stateless* writer is the enumerated follow-on). Server-only, no core change; `session.rs` `read_redirect_response` + `Hub::holds_room`; tests `crates/server/tests/follower_reads.rs` (10), routing/late_joiner regressions green. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-20), ARCHITECTURE §Horizontal-Scaling. **SDK redirect drain — DONE (#260).** FFI (`crdtsync_client_take_redirects` + `encode_redirects`, cbindgen header regen), Python (`Client.take_redirects`), Go (`(*Client).TakeRedirects`), wasm (`takeRedirects`) all drain the core queue and surface the `(room, leader_addr)` pairs — an exact mirror of the `take_rejected` drain (#240). SDK/FFI-only, no core change; per-layer tests (drive a `Redirect`, assert the pair, empty/second-drain, bad-handle → -1); FFI Miri clean. **Auto-reconnect-on-redirect** (the app dialing `leader_addr` and re-subscribing) stays deferred — app glue, not a core/SDK unit; core exposes the target, the app acts on it._
  - **Cluster Unit 4 — replication protocol (leader→followers wire) — DONE (#257).** The leader mirrors every committed op to its follower replicas, so a redirect (Unit 3) lands on a node that already holds the room's state. Two node-to-node wire frames (core `protocol.rs`): `Replicate { room, branch, ops, base_seq }` (tag 24, reuses the op codec) leader→follower, `ReplicaAck { room, through_seq }` (tag 25) follower→leader. The client surfaces neither and `step` rejects a client-sent one as a violation (the Redirect direction-guard pattern). Server (`crates/server/src/replication.rs` + `registry.rs`): after a commit advances a room's head the leader queues one `Replicate` per follower in `replicas_for(room)` minus self; a follower applies it via the ingest path and answers `ReplicaAck { through_seq = new head }`; the leader records a per-`(room, follower)` acknowledged watermark (built for Unit 5 to read — client acks are **not** gated on it yet). Every path gated on `Membership`: **single-node (no membership) dials no peer, sends no frame, is byte-identical to today** (regression tested). **Transport = reuse the WS relay path** (DECISIONS 2026-07-13): a minimal outbound peer-connection task per member (`runtime.rs`) dials over the same WebSocket clients use (empty-`app_id` `Hello` ⇒ relay) and redials on drop — no second listener/codec. **No epoch/term field** (DECISIONS 2026-07-13): fencing lands with Unit 6, breaking the wire then. Replication covers the `main` stream; branch replication is a later unit. Tests (`crates/server/tests/replication.rs`): in-process two-node (Miri) — replicate-to-every-follower, follower-applies-and-advances-watermark, stale-ack-floor, non-leader-originates-nothing, branch-frame-dropped, single-node-unchanged; loopback socket (`#[cfg_attr(miri, ignore)]`, both wire directions). Core codec (`protocol_replicate.rs`) round-trip/truncation/trailing, targeted Miri clean.
  - **Cluster Unit 5 — majority-ACK durability — DONE (#258).** The leader gates a client's write-ack (`Accepted`) on a majority of the room's replica set having acknowledged the write's server sequence, reading the per-`(room, follower)` watermark Unit 4 built. **Quorum = `floor(R/2)+1`** over `replicas_for(room)` (primary self + followers); self holds the committed write and is the implicit one, so `floor(R/2)` **follower** acks release it — R=3 → 1 follower, R=5 → 2. **Withhold, not immediate:** the write path parks a `(room, seq, conn)` record in `Registry::pending_acks` instead of queueing `Accepted`; each follower `ReplicaAck` (`record_replica_ack`) re-checks the room's withheld writes and releases every one the fresh watermark carries to a majority (`Cmd::PeerAck` flushes). **Immediate ack (byte-identical regression):** single-node / no membership, self-only set (R=1), a branch write (not mirrored yet), a no-op resend (nothing fresh). **A below-quorum write is never falsely acked — no timeout** (no failure detection until Unit 7); a withheld ack is dropped on author `disconnect`, so a stalled room retains no orphaned records. Gate rests on the from-start-follower sequence equality (`hub.seq` = monotonic `base_seq+log.len()`, stable under compaction); a late follower with no backfill legitimately stays withheld (deferred backfill scope). Server-only (no `crates/core/src`) → CI miri non-required/unaffected; a shared `quorum` helper keeps the majority count and `enqueue_replication`'s fan-out from diverging on who is a follower. Tests (`crates/server/tests/majority_ack.rs`, 10, in-process Miri-clean + 2 loopback end-to-end `#[cfg_attr(miri, ignore)]`): R=3 after-one-follower, 1-dead-follower still majority, R=5 stalled-below-quorum never acks, stale-ack does not release a later write, single-node + R=1 immediate, resend immediate, disconnect-drops-withheld; socket released-after-follower + stalls-with-no-follower. Two review agents (majority math cleared; `pending_acks`-on-disconnect leak fixed + regression-tested; drop-safe gate). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-13). → *Horizontal Scaling*.
  - **Cluster Unit 6a — leader failover by liveness (HRW-next-live promotion) — DONE (#267).** The **election gate is RESOLVED** (human ruling): leader election = **HRW-next-in-line + epoch fence, structured to evolve into Raft** (the epoch/term becomes Raft's term, `RoomLog.head` is already the log index, HRW-next is the deterministic selection that later swaps for a RequestVote quorum). 6a ships the **liveness-promotion half**: leadership tracks reachability, not just placement. `Membership` gains a per-node liveness view (`down: HashSet<NodeId>`, `self` always live; `mark_node_live`/`mark_node_down`/`is_live`) and `effective_primary_for(room)` = the first LIVE replica in `replicas_for` (HRW) order — **byte-identical to `primary_for` while every replica is up**, promoting the next live replica only when the placement primary is down. The leadership decision points route on effective leadership: the redirect target (`session.rs`), the replication-origination gate + the follower apply gate (`registry.rs`); `primary_for`/`is_primary_for` stay where *placement* (not leadership) is meant. **Liveness signal = the Unit-4 inter-node relay link** (works for idle rooms, no new wire frame): a peer's link connecting marks it up, dropping/failing-to-dial marks it down — `runtime.rs` peer task → `Cmd::PeerLive` → `Registry::set_peer_liveness`; peers default optimistically live (empty `down` ⇒ startup == placement). A non-owner seeing every replica down redirects at the placement primary rather than serving orphaned writes. **No split-brain fence yet** (a recovered stale primary can still serve a write it missed; a noisy single-link signal can transiently false-promote) — the accepted 6a gap, closed by 6b. Single-node (no membership) + steady-state all-live cluster unchanged — regression-tested. Server-only (no `crates/core/src`) → CI miri non-required/unaffected. Tests (`crates/server/tests/failover.rs`, 12, in-process injected liveness → Miri-clean): effective==placement steady state, primary-down promotes next-live, two-down third-leads, recovery reclaims, self-always-live+promoted, redirect follows effective leader, promoted node serves+originates replication, promoted node rejects the old primary's Replicate, non-owner-all-down redirects, single-node + steady-state regressions. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-13). → *Horizontal Scaling*.
  - **Cluster Unit 6b — epoch/term split-brain fencing (+ takeover reconciliation) — DONE (#PR).** The second failover slice: closes 6a's un-fenced-recovery gap with a per-room, monotone **leadership `epoch` = Raft's term**. On the wire, `epoch: u64` is added to `Message::Replicate` (core `protocol.rs`, after `base_seq`) — the **pre-release wire break** the Unit-4 log anticipated; `ReplicaAck` needs none (a demoted leader learns the higher epoch by receiving the new leader's stream, not from an ack). A mutable sibling of the pure-placement `Membership` — `LeadershipEpochs` (`crates/server/src/leadership.rs`), per room the highest epoch *seen* + the epoch this node *leads* at — drives it: a node **claims leadership at `max(seen)+1`** on a fresh generation (promotion bumps the epoch) but keeps its epoch while already leading `>= seen` (a **stable leader holds one term**, inert steady state). A follower **fences** a `Replicate` whose `epoch` is below the highest it has seen (dropped, not applied, not acked — a recovered stale primary cannot replicate, no resurrection), and a frame at a **strictly higher epoch supersedes** this node's own leadership even as the placement primary — it steps down and converges on the new leader's stream (step-down deferred until the frame is committed to, so a rejected frame never churns the epoch — caught in review, regression-locked). **Reconciliation scope:** no-resurrection is the delivered+tested guarantee; full Raft log-truncation of a divergent uncommitted tail is unnecessary under the **op-set CRDT merge** (concurrent ops converge by construction — only a fenced op could wrongly re-enter, which the fence closes), so nothing half-working ships. **Residual → Unit 7/6c (documented):** epoch state is in-memory (a single-node restart re-observes the room epoch from the live leader before it can re-lead), and epoch-aware *routing* after a stale primary recovers needs an epoch→leader-identity map (deferred); neither weakens the fence. Single-node + steady-state all-live byte-identical (regression-locked). **Core-touching (`protocol.rs`) → CI Miri gates the PR**; in-process fence logic Miri-clean. Tests: `epoch_fence.rs` (10), `failover.rs`/`replication.rs`/`majority_ack.rs` regressions, `protocol_replicate.rs` codec (epoch round-trip/truncation). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-13). → *Horizontal Scaling*.
  - **Cluster Unit 7a — gossip membership discovery (dynamic member-set convergence) — DONE (#270).** The member set was static config for the process lifetime (Unit 2); it is now **dynamic, converged by anti-entropy gossip** — a node need only know one seed peer at boot, and the full membership propagates cluster-wide within a few rounds. **Wire (core `protocol.rs`):** `Message::Gossip { members: Vec<(node_id, advertise_addr)> }` (tag 26) — a plain member list, hand-rolled length-prefixed pair codec mirroring `Replicate`, decode total (truncation/over-count/trailing → `ProtocolError`, never a panic). **No incarnation/version-vector yet** — a set union needs only the member list; incarnation earns its keep with failure-detection refutation (7b). Node-to-node — a client that sends one violates (`session.rs`), the core client rejects an inbound one. **Dynamic membership (`membership.rs`):** the fixed set becomes mutable — `add_member(node, addr)` (idempotent; rebuilds the HRW `Cluster` from the **canonicalized sorted+deduped set** only when the set changes) + `known_members()` (the `(node, addr)` payload). **Determinism = the load-bearing property:** two nodes that learned the same members compute byte-identical `primary_for`/`replicas_for` regardless of learning order, so placement converges as the set converges. Each member's advertise address is tracked so a learned member is dialable. **Gossip (`gossip.rs` + `runtime.rs`):** push-pull anti-entropy — each round a node picks a random known peer and both sides union the other's set; **purely additive for 7a, no removal** (leave/failure is 7b; a member's liveness still rides the 6a relay link in the interim). **Transport reuses the WS relay peer path** (empty-`app_id` Hello), spawned behind the **same cluster gate as replication** — single-node runs no gossip, byte-identical to today. Peer choice draws system entropy at the transport edge, kept out of the deterministic (socket-free, test-driven) membership logic. Core-touching (`protocol.rs`) → **CI Miri gates the PR**; union/placement Miri-clean. Tests: `crates/core/tests/protocol_gossip.rs` (8 — round-trip empty/one/many/binary, truncation/over-count/trailing errors, tag distinct); `crates/server/tests/gossip.rs` (12 — seed-only 3-node convergence + placement agreement, order-independent placement, idempotent re-gossip no-churn, empty-id-pair dropped, batch-union, single-node no-gossip + drops-stray-Gossip, learned-member-becomes-live-replica/redirect-target, registry-merge-grows-view; one loopback socket exchange `#[cfg_attr(miri, ignore)]`). **Residuals (documented → 7b/follow-on):** replication + relay-liveness peer-connections are still dialed from the static boot set — 7a converges the placement *view* a dynamically-joined node routes on, but the existing nodes' replication delivery + liveness reading of a member learned *after* boot is wired with 7b; a node's gossip identity is its `self_id` (a `node_id` distinct from how peers seed it is a pre-existing Unit-2 identity inconsistency, out of scope); an empty-id gossip pair is dropped at the union so one bad frame cannot inject a phantom. Local review (medium, 3 angles) drove the identity-divergence + empty-id + missed-tick fixes. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-13). → *Horizontal Scaling*.
  - **Cluster Unit 7b — gossip failure detection (SWIM suspicion + incarnation refutation) — DONE (#272). THE LAST CLUSTER UNIT — the distributed-cluster epic (§Horizontal-Scaling) is COMPLETE.** 7a converged the member set but every member was *optimistically live*; 7b makes the gossip layer detect and disseminate failure, feeding 6a's liveness so a dead node is excluded from room leadership **cluster-wide**, not just by whoever's relay link to it dropped. **Wire (core `protocol.rs`, breaking tag-26 change, pre-release, no migration):** `Message::Gossip`'s member tuple widens to `(node_id, advertise_addr, incarnation: u64, state)` where `state` is `MemberState { Alive, Suspect, Dead }` (a tag byte 0/1/2; `MemberState` derives `Ord` least-to-most-suspicious so `Dead > Suspect > Alive` is the merge tie-break). Decode total (bad state tag → `BadTag`, truncation/over-count/trailing → error, never a panic). **Detection (`membership.rs` + `gossip.rs`, reuses the gossip exchange — no separate detector socket):** a successful round is first-hand proof alive (`note_gossip_reachable`); a failed round counts toward suspicion (`note_gossip_unreachable`) — `SUSPECT_AFTER_FAILURES=3` failed rounds → `Alive→Suspect`, `DEAD_AFTER_FAILURES=6` → `Suspect→Dead`. Count-based not wall-clock, so the state machine is deterministic + test-drivable. **Merge (`merge_liveness`, SWIM anti-entropy):** higher incarnation always wins, equal incarnation the more-suspicious state wins; commutative + idempotent (order-independent convergence). **Refutation:** a node told it is itself `Suspect`/`Dead` bumps its incarnation above the received one and re-asserts `Alive` (`refute_if_stale`) — the correction outranks the stale suspicion everywhere, so a live node is never stuck dead. **Wired into 6a:** the gossip signal and the 6a relay-link signal are **UNIONED** (`is_live` = relay-up AND not-gossip-Dead) — neither masks the other (a gossip-`Alive` doesn't resurrect a relay-down node; a relay reconnect doesn't resurrect a gossip-`Dead` node — only a refutation does); a `Suspect` still routes optimistically, only `Dead` excludes from `effective_primary_for`, so failover fires on a confirmed death and a refuted/recovered node reclaims its rooms. **Core-touching (`protocol.rs`) → CI Miri gates the PR**; detection/merge/refutation Miri-clean. Tests: `crates/core/tests/protocol_gossip.rs` (12 — every-state round-trip, bad-state-tag/truncation/over-count/trailing errors, distinct tags, suspicion ordering); `crates/server/tests/gossip.rs` (24 — suspect→dead escalation + failover, both-survivors-independent, success-clears-suspicion, refutation-restores + overrides-Dead, higher-incarnation-wins, equal-incarnation-Dead>Suspect>Alive, order-independent merge, cross-node dissemination without a dropped link, relay-vs-gossip union both directions, single-node inert; one loopback socket `#[cfg_attr(miri, ignore)]`). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-14). **Deferred (documented, non-blocking — see Revisit):** SWIM indirect probing (ping-req via k relays), member *reaping* (Dead stays in the set marked down), 7a's late-joiner replication-dial wiring. → *Horizontal Scaling*.
- **Branch layer remaining (exposure-gated / out-of-scope):**
  - **Unit 6b — branch-management client-wire surface — DONE (#275, see Done).** The 2026-07-14 Q2 gate resolved to **new client-wire frames** (in-band, SDK-native, same-session auth), not the admin plane. `BranchList`/`Branches`/`BranchFork`/`BranchForkFromVersion`/`BranchRestore`/`BranchPublish`/`BranchDelete` (tags 27–33) mirror the Version* set, room-keyed; server handlers wire the `Hub` seams at the version-management authz tier; FFI/Go/Python landed (no wasm SDK exists). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-14).
  - **Unit 7b — diff query surface (core + server + FFI/Go/Python/wasm) — DONE (#277). THIS CLOSES THE BRANCH-LAYER CLIENT EXPOSURE (6b management + 7b diff query) — the branch epic's client surface is COMPLETE.** Unit 7 (#251) built the server-side `Hub::diff_versions`/`diff_branches` computation; 7b returns a diff to a *client* over a new client-wire frame, reusing 6b's client-wire-query approach per the user's 2026-07-14 Q2 ruling (app-facing queries ride in-band frames, not the admin plane). **Core (`protocol.rs`, tags 40/41 — deliberately distinct from the 27–33 Branch* block + the Zones sibling's SUBSCRIBE-region tags):** `DiffQuery { room, kind: DiffKind, a, b }` → `DiffResult { room, changes }`. `DiffKind { Versions, Branches }` (tag byte 0/1) selects the state space; `changes` is the **encoded `Change` list** carried as an opaque length-framed `Vec<u8>` — the same `encode_changes`/`decode_changes` codec the FFI diff bindings (#232/#113) already marshal, so protocol.rs stays free of diff internals. Room-keyed like Branch* (runnable before any subscription) — **superseded by C27 (#375)**, which made the query channel-keyed because a change list carries the room's own content and the channel is what carries the reader's zone scope. Hand-rolled codec, total decode (round-trip empty/many-change, truncation, bad-kind-tag, trailing, distinct-tag tests). `ClientSession` frames the query and folds a `DiffResult` into a per-room decoded-`Change` view (`diff_query`/`diff`); a malformed change payload → `ClientError::BadDiff`, no view touched. **Errors:** DiffError→client via a new `ErrorCode::NotFound` (wire tag 7) — unknown version/branch is a recoverable Error frame (connection stays open, mirroring a denied branch op); a snapshot that fails to decode → `internal` (server fault, closes). *(Superseded twice: C27 (#375) made the decode fault recoverable rather than closing, and C51 gave a branch this node holds but cannot read its own `Internal` answer, so `NotFound` now means a name the room does not have and nothing else.)* **Server (`session.rs`):** `DiffQuery` gated by the SAME read tier as `BranchList` (`branch_authorized(.., Action::Read)` — a diff is a room-state read, no new authz axis), dispatched per `kind` to `Hub::diff_versions`/`diff_branches`, `Change` list encoded into `DiffResult`; served locally (no leader redirect — a read). Unauthenticated → violation, denied → recoverable `Forbidden`, unknown → `NotFound`, never a panic. **SDK (full 4-way fan-out):** FFI `crdtsync_client_diff_query(client, room, kind:u32, a, b)` + `crdtsync_client_diff_result(client, room, out)` returning the encoded change bytes a binding feeds to the EXISTING `crdtsync_diff_decode` (cbindgen header regenerated, symbol-assert extended, `error_code_discriminant` maps NotFound→7); Go `(*Client).DiffQuery`/`DiffResult` + `DiffKind` consts; Python `Client.diff_query`/`diff` + `DiffKind` IntEnum + `ErrorCode.NOT_FOUND`; wasm `diffQuery`/`diff` (`crates/wasm`) + `ErrorCode::NotFound`. **Core-touching (`protocol.rs`) → CI Miri gates the PR**; targeted core + FFI Miri clean locally. Specs: `crates/core/tests/protocol_diff.rs` (7), `client_diff.rs` (5), `crates/server/tests/diff_query.rs` (7 — version/branch diff matches `Hub`, self-diff empty, unknown version/branch NotFound, read-denied Forbidden, before-auth violation), FFI `client.rs` diff round-trip, Go/Python/wasm marshal+receive tests. Local correctness review (medium) clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-14). → *Versioning and Branches / Schema-Aware Diff*.
  - _With 6b + 7b done, the branch layer's client surface is **COMPLETE** (subscribe #250, management #275, diff query #277); the only remaining branch work is cross-branch merge (out of v0.x, ARCHITECTURE line 636). Next cs-next replenishment epic: **distributed cluster** is COMPLETE (#272); the ungated frontier (Zones Unit 5 subscribe SDK surface now DONE #281 — the zones epic client surface is COMPLETE) is Schema-Migration Unit 8 or the smaller v0.2 items (webhooks — event-bus #191 substrate done; awareness auth-filter §850)._
  - **STILL GATED** (needs a human/seam ruling — do NOT cut): Unit 9c (repair-closure). **Ungated 2026-07-14** by the server **element-context index** (id→context over the room's document projection, PR#): Zones **1b-ii-b (cross-zone move)** is DONE, and its foundation unblocked Zones **Unit 2 (per-zone lamport + envelope `zone`, DONE #276)** — whose substrate unblocked **Unit 3 (server per-zone streams + zone-scoped subscribe/redaction, DONE #278)**, then **Unit 5 (zone-scoped subscribe SDK surface, DONE #281 — zones epic client surface COMPLETE)** — and Unit 8 type-scope + container-field migration filters (the id→declared-type resolution the fan-out seam lacked now layers on the same `element_paths` projection). After the branch layer: the **distributed-cluster** epic (§Horizontal-Scaling, also designed → ungated) is the next replenishment.

## ⏭ Next

**C130 — the live fan-out's exemption from the withheld-run frame rests on one replica per identity, and nothing enforces that within one actor (crates/server) — READY, needs a ruling. Filed by C14 (#PR), reproduced by its falsification pass.** C14 gives the catch-up delta a frame naming what it withheld of the recipient's own run, and deliberately gives the live fan-out none, on the invariant that a replica applies its own op at authoring time — so the live filter withholding its own echo leaves nothing missing. That is a property of **one replica per identity**, not of the frame, and the fan-out itself never asks who authored an op. Measured: a second connection declaring an existing `ClientId` **under the same actor** plants an op into a subtree the first may not read; the live filter withholds it entirely (`live frames to bob: []`); the first replica's next edit mints onto that sequence and the write is deduped away at ingest, `/a/after = None`. C23 (#389) refuses the *cross-actor* form (`OpsRejected{Forbidden}`, verified in the same measurement), so the residue is exactly the same-actor case — a client bug rather than an attack, but one that silently loses writes and that redaction makes worse than it is on an unredacted room. **The ruling is where it is closed, and there are two places.** Refusing a second live replica under one identity per room is the root fix and belongs beside **C96**'s ruling on when a claim is made; extending the frame to the live seam is the local fix and costs a per-recipient withheld-own computation on every fan-out batch, which the catch-up seam pays once per subscribe. → *Server / Fan-out*.

**C131 — a subscriber presenting another replica's `ClientId` is served that replica's withheld run (crates/server) — READY, needs a ruling. Filed by C14 (#PR); C9 (#357) named the boundary, C23 (#389) closed the other half of it.** A redaction hands the recipient back its own authorship, which is what keeps a restarted reader minting past ids the room's log binds — on the snapshot seam since C9, on the delta seam since C14. The recipient is the identity declared at Hello, and C23 bound a replica identity to the authenticated actor that first *writes* under it **at the write and deliberately not at the subscribe**, because a claim at the subscribe is a read lockout. So the read half is open: an actor that may read something in a room and presents another's `ClientId` is served that identity's withheld-run count and the id-space position it reaches — how much that replica published into subtrees the *subscriber* cannot read. C9 named this and filed the binding; C23 delivered the write half, and C14 widened what flows through the read half from a compacted room's projected frontier to every uncompacted room's delta. Bounded — one identity's own count and one scalar, no targets, no content, no other author — and it composes with **C96**'s squat rather than adding a new primitive. The ruling is C96's own (when a claim is made, and what an established claim refuses); the narrow answer available without moving that is to serve the withheld run only where the room's record already binds the identity to this actor and none where it is unclaimed, which costs a legitimate first-write-after-restart reader its repair. → *Server / Security*.

**C132 — the withheld-run frame buys durably-retained ids at eight wire bytes each, bounded only by the frame cap (crates/core) — READY, needs a ruling. Filed by C14 (#PR), measured by its falsification pass.** `Message::Frontier` names sequences the recipient reserves for its mint, and a reservation rides `encode_state` — deliberately, so a replica that persists its own state keeps the repair. Measured in release: a frame naming 1 000 000 sequences is **8 000 009 bytes** on the wire, decodes in 2.2 ms, costs 98 ms in `note_published`, and leaves **24 000 206 bytes** of retained state (from 117), with 20 such frames reaching 48 MB — a 1:3 amplification into durable state that nothing prunes. The decode bound is real in the narrow sense (the count sizes no allocation; a count beyond the body fails `UnexpectedEof`), and the CPU half is not a repeatable DoS — the mint's position advances past the walked block, so 1000 edits after a scattered million-entry frame cost 562 µs total. What is unbounded is *retention*, at a higher density per wire byte than ops manage, from a server the client has chosen to trust and which can already send a large `Ops` frame or a large snapshot. **The ruling is whether a cap is warranted and what happens past it**, which is the same question C96 has for the replica-identity record: the only fail-closed answer is refusing the catch-up, which denies the room. A bound derived from the delta's own window is the alternative worth pricing — the frame never honestly names more than the log holds. → *Core / Protocol*.

**C96 — a replica identity's claim is takeable in a room its owner has not yet written to, and the record it lives in grows on the squatter's terms (crates/server) — READY, needs a ruling. Filed by C23 (#389), which measured both.** C23 binds a replica identity to the authenticated actor that first *writes* under it in a room. A `ClientId` rides every op its replica authors, so it is public wherever that replica has written — and first-write is therefore a race the owner only wins where it has already written. In a room it has not, any actor that room already lets write can author one op under the owner's id and take the claim, after which the owner's own writes are refused there. Pinned by `a_claim_taken_before_the_owner_writes_locks_the_owner_out_of_that_room`. It is bounded — the squatter needs write authority on that room, the owner is told (`OpsRejected`/`Forbidden`) rather than disconnected, and the room the owner *has* written in is protected, which is the room its ids are actually spendable in — but it is a denial the pre-C23 tree did not have, and with no rotation (C94) the owner cannot recover from it. **And the squat still lands the original attack, not only the lockout**: the first op under an unclaimed identity is what establishes the claim, so a ceiling stamp spends the owner's mint in that room *and* leaves the identity in the squatter's name — the gate narrows the *second* actor, never the first. The same freedom sizes the record, and cold review measured that it is not bounded by connection count either: `ClientId::for_channel` derives a distinct identity per channel number and nothing caps channels on a connection, so one connection mints claims until the u32 channel space runs out. Each claim rewrites and fsyncs the whole `RoomMeta`, so N claims cost O(N²) bytes written with no eviction — 64 channels on one connection measured a 107→3761-byte record and 123 776 bytes rewritten and fsync'd, which extrapolates to ~3.8 MB of permanent room metadata and ~125 GB of synchronous writes from a single authorized connection. A cap has to answer what happens past it, which is the other half of this ruling. One more the same review found: `is_authenticated(b"")` is true, so an **empty** actor stored in `RoomMeta` is accepted as a claimant on load — unreachable unless the auth layer can produce an empty actor, and cheap to close with whichever rule the cap takes. **The ruling is when the binding is made.** Claiming at *subscribe* closes the race for a connected owner but turns the same squat into a **read** lockout, which is strictly worse and is why C23 did not take it; claiming at Hello has the same shape one step earlier. An operator surface to release a claim makes the squat recoverable without changing when it is made, and needs a rule for who may call it (the room's creator is the obvious root). A cap on the map bounds the growth but has to say what happens past it, and the only fail-closed answer — refusing further writes — is a denial of the room. Whichever wins also decides whether the record needs an eviction story at all. **C100 is the one filing that feeds this ruling** rather than being independent of it: a branch write whose tail persists and whose head pointer does not lands durably, is reported to its author as a failure, and takes no claim — so it produces the same unclaimed-identity-with-a-stamp-in-the-room state this entry is about, by a second route. Whichever way the binding moves has to cover it. → *Server / Security*.

**C99 — an `Ops` frame carrying no ops bootstraps a room's doc-ACL authority root (crates/server) — READY. Filed by C23 (#389), found by its review; pre-dates it.** `ensure_creator` runs on any `main` write whose ingest returns `Ok`, and an empty batch does: it appends nothing, persists nothing, and answers `Ok(vec![])`. So the first authenticated actor to send a *no-op* `Ops` frame at an unestablished room becomes its creator — the doc-ACL authority root that owns `/` — without ever authoring a byte the room retains. That is the same reserve-by-no-op C23 (#389) closed one tier down for the replica-identity claim, which now requires a batch that actually landed an op, and it is the sharper of the two: a creator holds authority over the whole document where a claim holds one id space. The fix is the same shape — establish on a batch that landed — but `ensure_creator` is also reached from the replication path (a root arriving over a peer frame is judged by the same rule, deliberately), so the condition has to be stated once for both callers rather than bolted onto the client seam. → *Server / Security*.

**C100 — a branch write persists its tail and its head pointer in two steps, and reports a durable write as a failure (crates/server) — READY. Filed by C23 (#389), found by its review; pre-dates it.** `ingest_branch` calls `store.append_branch` (durable) and pushes the records into the in-memory tail, then advances the branch head through `mutate_branches(...)?`. A failure at that second persist returns `Err` over ops that are already on disk *and* already in the tail: `handle_ops` answers `failed to persist ops` and closes the connection, while the ops it disclaims will be folded into the branch and served to every subscriber. The author is told the opposite of what happened, and its natural retry resends ops the tail already holds, which dedup away — so no seam keyed on "this batch landed" ever fires for that write. C23's replica-identity claim is the first such seam and shows the shape of the damage: the write's identity stays unclaimed in a room its stamp is now in, and the owner cannot claim it by retrying (input to **C96**'s ruling). The ordering is not gratuitous — the head is `fork_point + tail.len()`, so it cannot be computed before the tail is committed — so the fix is either a single durable record covering both, or a reported outcome that distinguishes "nothing landed" from "landed, pointer stale", the latter being recoverable at the next write since the head is recomputed from the tail's length. → *Server / Durability*.

**C105 — a Go mutator refused at the language's type boundary leaves the previous edit's refusal reading standing (sdks/go) — READY. Filed by C23 (#389), found by its review.** C23 routed every *inert* edit in `core::path` through one seam, so a call that resolved to nothing still opens an intention and the refusal reading answers for it rather than for the edit before. Go has three mutators that never reach that seam: `CrdtMap.Set`, `CrdtList.Insert` and `CrdtList.Append` return `marshalValue`'s error before a path is resolved, so no intention opens and `Doc.Err()` keeps reporting the *previous* edit's refusal. Narrow — each returns the type error directly, which is the answer to read at that call site, and `Doc.Err()` now says so — but it makes the two readings disagree where the surface promises they agree, and Go is the one SDK where `Doc.Err()` is load-bearing (its chaining and value-less mutators have no error return). The list-index case of the same shape *was* closed here, by letting an out-of-range delete reach the core's inert seam instead of short-circuiting; these three cannot take that route, because a value with no CRDT scalar produces no bytes to pass down. The fix is an explicit inert step on the edit boundary — a `crdtsync_doc_inert`/`crdtsync_client_inert` on the C ABI running an empty transaction, mirrored on the Go `Backend` — which is new public surface and wants its own ruling rather than being smuggled in. → *SDK / Go*.

**C109 — a networked document reports an over-size blob as stored, because the client edit surface answers a frame for an edit that enqueued nothing (crates/ffi + sdks/js + sdks/python + sdks/go) — READY. Filed by C23 (#389), found by its cold review; pre-dates it.** `CrdtMap.SetBlob`'s bool answers "the blob is at this key", and it is derived two different ways. Against a local `Document` the core answers `Option`, so an over-size blob reads `None` and the bool is false. Against a `ClientBackend` the only signal is the returned frame, and `ClientSession::enqueue_ops(channel, vec![])` frames an *empty* op list into 5 non-empty bytes — so `len(frame) > 0` is true for an edit that enqueued nothing, and every binding reads it that way: `sdks/go/crdtsync/backend.go` (`return frame, len(frame) > 0`), `sdks/js/src/handles.ts` (`ops.length === 0`), `sdks/python/crdtsync/__init__.py` (`self._framed(...) or None`). Reproduced in Go and JS: a networked doc returns `true` from `SetBlob` for a 1 MiB blob and `GetBlob` finds nothing, where the same call on a local doc returns `false`. The caller's whole reason to read that bool is to know whether to upload out of band, so the failure is a silently dropped blob. It is the *size* half of the answer only — the mint half C23 added is correct at both backends, since a refusal is carried by the error rather than by the frame. The fix is to stop conflating "nothing to enqueue" with "a frame to send" at the client seam, which means changing what an existing C ABI entry point returns (`crdtsync_client_set_blob` answering an empty buffer when the core declined to inline) and re-reading it in three bindings — a behavior change to shipped surface, with its own tests at four seams. The same conflation is worth checking at every `client_*` mutator whose core counterpart is fallible. → *SDK / Blobs*.

**C94 — a replica whose id space is spent has no recovery that does not discard its identity (crates/core + crates/server + every SDK) — READY, needs a ruling. Filed by C23 (#389), which closed the two parts that do not need one.** C23 makes exhaustion *legible* (a raised `MintExhausted`) and stops a peer *causing* it under someone else's `ClientId` through the client plane. Neither gives a spent replica a way back. Honest exhaustion still ends a replica after 2^63 edits; so does a room whose durable claim record was lost and re-taken by another actor before the owner's next write; and an offline or peer-to-peer replica has no server holding a claim at all, which #366 already recorded as why neither of C23's parts can be the whole fix. Today the only remedy is a fresh `ClientId`, which discards the replica: ARCHITECTURE §Client ID pins "Renewal | only on storage wipe; no rotation", and a rotation re-keys `op_id`, every stamp, and every id derived from one (`acl_id`, `ranged_id`, an XML child), while the outbox of unacknowledged ops and the undo history are both keyed by the identity being retired — so the unsent work and the undo stack go with it, and a peer that already holds the retired identity's ids has to be told the successor is the same author or it will not dedup a resend. **The ruling is what a rotation is.** A clean break (a new id, the old replica's unsent work re-authored under it, the undo history dropped) is simple and loses the outbox; a *linked* rotation (the successor declares the retired id, so peers dedup a resend and the undo history survives) needs a successor record the room can decide from, which is new persisted state on the same bar C21's resolved-key set is held to, plus a rule for what the *server's* claim does when an actor rotates — and a linkage a peer honors is itself a way to inherit an id space, so it has to be authenticated by the same claim C23 established. Neither is derivable from ARCHITECTURE, which is why C23 filed this rather than half-building it. → *Core / Security*.

**C95 — the replica-identity claim is a client-plane gate, so a promoted follower comes up holding a room's ops with none of its claims (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Filed by C23 (#389).** C23 records which actor owns each replica identity in `Room.client_actors`, persists it in `RoomMeta`, and enforces it in `handle_ops`. Two seams stand outside that. The **peer plane** reaches `Hub::ingest` without crossing `handle_ops` — `Replicate` and `ReplicateSnapshot` are gated on the cluster secret and `gate_replica_frame` and look at no op's `id.client` at all — which is not new exposure (a node that can send `Replicate` can write anything, §Peer Plane) but does mean the gate is a client-plane rule only. The **frames carry no claims**: they carry the room's `creator` precisely because the state bytes hold no server-side metadata, and the claims are the same species of fact, so a follower replicating a room holds every op in it and none of its ownership record. On failover that follower leads a room whose identities are all unclaimed, and re-bootstraps each from whoever writes next — the window C23's durability closes for a restart, re-opened for a promotion. The fix is symmetric with C29's (#374): the claims ride `Replicate`/`ReplicateSnapshot` beside `creator`, composed set-once on arrival exactly as a root is, and judged by the same anonymous rule at every door. It is a protocol field addition, which is why C23 filed it rather than folding it in. The same filing notes the standing limit that needs no ruling: an identity whose only writer was ever anonymous is never claimed, since an anonymous actor is minted per connection and a claim under one would refuse that client's own next connection. → *Server / Replication*.
**C118 — `BranchList` reports `main`'s head, which is the room's whole-log head, to any reader holding room read (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found by the falsification pass on C31 (#395); reproduced, and it is the larger half of the channel C31 set out to close.** `branches_list` copies `BranchInfo { fork_point, head }` straight out of `Hub::branches`, and `Hub::observed_branches` sets `main`'s head to `Room::head()` — the room's log head, unnarrowed on every axis. The frame is gated on `branch_authorized(Action::Read)`, where the doc-ACL tier abstains, so a zone-limited subscriber passes by construction, and `Message::Branches` is also the reply to every branch *mutation*, so it is reachable a second way without asking. Measured on C31's own fixture and window: the narrowed catch-up watermark sits flat at `7` across five writes into a hidden zone while two `BranchList` frames one window apart report `main.head` as `7` then `12` — the count handed over directly. **This is the same number the scalar C31 narrowed used to carry** — `BranchInfo.head` for `main` *is* `Room::head()` — reached far more cheaply: that scalar needed the room compacted (for a `Snapshot`) or a capture to enumerate (for a `VersionState`), while a branch list needs neither and can be asked at any rate. `fork_point` is the same number one step removed — `BranchFork` clamps to the source head and the list then reports it. **The ruling is what a branch head means to a narrowed reader**, and unlike the watermark it has no resume job to keep: it is a product fact (how far a branch has run, whether it is behind `main`) that a branch UI reads, so the candidate answers are the C31 treatment — the head of that branch's stream as this reader's scope sees it — or refusing the field to a narrowed reader outright, which C30 has precedent for. Whichever is taken, `fork_point` needs the same answer or the leak walks one field over. → *Server / Zones, Branches*.

**C119 — the read-routing gate and the compaction branch are each a one-bit oracle that binary-searches a room-wide sequence (crates/server) — READY, needs a ruling on probeable gates. Found by the falsification pass on C31 (#395); both reproduced.** Two seams answer a *yes/no* question about an attacker-chosen `last_seen_seq`, and a comparison answered honestly enough times is the number itself. (1) `read_redirect_response` serves locally when `hub.seq(room) >= floor` and redirects otherwise, so ~20 `Subscribe` frames recover the answering node's whole-room committed watermark exactly — reproduced on a two-node fixture, recovered `37` against a truth of `37`. It is also **scope-blind by construction**: the gate resolves at the top of the subscribe handler, before `zone_scope` is computed at all, so no narrowing this or any later unit adds to the *served* answer reaches it. (2) `Hub::catch_up` returns a `Snapshot` exactly when `last_seen_seq < base_seq` and an `Ops` delta otherwise, so the same sweep recovers the compaction floor — and auto-compaction fires on the retained log's *length*, which counts every partition's writes, so `base_seq` steps with the room's total volume: measured across successive hidden-only windows as `(7,9), (11,12), (15,15), (15,18), (19,21), (23,24)`. C31 narrowed what the server *tells* a reader; neither of these tells it anything, so neither is touched by that ruling. A third form needs no probing at all and belongs here for the same reason: a zone-limited reader's *catch-up* watermark collapses to `0` when a compaction passes its last visible op, and compaction fires on the retained log's length, so the collapse is one bit about the room's total volume. C31 could refuse the field on the version seam, where nothing consumes it, but not here — the catch-up scalar is the client's resume cursor. **The ruling is what a gate may reveal by refusing**, which is a different question from what a frame may carry, and the honest answers are all costly: both comparisons are load-bearing (the first is read-your-writes and monotonicity, the second is whether the ops still exist), so the candidates are to quantise or floor the compared value per reader's scope, to make refusal indistinguishable from service by always serving a snapshot on a below-floor claim, or to rate-limit a probe — each of which trades a real property. Retiring the shared sequence space (C115's end state) dissolves both at once, which is the argument for sizing that epic. → *Server / Zones, Follower reads*.

**C117 — a client's resume floor counts the ops it was *delivered*, so a zone-limited reader's floor drifts below what it holds and the follower-read gate admits a replica behind it (crates/core + crates/server) — READY, needs a protocol field. Pre-existing; C31 (#395) widened its window and measured it.** `ClientSession::receive` advances a room's cursor by `ops.len()` on each delivered `Message::Ops` batch, and the fan-out gives a zone-limited channel only its own partitions' ops — so the cursor counts *deliveries*, not room sequences, and falls further behind the reader's true position with every hidden write. Against `Hub::catch_up` alone that is safe, and was the reason it stood: an under-estimate re-delivers and the replica dedups, never skipping. Against `read_redirect_response` it is not. That gate admits a follower whose committed watermark reaches the client's floor, so a floor below what the client holds admits a follower **behind** it; if that follower's compaction floor sits above the stale floor, the catch-up is a `Snapshot` rather than a delta, and `ClientSession` adopts a snapshot by **replacing the replica outright** (`room.doc = doc`) — dropping an op the reader had already applied, silently. Before C31 the snapshot seam padded this by resetting the cursor to the room's head; narrowing that scalar is right on its own terms (the head is the leak C31 closed) and removes the padding. Measured on one fixture — a compacted room, one visible op above the floor, five hidden, then a hidden/visible pair: the client's cursor reads **13 against content held through 14 on `main`, and 8 against the same 14 after C31** — a one-op window widened to six, and in general to the hidden traffic below the reader's last visible op. **The fix is not on the watermark**, which cannot be both the disclosure-safe value and a delivery count: it wants the *delivered batch* to name the room sequence it lands at, so a filtered client tracks position rather than volume — a field on `Message::Ops` (or an explicit cursor frame), which is the first time this protocol tells a client where a batch sits rather than letting it count. Note the field itself must be narrowed on the same rule C31 settled, or it re-opens what C31 closed. Distinct from C115 (the shared sequence space a narrowed watermark names) and C116 (a frame never narrowed at all). → *Client / Zones, Follower reads*.

**C116 — a publish and a restore mint the room's sequence into the version *name*, and `VersionList` hands every name over unnarrowed (crates/server) — READY, needs a ruling on durable names. Found by the cold review pass on C31's own head (#395), out of C31's own premise.** C31 narrowed the `seq` scalar on `Message::Snapshot` and `Message::VersionState` so a zone-limited reader cannot chart a hidden partition's write volume from it, and leaned on "version names are a room-read fact — `VersionList` returns them all" as the *enumeration* vector. Those names turn out to carry the number itself: `Hub::publish` mints `publish/<published>/<source>@<seq>` and `restore_as_branch` mints `audit/restore/<branch>@<seq>`, both formatting the raw room (or editor-branch) sequence into the name, while `versions_list` answers `Message::Versions` from `hub.version_names(room)` with no narrowing at all to any reader holding room read. So two publishes spanning a window of hidden writes hand a zone-limited reader that window's op count in cleartext — the same inference C31 closed, one frame over, and reachable without a single fetch. `autoVersion` names are timestamp-templated and clean, so this is publish and restore-as-branch alone. **The ruling is what a name may say**, and each answer costs something real: the sequence is *in* the name deliberately — it is what makes two publishes of the same `(source, seq)` the same version and a repeat restore at an unmoved `main` a no-op, so it is a dedup key, not decoration. Replacing it with an opaque digest keeps the dedup and loses an operator's ability to read a publish's position off its name; narrowing `VersionList` per reader instead keeps the names and asks what a zone-limited reader may enumerate at all, which is a question C31 explicitly did not answer for the *existence* of a capture. Related to but distinct from C115: that one is the shared sequence space a narrowed watermark still names, this one is a frame that was never narrowed. → *Server / Versions, Zones*.

**C115 — a narrowed reader's watermark still names a position in the room's shared sequence space (crates/server) — READY, needs the per-zone sequence space. Residue of C31 (#395), named there rather than half-closed; sibling to C116, which is a frame that was never narrowed rather than the space this one names.** C31 stopped the catch-up watermark advancing on a write into a partition a zone-limited reader cannot see, so a hidden-only window now reads exactly like an idle one. What it could not remove is the *sequence space itself*: the value it serves is the reader's last visible op's position in the room's whole log, so a reader that may write into its own partition still learns where its own op landed, and the difference between two of its own writes' positions counts the room's total ops between them — the same volume the scalar used to hand over, at the cost of a write and a re-read instead of two reads. No narrowing of a shared counter closes it, and the shape C31 rejected for the resume floor (a per-reader count, an opaque token) does not become right here: the floor is indexed into the log and compared against a node's committed watermark. What closes it is the per-zone sequence space ARCHITECTURE §Zones already names as the end state — a zone is "a separately replicated stream with its own lamport clock and op-log partition" — under which a reader's watermark is a position in its *own* partition's space and carries nothing about any other. That is a log-partitioning epic (per-zone logs, per-zone compaction floors, a replication frame per partition, and a `Subscribe` floor that names which space it is in), not a scalar, so it is sized and scheduled as one rather than approximated. → *Server / Zones*.

**C108 — the ungoverned-clone binding removal is undone by the next sweep, which re-binds the name from its present subscribers (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found by the cold review pass during C30 (#392); pre-dates it, and undercuts C28's stated intent.** `Hub::clone_room`'s `None` arm removes the destination's governing binding when the source is ungoverned, and its comment states why: "letting that stand would govern the copy by an app whoever named the destination picked, which is the caller choosing the schema its own clone is read under." `Registry::deliver`'s post-clone re-point drops the live `room_apps` entry with it. **The next sweep puts it back.** `reconcile_room_apps` keeps a room in `live` while it has a subscriber, finds no incumbent, and takes `apps.and_then(pick_app)` — the lowest app id among *present enforcing subscribers*, which is exactly the population that includes whoever named the destination and subscribed to it. Measured during C30: after `Registry::sweep`, a channel that was being refused an unprojectable state read is served one again, because the name has re-bound to the subscriber's own app. So the protection is one sweep deep. **Three properties fall out, and each is its own question.** The heal is **non-durable** — `reconcile_room_apps` writes only the live map, never `hub.bind_governing`, so the room's own binding stays absent until a later `Subscribe` mirrors it, which means a restart in between resolves the room to nothing (the C101 shape). It picks by **lowest app id**, not by any relationship to the clone or to the channels already bound, so a second enforcing subscriber on an alphabetically-earlier app takes the room — and if that app declares no zones, the window closes into **C106** rather than into a correct scope. And a **relay-only** room never heals, since a relay contributes nothing to `present`. Severity is bounded by the clone gate already requiring the caller to read `src` whole (`reads_source_whole`), so this is not source-content exfiltration; what the caller gains is the choice of `@auth` grants and zone block under which *other* readers see the clone, which is the thing C28 refused them. The fix wants a ruling on what an ungoverned clone's binding should be — permanently ungoverned (so the sweep must learn to leave it alone, which needs the distinction to be recorded somewhere durable), or the source's absence of one treated as a value rather than a gap. → *Server / Rooms*.

**C107 — the archive follows leadership rather than the room, so a node's captures are reachable only while it leads (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C33 (#394), which created the condition and declined to fix it.** `AutoVersionSink` records `EngineEvent::Subscribed` and `Registry::deliver` ends in `drain_auto_versions`, so a replica serving follower reads fires its governing schema's `on: subscribe` triggers and captures versions of its own state. The version index is node-local — replication carries the room's log, never its captures — so those captures live only there; and since C33 routes every version read to the room's leader, no wire request can reach them unless that node is later promoted. They are durable storage nothing can read: the sweep's retention still prunes them by `keep`, the store still writes them, and a client asking the room for its versions is answered by a node that does not hold them. **The auto-version trigger is the reachable case, not the whole of it:** a **demoted** node — one that led the room and no longer does — holds every capture taken while it led, client-created ones included, and those go unreachable by the same mechanism the moment leadership moves. So the entry is really about the archive following leadership rather than the room, of which the follower's own auto-captures are the case that needs no leadership change at all. Not a regression in what a client is *served* — before C33 a client could only reach them by happening to be connected to that replica, which was itself the defect — but it is now a settled leak of disk and a schema promise (`autoVersion`) the deployment does not keep. Two rulings are available and they are not equivalent: **suppress the capture on a node that does not lead the room**, which makes `autoVersion` mean "the room's leader captures" and loses nothing a reader could have had; or **replicate the version index**, which makes a room's captures survive a leadership move and so closes both halves at once. The first is a few lines and a narrower promise — it stops the follower accumulating captures nothing will read, and leaves a demoted node's archive stranded exactly as it is today; the second is a durability feature and is the only one that makes a room's version history a property of the room. This entry owns both halves. → *Server / Versions, Auto-version*.

**C113 — a version read is answered by the node that *believes* it leads the room, with no lease and no caught-up test (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C33 (#394), which bounded the exposure to this window and filed the window.** C33 routes every version read to the room's leader because only the leader's `acl_records` are the room's current authority. But leadership on the read path is `Membership::effective_primary_for` — `replicas_for(room)` walked for the first node this node's own SWIM gossip believes live. There is no lease, no quorum, and no fence: the persisted leadership epoch fences *applying* replication frames, not serving. So a replica that suspects a still-committing primary dead promotes itself locally and answers version reads redacted by the records it has, which is exactly the stale-grant redaction C33 exists to close, re-opened for the promotion window. The second half needs no false suspicion at all: even on a genuine failover, `redirect_response` never consults `holds_room` or `hub.seq`, so a replica that was *behind* when the primary died is promoted and serves its own captures through records it knows are incomplete. Pinned today as a passing test of what the code does (`a_lagging_promoted_leader_still_answers_from_its_own_records`), deliberately not as a property. The ruling is which condition a promoted node must meet before it answers a redaction-bearing archived read — a caught-up test against the epoch it was promoted at, a lease the old leader must lose, or an explicit statement that bounded-by-the-promotion-window is the accepted contract. Touches the same election seam as the HRW+epoch design gate, so it is a ruling about leadership authority that reaches past this seam. → *Server / Replication, Versions*.

**C114 — a `Redirect` names a room and no channel, so the JS provider pairs it with a request positionally (sdks/js) — READY, no dependencies. Found by the falsification pass during C33 (#394), which widened it.** `Provider.drainSignals` fails one pending request per redirect drained, oldest first, because a `Message::Redirect` carries `{room, leader_addr}` and nothing that says which request it answers. The pairing is right whenever every redirect in a drain was provoked by a pending request — but an **ops flush** on a follower redirects too (`handle_ops` takes `redirect_response`) and has no request behind it, so that redirect fails whatever was oldest instead: a `BranchList`, say, which the follower answers ungated, leaving the app a rejected promise *and* a `Branches` reply with nothing awaiting it. Pre-existing in shape — the `Error`-frame branch beside it pairs the same way — but C33 is what made it likely, since a version read on a replica now redirects on every request rather than only on a mutation. The `for` loop C33 added is a strict improvement (before it, a drain carrying several redirects failed exactly one and stranded the rest to `requestTimeoutMs`) and is not the fix. **The fix is a wire change**: a redirect answering a channel-keyed request should name the channel, which also lets a multiplexed client route it to the right room's transport rather than to the connection. That makes it the same question C61 raises for `DiffResult` — a reply that cannot be attributed to the request it answers — so the two want one ruling on request/reply correlation, and this is the second frame to need it. Until then the provider's comment states the approximation rather than claiming the pairing is sound. Python and Go expose only the raw frame, so neither strands. → *SDK / Provider, Client*.

**C112 — a room-keyed branch mutation decides its authorization gate on a node that then redirects it, writing an audit entry for a change it never made (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C33 (#394), which fixed the same defect on the channel-keyed frames and stopped at its own seam.** `BranchFork`, `BranchForkFromVersion`, `BranchRestore`, `BranchPublish` and `BranchDelete` each run `branch_authorized` and *then* `redirect_response`. `branch_authorized` reaches `acl::authorized`, which calls `Authorizer::observe` unconditionally, and `audit::Audited::observe` writes a durable record through `AccessLog` — so a follower asked to fork a branch records a granted write it does not perform, and an operator's audit query answers with mutations that never happened on the node that logged them. `CloneRoom` states the rule ("a node that will not clone must not decide the gate at all"), C33 applied it to the three version mutations, both version reads and the diff query by splitting `channel_room` into `bound_room` + `channel_authorized`, and left these five because they are **room-keyed** — the room rides the frame, so there is no channel to resolve and the fix is a plain swap of two adjacent checks rather than a split. Two of the five (`BranchForkFromVersion`, `BranchRestore`) read the version index besides, so they are literally the seam C33 moved. The swap has the same observable cost C33 pinned for its own frames — a denied actor learns the room's leader where it used to get `Forbidden`, for a room name it supplied itself and a leader `Subscribe` already resolves for any authenticated actor — so the ruling is whether that cost is paid uniformly across the wire or the audit seam is taught to record only what the node will act on. → *Server / Branches, Audit*.

**C103 — a branch diff is answered from whichever node holds the channel, and a follower holds `main` alone (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C51, which named it and covered the rest.** `Hub::diff_branches` resolves both sides against local state with no routing gate at all — unlike `Subscribe`, which runs `read_redirect_response` and lets a follower serve only when it holds the room, holds a materialized copy, and its committed watermark has reached the client's floor. Replication carries `main`: a follower holds no branch registry entry for a named branch the leader forked, so C51's `BranchState::Absent` is the *locally honest* answer for a branch that plainly exists, and the client is told `NotFound` for a name `BranchList` on the leader enumerates. C51 deliberately did not widen `Absent` to cover it — locally the branch really is absent, and a seam cannot report a fact it has no way to learn; what is missing is the redirect, not the taxonomy. A channel legitimately bound to `main` on a follower reaches the same wall from the other side, since a diff naming `main` against a named branch has one side the follower can serve and one it cannot. **C33 (#394) settled half of it and disproved the input this entry proposed.** A client-named freshness floor is inert on a channel-keyed request: a diff query, like a version read, arrives on an already-bound channel whose cursor the answering node advances only from what it delivered, so it can never name a sequence that node is behind on. C33 therefore routed the *version* arm of this same `DiffQuery` to the room's leader outright — the arm whose sides are the room's own captures, redacted by the same log-borne records — and left the **branch** arm here, because it is the one with a question a version diff does not have: what a replica may honestly answer about a branch it does not replicate, and whether *one* unservable side redirects the whole query.
