The consequence is the one C25 removed everywhere else: a member chooses which rooms it replicates, and from inside the replica set it reaches the leadership and durability gates. **The fix is a placement change, not an evidence one: weigh the ring by trust unit.** Score hosts rather than node ids, so a room's replica set is `n` distinct hosts and which rooms a host holds follows from a name it cannot grind; the member serving a host's position is then a local choice among that host's adopted members, and it does not matter which, since they are one trust unit already. That is also the better replication property — `n` replicas on `n` failure domains instead of `n` ids that may share a machine — and it makes the `replicas_for(room).contains(sender)` gate ask about exactly what the certificate vouches for. Costs to settle: two members on one host stop both replicating a room (a capacity change, not a correctness one), the replication factor becomes a host count, and every gate/redirect/quorum that names a member has to resolve host → member. Note this is *not* reachable without a compromised member's certificate: without peer mTLS the whole binding vouches for nothing anyway (C13's residual), and with it the mint is bounded to a host the operator already trusts to run a node. → *Cluster*.

**C36 — a node that reaps every configured peer can never rejoin its own ring, and a cluster whose members share a host can never admit one (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Raised by cold review during C25 (#369), re-scoped by pass 12, all reproduced.** **The reachability is wider than first filed: it is not "a two-node cluster", it is any cluster of any size, from one node's one-sided egress outage.** `rebuild_placement` seeds `adopted` from `configured` and `verifier_units` counts only *adopted* verifiers, so once a node's adopted set falls to a single trust unit its own vouch is the only one that can ever count and the fixpoint is stuck at `{self}` however completely the roster recovers. A node that loses egress for `DEAD_AFTER_FAILURES × REAP_AFTER_DEAD_TICKS` reaps all of its peers while they, still reaching it inbound, never reap it. Measured: after 1000 healthy rounds in which every peer was dialed and vouched, roster 9 and ring 1. C25 ships a **fail-closed** guard for the dangerous half — expressed as an explicit `is_stranded()` state rather than an empty ring, because an empty ring already meant *single-node mode* downstream and read as "serve every room at a majority of one", which is the very behaviour it was meant to stop (measured: 256/256 writes accepted, identical to unguarded, plus an unrepairable divergence sink since an empty ring also refuses the real leader's `Replicate` frames) — — such a node now holds *no* room rather than becoming sole replica and primary of every room at a majority of one, which would have released `Accepted` for writes no other replica holds while the rest of the cluster led the same rooms (measured 890/1000 rooms with two disagreeing leaders). What remains is availability, not split-brain: the node serves nothing until an operator restarts it, with no signal. Note this is the one place C25 is *worse than its predecessor* — pre-C25 the roster was the placement set, so re-learning restored the ring. The fix wants the bar expressible in the cluster it runs in. Adoption asks for `ADOPTION_VERIFIERS = 2` trust units other than the candidate's own, and two shapes cannot raise them. **Re-admission:** reaping drops a member from `configured`, so a returning node is a fresh join; in a two-node cluster the only other unit is the surviving node itself, so it verifies the returner a thousand times and the count never passes one — the survivor leads every room, the returner places over both, and the split is permanent with no operator signal and no repair short of restarting the survivor. Reachable by an ordinary long partition with no attacker, and reachable deliberately with C35's unrefutable `Dead`. **Admission:** a cluster whose members all share one host has exactly one unit, so no joiner ever clears the bar — a real shape (several instances per machine or pod), failing closed and silently. Both want the same thing: a bar that is expressible in the cluster it runs in, without becoming node-local (which is what made it non-convergent before). Candidates: a cluster-wide `configured`-derived denominator, an operator-set bar, or — cleanest — C34's placement over trust units, which makes "how many units exist" a thing the ring already knows. At minimum a node should *say* when its roster holds a member the bar cannot admit, instead of quietly not placing it. Also unbounded, and the same root: a **wildcard** SAN. The inbound binding refuses a wildcard deliberately (`cert_names_member` compares whole names), but the only thing that mints verification evidence is this node's *outbound* dial, which is plain TLS and honours one — so a member holding `*.x.example` answers truthfully at every name under it, each of which reads as a distinct trust unit, and each of which is then adoptable. Whatever C34 does about trust units has to close the outbound side too, or say that per-host certificates are a deployment requirement. → *Cluster*.

**C35 — one member can evict any other from the ring for good, and can flood every node's roster (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Raised by cold review during C25 (#369), both reproduced.** Two gossip-plane inputs are unbounded and one is unrefutable. **Eviction:** `refute_if_stale` bumps a victim's incarnation with `received.max(me.incarnation).saturating_add(1)`, so a peer advertising `(victim, u64::MAX, Dead)` saturates the counter — the victim re-asserts `Alive` at `u64::MAX`, the equal-incarnation rule keeps `Dead` winning, the refutation never lands anywhere, and the victim is suspected, declared dead and reaped cluster-wide. The merge primitive predates C25; what C25 changes is the consequence — reaping now strips a member from `configured`, so the victim loses root-of-trust status and must be verified by two trust units to return. One compromised member can walk the cluster this way. Wants either a refusal of a self-tuple at or near the ceiling, or a bound on how far one merge may move a member's incarnation. **Flooding:** the *reply* half of a round learns every tuple a dialed peer sends, with no cap on tuples per frame or on roster size; each entry then rides every later gossip frame and takes a probe slot, so a compromised member replying with 10⁵ fabricated ids dilutes the roster on every node that dials it. Also pre-existing, and also worse now: a genuine joiner is adopted only once two members have *individually dialed* it, so diluting the roster starves adoption and keeps honest joiners out of the ring indefinitely without the attacker ever touching them. Wants a per-frame tuple cap and a roster ceiling, with an eviction rule that is not itself a lever. **C25's pass 9 measured the cost, and it is a latency attack on the whole node, not just a memory one:** `accept_hdr_async` is called with no `WebSocketConfig`, so tungstenite's default 64 MiB `max_message_size` applies, and one 31.1 MB reply frame (500 000 tuples, under half the cap) is absorbed in 868 ms — after which *every subsequent 1-tuple merge* costs 316 ms and each `known_liveness()` snapshot 269 ms, inside the single-threaded registry actor that serves all client traffic, and the node re-emits that 31.1 MB frame to every peer on every round. Measured scaling: 10 k tuples → 5.3 ms per later merge, 50 k → 27.9 ms, 200 k → 120 ms. The evidence map is a roster × senders cross-product, so it compounds: merge time rose 29 ms → 57 ms over 20 self-introduced aliases at roster 20 k. A `WebSocketConfig` bounding a peer frame to `expected_members × tuple_size` is the cheap half of the fix. **It is also what makes C25's accepted claim-delivery window unbounded:** a claim naming a member the receiver has not met is dropped and re-sent only on the next round with that same verifier, and `choose_peer` draws uniformly from the roster — so inflating the roster inflates the wait. Measured: 200 000 injected ids gave 0/20 000 draws of a given voucher (expected ≈10⁵ intervals, about 28 h), against ~488 intervals at 1 000 ids. **C25's pass 7 measured the third door into the same map, and it is the one C25 itself opened:** the inbound filter admits a sender's own tuple so that a peer can introduce itself, and `authenticate_peer` binds a link to any canonical id the presented certificate's host names — so one certified host self-introduces once per port per scheme, taking the roster from 4 to 1003 in a measured run, each entry then retained as a verifier claim. C25's own roster bound on claims (a sender must be a roster member) therefore does not bound this path, because the sender puts itself on the roster. No mint: they are all one trust unit and `verifier_units` counts units, so nothing is ever adopted by it. The cost is availability — `choose_peer` samples the roster uniformly, so at 1000 junk members a gossip round is useful 0.3% of the time and adoption evidence, `Dead` escalation and failover all stall. Wants the evidence bounded per trust unit rather than per id, and self-introduction capped at one pending id per certificate host. → *Cluster*.

**C37 — the canonical node id is finer-grained than the address the resolver dials, so one endpoint still holds many ring positions (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found by cold review during C25 (#369), measured.** C25 gave an advertise address one canonical spelling so that one endpoint holds one ring position, and enforced it at every door. The reduction decides "is this an IP literal" with Rust's strict `IpAddr::from_str`, while the thing that actually resolves the address is `getaddrinfo`, which accepts a much larger language: `0xa.0.0.1`, `10.0.1`, `10.1`, `167772161` and `10.0.0.01` all connect to `10.0.0.1`, and each is a *distinct* canonical node id **and a distinct trust unit** (`member_trust_unit` reduces the host by the certificate binding's relation, which also reads them as text). `[::ffff:10.0.0.1]` is the same shape across families — a distinct id whose dual-stack connect reaches the IPv4 listener. So the pass-3 write-stall returns through the resolver instead of through the spelling: one vouch from the attacker, two honest nodes dial the alias, are answered by the honest member, and verify it **truthfully**, and the ghost is adopted onto rooms nobody ever speaks as. Under peer mTLS the dial never opens (`ServerName::try_from("0xa.0.0.1")` is an `InvalidDnsNameError`, and a 16-octet V6 name matches no 4-octet `iPAddress` SAN), so this bites in exactly the postures C25 concedes — but the mechanism is the *resolver*, not the binding, and C25's concession is phrased about the binding. Note C34 does not close it either: these are distinct hosts, so weighing the ring by trust unit leaves them distinct units. Wants the canonical form to reject or fold every host form the resolver would, rather than every one Rust's parser recognizes. → *Cluster*.

**C38 — a member adopted after startup is placed on rooms this node cannot replicate to (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found by cold review during C25 (#369).** `spawn_peers` builds the outbound `peer_conns` map **once**, from the roster as it stood at startup, and `dispatch_replication` silently drops a frame for a follower with no entry. Pre-existing — nothing in C25 changed either — but C25 is what makes the path ordinary: its stated success case is a genuine joiner clearing the adoption bar "within a few rounds", after which every node places rooms on it. Those rooms now have a replica this node never sends to and never hears from, so the joiner degrades exactly the quorum adoption exists to protect, and the drop is silent. Wants the peer connection set to follow the adopted set rather than the startup roster, with a signal when a placed follower has no link. **Two additions from C57 (#380):** the map is stale in the *other* direction too — a member the sweep reaps keeps its dialer task, redialing on the redial cadence a node the roster has durably dropped — and a link the roster drives wants a **generation**, so a frame is attributed to the peer incarnation that sent it: today an ack is attributed by its peer link's node id alone, with nothing naming the incarnation behind it, so a frame from a departed incarnation is indistinguishable from the returned member's own (C57 drops it only while the id is off the roster). → *Cluster*.



**C53 — a log-shared branch materializes truncated once `main` compacts, and says nothing (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C27 (#375) by review, reasoned not reproduced; filed there as C38 and renumbered here because that id was already taken.** `catch_up_branch`'s shared-base slice is `r.log[last_seen_seq.max(r.base_seq) - r.base_seq .. fork_point.min(r.head()) - r.base_seq]` — the *retained* log, which begins at `base_seq + 1`. Every op at or below the compaction floor is therefore absent from it, and `materialize_branch` folds what it gets into a **fresh** `Document`, so a branch forked at 10 in a room compacted to 5 materializes from seqs 6..10 alone; with `base_seq >= fork_point` it materializes from its divergent tail alone. Nothing signals it: the answer is `Some(bytes)`, not `Catchup::Unavailable`, and `compact` advances the floor with no guard tying it to any live fork point. One consequence stands, and it is the read: a fresh subscriber to such a branch is caught up to a state missing that content. The two the entry was filed with have since been closed at the seam rather than at the cause — C60 (#386) made a clipped shared base yield no tree, so the diff answers `UnreadableBranch` rather than reporting pre-floor content as an add (C51), and `publish`, which materializes the room's *active* branch, freezes nothing rather than writing the truncated state over the published branch's base *and* into a captured version. That was the silent data loss, reachable once a restore-as-branch had made a log-shared branch the active HEAD (a `main` HEAD materializes through `export_room`, the live replica, and a snapshot-forked branch owns its base, so neither truncates). Fix wants the shared-base catch-up to answer `Catchup::Unavailable` when the base it must serve is no longer retained, and/or a compaction floor that will not advance past a live fork point. A second route to the same wrong `Some` — an *orphaned base* rather than a dropped floor — is **C104**, filed separately because its cause and its ruling are independent of this one's. Distinct from C51, which is about the ways the same seam answers no state at all; this is the way it answers `Some` with the wrong bytes. → *Server / Branches*.

**C129 — a subscriber admitted against an unbound room's high-water is never re-checked once it binds the room itself (crates/server) — READY, needs a ruling. Found by the falsification pass during C54 (#396), which narrowed it to the `Hub` API before filing it; the defect predates that unit and was verified pre-existing at `70698b9e` rather than assumed.** `subscriber_reaches_governing` abstains when the room names no governing app — `governing_target` requires one — so a room holding an op-version high-water *without* a binding admits **every** joiner, however low its version. The joiner's own subscribe then binds the room at *its* version, and nothing re-checks it: the stranded-peer eviction fires only on a genuine **lift**, and the high-water did not move. **How narrow this is now, measured rather than estimated.** The state was reachable through ordinary server traffic when it was found: a subscribe binds a room before the first write materializes it, a dormant-room sweep then prunes the *hub's* copy because the hub does not hold the room while the presence map keeps it, and the first write tagged from the map and raised the high-water — leaving `hub_governing=None, max_op_version=Some(2)`, durable across a restart, and reached again by a clone of that source and by a restarted leader's follower dial. C54 closed every one of those by having a write re-assert the binding it tagged from, so the room's own record carries both; each route is pinned by a test shown to reproduce with that one statement removed. The assertion is **unconditional on the lift** — it is made whenever an ops write resolved a binding, not only when the write moved the high-water — which is what keeps the relay and foreign-app cases safe: neither raises a number, and neither is asked to. What remains is `Hub::ingest`, which is public, takes a `schema_version`, and binds nothing — so an embedder driving the hub directly can still produce the pair, and `clone_room` refuses to carry a number it cannot attribute rather than propagating one. **The ruling is what an unbound room's high-water may be checked against.** The joiner is the room's incumbent-to-be and `acting_schema` already falls back to a subscriber's own app for exactly that reason, so checking the number against the joiner's own chain is the precedented answer — at the cost that a wrong guess refuses the only client that could ever bind the room, wedging it. The alternatives are to re-check on the *bind* rather than only on a lift, which admits and then immediately evicts, or to refuse to hold a high-water the hub cannot attribute at all, which makes the invariant total at the cost of `Hub::ingest`'s current freedom. → *Server / Migration*.

**C120 — a replica's op-version high-water can be pinned by a peer and never brought back down (crates/server) — READY, needs a human ruling; the same ruling C56 is waiting on. Raised by C54 (#396), which made the field replicated and so made this reachable; measured.** C54 carries the room's op-version high-water on `Replicate` and `ReplicateSnapshot` and composes it as a **max** — the all-time worst case a joiner must down-reach, so no frame may talk it down, which is what makes a re-sent or compacted-leader frame safe. `gate_replica_frame`'s own rustdoc says it cannot distinguish a forged epoch bump from a genuine promotion, so a cluster member in a room's replica set can assert `max_op_version: u32::MAX` on a frame for it. **Measured:** the replica's high-water becomes `u32::MAX`, is persisted, and every governing-app joiner is then refused with `onUpdateRequired` — v2 and v3 alike, since `reachable` walks a chain that reaches no such version and a broken chain fails closed — and a subsequent honest frame naming the true high-water leaves it untouched. So one frame permanently denies a room to every client landing on that replica, and to every client of the room once a failover makes it the leader. This is **C56's property at a second field**: C56 asks whether set-once (hijack-stable, unrepairable) or epoch-ordered-correction wins for the authority root; the high-water poses the same question with the consequence inverted — not a hijack of authority but a permanent denial of service, and against an attacker who gains nothing else from it, since a member that can send frames can already write the room's ops. **The candidate answers are the same three**, which is why this belongs with C56 rather than beside it: leave it unrepairable; let a strictly higher epoch replace rather than raise the value, buying a repair path at the cost of last-writer semantics on a monotone field; or record the epoch a high-water was installed at, so correction is ordered. A fourth is specific to this field — bound the accepted value by what the room's governing schema chain actually reaches, which fails closed against a nonexistent version without needing an election at all, and is worth weighing first because it is local. → *Server / Replication*.

**C125 — a replica cannot rebuild the metadata it does not log, and only the root has a repair (crates/server) — READY, unblocked by C54 (#396), which landed after this was filed. Filed by C55 (#397).** C55 gives the room's authority root a carrier that does not need an op batch: `Message::ReplicateMeta`, sent when a write establishes a root and broadcasts nothing, and by the dial where a caught-up follower would otherwise be sent no frame at all. The root is not the only replicated metadata with that shape. C54 (#396) carries the room's governing binding and its op-version high-water on the `Replicate`/`ReplicateSnapshot` frames, composed on arrival — so both fields ride a frame now, and the field set and its composition rules this filing waited on are settled. A follower logs its leader's batch **untagged**, so unlike a leader it cannot re-derive the high-water from its own log; a failed best-effort persist loses it for good, and a quiescent room replays nothing that would restore it. Same defect as C55's second route, at a second field, with the carrier already built and empty of it. The fix is to widen the frame to the metadata record rather than the root alone — which wants a decision C55 did not have to make, because a root is set-once and these are not: the high-water composes as a **max** and the binding follows `bind_room_app`'s incumbent-app rule, so a metadata-only assertion has to say whether it is an *assertion of the leader's current value* (which a max already tolerates) or a *repair* (which the max cannot express downward, C120's shape at the same field). Deliberately after C54 lands, so the field set and its composition rules are settled in one place rather than duplicated. → *Server / Replication*.

**C126 — a durable epoch record with no room behind it materialises an empty, servable replica on load (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Filed by C55, which stopped producing one and left the load path as it found it.** `Store::load` does `rooms.entry(room).or_default()` before it looks at what kind of record it read, and `Hub::install_room`'s non-snapshot arm reaches `ingest_records(room, vec![])`, which inserts a `Room` unconditionally. So a lone `<room>.epoch` file comes back as a room the node holds at sequence `0` — `holds_room` reports it servable, and `read_redirect_response` then serves a client from it rather than redirecting, handing back an empty document for a room that has content. **A frame can still produce that file, and the in-memory half is worse than the record.** `gate_replica_frame` persists the fence on `Apply`, before its caller runs, and `apply_replicate`'s ingest is fallible — but `ingest_records` inserts the `Room` *before* `store.append`, so a failed append leaves the hub holding an **empty room immediately**, not only after a restart, with the connection dropped and the fence already written. Narrow — it needs a durable-write failure, and `Store::append` early-returns `Ok` on an empty batch so the frame must carry ops — but it is the same shape C55 measured and closed at its own seam by refusing to let a contentless frame reach the gate, and that fix does not reach this one. The fix is on the load path rather than at each writer, since the writers are exactly the frames whose whole job is to fence before they apply: a record set carrying no ops, no snapshot and no branch is metadata about a room this node does not hold, and should restore the fence without materialising a replica — which needs a decision about where a fence for an unheld room lives, given `LeadershipEpochs` is keyed by room and `Hub::from_rooms` builds both from one record. → *Server / Durability*.

**C128 — a peer that names `u64::MAX` as its leadership epoch wedges a room's fence and overflows the next claim (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Filed by C55 (#397), measured; pre-dates it.** `gate_replica_frame` calls `epochs.observe(room, epoch)` for any frame it admits, with no ceiling on the value, and `LeadershipEpochs::claim_leadership` then computes `let epoch = seen + 1` **unchecked**. So one `Replicate` or `ReplicateSnapshot` from a member of the room's replica set, stamped `u64::MAX`, pins the receiver's `highest_seen` there — after which the node's own next claim panics in debug and wraps to `0` in release, and a wrapped epoch is fenced by every peer that saw the real one. Not a new reach: `gate_replica_frame`'s own rustdoc records that it cannot tell a forged epoch bump from a genuine promotion, so a replica-set member could already assert any epoch it liked. What is new is the *arithmetic* — C55 measured it while scoping a guard that closes the contentless-frame door and deliberately not this one, since the same value arrives on the frames that carry ops. The fix wants a saturating or checked claim, and a ruling on what a node does when its fence is at the ceiling: saturating leaves the room permanently unwritable by anyone (every claim ties rather than exceeds), so the answer is probably a bound on how far one frame may move a fence — the same shape C35 asks for on the gossip incarnation counter, and worth settling once for both. → *Server / Cluster*.

**C127 — the room-metadata write is best-effort at every seam and nothing retries it, so four set-once facts are each lost independently (crates/server) — READY, needs a ruling. Filed by C55 (#397), with the arm C54 (#396) measured and the one C23 (#389) left.** `RoomMeta` persists the governing binding, the op-version high-water, the room's creator and the replica-identity claims as one record, through a `persist_meta` whose `io::Result` is discarded at all seven of its call sites. Set-once means nothing re-attempts any of them, and the record is not re-derivable: a stored op carries the per-device `ClientId`, never the credential actor. **Three measured consequences, one per field.** A **replica** reloads creatorless and, serving no client write, has no seam that would re-establish a root — C55 gives it a carrier (`ReplicateMeta`) rather than making the write land. A **leader** whose write was lost still holds the versions its ops were tagged with in its log, so it emits `governing: None` beside a real op-version high-water, and a fresh replica adopts a floor with no chain to read it in — C54 gates against it at the arrival seam. And the **claims** strand the identity binding the same way, on top of C95's finding that they do not replicate at all. **The ruling is what reliability costs.** Failing a client's write because a metadata *cache* write failed is the shape C100 files as a defect, and the record's own contract calls it a durability cache. Folding it into the durable op record makes the two one write and is a store-format change. A dirty-flag retry is the cheap shape but needs a **sweep** to be a fix rather than a narrowing, since the node that most needs the retry — a quiescent replica — is the one with no next write to hang it on. Note that no carrier removes the need: C55's frame repairs a replica precisely because the write is unreliable, and would still be wanted if it were not, for the root a deduped write establishes with no batch to ride. → *Server / Durability*.

**C56 — a replica rooted wrongly can never be corrected (crates/server) — READY, needs a human ruling. Raised by C29 (#374), which deliberately took the conservative half; filed as C36, renumbered because that id was taken.** C29 made the doc-ACL authority root replicated metadata and adopted it **set-once** on the follower: `ensure_creator` on the ops path and `install_room_state`'s `standing.or(incoming)` both refuse to displace a root already standing, and the root is persisted. So no later frame, no full `ReplicateSnapshot` state replacement, no restart and no admin path can correct one. **Two ways a replica ends up rooted wrongly.** A member that forges an epoch bump — `gate_replica_frame`'s own rustdoc says it cannot distinguish that from a genuine promotion — sends a `Replicate` for a room the target does not yet hold, and roots it at an actor of its choosing; that actor then owns `/` on that replica and reads through it whatever the room's denies withhold elsewhere. And, with no attacker at all, a room that was *creatorless* when it replicated (anonymous-only writers, or `import_room`) can be rooted independently on two nodes — a promoted replica takes a client write and roots at that writer while the recovered primary roots at another — after which the two decide the same room's ACL under different owners forever. **The ruling is which property wins**, since both are coherent. Set-once is hijack-stable and unrepairable. Letting a *strictly higher* epoch replace the root buys a repair path — the true leader's next frame corrects a wrong one — at the cost of making the authority root follow whoever wins the epoch race, which is already the arbiter the replicated ops themselves follow, so it grants a forger nothing it did not already have. A third reading records the epoch a root was installed at, so correction is ordered rather than last-writer. The answer belongs with the real election C25 and Unit 6 are heading toward; C29 states the trade in DECISIONS rather than settling it. → *Server / Replication*.

**C73 — a filtered subscriber's seen sequence counts the ops it received, not the server sequences they occupied (crates/core + crates/server) — READY, needs a wire-format change. Found by cubic's review during C59 (#384); measured on the write path, so it predates that unit and is unrelated to replication.** `ClientSession::receive` advances a room's `last_seen_seq` by the *length* of the `Ops` batch — "the delta is a contiguous run of ops at the head, each holding one server sequence". That premise holds only for a subscriber served the stream whole. Every outbound filter breaks it: doc-ACL per-path redaction, the per-channel zone filter, and the migration chain (which can drop ops) all deliver **fewer** ops than the server sequence advanced, so the recipient's counter under-counts permanently and by exactly what it was denied. Measured on a single node with no follower and no replication in play: a `za`-scoped subscriber sits at `last_seen_seq = 2` against a server sequence of `6` after two writes into the zone it cannot read. The consequences are on reconnect, where that counter is both the catch-up cursor and — since follower read-serving (#316) — the **read floor**. As a cursor it re-draws a span the replica already holds, which the dedup makes harmless to state but which is unbounded redundancy proportional to how much the subscriber is denied, on every resume for the life of the subscription. As a floor it is *understated*, which is fail-safe in the monotonicity direction (a lower floor never wrongly admits a stale follower) but gives up the read-your-writes tightness the floor exists to provide. The fix wants the stream to carry an explicit server-sequence high-water rather than have the client infer one by counting — a field on `Message::Ops` (or a companion frame), with `ClientSession` adopting it separately from the op list, so a filtered delta says "you are now at sequence N" instead of "here are k ops". That is a wire-format change plus a decision about what a *snapshot*-served or reveal-shell-prefixed batch reports, since neither's length corresponds to a server-sequence span either. → *Core / Wire, Redaction*.

**C71 — a replicated batch reaches a follower's subscribers untranslated, whatever version they speak (crates/server) — READY, needs a wire-format change. Found during C59 (#384), which established the fan-out it rides.** A leader replicates its writers' ops **untagged** — `Message::Replicate` carries no schema version, and `apply_replicate` ingests with `None`. So a follower has no source version for the batch and no local author whose declared app could stand in for one, and both the replication fan-out and the follower's own catch-up serve it verbatim. That is self-consistent (a follower-served subscriber and a follower-served reconnect see the identical stream, which is what C59 gates on) and it is *not* what the leader does: a client on the leader at another version is served the batch translated along the room's migration chain, and the same client moved to a follower is served it raw. A room mid-rolling-upgrade therefore converges differently depending on which node a client landed on, which is the divergence migration translation exists to prevent — and it is silent, since an untranslated op applies rather than failing. The handshake range-check bounds the blast radius (a subscriber that cannot reach the room's op-version high-water is refused outright) but does not close it: two versions inside the invertible window both subscribe and only one gets bridged. **C54 (#396) sharpened both halves of that sentence.** The bound is real now rather than nominal — before it, a replica's high-water was `None` and the check refused nobody there, so the blast radius was unbounded on exactly the node this unit is about. And the follower now holds the room's governing binding, so it knows *which* chain a translation would walk; what it still lacks is the per-op source version to walk it from. C54 settled explicitly that it would not supply that by re-tagging the batch — one version per frame would mislabel a catch-up delta `Catchup::Ops` says may mix versions — so the per-op carry, and the mixed-version question under it, are wholly this unit's. Fix wants the author's version to ride the frame — a `Replicate` field, so the follower logs the ops tagged as the leader does and both its seams translate — which is a wire-format change and a decision about a batch whose ops were authored at *different* versions (the leader tags per-ingest-batch, so one frame is one version today, but that is a property of the current enqueue rather than a stated invariant). → *Server / Replication, Migration*.

**C72 — a below-floor snapshot install replaces a follower's replica underneath its subscribers, silently (crates/server) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C59 (#384), which scoped it out.** `apply_replicate_snapshot` is the catch-up a follower takes when its acked watermark falls below the leader's compaction floor: it `decode_state`-loads the leader's whole replica, **replacing** what stood there, and acks the new sequence. C59 made the *ops* path fan out to the follower's local subscribers; this path cannot use that seam, because there is no op batch — a `Message::Ops` can express an advance and not a replacement, and the span the subscriber missed is by definition compacted away, so no delta over it exists to send. A client subscribed on that follower therefore holds a replica pinned at the pre-install state while the follower's own jumps to the leader's, and — unlike the C59 shape, where the gap was a stalled stream — the two are not merely apart but **derived from different states**: the ops the subscriber does hold may name elements the installed snapshot no longer carries. It self-heals on reconnect, when the subscribe re-serves a snapshot. The fix is a `Message::Snapshot` fan-out from this path, per recipient — which needs the per-recipient snapshot *projection* the subscribe seam already runs (`project_read_paths` / `project_zones`, so a partial reader is not handed the whole replica) and a ruling on what a client does with a replacement mid-session: the subscribe path's `Snapshot` adopts the server state while keeping the channel's own identity and op-seq position, and whether an unacked local write survives that adoption is the open half. → *Server / Replication, Fan-out*.

**C61 — a `DiffResult` names the channel that asked but not the query it answers (crates/core) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C50 by review, the residual of its own fix.** `DiffResult { channel, changes }` closes the case C50 filed — two channels of one room, narrowed to different zone scopes, no longer overwrite each other — and leaves the finer one: two diff queries issued on **one** channel are still told apart by arrival order alone, since the reply names no `kind`/`a`/`b`. Strictly weaker than C50, and for a structural reason worth stating: one channel is one scope, so the second answer is never *narrower* than the first was asked for — the confusion is over which pair of states a change list describes, not over what it withholds. Reachable from `ClientSession`, the FFI, Python and Go, which all let a caller have two queries in flight on a channel; `sdks/js` escapes only incidentally, because `drainReplies` runs once per inbound frame so each pending settles against the view as of its own frame. Fix wants a request discriminant on the reply — the `(kind, a, b)` triple the query named, or an opaque correlation id the client mints — which is the first request/reply correlation in this protocol that a natural key does not already supply, so it decides the shape for every later one. → *Client / Diff*.


**C76 — a container create under a *live* leaf is not carried to the old key by a snapshot migration (crates/core) — READY, no dependencies. Found during C68 (#388), which put the create within reach; pre-existing, and out of C68's scope, which was the encoding.** `MapCreate{k}` then a higher-stamped `MapSet{k}` leaves a live scalar at `k` over a retained container create. A rename `k → j` translated at the *op* seam carries the create verbatim (the container stays live at `k`) and re-keys the set, so an op-served joiner holds a live map at `k` and the scalar at `j`. `migrate_leaf_slots` sees a live leaf slot, moves it to `j`, and leaves `k` absent — the same op↔snapshot divergence DECISIONS 2026-07-15 closed for a *deleted* container, in the one shape it did not reach. C68 makes the create available at a live slot (slot tag 3), so the fix is the resurrect path the deleted case already has, taken on a live leaf whose key retains a create it outranks; the open question is what becomes of the leaf body, which unlike a delete has somewhere to be. → *Core / State codec*.

**C67 — a snapshot projection serves a displaced container whole, whatever partition it fell in (crates/core) — READY, needs a ruling. Not closed by C52, which took the sibling ruling for the entries carrying no container (an orphaned annotation, an unresolvable element scope) and left this one open by name — so this unit also now owns the zone attribution those entries lose: C52 fails them closed, which withholds one from the subscriber that genuinely owns the partition, and the third reading below is what recovers it. Found during C58 (#379), which widened it; measured against `main`. Filed as C59 and renumbered twice, since siblings in flight took that id and the next.** `project_zones` and `project_read_paths` both build their purge set from `element_paths`, which walks the **live** tree. A displaced container therefore has no path, resolves to no zone and to no read verdict, is never purged, and is served whole — content, structure, ids and all — to a subscriber scoped away from the partition it belongs to. Both functions' own contracts say otherwise: "an unauthorized zone is wholly absent rather than redacted-but-present", "so a re-encode carries no trace of it". Reproduced on a zone schema with `/notes/inner` displaced inside an unauthorized `zb`: the retained map and its slot keys survive a `za`-only projection. **C58 widened it rather than opening it.** Before C58 an op addressed to a displaced container waited in the buffer, and the buffer *is* filtered — `project_zones` by `op.zone`, `project_read_paths` by clearing it whole — so a write made after the displacement was withheld while one made before it leaked. C58 lands both in the container, so both leak; measured, `main` withholds the post-displacement write and leaks the pre-displacement one. **The ruling is what a projection may do with retained state whose path is gone**, and the two obvious answers each cost something real. The key is unrecoverable (`ElementId::derive` is one-way), so the container's own zone cannot be re-resolved; its nearest ancestor that still has a live path bounds the answer but does not give it, since a zone declared deeper than that ancestor would be attributed to the ancestor's. And purging every path-less container outright would drop retained state a scoped subscriber is *entitled* to — its slot re-won later would materialise empty there and full at the server, which is divergence inside the partition it is served. A third reading persists an element's zone (or its key) at create, so a displaced container keeps the answer with it. → *Core / State codec*.



### SDK follow-ons (planned 2026-07-25, human — cs-next dispatches in this order; undo fork RESOLVED in DECISIONS/ARCHITECTURE)

**Epic A — Networked socket-owning Provider for Python + Go (do FIRST; non-gated).** Makes Python/Go first-class *networked* collab clients (they have only the offline provider today). Mechanical — the wasm `WasmClient` already has the surface; port it to the C ABI + wrap.
  - **A1 — C-ABI per-channel expansion (crates/ffi) — DONE (#340, see Done).** The eleven per-channel `list_*`/`text_*`/`set_scalar`/`get_scalar`/`map_keys` entry points now sit on `CrdtClient`, routed through the client edit surface + outbox; cbindgen header and FFI symbol-assert extended. A2, A3, and B1 are unblocked.
  - **A1b — C-ABI client-seat read parity (crates/ffi) — DONE (#343, see Done).** The remaining per-channel reads: `channel_state` (the snapshot the ergonomic SDKs diff for per-channel reactivity), `get_blob`, `marks_at`, `relative_position`, `resolve_position`, `xml_tag`, `xml_children_len`, plus `get_counter` (the read back for `inc`/`dec`). A2 and A3 rebase onto it.
  - **A2 — Python networked Provider (sdks/python) — DONE (#345, see Done).**
  - **A3 — Go networked Provider (sdks/go) — DONE (#344, see Done).**

**Epic B — Undo/redo (do AFTER Epic A; DESIGN RESOLVED = core record-seam + origin-scope, DECISIONS/ARCHITECTURE 2026-07-25).** Inversion in the shared core, thin per-SDK handle.
  - **B1 — core undo record-seam (crates/core + crates/wasm/ffi) — DONE (#348, see Done).**
  - **B2 — SDK undo handle (JS/Python/Go) — dep B1 satisfied; READY.** Thin `undo()/redo()/canUndo/canRedo`, origin-scoped, over the core stack — no per-language inversion logic. The doc-level surface is in place in all three bindings (`Undo` in Python/Go, `WasmUndo` in wasm); what remains is (a) the **per-channel** seat in Python/Go — the C ABI `crdtsync_client_set_undo_origin` / `_clear_undo_origin` / `_undo` / `_redo` / `_can_undo` / `_can_redo` exist and wasm already wraps them, the two `ctypes`/cgo `Client`s do not — and (b) the ergonomic handle-graph layer: a `doc.undo` handle whose `mutate` flow sets the origin, and the JS package's first undo surface (it has none today). → *SDK-Ergonomic-Surface / Undo*.

- _(The Schema-layer epic below — Schema Units 1–8 — is **complete** (all merged, latest 8f #190); it lingers here as board drift, pending a Next-section cleanup.)_

- **Schema layer — the opt-in feature tier that unblocks awareness-TTL, `@auth`, invariant repair, and migration** (§Schema, §Schema Migration; design settled 2026-07-03, DECISIONS). The distribution/trust/versioning model is now pinned (schema is code, registered per `app_id`, relay-vs-enforcing tiers, client-declared version ranges + per-edge compat class + per-recipient in-flight translation, `onRepaired`/`onOpsRejected`/`onUpdateRequired` hooks). Sliced dependency-order; the first units consume only **built** primitives (map/list/text/register/counter + awareness), so they land now — marks/attrs/structural clauses parse but stay enforcement-inert until XmlElement/marks exist:
  - **Unit 1 — schema parse + validation engine (core)**: `core::schema` parses the JSON file → a `Schema`, validates it at parse time (closure-of-violations: every declared dimension maps to a repair rule, so no schema admits an unrepairable state). Op / materialized-state validation against the schema is **not** part of this unit — it moved to Unit 2 (invariant repair), where the violation set it produces is consumed over the live element tree (DECISIONS 2026-07-03). Built-primitive constraints only (`root`/`types` for map/list/text/register/counter, `min`/`max`, `children` cardinality). The sole validator — SDKs forward bytes, never reimplement. Foundation for every unit below. **JSON-parser decision (cs-next, 2026-07-03):** core carries no serde/json and is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` with a hand-rolled binary codec — so parse the schema with a **hand-rolled minimal JSON value parser in core** (total-decode → a `SchemaError`, never a panic), NOT `serde_json` (adding it would break core's deliberate dep-minimalism, which even keeps `getrandom` out). Log this in DECISIONS when Unit 1 lands. **Split into two PRs:** **1a — DONE (#135)** — `core::json` minimal JSON value parser (`Json` value + total-decode `Json::parse`: object/array/string with `\uXXXX`+surrogate pairs/int-vs-float/bool/null, depth-capped nesting-bomb guard, byte-offset `JsonError`; own test suite; hand-rolled, no serde — DECISIONS 2026-07-03); **1b — DONE (#136)** — `core::schema` model (`Schema`/`TypeDef` over map/list/text/register/counter, order-preserving) + total-decode parse (`Json` → `Schema`, a `SchemaError`, never a panic) + parse-time closure validation (every named type declared, `root` is a map, `min`/`max` bounds well-formed). Op/state validation was moved out of 1b into Unit 2 — it produces a violation set whose only consumer is repair, both walking the live element tree, so a `Json`-stand-in validator here would be throwaway (DECISIONS 2026-07-03). **Hardening (#140, #141):** the parser now rejects an unknown key at every level (`SchemaErrorKind::UnknownField`) instead of silently ignoring it, so a typo'd key fails loud — top-level (`schema`/`version`/`root`/`types`/`marks`/`awareness`/`auth`; `marks`/`auth` accepted structurally, modelled by their own units), per-type-kind fields (e.g. a `register` allows only `kind`/`min`/`max`), and awareness-entry fields (`ttl`/`throttle`).
  - **Unit 2 — invariant repair (built-primitive rules) + schema state/op validation**: deterministic convergent repair at the apply boundary — out-of-range scalar/counter clamp, exclusive-collision keep-lamport-oldest, disallowed-value drop — plus the `onRepaired` observation event. **Includes the schema state/op validation moved here from Unit 1b** (DECISIONS 2026-07-03): the validator walks the materialized element tree against the `Schema` producing the violation set, and repair consumes it in the same pass — inseparable, both over the live tree, not over `Json`. Convergence-harness tested (all replicas repair identically given a shared schema). **DESIGN RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-03, DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Invariant Repair):** (1) **Mechanism = read-time normalization, never a minted op** — repair is a deterministic pure function of merged state applied at read; the stored/encoded state is the raw merged ops; lamport order (keep-oldest / drop-newest) comes from the stamps already in state, not the local clock → convergent by construction, no new stamp accessors needed. `onRepaired` fires at apply (validator detects); the repaired value is produced at read. (2) **Sequence `min` rejected at parse** — an over-`max` is repairable (drop lamport-newest), a `min` underflow is not (can't invent items) and would break closure; `List.min` (the only such field) is rejected, min-cardinality is app-layer (structural floor: fixed Map slots + a list for extras; or a publish gate). Build slices: **2a — validator (core) — DONE (#170).** `core::validate::validate(doc, schema) -> Vec<Violation>` walks the materialized element tree against the parsed `Schema` and reports every constraint the model expresses, per built primitive: a map slot outside its type's declared allowlist (`UnknownSlot`), an element whose runtime kind ≠ its declared type (`KindMismatch`, not recursed into), a register/counter `Int` outside `min`/`max` (`BelowMin`/`AboveMax`), a list/text over `max` (`TooLong`). `Violation { path: Vec<Step>, kind }` is the shared currency 2b consumes; `Step::Key`/`Index` locate it. Pure read, deterministic (map keys sorted, list index order, DFS) so replicas converge on the same set. **Total on any decoded document**: an iterative walk over an explicit work stack with an `ElementId` descend-once guard — a crafted shared or cyclic slot graph is bounded by edge count (own-constraint checked per reach so a shared leaf is still flagged, subtree entered once), and the location is an `Rc<PathNode>` cons-list with an iterative `Drop`, so neither the walk nor the teardown recurses off the stack at depth. `List.min` is now rejected at parse (dropped from `TypeDef::List`; a below-min list is unrepairable → `UnknownField`, ARCHITECTURE §closure). Miri-clean; spec `crates/core/tests/validate.rs`. (Exclusivity + disallowed-value have no `TypeDef` field, so they are out of 2a scope — correctly deferred.) **2b — read-time normalization + `onRepaired` (core)**, sliced (cs-next): **2b-1 — read repair pass — DONE (#172).** `core::repair::repairs(doc, schema) -> Vec<Repair>` turns the `validate()` violation set into the normalization a conformant read applies over merged state: `Clamped { value }` (register/counter → its crossed bound) and `Truncated { keep }` (list/text over `max` → the survivor sequence indices after dropping the `len - max` lamport-newest by the `(lamport, client)` stamp total order in state). Pure read, no minted op; kind-mismatch/unknown-slot have no repaired reading and are omitted; repair order mirrors `validate()`. Merged replicas repair identically (convergence test). Miri-clean; spec `crates/core/tests/repair.rs`. **2b-2 — `onRepaired` observation — DONE (#174).** `Document::set_schema` binds an opt-in schema; `Document::take_repairs() -> Vec<Vec<Step>>` reports the located paths whose repaired reading has newly changed against it since the last call. Reports *locations*, not values — the repaired value is produced by a read (`repairs()`), so a consumer never caches a stale reading. Computed lazily from settled state (not eagerly per-apply, which was O(ops·doc) on catch-up and leaked transient mid-delta violations), diffed on a reading-stable identity (clamp target / surviving node stamps): a location surfaces on newly needing repair, a re-clamp to the other bound, or a different surviving item — but not a pure index shift (the consumer observes that via a normal read). A violation that appears+resolves between calls is never reported; empty while a local atomic transaction is open (settle-point contract) or with no schema bound. Miri-clean; spec `crates/core/tests/repair_event.rs`. **Known deferred:** a bound schema is runtime state, not part of a snapshot — a replica rebuilt via `decode_state` must re-bind (wiring `set_schema` through `ClientSession` snapshot adoption is the client-integration unit's job). (Disallowed-value drop needs a schema enum field first; exclusive-collision-demote, orphan→wrap-default-block, attr/mark repairs are XmlElement/marks-gated, deferred.) **Unit 2 complete.**
  - **Unit 3 — awareness timed-TTL + throttle**: read `awareness.<kind>.ttl`/`throttle`/`value` from the schema; wire per-entry auto-expire-after-silence (reuse `AwarenessClear` + the `Clock` seam #71 + periodic sweep #72) and server-side throttle/coalesce of high-frequency entries. **Closes the queued awareness-TTL item** (was blocked only on "where the trigger values live"). **Split (cs-next 2026-07-03):** **3a — DONE (#138)** — `core::schema` now parses the `awareness.<kind>` block (`AwarenessEntry { ttl, throttle }`, ms, both optional non-negative; order-preserving; `awareness()`/`awareness_entry(kind)` accessors). Pure core, mirrors the 1b bounds parsing. **3b — the *enforcement* half — UNBLOCKED (Unit 5 complete, 5c landed the shared registry).** An enforcing server reads `awareness.<kind>.ttl` from its *registered* schema, resolved per connection via the shared `Arc<Mutex<SchemaRegistry>>`. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04), wire → pure enforcement → schema wiring → throttle:
      - **3b-i — per-key awareness removal on the wire (core protocol) — DONE (#166).** `Message::AwarenessClearKey { channel, actor, key }` (wire tag 20) drops one of an actor's entries, complementing the actor-wide `AwarenessClear`; needed because timed TTL expires a single kind while the actor's other entries and connection live on (ARCHITECTURE §TTL: "Timed TTL cleared on expiry; removal broadcast", per-entry). Codec round-trip + truncation coverage; the core client `receive` drops just that `(actor, key)`; the server `step` rejects a client-sent one as a violation. Pure core wire change (break compat, pre-release), Miri-clean.
      - **3b-ii — Hub per-entry last-seen + pure timed-expiry (server) — DONE (#167).** `Hub::set_awareness` stamps a `last_seen` millis per entry (`(actor, value, last_seen)`); `Hub::expire_silent_awareness(now, policy)` drops every entry whose silence exceeds the TTL an injected `AwarenessPolicy` assigns its kind, returning `(room, actor, key)` to broadcast. `Registry` holds an `AwarenessPolicy` (default `NoTimedTtl` → every entry session-lifetime, behaviour unchanged) with a `set_awareness_policy` setter; `sweep` calls the expiry and fans `AwarenessClearKey` out per removed entry. `now` is threaded into `step`/`set_awareness` from `Registry::deliver`, which reads the clock **only for an `AwarenessSet`** (keeping the op hot path clock-free and Miri-safe); a re-set on activity refreshes the TTL. Unit-tested with a `ManualClock` + map-backed policy (expiry past TTL, boundary at exactly TTL, session-lifetime survives, refresh resets, default never expires); Miri-clean. (Awareness-set tests on the default `SystemClock` were switched to a `ManualClock` since the set path now reads the clock, which Miri isolation blocks.)
      - **3b-iii — schema→TTL bridge, wire into the sweep (server) — DONE (#168).** With no injected policy the `sweep` resolves each room's timed TTLs from its governing schema: `SchemaAwarenessPolicy` (`lib.rs`) maps `room → Arc<Schema>` and answers `ttl(room, key)` via `awareness_entry(kind).ttl`. A room's governing `{app_id, version}` is a **sticky binding** (`Registry::room_apps`) recorded when an enforcing client subscribes and lifted to the highest version seen — so presence keeps timing out after the last enforcing client leaves (no ghost cursor) and a rolling upgrade resolves deterministically (newest version governs), not from a nondeterministic scan of live subscribers. Parsed schemas are cached per `{app_id, version}` for the process lifetime (`schema_cache`, `Arc<Schema>` — a registry link is immutable), and the sweep skips the registry lock when no room holds presence. An injected `AwarenessPolicy` still takes precedence (tests / custom). Tested end-to-end with a `ManualClock` + a registered schema (expiry, boundary, session-lifetime kind, relay room never expires, enforcing governs a relay peer, version-0 adopts head; regressions: bound room survives its last enforcing client, newer version governs). Miri-clean. (DECISIONS 2026-07-04: sticky per-room binding over per-sweep scan.)
      - **3b-iv — server-side throttle/coalesce (server) — DONE (#169).** `Hub::set_awareness` takes the kind's `throttle` window (resolved on the set path by `Registry::resolve_throttle` from the injected policy, else the room's governing schema) and enforces it as a **leading-edge inbound cap**: the first update, an unthrottled kind, and the first update once the window has elapsed fan out and become the entry's stored value; an update inside the window is coalesced — it refreshes last-seen (so a fast stream does not TTL-expire) but is neither stored nor sent. The server runs **no trailing-edge timer** — the client SDK's debounce owns delivering the final value on its next past-window send — so the stored value is always what the room was last sent and a joiner replays exactly what existing peers hold (no joiner-ahead disagreement). Fan-out still excludes only the originating connection (`ConnId`), so a second connection of the same client keeps receiving. `checked_sub` treats a backward clock step as elapsed (fans out, never freezes). `ManualClock`-driven tests (first-fans/burst-coalesced, no-sweep-flush, past-window re-fans, unthrottled-every-update, originator-not-echoed, sibling-still-receives, joiner-replays-broadcast-value, coalesced-set-refreshes-TTL); Miri-clean. (DECISIONS 2026-07-04: leading-edge cap over timer-driven flush — an earlier held-and-flush-on-sweep revision lagged the trailing value to the 1s sweep, raced TTL expiry, and let a joiner disagree with peers.) **Completes 3b — and Unit 3 (awareness timed-TTL + throttle).**
  - **Unit 4 — schema-level `@auth`** — **DESIGN RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-03, DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Authorization).** The grant DSL is now pinned: `auth.roles` (static vocabulary) + `auth.grants` (`{allow|deny, to: role/subject/${template}, on: path}`), with `${actor_id}`/`${author_id}`/`${room_id}`/`${branch_id}` bound at check time. `@auth` holds **only static role-based defaults** — ownership + per-actor grants are runtime doc-level ACL state, not schema. Build slices:
    - **4a — claims model (server) — DONE (#152).** `Verifier::verify` now returns `Option<Identity>` (`Identity { actor, roles, groups }`, server crate); `AllowAll`→actor-only, `StaticTokens` row grows to `<credential> <actor> [roles] [groups]` (comma-lists; `-` = empty; arity 2–4; `EmptyName` on a stray comma). The `Session`/`Registry`/anon paths thread and store the full `Identity`; `Session::identity()` exposes it, `actor()` still derives from it so the authz callsites are untouched. **Captures** membership; the evaluator **consumes** it in 4c (`Subject::Role`/`Group` + `authorize(&Identity)`), so `acl.rs`/`authz.rs` stay unchanged here. Wire unchanged (`AuthOk` carries actor only).
    - **4b — `@auth` parse (core) — DONE (145)**: `core::schema` now parses the `auth` block into `Auth { roles, grants }`. Grant JSON `{ "allow"|"deny": "<action>", "to": "<subject>", "on": "<path>" }` (effect + action fused in the allow/deny key); `Grant { effect: Effect, action: Action(read|write|publish_awareness), subject: Subject(Role|Class|Template), path }`. Closure-validated: `to` role refs against `auth.roles`, subject classes/`${actor_id,author_id,room_id,branch_id}` templates recognized, `on` a well-formed absolute path, `own`/meta-auth actions rejected (dynamic doc-ACL only), duplicate roles rejected. `Schema::auth()` accessor; `own`-exclusion + JSON encoding logged in DECISIONS. Pure core, mirrors 3a. (Value-shape enforcement of grants at check time is 4c.)
    - **4c — role-grant evaluator tier (server)**: feed parsed `@auth` grants into the `acl.rs` evaluator as decision-flow step 4 (the `Subject::Role`/`Group` match against `Identity`), completing explicit-deny → owner → explicit-allow → **schema role-grant** → default-deny. Needs the schema at the server → sequenced with Unit 5. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04) into the identity seam and the schema-grant tier:
      - **4c-i — Authorizer takes `&Identity` + acl `Subject::Role`/`Group` (server) — DONE (#175).** The `Authorizer` trait now authorizes an `Identity` (not a bare actor) at all enforcement call sites (`session.rs` Subscribe/Ops/AwarenessSet/version, `registry.rs` `peer_may_read`, `admin.rs` RegisterSchema); `audit.rs` still records `identity.actor()`. `acl::Subject` grows `Role(String)`/`Group(String)`, matched against `Identity::roles()`/`groups()`; policy tokens `role:<name>`/`group:<name>` (empty name → `PolicyErrorKind::Subject`). Deny-wins/allow/default-deny unchanged. **Deferred to 4c-ii:** feeding the schema's parsed `@auth` grants into the evaluation (this slice is the deployment-ACL seam only).
      - **4c-ii — schema-grant tier (server) — DONE (#176).** The room's governing-schema `@auth` grants (`core::schema::Auth { grants }`, 4b #145) feed into the data-plane evaluation as decision-flow step 4. `authz::Decision { Allow, Deny, Abstain }` + a provided `Authorizer::decide` (a bool authorizer never abstains → its `false` is final; the schema tier is reached only under an `Acl`, whose `decide` returns `Abstain` on no matching rule). `acl::authorized(deployment, schema, id, action, resource)` composes `decide` → schema grants (deny-wins over root-path grants, `Role`/`Class` subjects, `read`/`write`/`publish_awareness` actions) → default-deny; `session::step`/`version_room` + `registry::peer_may_read` consume it. **Governing schema is the room's, not the connection's self-declared app** (`Registry.room_apps`→`parsed_schema`), with the connection's own app as the bootstrap fallback only for a not-yet-bound room — so a foreign app cannot pick a permissive schema to escalate. `room_apps` is now bound + reconciled under any awareness policy. A new `Authorizer::observe` hook lets `Audited` log the *composed* final verdict (schema grants included) rather than the deployment tier's intermediate one. **Deferred (no carrier):** grant `on` path-scoping, `${…}` ownership templates, group-subject schema grants. **Completes 4c — and Unit 4 (schema-level `@auth`).**
  - **Unit 5 — schema registration + trust (server)** — **DESIGN RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-03, DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Schema Registration).** The keystone (unblocks 3b, 4c, 6). Decisions pinned: **HTTP admin endpoint** (control plane, separate from the sync WS); **app-admin meta-auth** via `Action::RegisterSchema` on `Resource::App(app_id)` with a `StaticTokens`-style registration credential → admin `Identity` (the app admin is a superuser over the app's docs, distinct from an owner); **hash-lock = SHA-256 in the *server* crate** (`sha2`), never in dep-minimal core — a client never hash-verifies (it trusts the server); **per-`app_id` registry**; **handshake carries app_id + schema_version**, resolved against the registry (unknown → reject). Build slices:
    - **5a — `Action`/`Resource` extensions (server) — DONE (#153).** `Action::RegisterSchema` (app-admin meta-auth) + `Resource::App(app_id)` in `authz.rs`; `ResourceMatch::App` in `acl.rs` with room/app scopes **disjoint** (a `room:`/`*` rule never covers an app, nor the reverse); policy tokens `register_schema` + `app:<id>`. Trait untouched. **Deferred:** the `Room`→path-carrying + `author` (for `${author_id}`) change is folded into its consumer (4c / doc-ACL) — `Resource` is an in-memory enum (not wire), so adding those fields later is a pure refactor with no compat cost; adding them now would be speculative fields nothing reads.
    - **5b — registration endpoint + registry (server)**. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04) into the pure registry core and the HTTP transport, so the security-critical hash-lock lands and is exhaustively tested without a transport, and the HTTP surface is a thin writer over it:
      - **5b-i — schema registry + SHA-256 hash-lock (server) — DONE (#154).** `schema_registry::SchemaRegistry` — a per-`app_id` chain of `{schema, migration}` links, each locked by SHA-256 over `version‖schema‖migration` (length-framed). `register(app_id, version, schema, migration)` appends the next contiguous version (`Appended`), no-ops an identical head re-push (`Unchanged`, idempotent CI retry), and refuses `Gap` (skip-ahead) / `OutOfSequence` (backward or zero) / `HashMismatch` (content change under a locked version) — gate-3's "gap / out-of-sequence / hash mismatch". `resolve(app_id, version) → Option<&[u8]>` is the handshake lookup (unknown app/version → `None`, never fabricated); `migration`/`hash`/`head_version` accessors. `sha2` added to the **server** crate only (never core; a client never hash-verifies). Pure logic, Miri-clean. **Deferred to 5b-ii:** the HTTP admin route + meta-auth wiring + persistence.
      - **5b-ii — HTTP admin endpoint + meta-auth (server)**. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04) into the pure meta-auth handler and the HTTP transport, mirroring the `session`/`runtime` split (pure decision logic vs I/O shell):
        - **5b-ii-1 — admin register handler (server) — DONE (#155).** `admin::register_schema(req, verifier, authorizer, registry) → RegisterOutcome` — the pure control-plane decision flow: authenticate the request credential to an `Identity` (`Verifier`), authorize `RegisterSchema` on `Resource::App(app_id)` (`Authorizer`, 5a), then append to the 5b-i `SchemaRegistry` — **authenticate → authorize → register**, so an absent/unknown credential is `Unauthenticated` and an authed-but-unpermitted one `Forbidden`, each before any chain write; a hash-lock refusal is `Rejected(RegisterError)`. `RegisterRequest<'a> { app_id, version, schema, migration, credential }`. Socket-free, Miri-clean. Composes 4a (Identity) + 5a (Action/Resource) + 5b-i (registry). **Deferred to 5b-ii-2:** the HTTP/1.1 wire.
        - **5b-ii-2 — HTTP/1.1 admin transport + listener (server) — REPLACED BY AXUM (#162).** The admin plane now serves on axum over the tokio runtime the server already runs — an untrusted network boundary where request-smuggling/framing edge cases are exactly what a hand-rolled parser gets wrong, and the server crate (unlike dep-minimal core) already carries tokio. `admin::admin_router` (route `POST /apps/{app_id}/schemas/{version}` + `DefaultBodyLimit` → 413 + `tower_http` `TimeoutLayer` → 408) + `serve_admin`. The pure `register_schema` decision + its 200/401/403/409 mapping are unchanged; 400/404/405 are now axum's. Status matrix driven in-process via `tower::oneshot`; socket tests cover wiring, cross-request registry state, and malformed-framing 400. **`http::parse_head` (2a #156) + `dispatch`/`AdminResponse` (2b #157) deleted.** The two slices below are historical.
          - **5b-ii-2a — minimal HTTP/1.1 request-head parser (server) — DONE (#156), later deleted (#162).** `http::parse_head(&[u8]) → Result<Option<Head>, HeadError>` — strict, total-decode, hand-rolled (no HTTP dep). Request line `METHOD SP TARGET SP HTTP/1.1` + `CRLF` header lines + blank-line terminator; `Content-Length`-only framing (`Transfer-Encoding` rejected as a smuggling surface); 16 KiB head cap (memory guard); `Ok(None)` = incomplete (read more), typed `HeadError` for malformed/unsupported. `Head` accessors `method`/`target`/`content_length`/`head_len`/case-insensitive `header`; the body is left for the transport. Pure, Miri-clean. **Deferred to 5b-ii-2b:** the socket + route + response.
          - **5b-ii-2b — admin listener + route + response (server) — DONE (#157), transport rewritten on axum (#162).** `admin::serve_admin` — a dedicated admin `TcpListener` (`CRDTSYNC_ADMIN_ADDR`, optional; unset → relay-only, no admin plane) reading a request via `parse_head` + the `Content-Length` body (1 MiB cap), pure `admin::dispatch` routing `POST /apps/{app_id}/schemas/{version}` → `RegisterRequest` (credential = `Authorization` header verbatim, matching the data plane) → `register_schema` → an `AdminResponse` mapping `RegisterOutcome` (200 Accepted / 401 Unauthenticated / 403 Forbidden / 409 Rejected) + 400 malformed / 404 unknown-route / 405 non-POST. **Sequential** (registration is rare → the registry needs no lock), each request bounded by a 30 s timeout (slowloris guard). `main.rs` runs it alongside the data plane via `try_join!`. **Serve-chain verifier/authorizer boxes unified to `+ Send + Sync`** (all impls already were) so `serve_admin` is spawnable and both planes share the seam. `main.rs` builds a second verifier/authorizer for the admin plane from the same env config. Registration reachable end-to-end (socket integration test). **Registry is admin-plane-owned for now** — 5c shares it into the data-plane handshake. Migration empty until Unit 7. Persistence a follow-on.
    - **5c — handshake resolve (server + client)**: add app_id + schema_version to the handshake; enforcing server resolves → registered schema (unknown → reject); relay path unchanged. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04) wire → server → client:
      - **5c-i — Hello carries app_id + schema_version (wire, core) — DONE (#158).** `Message::Hello` gains `app_id: Vec<u8>` (empty → no app, relay) + `schema_version: u32` (`0` → none declared, a dynamic client that adopts the server's) alongside `client`; codec length-frames `app_id` + `u32` version; round-trips + total-decode/truncation coverage. Pure core wire change (break compat, pre-release). The server/client just carry the fields here — the client sends empty/0 (relay default) until 5c-iii declares real values, and the session ignores them (`{ client, .. }`) until 5c-ii resolves.
      - **5c-ii — server resolve + reject (server)**. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04): the accept/reject *policy* is pure; the data-plane wiring is the risky part.
        - **5c-ii-a — `resolve_handshake` decision (server) — DONE (#159).** `SchemaRegistry::resolve_handshake(app_id, version) → Resolution` — the pure tier decision: empty `app_id` or an unregistered app → `Relay`; a registered app with a declared known version → `Enforcing { version }`; version `0` (dynamic) → `Enforcing { head }`; a declared version the registry does not hold → `Reject`. Pure, Miri-clean. **Deferred to 5c-ii-b:** sharing the registry into the data plane + acting on the resolution at `Hello`.
        - **5c-ii-b — wire resolve into the handshake (server)**. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04) logic → serve-chain wiring:
          - **5c-ii-b-1 — resolve at Hello (session + registry) — DONE (#160).** `step` gains a `&SchemaRegistry` param; the `Hello` handler calls `resolve_handshake` — `Reject` → an `UnsupportedVersion` error + close (before the client is a subscriber), `Relay`/`Enforcing` → proceed. `Session` stores `app_id` + the resolved `schema_version` (`None` = relay) with accessors. `Registry` holds an `Arc<Mutex<SchemaRegistry>>` (empty by default → all Relay, so existing tests/`Registry::new` are untouched) with a `set_schema_registry` setter; `deliver` locks it and passes it to `step`. Spec at both the `step` and `Registry::deliver` levels; Miri-clean. **Deferred to 5c-ii-b-2:** threading the shared registry through the serve chain + sharing it with the admin plane in `main.rs`.
          - **5c-ii-b-2 — share the registry across planes (serve chain + main) — DONE (#161).** One `Arc<Mutex<SchemaRegistry>>` threads through `ServeConfig.schema` → `registry_actor` → `Registry::set_schema_registry` (data plane) and into `serve_admin` (admin plane writes it, locking per request around the sync dispatch — never across an await). `ServeConfig` dropped `Copy` (kept `Clone`) to hold the `Arc`; it's only ever moved once, built via `..default()`, so the drop is transparent. `main.rs` creates the shared registry and wires both planes; a registration over the admin plane is now visible to the data-plane handshake (ws integration test: serve with a registered app, an unknown version is refused at the handshake). Miri-clean.
      - **5c-iii — client declares app_id + schema_version (core client + SDK)**. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-04): the core `ClientSession` API is pure and self-contained; the SDK surface spans four language bindings.
        - **5c-iii-a — `ClientSession` declares the app (core) — DONE (#164).** `ClientSession` gains `app_id: Vec<u8>` (empty → relay) + `schema_version: u32` (0 → relay or dynamic) fields, a `declare_app(app_id, schema_version)` setter, and `app_id()`/`schema_version()` accessors; `hello()` carries the declared values instead of the hardcoded empty/0. `new` still opens a bare relay, so existing callers are untouched. Pure core, Miri-clean.
        - **5c-iii-b — SDK surface for the app declaration (FFI + Python/Go/wasm) — DONE (#165).** `crdtsync_client_declare_app(client, app_id, app_id_len, schema_version)` on the C ABI (empty `app_id` → relay; returns 1/-1), regenerated into the cbindgen header, plus `Client.declare_app` (Python ctypes), `(*Client).DeclareApp` (Go cgo), and `WasmClient.declare_app` (wasm-bindgen). Each binding sets the app before `hello`; smoke tests in FFI/Python/Go assert the declared app rides along in the Hello frame. Completes 5c-iii; the whole 5c handshake-resolve arc is done. (Advisory client schema / handshake advertisement is Unit 6.)
  - **Doc-level ACL-as-CRDT — the dynamic ownership + per-doc role tier** (NEW gate, design pinned 2026-07-03 in ARCHITECTURE §Authorization). The runtime half of the two-tier model: doc-ACL tuples `{ subject: Actor|Group|class, grant: Capability(read|write|publish_awareness|own)|Role(name), effect: allow|deny, path, grantor }` as CRDT-merged doc state. Delivers: **per-doc role assignment** (an owner grants a role to an actor *or group* scoped to a path — the doc-scoped complement to global token roles) and **group subjects** (token carries group membership; tuples target groups); the walk-up-the-path evaluator computing **effective roles** (token ∪ per-doc) then mapping through `@auth`; **provenance-based revocation** (removable only by the tuple's `grantor` or above the grant chain); **provenance-bounded deny** (deny beats static defaults always, but between doc-ACL grants overrides only the deny author's own subtree — never a peer/superior, so it isn't a backdoor around revocation); attenuated recursive delegation; superior carve-outs (`deny own` on a subpath); creator-auto-owns-`/`; and the `AclGrant`/`AclRevoke` enforcement points. Feeds the same decision flow as `@auth` (steps 1–3). Depends on Units 4+5 (evaluator + claims + resource model). Large — sliced per piece:
    - **Slice 1 — tuple CRDT state (core) — DONE (#219).** `src/acl.rs`: `AclTuple { id, subject, grant, effect, path, grantor }` — `AclSubject { Actor(ClientId) | Group | Authenticated | Anonymous | Anyone }`, `AclGrant { Capability(Read|Write|PublishAwareness|Own) | Role }`, `AclEffect { Allow | Deny }`. A document-level CRDT set mirroring RangedElement 3a: `AclGrant`/`AclRevoke` ops (codec tags 19/20), tuple id derives from the op stamp under a fixed `crdtsync\0acl` namespace (concurrent grants union to distinct ids), a tuple is immutable, revoke tombstones (retained, delete-wins) + buffers until its grant + a local revoke of an unseen id emits nothing. Held on `Document` (`acl_tuple`/`acl_tuples`/`acl_on`), authored via `tx.acl().grant(...)`/`revoke(...)`, persisted id-sorted (tombstones incl.); STATE_VERSION 7→8. **Storage + merge only** — `grantor` is an explicit op field the caller sets; core stores it faithfully and enforces no authority (no provenance/delegation/deny rules — later slices, server-side). Spec `tests/acl.rs`; Miri-clean.
    - **Slice 2 — evaluator + effective-roles (core) — DONE (#224).** Pure decision function over the slice-1 tuple set: `decide_capability(tuples, actor, path, cap) -> AclDecision{Allow|Deny|Abstain}` (the composable tier seam), `evaluate(...) -> bool` (total, deny-by-default entrypoint), `effective_roles(tuples, actor, path)` (token ∪ per-doc assignments). `AclActor{id,groups,roles,authenticated}` — the caller supplies membership (core hosts no identity provider). **Deny-overrides** (a matching deny beats any allow, path specificity not a tiebreaker), **default deny-unless-granted** (empty → Abstain at the tier so `@auth` composes above), **`Own` implies Read/Write/PublishAwareness** (deny is capability-separated), **roles are grants not subjects** (a `Role` grant is a per-doc role assignment; single-pass, no role→role cycle). Reads tuples as present — no authority check (that's slice 3). Landed in CORE (evaluator is pure), not server. `tests/acl_eval.rs` (20); Miri-clean. **Server composition** (path-carrying `Resource`, decision-flow step 4 `@auth` mapping) is the enforcement-wiring slice's job. See Done + DECISIONS.
    - **Slice 3 — delegation / provenance / bounded-deny (core→server).** Sub-sliced (cs-next 2026-07-08) foundation → the rest:
      - **3a — provenance foundation: creator-auto-owns-`/` + provenance-based revocation (core) — DONE (#226).** Creator implicitly owns `/` (a synthesized root `Own` tuple, bootstrap authority root); a revoke tombstone is *honored* only when its author is the grant's `grantor`, the creator, or an actor-id `Own`-owner of the grant's path — an unauthorized revoke is disregarded (grant stays effective), evaluation-layer only (the set still tombstones content-neutrally; authority decided over the merged view, never rejected at merge). `AclRecord{tuple, revoked_by-set}` + `decide_capability_with_authority`/`evaluate_with_authority` + `Document::acl_records()`. Revoke authority is actor-id-scoped (class/group `Own` confers none — fails closed). See Done + DECISIONS. Deferred to 3b: the recursive chain (a revoked owner can still authorize within 3a), bounded-deny, group-aware authority.
      - **3b — bounded-deny + attenuated delegation + carve-outs (core→server).** Sub-sliced (cs-next 2026-07-08) chain → deny-scoping:
        - **3b-i — attenuated recursive delegation chain (core) — DONE (#228).** Authority is a recursive attenuated walk up the grant chain to the creator root: an ALLOW grant confers authority only if its grantor validly owned the path (or an ancestor), recursively to the creator (the un-granted root); an unrooted grant is inert; only ownership confers granting power, a grant can't exceed the granter's scope; a revoked owner no longer authorizes a revoke (closes the 3a hole). Rooting + revocation are mutually recursive, solved as an **iterative least-fixpoint** (no native recursion, order-independent, bounded pass count `2N+2`, fail-closed on oscillating/forged cycles → a crafted cycle roots at no creator and confers nothing, can't stack-overflow). Rooting gates only ALLOW grants — a `Deny` stays as-present (deny provenance is 3b-ii). `tests/acl_delegation.rs` (13); Miri-clean. See Done + DECISIONS.
        - **3b-ii — provenance-bounded deny + superior carve-outs + creator-deny immunity (core) — DONE (#230).** A deny overrides a grant only when its author has authority over that grant (own subtree) — an out-of-authority (subordinate/peer) deny is disregarded (a deny isn't a revocation backdoor); in-authority deny still works (slice-2 deny-overrides + `deny own` superior carve-outs preserved); a deny still beats static defaults always; the synthesized creator-owns tuple is immune to a subordinate's `Deny(Own)`. Folds deny-provenance into the 3b-i fixpoint (fail-closed on ambiguity). **Completes the core ACL provenance stack** — the doc-ACL evaluator is now correct (creator-owns, rooted delegation, bounded deny). See Done + DECISIONS.
    - **Slice 4 — server enforcement + grant/revoke wire + SDK. The identity-model gate is RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-13): the ACL principal is the AUTHENTICATED ACTOR** (`Identity.actor`, credential-derived), not the per-device `ClientId` — same-human-across-devices ownership. Sub-sliced:
      - **4a — server enforcement of the doc-ACL tier (actor-keyed) — DONE (#PR).** `Hub::acl_records(room)` (mirrors `logged_versions`) + `Hub::room_creator`/`ensure_creator`; `Room.creator` = the first main-log writer, set-once, **durable** (new `RoomMeta.creator` field, restored on load).
