The tier slots into `acl::authorized` (now takes a pre-resolved `doc_acl: Decision`) between the deployment and schema tiers — deployment `Deny`/`Allow` terminal (a grant can't re-open a deployment refusal), then `doc_acl_tier` (maps `Action`→`Capability`, builds the `AclActor` from the `Identity`, runs `decide_capability_with_authority` at the root path), then schema `@auth`, then default-deny. **Consumed at op-submit Write only** (`session.rs`); Subscribe-read/awareness/version/fan-out pass `Abstain` (read is held for 4b so subscribe-read + fan-out redaction land together and never disagree). `Room.creator` is set only for an **authenticated** first writer (anon ids are ephemeral → never the authority root); creator is durable but **not** re-derivable (best-effort persist, accepted). **Representation:** a **bridge, not a core re-key** — the server derives a stable `actor_key = SHA-256(actor)[..16]` and feeds it to core as the actor id / creator / grantor, so core (still `ClientId`-keyed) is untouched → **server-only, CI Miri (`-p crdtsync-core`) unaffected**. Trade-off (hash collision space, opaque id) + a possible later native-actor core re-key logged in DECISIONS. Spec `tests/acl_enforcement.rs` (11, incl. two-devices-one-principal + no-tuple regression) + creator-durability round-trip. → *Authorization*.
      - **4b — outbound per-recipient doc-ACL redaction (server) — DONE (#PR).** When a committed op fans out to a room's subscribers, each recipient receives only the ops in subtrees its actor may **read** — the rest silently withheld from *it* while an authorized peer still gets them (per-recipient, from the same batch). Redaction is **per-op-path**: `Hub::element_paths(room)` walks the doc into an element-id→key-path index over **every** container (a map keys its children; a non-map container's node-addressed descendants — list items, an XML element's attrs/children/nested elements — inherit its path), `acl::op_read_path` resolves each op's document path (a keyed op = target map path + key; an id-addressed op = target container's path; a doc-level ACL/Ranged op, or an unresolved target, = **root** — the strictest authority, so it never leaks yet a whole-document reader stays convergent), and `acl::recipient_reads_path` composes the doc-ACL **Read** verdict at that path (creator-owns-`/`) with the deployment+schema tiers exactly as the write gate composes. A room with **no doc-ACL tuples** takes the pre-4b path byte-for-byte (no walk, no per-op check) — regression-locked. **Redact-then-translate**: redaction runs on the *authored* ops, then the surviving subset is migration-translated per recipient (translation can drop/rekey ops and would otherwise desync the path lookup). **Catch-up**: the Subscribe `Catchup::Ops` delta is filtered by the same per-path read authority, so a fresh partial reader replays only its granted subtrees; a compacted-room `Catchup::Snapshot` can't be op-filtered, so a reader **without whole-document read is refused** it (closing the leak the widened gate would open), snapshot projection deferred **(now CLOSED for doc-ACL by #284 — the `partial-reader-snapshot-refused` / `deny-carve-out-snapshot-refused` deferrals are served a projected snapshot, see 4d below)**. The **subscribe read gate** widens to admit a subtree-scoped reader (holds read on *any* subtree via `has_any_read_grant`) — but only where the deployment abstains, so a **deployment read-deny stays terminal**. Awareness fan-out stays on the room-level `peer_may_read` gate (unchanged). Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src` → CI Miri unaffected); spec `tests/acl_redaction.rs` (9: per-recipient subtree redaction, creator-reads-all, no-ACL regression, catch-up partial replay, two-devices-one-redaction, nested-XML reaches subtree reader, deployment-deny-not-reopened, partial-reader-snapshot-refused, deny-carve-out-snapshot-refused), Miri-clean. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-13). → *Authorization*. (v0.2)
      - **4c — grant/revoke SDK surface (FFI + Python/Go/wasm) — DONE (#271).** The SDK API to author `AclGrant`/`AclRevoke`, completing the Doc-ACL epic. Core already carried the `AclGrant`/`AclRevoke` ops + the `Document::acl()` cursor, so this is **SDK/FFI-only** (zero `crates/core/src` change → CI Miri `-p crdtsync-core` unaffected). **FFI** (`crates/ffi`): a doc surface `crdtsync_doc_acl_grant(subject_kind, subject, grant_kind, capability, role, effect, path, grantor, out_id, out_ops) -> i32` (1 ok / -1 bad enum|null) + `crdtsync_doc_acl_revoke(id, out_ops) -> i32` (1 emitted / 0 inert / -1 malformed), and a client surface `crdtsync_client_acl_grant`/`crdtsync_client_acl_revoke` routing through `ClientSession::enqueue_ops` (acked/resent). The grant marshals the `AclGrant` fields as discriminants + byte-strings matching the op codec's tags (subject 0 actor/1 group/2 authenticated/3 anonymous/4 anyone; grant 0 capability(0 read/1 write/2 publish-awareness/3 own)/1 role; effect 0 allow/1 deny); a grant hands back the tuple's 16-byte id (the revoke handle). Total — a bad handle/enum → -1, never a panic. **`crdtsync_actor_key(actor) -> 16 bytes`** exposes the server's `actor_key = SHA-256(actor)[..16]` derivation so an app builds an `Actor` subject + grantor the server matches (the SDK marshals a 16-byte id faithfully; the derivation is a separate composable call). Regenerated cbindgen header + header symbol-assert. **Python/Go/wasm** forward the same authoring idiomatically (`SubjectKind`/`Capability`/`Effect` enums; wasm binds core directly, deriving `actorKey` in-Rust with `sha2`). Specs: `ffi.rs`/`client.rs` (decode the emitted op → assert subject/cap/effect/path/grantor, converge on a second replica, revoke tombstones, bad-input totality, actor-key determinism), wasm `web.rs` + Go `acl_test.go` + Python `test_acl.py` (op-shape decode incl. wasm's independent discriminant mapping, convergence, unheld-channel/inert cases). Targeted FFI Miri clean; two review agents (added the wasm op-decode assertion + the actor_key surface). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-13). → *Authorization*.
      - **4d — doc-ACL snapshot projection for partial readers (core + server) — DONE (#284).** Closes the 4b deferral: a subtree-scoped doc-ACL reader is no longer **refused** a compacted room's cold-start `Catchup::Snapshot` — the snapshot is **projected** to its readable paths and served, mirroring how a zone-limited subscriber is served a `project_zones`-narrowed snapshot. **Core:** `Document::project_read_paths(reads: impl Fn(&[Vec<u8>]) -> bool)` — the doc-ACL analogue of `project_zones`. `reads` is the server's composed read verdict at a `core::path`, the same authority `op_read_path` gates each op on, so the projection drops exactly what the op stream withholds. A container survives only if **every prefix** of its path reads (an unreadable level withholds its create op on catch-up, so a deeper grant that re-opens a descendant can never apply — an unreadable container drops its whole subtree, no orphan left in the registries); a **leaf slot** is gated at the map path + slot key, so a leaf-level deny drops just that slot inside a readable container; doc-level ACL tuples + ranged annotations are root-path-governed, so they go with an unreadable root; a whole-document reader is an identity projection (byte-identical re-encode). Clears `seen`/buffer once anything drops (no op-count leak), prunes a cut counter's registry entry (no phantom tally). **Server:** `session::project_snapshot_reads` drives the projection from `recipient_reads_path` per path, then the existing zone projection stacks on top (doc-ACL ∩ zones). **Convergence (asserted):** a snapshot-served partial joiner materializes the same replica as an op-served one — both drop the same subtrees. Specs `crates/core/tests/state_project_read.rs` (9: subtree retain/drop, whole-subtree drop, all-deny/all-admit identity, codec round-trip, root-leaf gating + counter-phantom, leaf-level deny, nested-grant-no-orphan, doc-ACL × zones intersection) + `tests/acl_redaction.rs` extended (partial-reader served-not-refused, op-join↔snapshot-join convergence, carve-out projected, leaf-level deny projected, no-ACL full-snapshot regression). Two review agents caught the container-granularity leaf/nested-grant leak (the first-cut `HashSet<ElementId>` couldn't express a leaf-level or below-a-denied-ancestor deny) → reworked to the path-predicate with all-prefixes subtree semantics + regression tests. Targeted core + server Miri clean; no STATE_VERSION change. Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Authorization*. (v0.2)
      - **4e — per-recipient ACL-tuple redaction by governing path (server + core) — DONE (#285).** ACL state is itself privacy-sensitive — a tuple reveals a subject, an effect, and the existence of a governed path — so redact it by the tuple's **own** path, not root. 4b/4d sent every `AclGrant`/`AclRevoke` op and every persisted ACL tuple only to whole-document (root-gated) readers; now a subtree reader receives the ACL tuples governing paths it may read and **none** on paths it may not. **Op-stream (`acl::op_read_path`, now takes the server's `records`):** `AclGrant{path}` reads at that path (already the encoded-path format `op_read_path` returns); `AclRevoke{id}` resolves its id to the tombstoned tuple's path via the full record set (root fallback for an id no held tuple carries → a moot revoke reaching only a whole-doc reader), so a recipient sees the revoke exactly where it saw the grant. `RangedCreate/SetPayload/Delete` stay root-gated (path-scoped ranged/marks redaction is a separate privacy axis — noted follow-on). **Snapshot (`Document::project_read_paths`):** each ACL tuple kept iff its own path is readable by the predicate, mirroring the op rule (a dropped tuple joins the `seen`/buffer scrub condition); ranged annotations stay root-gated. **Outbound-only:** the server keeps its FULL tuple set + evaluates authority over all of it — only what each recipient *receives* narrows, so a partial-ACL client's view is advisory/incomplete **by design** (no ancestor-chain "completion", which would leak ancestor ACL — the opposite of the goal). **Convergence (asserted):** an op-served and a snapshot-served partial reader materialize the same ACL subset — same path authority, same result. Specs `tests/acl_redaction.rs` (+grant-by-path, +revoke-by-path, +creator-gets-every-ACL-op, catch-up + op↔snapshot convergence extended for ACL) + `tests/state_project_read.rs` (+kept/dropped-by-path, +whole-doc identity, +root-unreadable subtree-tuple survives, +op-join↔snapshot-join ACL convergence). No STATE_VERSION change (redaction narrows what is sent, not the persisted format); targeted core + server Miri clean, full sharded CI Miri green (core ~21m slowest, ≤30min). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Authorization*. (v0.2)
      - **4f — per-recipient RangedElement redaction by anchor path (server + core) — DONE (#287).** The final wire-redaction axis: marks/annotations are privacy-sensitive exactly like ACL tuples — a RangedElement's existence, name, and payload leak content-region info at both endpoints — so redact each by the path(s) of the sequence(s) its endpoints anchor, not root. 4b/4d/4e sent every `RangedCreate`/`RangedSetPayload`/`RangedDelete` op and every persisted RangedElement only to whole-document (root-gated) readers; now a subtree reader gets only the annotations over content it can read. **Governing rule (require-all, fail-closed):** a RangedElement is visible to a recipient iff it can read the path of **every** sequence its endpoints anchor — a single-sequence mark (the common case) has one governing path (`start.seq == end.seq`), a cross-element range (ranged 3b) two, and BOTH must read. **Op-stream (`acl::op_read_paths`, new):** returns an op's governing path *set* — a Ranged op's distinct anchor seq paths, a single path (delegating to `op_read_path`) for every other op; the fan-out (`registry.rs`) and catch-up (`session.rs`) filters require `recipient_reads_path` on ALL of them. A `RangedCreate` carries its anchors; a `SetPayload`/`Delete` carries only the range id, resolved through `Hub::ranged_anchors`/`Document::ranged_anchors` (the held anchor set, **tombstoned ranges included** — a just-applied delete's target is already tombstoned — mirroring `acl_records` for revokes). An unresolved anchor seq or an unheld id falls back to root (only a whole-doc reader carries it). **Snapshot (`Document::project_read_paths`):** keeps a RangedElement iff every anchor seq's path is readable by the predicate, same require-all rule; a dropped range joins the `seen`/buffer scrub. **Outbound-only:** the server keeps the FULL RangedElement set + evaluates authority over all of it — only what each recipient *receives* narrows. **Convergence (asserted):** a fresh op-served and a fresh snapshot-served partial reader materialize the same RangedElement subset — same require-all path authority + same root fallback both sides. Specs `tests/acl_redaction.rs` (+single-seq mark reaches its seq's reader only, +cross-element range require-all — the load-bearing test, +SetPayload/Delete follow the range's visibility, +creator-gets-every-Ranged-op, +no-ACL regression, +partial-reader snapshot keeps only fully-read ranges) + `tests/state_project_read.rs` (+single-seq kept/dropped by anchor path, +cross-element require-all, +op-join↔snapshot-join ranged convergence, +deleted-anchor root fallback). No STATE_VERSION change (redaction narrows what is sent, not the persisted format); targeted core + server Miri clean, full sharded CI Miri green (slowest job server 1/3 ~17m25s, ≤30min). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-15). → *Authorization*. (v0.2)
    → *Authorization*. (v0.2 — **Doc-ACL epic COMPLETE: 4a enforcement · 4b redaction · 4c grant/revoke SDK · 4d snapshot projection · 4e ACL-tuple redaction · 4f RangedElement redaction — per-recipient doc-ACL wire redaction now COMPLETE across every op/state axis: ops, snapshot, ACL tuples, and RangedElements**)
  - **Unit 6 — handshake schema advertisement + version negotiation**: enforcing server advertises its active schema+version; client caches across restart; client declares on handshake the single version it wants ops delivered at (server is the compatibility layer — a "range of one" normally); relay stays schema-free. The client-declares half landed in 5c-iii (`declare_app`); this unit is the *server→client advertisement* + client cache/adopt. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-05) wire → server → client, mirroring the 5c handshake arc:
    - **6a — advertisement carrier (wire, core) — DONE (#177).** `Message::SchemaAdvert { schema_version: u32, schema: Vec<u8> }` (wire tag 21) carries the enforcing server's active version + schema bytes. Codec length-frames the `u32` version + bytes; round-trip (empty + non-empty), truncation-at-every-cut, trailing-bytes coverage. Carried only: the core client `receive` accepts it without effect (drives no replica — adoption is 6c), the server `step` rejects a client-sent one as a protocol violation (server→client only). Break wire compat (pre-release); Miri-clean. Mirrors 5c-i (#158).
    - **6b — server advertises on an enforcing handshake (server) — DONE (#178).** `step`'s `Hello` handler answers an `Enforcing` handshake with `Message::SchemaAdvert { schema_version, schema }` carrying the resolved version + its registered bytes; `Relay`/`Reject` advertise nothing. Resolved under the single registry lock — `resolve_handshake` now returns the bytes in `Resolution::Enforcing { version, schema }` (it already resolved them to validate the version), so the handler advertises without a second lookup and no dead empty-bytes fallback. Tests: enforcing yields the advert (resolved version + bytes), relay/unregistered none, dynamic v0 the head; the socket handshake consumes the advert before AuthOk. Miri-clean.
    - **6c — client caches + adopts the advertised schema (core client + SDK)**. `ClientSession` receives the advert, records the active version, and (for a dynamic client) adopts the schema bytes; caching across restart is the SDK's persistence surface. Feeds `Document::set_schema` for repair/validation once the client-integration wiring lands. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-05) core → SDK, mirroring 5c-iii:
      - **6c-i — `ClientSession` records the advertised schema (core) — DONE (#179).** `receive(SchemaAdvert { schema_version, schema })` stores the server-advertised *active* `{version, bytes}` (in `active_schema: Option<(u32, Vec<u8>)>`, distinct from the declared `schema_version`, which a dynamic client leaves 0) instead of accepting it inertly; latest advert wins; `active_schema_version() -> Option<u32>` + `active_schema() -> Option<&[u8]>` accessors. Pure core, Miri-clean, mirrors 5c-iii-a (#164).
      - **6c-ii — SDK surface for the advertised schema (FFI + Python/Go/wasm) — DONE (#180).** `crdtsync_client_active_schema_version(client, out) -> i32` and `crdtsync_client_active_schema(client, out) -> i32` on the C ABI — the out-param + status idiom (1 present / 0 none / -1 bad handle|null out, mirroring `crdtsync_client_actor`), regenerated into the header, plus `active_schema_version -> int|None` / `active_schema -> bytes|None` (Python), `ActiveSchemaVersion() (uint32,bool)` / `ActiveSchema() ([]byte,bool)` (Go), `active_schema_version -> Option<u32>` / `active_schema -> Option<Vec<u8>>` (wasm). Status idiom over an in-band sentinel so an advert whose body is empty (or version 0) reads as *present*, consistent across all four SDKs (empty-body regression test in every harness). The app persists it across restart itself — the SDK caches, owns no storage. Completes Unit 6c and **Unit 6**. Mirrors 5c-iii-b (#165).
  - **Unit 7 — migration engine (core)**: versioned transforms with the built-in step kinds, each carrying a bidirectional op-rewrite (forward always; inverse iff back-compat); back-compat vs breaking edge classification; migration-as-log-entries; the four detection gates (CI drift/verify, boot hash-lock, per-doc reachability). Unit 6 (handshake advertisement) is complete, so the client now learns its served version; this builds the transforms that bridge versions. Core, dep-minimal. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-05) model → classify → op-rewrite → chain/gates, mirroring the schema arc:
      - **7a — migration model + parser (core) — DONE (#181).** `core::migration` total-decodes a migration-edge JSON into a `Migration { from, to, steps: Vec<Step> }` over the six structural step kinds — `addType`/`removeType`/`renameType`/`addField`/`removeField`/`renameField`, aligned to `schema::TypeDef`. Hand-rolled over `core::json` (no serde); `addType`'s `def` reuses `schema::parse_type_def` (now `pub(crate)`) — one type-def parser. Parse-time validation: contiguous `to == from + 1` (via `checked_add`, no boundary overflow), `from >= 1`, `u32` versions, non-empty names, unknown key rejected at every level; total-decode (every input → a `MigrationError`, never a panic). Model + parser only. Format + step-kind set logged in DECISIONS. Mirrors schema Unit 1b (#136); reuses `core::json` (#135). Value-transform kinds (wrap/setAttr/mapValues) deferred — need marks/XML (unbuilt).
      - **7b — compatibility classification (core) — DONE (#182).** `migration::Compat { BackCompatible, Breaking }`; `Step::compat` (addType/addField → back-compat, remove*/rename* → breaking) + `Migration::compat` (back-compat iff every step is; empty edge inverts trivially). The class the gates + handshake range-check consume. Pure model-level; builds on the 7a model (#181).
      - **7c — bidirectional op-rewrite (core) — DONE (#183).** `migration::OpRewrite { Keep(Op), Drop }`; `Step::rewrite_up(&Op)` (always) + `Step::rewrite_down(&Op) -> Option` (Some iff back-compat). Only field steps rewrite (RenameField rewrites the slot key, RemoveField drops up, AddField drops down); type steps inert since an op never names a type. Pure per-op — type-scoped applicability + edge composition deferred to Unit 8 (DECISIONS). Builds on 7a/7b (#181/#182).
      - **7d — chain composition + reachability (core) — DONE (#184).** Edge-level `Migration::rewrite_up/rewrite_down` fold an edge's steps (up forward, down last-to-first, `None` for a breaking edge, a `Drop` short-circuits); chain-level `rewrite_up_along`/`rewrite_down_along` compose a contiguous ascending version path into one `OpRewrite` (up walks it forward, down in reverse); `reachable_down` is the per-doc gate — true iff every edge is back-compatible. `rewrite_down_along` checks `reachable_down` up front so `rewrite_down_along(..).is_some() == reachable_down` holds for every op (a `Drop` from an upper edge can't hide a lower breaking edge). Completes Unit 7's core migration engine. Consumes 7a/7b/7c (#181/#182/#183); composition order + reachability + path-contiguity precondition logged in DECISIONS.
  - **Unit 8 — in-flight per-recipient translation (server)**: compose the per-edge op-rewrites along the chain per recipient on the fan-out seam (the redaction seam #83), over the immutable heterogeneous log (each op stored at creation version); cold-start migrates materialized state into the snapshot; the handshake range-check refuses an unbridgeable-breaking-gap recipient (`onUpdateRequired`) so fan-out only traverses invertible edges. Consumes Unit 7's `rewrite_up_along`/`rewrite_down_along`/`reachable_down` (#184). Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-05) engine → wiring → gate → cold-start:
      - **8a — migration chain resolution + per-op translation engine (server) — DONE (#185).** Pure `crate::translate` module over `SchemaRegistry`: `translate_op(reg, app_id, op, from, to)` rewrites one op from its creation version to a recipient's (identity / up via `rewrite_up_along` / down via `rewrite_down_along`, `Unreachable` on a breaking down-gap, `Drop` preserved); `reachable(reg, app_id, from, to)` is the handshake gate predicate (up always once edges exist, down via `reachable_down`); private `edge_slice` walks `SchemaRegistry::migration(app_id, v)` for `v in low+1..=high`, `MissingEdge` (gap/past-head/unknown-app) vs `BadMigration` (unparseable), overflow-free. Uncached free fns (the parse cache lands on `Registry` in 8b). Versions are ≥ 1 (documented precondition); migration bytes parse lazily at fan-out, matching the opaque-store + lazy-parse of schemas. Consumes Unit 7 (#184); logged in DECISIONS.
      - **8b — heterogeneous op tagging + persisted log (server + store) — DONE (#186).** Each stored op carries `StoredOp { op, schema_version: Option<u32> }` (`None` relay, `Some(n)` the writing connection's version); `Hub::ingest(room, ops, schema_version)` tags the batch, a private `ingest_records` is the shared commit path so store replay preserves per-op versions rather than re-tagging. The store record gained a `u32` version prefix (`0` = relay) — a format break taken freely pre-release, no old-log migration; core `encode_op`/`decode_op` untouched. `Hub::logged_versions` exposes the retained log's tags. Broadcast/catch-up still bare ops. Tag source is the connection's negotiated version — a wire-declared per-op version is the authoritative source, deferred (DECISIONS 8b). Consumes 8a (#185).
      - **8c — live fan-out op translation (server) — DONE (#187).** Wired translation into the `deliver` broadcast loop: `Response.broadcast_version` carries the writer's version; per recipient, translate the batch from it to `session.schema_version()`. **App-scoped** — translation walks the room's governing app's chain, so it applies only when the writer speaks that app and only to recipients of that app (a foreign-app version is a different space; verbatim otherwise). **Container-verbatim** — `Chain::translate_ops` never drops or key-rewrites a container-create (`OpKind::creates_container`, an exhaustive core predicate); a key-local rewrite cannot reach the container's keyless descendants, so dropping/renaming the create would tear the subtree. **Destrand-for-convergence** — a partly-translatable tx delivers its representable survivors with the `tx` tag stripped (they apply standalone rather than buffering for a `count` that can't complete); dropping them would diverge the recipient from the writer's down-projection. **Resolve-once + per-target memo** — the parsed `Chain` per distinct target is resolved off the lock and the batch translated once per target version (not per recipient); the verbatim common case never locks/parses and clones once per channel. An unreachable/broken chain drops that recipient's batch, fail-closed, until 8e refuses it at the handshake. Consumed 8b (#186) + 8a (#185); DECISIONS 8c.
      - **8d — catch-up delta op translation (server) — DONE (#188).** `Catchup::Ops` carries `StoredOp` (each op's creation version); `session::step`'s Subscribe handler translates the heterogeneous delta to the joiner's version along the governing app's chain (new `governing_app` param) via `translate::translate_delta`, which segments consecutive same-version runs (one resolved `Chain` per source version, order + per-tx destrand preserved) and drops an unreachable run fail-closed. Ingest now tags a write's version only when the writer speaks the governing app (else `None`), so a foreign write is logged relay-like and passes verbatim on both seams. Consumed 8b/8c; DECISIONS 8d.
      - **8e — handshake range-check + `onUpdateRequired` (server + wire) — DONE (#189).** At subscribe, `session::step` refuses a joiner that cannot reach the room's governing version across a back-compatible path — new `ErrorCode::UpdateRequired` (wire tag 6, the `onUpdateRequired` signal) returned with no catch-up, before it becomes a subscriber, so fan-out down-translation only ever traverses invertible edges. `subscriber_reaches_governing` range-checks only an **enforcing joiner of the governing app** (`reachable(reg, app, governing_version, client_version)` — governing is the room's worst-case op version, so its down-path subsumes any op stored below it); a relay / foreign-app / versionless joiner is a different version space, never refused; a broken chain refuses fail-closed. `governing` now threads the bound `(app, version)` (was app-only) from `deliver` through `step`; the enforcing-governing-app-speaker predicate is one helper (`governing_target`) shared by the write-tag, catch-up-translate, and range-check sites. Consumed 8a/8c/8d; DECISIONS 8e.
      - **8f — cold-start snapshot migration (server) — DONE (#190).** `translate::translate_snapshot(reg, app, state, from, to)` translates the below-floor materialized snapshot to the joining recipient's version via `Document::migrate_leaf_slots(fate)` — the op seam's key rewrites at snapshot granularity: across every map each leaf slot (scalar/register/counter, live or tombstoned) is `Keep`/`Drop`/`Rename`d per a per-key `SlotFate` (probed through the same `translate_op` the ops seam uses), a live container carried verbatim (mirroring container-verbatim on the ops seam); re-encodes only on change (same/newer/unknown-app/unreachable → bytes verbatim). `session.rs` wires it into the `Catchup::Snapshot` arm, gated by `governing_target` and short-circuiting `from == to` before locking. **Counter-registry migration is decoupled from the slot value** — a counter is retained in the registry at `derive(map, key, Counter)` across displacement (#48), so Drop prunes it and Rename re-homes it from the *key*, independent of whether the slot still holds it live (fixing a displaced/tombstoned-counter phantom on both paths). **A renamed register re-derives its id** to `derive(map, newKey, Register)`, matching the id an op-served peer's renamed `RegisterSet` derives (else the encoded id diverges). Rename is two-phase with an isolated tally capture, so any fate (chains, cross-type collisions) is order-independent. Closes the last untranslated seam — a snapshot-served joiner now converges byte-identically with an op-delta peer. Consumed 8a/8e; DECISIONS 8f. **Completes Unit 8 (in-flight per-recipient translation).**
      - **type-scope applicability filter — DONE (#274).** A migration field step (`renameField`/`removeField`/`addField`) is declared for a schema type `ty`, but the per-op key rewrite (`migration::Step::rewrite_up`/`rewrite_down`, key-based `rename_key`/`drop_on_key`) discarded `ty`, so it rewrote any slot of that name regardless of the owning element's declared type — correct only while field names were unique across types. Narrowed at the **server seam**: `index::element_types(doc, schema)` / `Hub::element_types` projects the room document to `element id → declared type name` by the same root-down, position-keyed descent `core::validate` walks (the mirror of `element_zone`, layered on the #273 element-context index). The fan-out (`registry.rs`) and catch-up delta (`session.rs`) resolve each op's owning type via `op.target` (the owning map) and gate each field step through `Chain::translate_ops_scoped` — a step applies only when the owning type matches its `ty`; an **unresolved** owning type falls back to the key-based rewrite (the field-name-unique common case, unregressed). The **snapshot seam** narrows identically: the one additive core change, `Document::migrate_leaf_slots_scoped`, threads the owning map id to the fate closure (the op's `target` analogue), and `translate_snapshot_scoped` resolves types over the decoded snapshot tree with the same governing schema — so an op-served joiner and a snapshot-served one converge **byte-identically**. Type steps stay inert per-op (an op never names a type). Server-only but for the additive `migrate_leaf_slots_scoped`; specs `crates/server/tests/migration_type_scope.rs` (narrowing, no-schema key fallback, op/snapshot parity, multi-edge chain, unresolvable-owner totality) + core `state_project.rs` scoped-fate regression; targeted + full Miri clean; local review (refuted a live-vs-catch-up schema-asymmetry finding — both seams type against the room's governing/acting schema). Design in DECISIONS (2026-07-14). **Deferred:** a field step crossing a `renameType`, and a container created under a since-renamed field key, both fall back to key-based (convergence preserved) — subsumed by the faithful container-field item below.
      - **faithful container-field migration — DONE (#289). Gate RESOLVED as PERSIST (2026-07-15).** The create-then-delete-then-rename divergence — op seam resurrects the create at the old key + re-keys the delete, but the materialized snapshot had dropped the create's LWW stamp so it carried the tombstone verbatim under the old key — is closed by **persisting the deleted container's `(create-stamp, kind)` on its tombstone slot** (new map-slot tag `SLOT_CONTAINER_TOMB`, `STATE_VERSION 9→10`). `migrate_leaf_slots_scoped` now resurrects the exact recorded-kind container live at the old key and re-keys the delete, so an op-served and a snapshot-served joiner reach **byte-identical shared state** — extending the op↔snapshot convergence #274 gave leaf fields to container fields. The persisted kind disambiguates a key that hosted more than one container kind over its life; `Map::merge` carries only the LWW winner's identity so the merge stays commutative. Cost is narrow — **25 bytes (a stamp + 1-byte kind) per deleted-container tombstone only**; live slots and leaf tombstones pay nothing, and the baseline per-slot stamp was already universal. Chose persist over accept because the tax is that narrow and it restores strict op↔snapshot byte-convergence. Pre-release: `STATE_VERSION` mismatch is rejected hard, no dual-decode. **The migration follow-on list is now empty.** (DECISIONS 2026-07-15.)
      - **8g — op-version high-water range-check (server) — DONE (#241).** The handshake range-check now keys on the room's **op-version high-water** — the highest governing-app op version ever folded into the merged replica (a persistent per-room `max_op_version`, updated on ingest, surviving compaction which drops the log) — instead of the sticky TTL-grace governing floor. Fixes the fail-closed false-positive the 8e review pinned: a transient higher-version peer that subscribes once and leaves without writing lifts the floor but not the high-water, so a returning lower-version client whose every stored op it can reach is now admitted and served, not refused `UpdateRequired`. Keying on the true high-water is strictly more precise and preserves the invertible-edge fan-out guarantee (a joiner admitted at the high-water down-reaches every op the log holds). The cold-start snapshot seam is sourced from the same high-water (the version the merged state actually embodies) rather than the governing floor, so admission and the snapshot projection agree — closing a latent divergence when a phantom higher-version peer lifts the floor above the highest writer. The sticky `room_apps` binding still governs awareness TTL. Stranded-peer eviction and durable-across-restart high-water remain deferred (below). DECISIONS 8g.
      - **8h — durable governing metadata across restart (server + store) — DONE (#242).** Each room's governing metadata — its `{app_id, version}` binding and its op-version high-water — now persists to a per-room `<room>.meta` record beside the log/snapshot (`Store::write_meta` / total-decode `parse_meta`; atomic temp→rename→dir-sync, both extracted with `compact`/`write_versions` into shared `atomic_write`/`remove_if_present`), restored on load. `Hub` seeds `max_op_version` past what the replayed tail yields (the persisted all-time high-water dominates) and holds a durable `governing` mirror; `Registry::with_store` seeds `room_apps` from the persisted binding, `bind_room_app` / the `deliver` binding lookup fall back to the hub's durable binding, so a populated room a compacted restart or a dormant sweep left unbound is still bound + high-watered before the first subscribe — the range-check no longer under-refuses a below-gap joiner and a first subscriber is served **translated** catch-up, not verbatim. Metadata is a best-effort durability cache: an absent/malformed record (or a store-less hub) falls back to the standing rebuild-from-log / bind-on-subscribe, never panicking. Server/store-only diff (no `crates/core/src`). Closes the two 8g/8d-deferred durable-metadata items. DECISIONS 8h.
      - **8i — stranded-peer eviction on a version lift (server) — DONE (#243).** A write that raises a room's op-version high-water past a joined enforcing peer's back-compat reach now evicts that peer with `UpdateRequired` (was: a silent fan-out down-drop — fail-closed, no divergence, but the peer received no signal and silently stopped updating). After an ingest in `Registry::deliver` lifts `max_op_version` (pre/post delta over the acted-on room), each already-joined enforcing subscriber of the room's governing app is re-checked with the exact `subscriber_reaches_governing` predicate the subscribe gate admits on — so admission and eviction agree — and any now-unreachable peer is sent `Message::Error { UpdateRequired }` (one frame; `Error` names no channel) and dropped from the room via `Session::drop_room`, so it stops receiving fan-out and must re-subscribe after updating. Only a **breaking** gap the new high-water opens past the peer's version evicts; a back-compatible lift (the peer down-reaches it), the writer of its own lift (reaches the new high-water trivially), and a relay / foreign-app / versionless peer are untouched. A same-or-lower write moves nothing, so it re-checks nobody. Re-framed the trigger from the pre-8g "subscriber lift" to the post-8g "high-water-raising write" (a subscribe no longer changes reachability). Server-only diff (no `crates/core/src`; CI miri non-required). DECISIONS 8i.
      - _Deferred (wire/SDK, post-Unit-8):_ a **client-declared per-op creation version on the wire.** 8b tags each op with the writing connection's negotiated `schema_version`; that is exact for the common case but mislabels an op authored under an older version and resent from the offline queue after a client upgrade. The authoritative source is the client declaring each op's creation version on the wire (ARCHITECTURE "ingest validates an op against its own creation version"); `ingest` then reads it from the frame instead of the session. Storage/translation are unchanged — only the tag origin sharpens (DECISIONS 8b).
  - **Unit 9 — lifecycle hooks + producer validation (SDK surface)**: `onRepaired`/`onOpsRejected`/`onUpdateRequired` + producer-side reject-before-send over FFI + Python/Go/wasm. Sliced by hook:
      - **9a — server error code surface (`onUpdateRequired` observation) — DONE (#238).** `crdtsync_client_receive` gains an `out_error_code` out-param (breaking C ABI signature change, pre-release); on a server `Error` frame it writes the `ErrorCode` discriminant (`6` = `UpdateRequired`, the `onUpdateRequired` signal) and returns 0. Every SDK forwards it idiomatically: Python raises `ServerError(code: ErrorCode)`, Go returns `(int, ErrorCode)`, wasm throws the exported `ErrorCode`. `onRepaired` was already surfaced (`take_repairs`, #233/#235/#236/#234). FFI/SDK-only diff. See Done + DECISIONS.
      - **9b — `onOpsRejected` wire frame + server emit + client emit (core + server mechanism) — DONE (#239).** New server→client, sender-directed, non-fatal wire frame `Message::OpsRejected { channel, seqs: Vec<u64>, reason: ErrorCode }` (wire tag 22): the server names the refused ops by their per-client `OpId.seq` and the reason (`Forbidden`), the connection stays open. On the Ops-Write path `session::step` now replies with this non-fatal frame (via `ops_rejected`) instead of the connection-closing `forbidden("write denied")` when `authorized(Write)` denies — ops NOT ingested, NOT acked, NOT fanned out; every other `forbidden`/`violation` (subscribe/awareness/version) stays fatal. `ClientSession::receive(OpsRejected)` drains the named ops out of the channel's outbox (so `resend` never replays them) into a session-level rejected buffer; `take_rejected() -> Vec<Rejected>` drains it (mirrors `take_repairs`), each `Rejected { channel, reason, ops }` keeping the op bytes for the app to show/discard/export. Direction guards: client `receive` accepts it, server `step` rejects a client-sent one as a violation. Scoped to the authz-revoked trigger; the enforcing-schema-invalid trigger folds into 9c's producer-validation design. See DECISIONS (2026-07-08).
      - **9b-SDK — `onOpsRejected` SDK surface (FFI + Python/Go/wasm) — DONE (#240).** Forwards `ClientSession::take_rejected()` across the FFI boundary and each SDK, mirroring the `take_repairs` drain idiom + 9a's error-code surface — pure plumbing over the core mechanism 9b landed, no `crates/core/src` change. `crdtsync_client_take_rejected(client, out) -> i32` drains the session's rejected buffer into one buffer the SDK decodes: a `u32` count, then per batch the channel (`u32`), the reason discriminant (`i32`, reusing 9a's `error_code_discriminant`), and the ops (a `u32` op-count then per op a `u32`-length-prefixed `encode_op` byte string, so a refused op crosses carrying its bytes). `1` on success (empty → bare zero count), `-1` on a bad handle / null out; caller frees via `crdtsync_buf_free`; header regenerated. Each SDK decodes to an idiomatic list + its exported `ErrorCode`: Python `take_rejected() -> list[Rejected]` (a `Rejected` NamedTuple, both exported), Go `TakeRejected() []Rejected`, wasm `takeRejected()` → array of `{ channel, reason, ops }`. FFI/SDK-only diff (CI miri non-required). Harnesses in all four languages receive an `OpsRejected` frame and assert the drain yields the batch (channel + reason + ops), an empty drain is empty, a bad handle / null out is `-1`. See Done + DECISIONS (2026-07-09). **Unit 9 is now mechanically complete pending the 9c design gate — 9c is the sole remaining Unit-9 slice.**
      - **9c — producer-side reject-before-send — DONE (#302, see Done). Completes Unit 9.** The design question ("what does a producer edit reject *on*?") resolved to: the **enforcing server's op-ingress is the authoritative reject boundary** (a malicious/buggy client bypasses any client-side check), refusing a batch that introduces the one non-repairable violation — a **declared-slot runtime-kind mismatch** — with `OpsRejected` / `ErrorCode::SchemaViolation`; every repairable violation rides into the log for read-time repair, and an **undeclared map slot is admissible** (a Map is an open container, not a schema dimension). Folded the enforcing-schema-invalid `OpsRejected` trigger 9b left out. **Forced deviation logged in DECISIONS (2026-07-16)** — the entering design's other two rejectables (undeclared slot; sequence-`min` underflow) are not rejectable under the pinned schema model, narrowing the reject set to the single kind-mismatch case.
        - **9c-follow — client-local advisory pre-check (SDK) — OPTIONAL, not started.** An SDK-side mirror of the same validator run before send for fail-fast UX (reject locally, surface synchronously) — **never the trust boundary** (the server-side path in 9c is authoritative). Thin plumbing over the existing core `validate`; low priority, cut only if a concrete UX need surfaces.
        - **9c-follow — branch-scoped schema reject — DEFERRED (same gate as cross-zone move).** The 9c ingress gate is `MAIN_BRANCH`-only: enforcement resolves against the room's materialized document, which a branch's divergent tree is not part of, so a kind-mismatch write to a non-main branch (then frozen by `publish`) is currently un-refused — the identical deferral the cross-zone move check carries. Unblocks with the per-zone/branch stream work that models branch tree materialization.
  - **Unit 10 — typed SDK API / codegen — Python DONE (#306), Go DONE (#307), identifier-collision guarding DONE (#308).** A build-time generator (`crdtsync-codegen`, a workspace bin, dep-liberal) reads a registered `Schema` — validated through the **core parser** (the sole validator; codegen never re-implements schema semantics) — and emits a source file of typed accessor wrappers that forward to the SDK's existing path surface. One wrapper class/struct per declared map type (holding a `Document` + the path prefix it lives at); each slot a typed accessor with its path key + type baked in: register → int `get`/`set`, counter → `get`/`inc`/`dec`, text → `get`/`len`/`insert`/`delete`, list → `len`/`get`/`insert`/`delete`, nested map → an accessor returning the nested wrapper rooted at the extended path. So `note.get_title()` (Py) / `note.GetTitle()` (Go) replaces `doc.text_get([b"title"])` — a pure convenience layer over primitives core already implements (no new runtime behavior, nothing new to keep convergent). **Deterministic output** (declaration-order emission, no timestamp) so a regenerate is a no-op diff — the property a checked-in generated artifact needs. Each target pinned two ways: a Rust golden test (`crates/codegen/tests/{python,go}.rs`: determinism, golden match, header, per-slot forwarding, identifier sanitization) and a live round-trip test in the target SDK (`sdks/python/tests/test_codegen.py`; `sdks/go/notegen/note_generated_test.go`: import/compile + text/counter/list/nested-map round-trips against a real `Document`, set via generated setter → read via generated getter *and* the equivalent raw path). The Go emitter (`generate_go`) mirrors Python's per-language shape: exported PascalCase methods, `(value, ok)` getters forwarding the SDK's own return shape, a copying `joinPath` helper so sibling/nested accessors never alias a shared path slice, `[]byte("…")` byte-key literals, `--package` names the emitted package. Codegen only *reads* the schema — the `Schema` type + wire format are untouched. **TS-for-wasm target — JUSTIFIED SKIP (follow-on).** The wasm SDK is already statically typed by wasm-bindgen's generated `.d.ts`, and its methods take a single **pre-encoded opaque path buffer** (`encodePath(keys) -> Uint8Array`), not the append-a-key list façade Python/Go share — so there is no natural path façade to forward to (both of the task's named skip conditions hold), and the repo has no in-tree TS build/test harness to satisfy the compile+round-trip spec gate without standing up a new toolchain. Low value-add (naming sugar only) at high infra cost; revisit if a first-class npm/TS package lands. **Identifier-collision guarding — DONE (#308).** Sanitization is many-to-one (`a-b`/`a_b`/`a b` → `a_b`; Go also folds first-letter case, `fooBar`/`FooBar` → `FooBar`), and a map slot's bare-named accessor can even match another slot's *prefixed* accessor (`get_title`), so two slots in one map type could derive the same method → duplicate, uncompilable (or silently-shadowing) code. Both emitters now disambiguate identically at the fully-emitted method name via a shared `disambiguate(base, prefixes, used)`: fed the slot kind's verb set, it reserves every `prefix+base`; the first claimant keeps its base, each later collision appends the smallest free `_N`, driven by declaration order (deterministic + stable — a new colliding slot takes the next suffix without renaming earlier ones). The forwarded path key stays the original slot bytes, so a disambiguated accessor round-trips to its own distinct field. Colliding-schema fixture pinned per language (Rust golden + unique/forwarding asserts, incl. case-fold + bare-vs-prefixed cross-kind + retry-past-a-claimed-suffix, + a live SDK round-trip in `sdks/python` / `sdks/go/collidegen`); RED→green both languages. Type-*name* collisions (two map types) are a separate pre-existing axis, deferred. **Other follow-ons:** xml-slot typed accessors (currently skipped-with-comment); a bytes-valued register accessor (register is treated as an integer scalar — its bounds are numeric). See DECISIONS (2026-07-17, 2026-07-18).
  → *Schema / Invariant Repair / Schema Migration / Awareness / Authorization*. (v0.2 consumers land now; marks/attrs/XML/zones clauses gated on those primitives)
- **RelativePosition — SDK surface — DONE (#137).** `RelativePosition` capture/resolve exposed uniformly over one path-addressed surface across all four layers: `core::path::relative_position`/`resolve_position` (List + Text, order-preserving), the C ABI (`crdtsync_doc_relative_position` → encoded position bytes, `crdtsync_doc_resolve_position` bytes → index, `side` as 0=left/1=right), and Python (`Document.relative_position`/`resolve_position` + a `Side` IntEnum), Go (`Document.RelativePosition`/`ResolvePosition` + a `Side` type), and wasm (`relativePosition`/`resolvePosition`). Encode/decode ride implicitly on the capture-returns-bytes / resolve-takes-bytes shape (no separate encode/decode entry points needed — the position only ever crosses as opaque bytes). Each layer has a capture→resolve-round-trip + tracks-an-insert + non-sequence/malformed-is-absent test. → *Anchors*. (v0.5 foundation for editor bindings)
- **Op acknowledgement — the gate that unlocked the offline queue + tombstone compression** (§Op Acknowledgement, opened 2026-07-02, DECISIONS). One ack concept, two directions, sliced dependency-order. The server→client `Accepted` frame drives the offline queue; the client→server `Ack` frame is reserved with no consumer (its GC-watermark purpose was dropped when tombstone GC became compression):
  - **Unit 1 — wire frames — DONE (#121)**: `Message::Accepted`/`Ack` (tags 18/19) + codec + `protocol_ack.rs`; exhaustive-match placeholder arms in client `receive` / server `step` that Units 2–3 replace.
  - **Unit 2 — client outbox / offline queue — DONE (#122)**: `ClientSession` retains authored ops per channel; `edit`/`atomic_edit`/`commit_atomic` enqueue; `receive(Accepted{through})` prunes `id.seq <= through`; `resend(channel)` re-emits the unpruned tail; `outbox_len(channel)` exposes the queue depth. `client_outbox.rs`. Delivers the **offline-queue** half at the client seat (the server ack that drives it is Unit 3).
  - **Unit 3 — server ack — DONE (#123)**: session `step` replies `Accepted{channel, through=max OpId.seq of the submitted batch}` to the author after a durable `Hub::ingest` (computed over the whole batch, not just fresh ops, so a resent-but-known op is still acked and pruned; an empty batch acks nothing). Sender-directed reply, distinct from fan-out. **Offline queue now works end-to-end** (client outbox ↔ server ack), proven by a client↔`step` drain test. Scope note: `Ack{seq}`-recording-into-the-watermark was folded into Unit 4 (its consumer + the Clock/horizon live there), so `step` still accepts-and-ignores `Ack` for now.
  - **Unit 4 — tombstone GC → REPLACED by tombstone *compression* — DONE (#125).** Building removal-based GC surfaced that it's a false target: a positional node can be safely removed only when no replica still references it, but clients never compact, so a live client holds every tombstone forever and can always anchor an insert to one → removal forces either a replicated GC-op + forwarding (reparent) or edit-rejection on cold regions (data loss). Both were rejected. **The human directed a step back: the goal is "tombstones don't bloat," not "tombstones get removed."** New approach (DECISIONS, 2026-07-02): never remove — *compress*. The state codec run-length-encodes contiguous deleted runs (consecutive char/list ids on one insert's parent-chain) into a single range record and drops dead values; `STATE_VERSION` → 2. Pure encoding optimization, convergence-trivial (identical logical state), no watermark/ack/horizon/orphan-handling. The `Ack` frame is now a reserved no-consumer wire slot. The in-memory range representation (bounding RAM, not just encoded bytes) followed in #341.
  - **Unit 5 — SDK surface — DONE (#124)**: `resend` + `outbox_len` over FFI (`crdtsync_client_resend`/`_outbox_len`) + Python/Go/wasm, each with an offline-queue drain test. **Also fixed a bug this surfaced:** the FFI/wasm façade edit path (`document_mut` + `path::*`) built the Ops frame directly and bypassed the outbox, so SDK edits never enqueued — now routed through a new `ClientSession::enqueue_ops` (the three closure methods share it too), so a façade edit is acknowledged/resent exactly like a closure `edit`.
  → *Op Acknowledgement / Tombstone compression*. (v0.2 — offline queue + tombstone compression DONE; `Ack` reserved, no consumer)
- **Atomic transactions — DONE (#102–#106)** — core (`Tx{id,count}` envelope, buffer-until-whole, seq-order commit), drive path (`ClientSession::atomic_edit`; server unchanged), the SDK surface at both the doc seat (`begin_atomic`/`commit_atomic` over FFI + Python/Go/wasm) and the client seat (per-channel begin/commit), and atomic-tx undo (`UndoManager::atomic_group`, #106). All-or-nothing view boundary, convergence preserved, verified end to end. Remaining scope constraints (one branch/zone/schema version, member cap) need the branch/zone/schema layers. → *Transactions*. (v0.2)
- **Authorization — remaining policy layers** — atop the seam (#82), read redaction (#83), and the ACL decision flow (#84): doc-level ACL as CRDT-merged state (tuples live in the document, merge, and feed the #84 evaluator — DONE: ACL-CRDT #202-arc, per-recipient ACL-tuple redaction #285), role/group subjects (need a claims model threaded from the verifier to the enforcement point), and finer-grain wire redaction (**DONE — per-recipient doc-ACL wire redaction complete across every op/state axis: ops #4b, snapshot #284, ACL tuples #285, RangedElements #287; zones #278; room-level per-send landed in #83**). Schema-level `@auth` defaults and zones are gated on the unbuilt schema + zone layers. Large — slice per layer. → *Authorization*. (v0.2, needs design)
- **Audit log — query surface + distinct read-only actions** — the access-decision half landed in #85 (`Audited` emits every authz decision to a pluggable sink). Remaining: an audit *query* surface (admin/CLI over the trail — pairs with the file-log-vs-DB revisit note) and distinct audit actions for accesses that today reach no `authorize` call — `Connect`, snapshot export, branch/version reads — each gated on that action/resource existing (`Action::Connect`, the branch/version layers). → *Authorization / Audit*. (v0.2, partly blocked)
- **Named versions — DONE (#86–#92)** — index → durable persistence → wire → server handling → client view → FFI → Python/Go/wasm, all merged. Remaining versioning work is dependency-gated: **restore-as-branch** (needs the branch layer) and **auto-version triggers** (need engine-event hooks / schedules) — both tracked under *Auto-version triggers* in the queue.
- **mTLS credential carrier — DONE (#305)** — a verified client certificate authenticates AS the ACL actor. `CRDTSYNC_TLS_CLIENT_CA` gates it on; the #300 `with_no_client_auth()` slot becomes a `WebPkiClientVerifier` (fail-closed handshake); the leaf cert's SAN→CN is bound as the connection's actor through the existing `connect_authenticated`/`Identity` path. See the Done frontier. → *Networking / Handshake*. (v0.2)

---

## 📋 Queue — current cut of ARCHITECTURE, prioritized

Not exhaustive — the full backlog **is** ARCHITECTURE. This is the prioritized slice `cs-next` has broken out so far; when it drains, `cs-next` cuts the next slice from ARCHITECTURE. Order = dependency → foundational/forward-compat → roadmap/value. Each item cites the ARCHITECTURE section it derives from.

- **Element-ref value slot** — **DONE (#151).** `Scalar::ElementRef(ElementId)` reserved as scalar tag 5 in the op/state codec — a bare same-room element id, a plain LWW leaf (no substructure, no merge), mirroring `Scalar::BlobRef` (#60); round-tripped + total-decode, no producer/consumer yet, `kind` hint deferred. wasm surfaces it as `{t:"elementref", v:<16-byte id>}`. → *Internal Data Model*.
- **Channel multiplexing — remaining** — logical channels currently key on `room`; widen to `(room, branch, zone)` once branches/zones exist. The `Channel` handle already abstracts this (no wire change needed then). → *Networking / Connection*. (v0.2, blocked on branches/zones)
- **Awareness timed-TTL + throttle** — per-entry auto-expire-after-silence (timed TTL, distinct from the session TTL the grace sweep already handles) + removal broadcast (reuse `AwarenessClear`), and server-side throttle/coalesce of high-frequency entries (cursor/mouse). **Now sliced as Schema Unit 3** (Next) — the schema design settled 2026-07-03 gives the trigger values a home (`awareness.<kind>.ttl`/`throttle`); the clock seam (#71) + periodic sweep (#72) are ready to enforce them. → *Awareness / Schema*. (v0.2, unblocked)
- **Schema-aware diff — blocked detail only** — the diff runs end to end (core #109/#110/#111 → codec #112 → C ABI #113 → wasm #115 / Python #116 / Go #117 → default renderer #119). Remaining is **XmlElement / marks / attrs diffs**, blocked on those unbuilt primitives; the SDK-side renderer wrappers (expose `render` over the FFI/SDKs) are an optional convenience. → *Versioning / Schema-Aware Diff*. (v0.2, blocked on primitives)
- **Snapshot clone (live template)** — **DESIGN RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-04, DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Export/Import).** The feared id-namespacing is unnecessary — room-scoping already prevents every collision (per-room server-seq renumber #107; per-client-global `client_seq` never reused; separate per-room replicas keep shared `element_id`s distinct; clock-bump rides the snapshot-adoption high-water #126). Build: a thin `Hub::clone_room(src, dst)` = `export_room(src)` + `import_room(dst, …)` under a fresh room id, with a create-only + persist-before-commit contract like import; a clone/move test proving origin + clone diverge independently. Prefix-namespacing deferred until cross-room refs/merge exist (they don't). → *Snapshots / Export-Import*. (v0.2, design-ready)
- **Tombstone compression** — DONE (#125 encoding, #341 in-memory); see the Op-ack Unit 4 entry above and the in-memory range entry in Done. Resolved the "GC" question by *not removing* tombstones (removal forces divergence-or-loss since clients never compact) and instead run-length-compressing deleted runs — in the state codec (`STATE_VERSION` 2) and, since #341, in the live `List` too, so RAM tracks runs as well. Time/migration compaction triggers + retention window ("keep last N") are separate, still queued. → *Snapshots / Tombstone GC*. (v0.2)
- **Declarative policy + audit log** — authorization enforcement. → *Authorization*. (v0.2)
- **UndoManager — DONE (#94–#101)** — core undo across every value type (scalar/counter/list/text, root or nested), grouping, the FFI `CrdtUndo` handle, and Python/Go/wasm wrappers all merged. The whole subsystem is complete end-to-end. Remaining refinements are optional (bytes-in-Register has no reader to surface; a fluent group builder in the SDKs).
- **Auto-version triggers** — **DESIGN RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-04, DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Auto-Version Triggers).** A general engine event bus (`EngineEvent` + pluggable `EventSink`, generalizing the audit `AccessLog` sink #85); auto-version is the first built-in sink. Schema-declared `autoVersion` triggers (`{ on: <event> | every: <duration>, name: <template>, keep: <N> }`). Sliced:
  - **Unit 1 — engine event bus (server)**: `EngineEvent` enum (available events: version created/deleted, connect, subscribe, snapshot/compaction; branch/migration events reserved) + pluggable `EventSink`; emit at the lifecycle points.
  - **Unit 2 — schema `autoVersion` parse (core)**: parse the block into triggers (event/schedule + name template + `keep`); closure-validate.
  - **Unit 3 — AutoVersion sink (server)**: on a matching event, `create_version` (#86) with the templated name; `keep: N` prunes the oldest auto-versions of that trigger (retention window).
  - **Unit 4 — schedule triggers (server)**: `every: <duration>` via the `Clock` seam + periodic sweep (#71/#72).
  Branch/migration events (`before-publish`/`after-restore`/`before-migration`) fire once those layers land (declarable + parked meanwhile, never error). Also delivers the queued retention-window item. → *Versioning / Debugging (event bus = webhook substrate)*. (v0.2, design-ready)
- **Admin dashboard** — the operator UI (rooms, connected users, ops/sec, snapshot size, replication lag, cluster health, op-log viewer). **Deferred/last** (per the roadmap: UI comes after the non-UI operator tools). **Replay tooling landed** as the `crdtsync-replay` CLI (see ✅ Done / Tooling). → *Admin UI*, *Debugging*. (v0.2) _(composition cookbook landed #93)_
- **Binary Blobs** — refs travel in ops, bytes live in a separate content-addressable store (§Binary Blobs). Sliced:
  - **Slice 1 — local-FS blob store (server)** — **DONE (#259).** `crates/server/src/blobs.rs`: `BlobStore::put(bytes, mime) -> BlobRef` mints a random public UUID handle, inlines blobs ≤ 4096 B in the ref (no FS touch), content-addresses larger ones by sha256 under `objects/<sha>` (identical bytes dedupe to one object) with a durable `refs/<uuid>` → sha256 map; `get(id)` resolves the handle to stored bytes, `None` on unknown. Durable across reopen (index rebuilt from `refs/`), atomic tmp+rename+dir-fsync writes. The bytes-at-rest layer only.
  - **Slice 2a — inline ref producer (core)** — **DONE (#262).** `path::set_blob(doc, path, host, mime, bytes) -> Option<Vec<Op>>` mints a fresh unguessable 16-byte handle from injected `Host` entropy and sets a `Scalar::BlobRef { inline: Some(bytes), .. }` (#60 slot); bytes `> INLINE_MAX` (4096) belong to the store-backed path and are rejected with `None`, never silently inlined. `get_blob(doc, path) -> Option<BlobRef>` reads a ref back. Pure core; `tests/blob_path.rs`, Miri-clean.
  - **Slice 3 — ref-only ref producer (core)** — **DONE (#264).** `path::set_blob_ref(doc, path, id: [u8;16], mime, size) -> Vec<Op>` sets a `Scalar::BlobRef { inline: None, .. }` from a caller-supplied handle — the one `POST /blobs` (#263) returned after an out-of-band upload. No entropy (the id is a given, not minted) and no size guard (a ref names bytes it never carries, so it returns ops unconditionally). `get_blob` reads both producers unchanged. Pure core; extends `tests/blob_path.rs`, Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-13).
  - **Slice 2b — HTTP upload/fetch route (server)** — **DONE (#263).** `crates/server/src/admin.rs`: `POST /blobs` stores a body → `200 {id,size,inline}` handle (hex id); `GET /blobs/{id}` serves the stored bytes as `application/octet-stream` (store persists no mime), `404` unknown, `400` malformed id (no panic); body cap `MAX_BLOB_BODY` (8 MiB) → `413`. Both authenticate the `Authorization` credential through the same `Verifier` seam as schema registration (missing/unknown → `401`); per-reference ACL deferred. Served on `CRDTSYNC_BLOB_ADDR`, store root `CRDTSYNC_BLOB_ROOT` or `<CRDTSYNC_DATA_DIR>/blobs`. `BlobStore::put_fetchable` persists every blob (small ones too) so an uploaded handle is always fetchable, unlike `put`'s inline-only path. Server-only, no `crates/core/src` change; socket + fs tests miri-ignored.
  - **Slice 4 — SDK blob API** — **DONE (#265).** The blob CRDT surface across FFI/Python/Go/wasm: the two producers `set_blob` (inline, mints the handle from boundary entropy) / `set_blob_ref` (store-backed) and the reader `get_blob`, on both the doc surface and the client edit surface (routed through the outbox; reads stay doc-surface), mirroring the existing path-fn bindings and the xml doc-vs-client split. `get_blob` frames the `BlobRef` as id[16] · u32-len mime · u64 size · present-flag · optional u32-len inline; marshalled idiomatically per layer (Python `BlobRef` NamedTuple, Go `[16]byte`-id struct, wasm `{id,mime,size,inline}` object). getrandom injected in ffi/wasm mints the inline handle; core stays getrandom-free. SDK/FFI-only, no `crates/core/src` change. FFI blob suites Miri-clean. See DECISIONS (2026-07-13). → *Binary Blobs*.
  - **Slice — byte-upload HTTP helper (app-level / thin later slice)** — **DONE (#303).** `upload_blob`/`UploadBlob`: POSTs raw bytes to `POST /blobs` (#263) and hex-decodes the returned `{id,size,inline}` handle to the 16-byte id, ready for `set_blob_ref`. Python (`upload_blob(base_url, data, credential, mime="application/octet-stream") -> bytes`, stdlib `urllib`) + Go (`UploadBlob(baseURL, credential, mime string, data []byte) ([16]byte, error)`, stdlib `net/http`) — the SDKs carry no HTTP layer (transport is app-owned; the wire `Client` only produces/consumes frames), so this is a standalone helper taking the blob-plane origin + `Authorization` credential (the same one `Client.auth` sends). Upload stays auth-only, whatever `POST /blobs` enforces; no core change. Deterministic mock-server tests: request construction, response parse, `set_blob_ref`/`get_blob` round-trip, error surfacing. **Follow-on — wasm helper — DONE (#304).** `uploadBlob(baseUrl, data, credential, mime?) -> Uint8Array` (async), the browser `fetch` glue the Rust→wasm crate needs: builds the `POST {baseUrl}/blobs` request (`Authorization` credential + `Content-Type` mime, raw bytes body) via `web-sys` `Request`/`RequestInit`/`Headers`, awaits the global `fetch` (resolved off `globalThis` so it works in browser/worker/Node), and hex-decodes the returned `{id}` handle to the 16-byte id — matching the Python/Go shape. Pure parse/decode core (`parse_blob_handle`/`hex_decode_id`) split out and unit-tested under `wasm-pack test --node` (hex→16 bytes, uppercase, length/non-hex guards, JSON parse + missing/short id); the live `fetch` round-trip is a browser-integration gap not exercised headlessly (no server in the node harness). → *Binary Blobs*.
  - **Slice 5 — ACL-per-reference-site (blob-fetch authorization)** — **DONE (#301).** Closes the authenticated-but-not-authorized gap: `GET /blobs/{id}` is allowed iff the caller holds READ on ≥1 live `core::path` that references the id, resolved through the *same* `acl::recipient_reads_path` evaluator op redaction uses — no parallel lattice, `Resource` = the referencing path(s), `Action` = read. `index::blob_ref_paths(doc)` derives the `id → referencing paths` index off the live tree (walked like `element_paths`; map-slot ref at its leaf path, node-addressed ref at its container's subtree); `Registry::authorize_blob_fetch(identity, id)` scans every room (a handle is room-independent). The out-of-band blob plane owns no replicas — it round-trips the decision to the registry actor over `runtime::RegistryHandle` (a `BlobAccess` impl), wired via `serve_with_authorizer_handle`. **Fail-closed**: an unreferenced/deleted id, or one referenced only from a denied/redacted position, is `403` — even for an authenticated caller, even for the creator who owns `/` (the drag-to-exfil analogue for blobs, consistent with element-id redaction). Upload stays auth-only. `tests/blob_acl.rs` (decision, Miri-clean) + `tests/blob_http.rs` (route 403 wiring). See DECISIONS (2026-07-16). → *Binary Blobs*.
  - **Deferred to v0.5**: S3 / object-store backend, dedup ref-counting + GC, range requests. → *Binary Blobs*.
- **XmlElement / XmlFragment / RangedElement / marks** — **DESIGN RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-03, DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §CRDT Model / §Marks).** The last big primitive; unblocks rich text, doc trees, attrs, the deferred invariant-repair rules (orphan-wrap, exclusive-collision-demote), and XmlElement/marks/attrs diff detail. Reuses built machinery — Map (attrs), Fugue (children), RelativePosition (anchors; gravity = anchor-expansion). Sliced:
  - **Unit 1 — XmlElement + XmlFragment structure (core)**: `Element::XmlElement { tag, attrs: Map, children: Fugue<XmlElement|Text> }` + `XmlFragment` (tagless children sequence); create/edit/delete children, element_id derivation, codec, convergence harness. **No tree-move.**
  - **Unit 2 — tree move (core)**: Kleppmann-2021 move op (lamport-ordered apply, undo-and-replay on out-of-order, bounded undo log; one-parent / no-cycle / no-dup / deterministic). The hardest slice, isolated.
  - **Unit 3 — RangedElement primitive (core) — DONE (3a #204, 3b #205)**: `RangedElement { start, end, payload }`, endpoints `(element_id, RelativePosition)`, in a document/fragment-level annotation set (CRDT set keyed by id); generic (ranges may span elements). Codec + convergence. **3a — annotation-set spine — DONE (#204)** (scalar payload); **3b — composite (Element) payload — DONE (#205)** (payload generalized to a nested Map/List/Text via a virtual-container reachability model; see Done). Unit complete.
  - **Unit 4 — marks (core) — DONE (4a #206, 4b #207)**: convention over RangedElement — schema `marks` merge flavors + anchor-expansion via RelativePosition gravity; per-character active-mark state as a read-time computation over the annotation set, no new op. **4a — schema `marks` parse — DONE (#206)** (MarkDef flavor + expand). **4b — the mark read model — DONE (#207)** (a named RangedElement; `Document::marks_at` combines per flavor; presence-aware anchor resolution; STATE_VERSION 7 — see Done). Unit complete; the read-model half of the epic is closed.
  - **Unit 5 — retires the deferred bits — DONE (5a #208 · 5b #209/#210 · 5c #211/#212/#213/#214 · 5d #215/#216).** Every enforcement rule (§Schema repair table: disallowed/mistyped attr → drop, mark on disallowed type → drop, disallowed child → drop, orphan inline → wrap default block, exclusive collision → keep-lamport-oldest) needs the **XmlElement schema type** — which the schema layer (Units 1–8) never parsed (types cover map/list/text/register/counter only). So parse-first, mirroring 4a. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-06):
    - **5a — schema XmlElement/XmlFragment type parse (core) — DONE (#208)**: `TypeDef::Xml { tag, children, attrs, marks, orphan_inline }`, `kind: "xml"`/`"fragment"`, reference closure over child/attr/orphan types + `marks` (new `UnknownMarkRef`). Parse-only, enforcement-inert. See Done.
    - **5b — attrs + marks enforcement (core)**: read-time repair over the validate/repair engine (Unit 2 #170/#172/#174), consuming the 5a `TypeDef::Xml` allowlists. Integration map (2026-07-06): the validator already resolves element→type contextually as it walks (carries `type_name` per `Work` item), so an xml arm gets the type for free; a child element's type resolves by matching its `tag()` against the tags of the parent type's `children` allowlist (contextual — disambiguates a tag reused across types). `parents: HashMap<ElementId, ElementId>` (doc.rs) walks a seq→its children-list→its XmlElement; there is **no** element-id→type registry, so the type binding must come from the walk. Two asymmetric sub-slices:
      - **5b-i — attrs enforcement + xml-subtree recursion (core) — DONE (#209)**: `(TypeDef::Xml, XmlElement)`/`(_, XmlFragment)` validator arms descend attrs (→ `DisallowedAttr`/`MistypedAttr` → `RepairKind::Dropped`; right-kind value recurses → `Clamped`) + children (tag-matched, recursed). `element_at` walks xml; violations emit in sorted tree order via `Work::Report`. See Done.
      - **5b-ii — mark-on-disallowed-type filter (core) — DONE (#210)**: `marks_at` drops a covering mark whose name is outside the containing XmlElement type's `marks` allowlist. See Done.

Reuses 5b-i's attr-drop path whole — `Work::Report` tree-order emit, `RepairId::Drop`, existing `element_at` xml `Step::Index` traversal; no new `RepairKind`, no new state. Shared by element + fragment children. `tests/xml_schema.rs` +5. See Done.

The wrapper `id = derive(orphan.id(), block, XmlElement)` is position-stable (the orphan's own derived id), so `RepairId::Wrap { id }` keys onRepaired by id alone — immune to the `Step::Index` churn `Drop`/`Clamp`/`Keep` carry. `tests/xml_schema.rs` +3. See Done.

Requires the 5a `children` allowlist (a plain `Vec<String>` array) to become an **object** keyed by type name with an optional per-type `max`. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-07):

New `parse_xml_children` (per-child `reject_unknown_fields` allowing only `max`, negative → `BadRange`). `resolve_child_type`/`queue_xml_children`/`validate_references` iterate `(name, _)`; `max` stored, unread (5c-iii-b). All xml test schemas converted array→object. Broke the schema format (pre-release). See Done.
        - **5c-iii-b — per-type cardinality drop repair (core) — DONE (#214)**: for each child type `T` with a declared `max`, `queue_xml_children` counts the element's children resolving to `T`; the lamport-newest excess reads dropped via new `ViolationKind::ExcessChild { max }` → `RepairKind::Dropped` (keep-oldest-by-stamp, same as the sequence-`max` `survivors`; `max` 0 drops all). Reuses the drop path whole; excess child not descended; only resolving children participate; stamp walk lazy (only when a cap is declared). `tests/xml_schema.rs` +8. Completes 5c-iii + the 5c arc. See Done.

Fixes the latent `unreachable!` panic (xml fell through to `scalar_of`). No new `Change` variant, no path-model change. Sequence-composite survivor recursion (+ index-stepped path) DEFERRED — unreachable via the current edit API (no in-place xml-child cursor; plain lists hold immutable scalars), so no drivable state to diff or test. See Done + DECISIONS.
        - **5d-ii — marks value diff (core) — DONE (#216)**: three path-less `Change` variants (`MarkAdded`/`MarkRemoved`/`MarkChanged`, each `{ id, seq, name, value|old/new }`) + `diff_marks` — a merge-join over the two id-sorted `ranged_elements()` slices (new-not-old = added, old-not-new = removed w/ old value, in-both value-differs = changed), folded into `diff()` after the tree walk, deterministic by `id.as_bytes()`. Scalar-payload marks only (composite-payload marks unconstructible via the `mark()` API — deferred); codec tags 8/9/10 round-trip + total on truncation; render `± mark <name>` lines; wasm `change_to_js` mark arms. See Done + DECISIONS. **Completes 5d + Unit 5.**

Core is the sole engine; the SDKs forward bytes over the path-addressed `core::path` façade (as every other primitive does). The façade is xml-blind today (`resolve_map`/`descend` follow nested **Maps** by key only — no xml attrs descent, no children, no marks), so the foundation is teaching `core::path` to navigate xml + expose marks/diff, *then* the four bindings forward it. Sliced (cs-next 2026-07-07) core façade (by capability) → C ABI → Python/Go/wasm:

New `xml_element`/`xml_fragment` create fns + `xml_tag` read. A fragment has no attrs → a `writable` read-only pre-check zero-ops a dead-end write at any depth (the emptiness-as-effect contract). `tests/path_xml.rs` (16); targeted Miri clean; local review quiet (caught + fixed a nested-fragment emptiness regression). See Done + DECISIONS.

In-place navigation *into* an existing composite child stays deferred (no core cursor — 5d-i): the façade addresses the children list, not a grandchild. See Done + DECISIONS.

Inert (empty ops, None id) on a non-sequence path or an unresolvable anchor; the mark id crosses as `ElementId` bytes. Cross-element spans deferred (4b-ii). See Done + DECISIONS.

Engine unchanged. See Done + DECISIONS.

All slices touch the same ffi lib + header head-on → **serial, not parallel** (one 6b sub-slice in flight at a time). 6b-i established the doc-surface (ops-returning) + client-surface (enqueue/ack) split every edit slice follows. Header regenerated + symbol-assert extended. See Done + DECISIONS.

Doc-surface only. Settle-point contract preserved through the FFI. **Completes Unit 6b — the entire C ABI.** See Done + DECISIONS.

Doc-vs-client split mirrors the C ABI (edits on both Document+Client, reads doc-only); each added the diff decode side + fixed its change decoder's mark-variant gap (decoders predating the 5d mark tags 8/9/10 errored on mark-bearing diffs). **Completes Unit 6 — the whole XmlElement SDK surface across all four SDKs.** See Done + DECISIONS.
