- **Zones (coarse auth partition)** — **DESIGN RESOLVED (human, 2026-07-04, DECISIONS + ARCHITECTURE §Zones).** Static, path-rooted, schema-declared subtree partitions, each a separately replicated stream (own lamport, own op-log partition) — stronger isolation than doc-ACL redaction (unauthorized zone withholds ops/snapshot/structure/existence/size). Sliced:
  - **Unit 1 — zone declaration (core)**:
    - **1a — parse the `zones` block — DONE (#218)**: `zones` is a top-level key; each entry is a zone name → its subtree root path (a bare absolute-path string, same grammar the auth `on:` field uses). Parse-only, enforcement-inert — order-preserving `zones()`/`zone(name)` accessors, syntactic path validation, duplicate names caught by the JSON layer. Mirrors the marks-parse (4a) / xml-type-parse (5a) slices.
    - **1b — cross-zone move/anchor rejection (core)**: reject cross-zone tree moves + cross-zone anchors at schema validation. Sub-sliced (cs-next 2026-07-08) resolver → enforcement, then enforcement split anchor (static) vs move (op-seam):
      - **1b-i — zone-membership resolution + cross-zone detection (core) — DONE (#222)**: pure `zone::zone_of(schema, path)` longest-prefix resolver + `same_zone`/`crosses_zones` over the parsed `zones` block; innermost-wins nesting, unzoned distinct from every zone, `/` = doc root. Detection only, no enforcement. See Done + DECISIONS.
      - **1b-ii-a — cross-zone anchor rejection (core) — DONE (#237)**: a `RangedElement` whose two endpoints straddle zones is flagged `ViolationKind::CrossZoneAnchor` by the read-time `validate()` pass and repaired as `Dropped` (reads absent) — a pure additive static check over `doc.ranged_elements()`, no op-reject, mirrors `DisallowedChild`→`Dropped`. No new seam. See Done + DECISIONS.
      - **1b-ii-b — cross-zone move rejection (server) — DONE (#PR).** Resolved the op-apply-seam gate: refused at the **op-submit gate** (`session.rs`, `OpsRejected{Forbidden}` — the author keeps its ops, the op never enters the log, replicas converge on its absence), detected by **simulating** the batch on an independent copy of the room document and flagging any `XmlMove` whose mover's zone differs before vs after (a move absent on one side — created/deleted in-batch — is not a crossing). Built on the new **server element-context index** (below): id→path/zone resolution over the room's already-materialized document projection (`element_paths` + `zone::zone_of`), *not* a hand-maintained parallel structure (which would drift from the doc's buffering/refold/LWW). Main-branch-gated (branch/zone interaction is Unit 2/3). See Done + DECISIONS (2026-07-14).
  - **Unit 2 — per-zone lamport + envelope `zone` (core) — DONE (#276)**: `Op` gains `zone: Option<u32>` — a **compact zone id** (the target's index in the order-preserving `Schema::zones()`, `None` = root/unzoned). **The board's "already reserved in the op envelope" was wrong** — the field did not exist on `Op`; this unit added it (pre-release wire/state break, no migration). The Document's lamport allocation is partitioned: root rides `lamport`, declared zones ride `zone_clocks`; a local emit stamps from the target's zone clock, a merge advances the *op's envelope zone* by per-partition `max` (honored from the envelope, never re-derived). Causally-independent partitions — an op in one zone never bumps another's, within-zone causality intact, no-schema/no-zones is the old single global clock exactly. State codec persists the per-zone clocks (STATE_VERSION 8→9). See Done + DECISIONS (2026-07-14).
  - **Unit 3 — zone-scoped subscription + authz + wire redaction (server) — DONE (#278)**: `Message::Subscribe` gains a `zone` selector (empty = whole room, a name = one partition); `Resource::Zone` subscribe-gate with a generic denial that concealed existence (nonexistent ≡ unauthorized), plus a shipped-`Acl` `ResourceMatch::Zone`. Per-zone streams = one channel per room + a per-recipient `op.zone` filter reusing the doc-ACL/awareness redaction seam across fan-out, catch-up delta, and cold-start snapshot (`Document::project_zones`), so an unauthorized zone is wholly absent (ops/state/structure/count/existence/clock). Refined Unit 2 so a container-create belongs to the created child's zone (closing a stream↔snapshot divergence + strengthening isolation). First-cut limitations (doc-level ACL/Ranged ops on the op stream; reachable-only snapshot projection; nested inner-authorized/outer-denied) documented as follow-on hardening. See Done + DECISIONS (2026-07-14).
  - **Unit 4 — cross-zone move via an AEAD capability token — DONE (#PR)**: un-deferred and shipped as a **cross-zone MOVE** capability token — a server-sealed (ChaCha20-Poly1305, zone-master key) token binding `(room, actor, element, src_zone, dst_zone, expiry)` that authorizes exactly one otherwise-rejected cross-zone tree move; issued only to an actor with move-authority-on-element AND dst-zone-write (existing ACL evaluator), redeemed at op-ingress against the actual crossing, fail-closed on every mismatch/forgery/expiry. See Done + DECISIONS (2026-07-18). _(The cross-zone **anchor** opaque-reference — per-recipient sealed handle at fan-out, ARCHITECTURE §Zones "Cross-zone references" — remains a separate deferred item, unrelated to this move token.)_
  - **Unit 5 — SDK surface — NEXT**: zone-scoped subscribe over the SDKs (FFI + Python/Go/wasm) — forward the `Subscribe` `zone` selector through the client-session subscribe surface, the wire carrier and server enforcement already in place (Unit 3).
  Depends on the tree primitives (meaningful subtrees) + the authz seam (design done). Unblocks channel-mux widening `(room,branch,zone)` and awareness zone-scoping. → *Authorization/zones / Networking*. (v0.5, design-ready)
- **Mixed-version migrations** — migration log entries, per-op `schema_version`, four detection gates. → *Schema Migration*. (v0.6)
- **Distributed cluster** — sharding, replication, failover, leader election, branches. → *Horizontal Scaling*, *Versioning and Branches*. (v0.4)

---

## 🔍 Revisit / tech-debt (accepted decisions flagged for a later look)

- **23 test files each define their own `TempDir` + `tempdir()` (crates/server/tests).** Same newtype, same `Drop`, same `pid`+`AtomicU32` naming, differing only in the directory prefix; there is no `tests/common/` module. Each integration test is its own crate, so `tests/common/mod.rs` + `mod common;` is the idiomatic share. Raised by cubic during C30 (#392) against the 22nd and 23rd copies; declined there because a 23-file refactor inside a zone-scope PR would rewrite twenty-one files it does not otherwise touch, and converting only some callers would leave two competing patterns — which is the drift the observation is about. Worth doing as its own change, all callers at once.

- **File-log vs. embedded DB for the query/metadata side** — the append-only file log is right for the op hot-path, but admin UI / op-log viewer / retention want queryability, and durability is hand-rolled (a dir-fsync crash bug already shipped + fixed). Reconsider SQLite/redb for the metadata/index side when those consumers land. Checkpoint, not a reversal. **audit-query landed (2026-07-20) on the file log with a straight scan** — a rebuildable in-memory index (or a DB) over actor/room/time is the scale follow-on once the trail is large enough for the scan to hurt.
- **Audit-trail rotation / retention** — the `AuditLog` append-only file grows unbounded, bounded only by the security-relevant-event filter (denials + writes + connect/export/version-read; routine reads excluded). A size- or time-based rotation, or an operator-triggered prune of records past a retention window, is the noted follow-on before a long-lived deployment's trail outgrows a single file.
- **Audit-trail tamper-evidence** — the append log is not tamper-evident: a corrupted/altered *length* prefix that overruns the file is indistinguishable from a crash-torn tail (both drop the remainder), so an actor who can flip on-disk bytes can silently truncate the trail without tripping the corrupt-body `500`. A hash-chained record (each record carries a MAC over `prev_hash || body`) is the follow-on to make truncation/edits detectable at read.
- **Cold-start snapshot CPU** — a below-floor subscriber triggers a whole-replica `encode_state` regenerated live per cold-start (O(state) CPU/connection). Fine now; cache the encoded snapshot per compaction floor if snapshots grow or cold-starts get frequent.
- **CI concurrency group — DONE (2026-08-06, direct to main).** `rust.yml` now groups a ref's runs under `rust-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress` restricted to `pull_request`, so a force-push supersedes its predecessor while a push to main is never cancelled. Landed mid-batch after the deferral below became the binding constraint rather than a courtesy: with four units rebasing through sibling merges, the pool reached 10 active `Rust` runs and 7 Copilot runs, one matrix sat `queued` with `updated_at == created_at` for over an hour holding 17 job slots, `gh run cancel` answered `HTTP 502` on it, and a newer head then went unscheduled entirely — so coverage could not converge for any unit. Original filing follows.
- **CI has no `concurrency` group, so a force-push leaves its superseded run burning runners** — `.github/workflows/rust.yml` declares `push: [main]` + `pull_request` and nothing else, so every force-push during a review cycle starts a second full matrix (17 checks, 11 of them Miri shards) while the first keeps running to completion against a sha nobody will merge. A unit that rebases three or four times through sibling merges — the ordinary shape now that units run concurrently — pays that several times over, and the runners it holds are the same pool the ≤30min Miri gate depends on, so the waste shows up as queue time on every *other* unit. Wants a `concurrency: { group: <workflow>-<ref>, cancel-in-progress: true }` so a superseded run is cancelled rather than raced. Deliberately not done mid-batch: it changes what an in-flight unit's CI monitor observes, so it wants a quiet moment. Note the observed duplicate `pull_request` runs on a single sha were a transient re-dispatch, not a trigger-config defect — the triggers are correct as written.
- **Cluster gossip failure detection — deferred refinements (Unit 7b, #PR).** The first cut ships detection + dissemination + refutation. Follow-ons being built as the **cluster-hardening** set: (1) **SWIM indirect probing** (ping-req via k relays) — **DONE (#PR1)**: a failed direct probe consults up to k=3 relays (`PingReq`/`PingAck`) before counting the failure; each relay answers from its cached liveness view synchronously off the registry actor (no fresh dial — actor-clean, no SSRF/task-spawn surface), so a member reachable through any relay is not falsely suspected. Cuts false positives from one node's bad/asymmetric link. (2) **Member reaping** — **DONE (#PR2)**: a member `Dead` past `REAP_AFTER_DEAD_TICKS=30` reap checks (one per sweep) is removed from the roster and placement rebuilt. Convergent + resurrection-proof + rejoin-safe via a state-based tombstone: a peer still gossiping the reaped member `Dead` cannot re-add it, while a live return (an `Alive` tuple, which only a reachable node produces) escapes — so a crash-restarted node back at incarnation 0 rejoins (an incarnation gate would exile it forever). *Follow-on — tombstone pruning — **DONE (#PR-B)**: the tombstone (`HashMap<NodeId, u32>`, a per-tombstone prune clock) is pruned once retained past `TOMBSTONE_RETENTION_TICKS=300` reap checks — an order of magnitude beyond the 30-tick reap window, so well past any in-flight gossip that could name the member (a reaped member is off the roster and no longer gossiped). Aged on the same per-sweep tick clock as the reap; convergent (shared tick-count rule) and resurrection-safe (a pruned member reappears only as a fresh join). Bounds the set on a long-lived churning cluster.* (3) **Late-joiner replication-dial wiring** — a member learned by gossip after boot now gets a real liveness reading, but the existing nodes' replication *peer-connections* to it are still dialed from the static boot set (`spawn_peers`), so a dynamically-joined node is routed to but not yet replicated to. (4) **Epoch persistence** (from 6b) — **DONE (#PR3)**: the highest leadership epoch seen per room is written to a dedicated durable `.epoch` store file as it advances and reloaded on startup, so a restarted node cannot forget the fence and re-accept a demoted leader's stale-epoch writes. Written only on an advance (off the hot path); store-less deployments are a no-op. (3) **Late-joiner replication-dial** — **DONE (#PR4)**: a follower whose relay link comes up (`PeerLive`) after the leader advanced is caught up — the leader dials the ops it is missing from its acknowledged watermark (whole retained log for a brand-new follower, tail for a reconnecting one), ingested + deduped via the ops path, converging byte-identically before it is routed to. *Follow-ons: (a) below-floor snapshot state-transfer — **DONE (#PR5)**: a follower whose acked watermark is below the compaction floor (a brand-new follower joining a compacted room, or one whose acked position predates a compaction) is caught up by a whole-replica snapshot instead of a futile ops-replay. The leader branches on floor-vs-watermark (the existing `Catchup::Snapshot` arm), sends the current `encode_state` snapshot in a new `ReplicateSnapshot` frame (wire tag 49, epoch-fenced like `Replicate`) tagged with the head sequence; the follower `install_snapshot`-loads it (replacing its replica — a re-sent snapshot is idempotent), lands its sequence at the head, acks, and resumes the ops tail via the steady path, converging byte-identically. Single-frame transfer bounded by the transport's 16 MiB WebSocket max message (chunking a larger state is a follow-on); (b) **wiped-follower self-heal — DONE (#PR-A)**: a follower whose durable state was wiped below its acked watermark now **reports its true durable head** (`durable_heads` = `hub.seq` per owned room) in a self-describing `FollowerHeads` frame (wire tag 50, carries the reporter's node id, intercepted in `deliver` like `Gossip`) on its peer link coming up (`report_heads_to`, driven by `PeerLive`); the leader **honors the reported head over any remembered ack** (`catch_up_follower_reporting` sets the watermark to it — a non-monotonic `set_watermark` that may LOWER it) and re-converges from there (snapshot below the floor #315, else ops tail #314), so majority-ack durability stops counting it for data it can no longer prove. Fail-closed: a room absent from the manifest is head 0 (full catch-up), never trusted at the stale ack.* **All four cluster-hardening follow-ons (1)–(4) are now DONE, and the below-floor state-transfer follow-on (a).** Separately still deferred (out of this set's scope): (c) **snapshot-transfer chunking** — a state exceeding the transport's single-message bound. (d) the larger **transparent-proxy / follower-read-serving** item — a follower serving reads directly rather than converging-but-routing-to-leader — **DONE (#316)**: a caught-up follower serves a READ from its own replica (bounded staleness + read-your-writes/monotonicity via the client's `last_seen_seq` floor), redirecting to the leader on any unsafe read (uncaught, non-replica, past the floor) or a write. *Follow-ons: (a) a pre-echo read-your-writes token — carry the write's server seq on `Accepted` (today only the per-client `OpId.seq`) + a distinct `min_read_seq` — so a stateless writer can gate a fresh follower read on its write's server seq before the fan-out echo lands; a stateful client already holds its own writes, so its cursor covers them. (b) caught-up-leader election — a promotion (Unit 6a HRW-next-live) should prefer the most-caught-up live replica (Raft's up-to-date-log election restriction), so a freshly-promoted effective primary never serves a read behind a client's floor; the follower gate is airtight, but the "leader always serves" boundary is only as strong as the election policy under failover. (c) branch/active-branch replication — a follower serves only the `main` stream (branches aren't replicated), so a named-branch read redirects and a leader-side active-branch override (restore-as-branch) isn't reflected on followers; mirroring branch state would let followers serve non-main reads.*

---

## Loop

`cs-next` (derive worklist from ARCHITECTURE → break down → prioritize → refill when thin) → `cs-implement` (spec-first tests → implement to green → Miri gate → `make fmt` → PR → react to CI + review → squash-merge → update this board; log any forced ARCHITECTURE change to DECISIONS.md) → continue up the chain.
